# DECISIONS

Append-only. Format: `- [phase] decision, one line`.

- [P0] Dev host is Windows 11 with no Docker and no make preinstalled; installed GNU make 4.4.1 via winget (ezwinports.make) and Python 3.12.13 via uv so the spec's `make` gates run literally.
- [P0] `make up` detects `docker compose`; when absent it starts both services natively (uv run uvicorn + npm run dev) in the background and waits for /health. docker-compose.yml is still shipped for Linux/CI.
- [P0] Python dependency management is `uv` with `pyproject.toml` + `uv.lock` (Python 3.12 pinned by `.python-version`); web uses plain `npm` with a committed `package-lock.json`.
- [P0] Agent definitions use the models named in 05 (sonnet/opus); the orchestrator session itself runs on the session model.
- [P0] Frame width is fixed at 6 mm (04 Stage 2) and is not a user slider; it enters the scale formula only when `frame` is on.
- [P0] `rotation_deg` lives on SceneRequest (server-side crop), so rotation changes trigger a /scene refetch like location and radius do; all other params are client-only.
- [P1] SceneRequest: `lat`, `lon`, `radius_m`, `rotation_deg` are required with no schema default (02 lists only `preset_id` as optional); `radius_m` is bounded [250, 3000] and `rotation_deg` [0, 360] per 01's editor table.
- [P1] BakeResult gains two ADDITIVE fields per orchestrator pre-approval: `progress` (number 0..1, optional, default null) and `error` (string or null, optional, default null, names the failing validator). Both are anyOf[T, null] in the schema, Optional in Pydantic, `T | null` (`?:`) in TS.
- [P1] SceneGraph.roads[].class enum is exactly `motorway|primary|secondary|residential|service|path` per 02's contract text, a smaller set than 03's Overpass highway-tag list (trunk, tertiary, unclassified, pedestrian, footway). geo-ingest must bucket the extra OSM highway tags into these six (e.g. trunk->primary, tertiary/unclassified->residential, pedestrian/footway->path) when it implements normalize.py.
- [P1] PrintParams ranges are expressed as fractions matching 02's example values (e.g. `small_scale` 0.5-1.5, `road_scale`/`large_scale` 0.5-2.0, `terrain_exaggeration` 0.0-3.0), not the 0-100 percents shown in 01's prose table. `nozzle_mm` has no 01 slider; bounded [0.1, 1.2] mm as a physical sanity range, default 0.4 per 02.
- [P1] Pydantic field names that collide with JSON keys that are not valid Python identifiers use an alias: `BakeFiles.file_3mf` (alias `"3mf"`) and `Road.class_` (alias `"class"`), with `populate_by_name=True` on every generated model. Any phase serializing these to JSON must call `model_dump(by_alias=True)` (or `by_alias=True` in FastAPI response models) or the wire JSON will show `file_3mf`/`class_` instead of `3mf`/`class`.
- [P1] packages/contracts/gen_py.py and gen_ts.py are hand-written, stdlib-only (no deps) generators that understand exactly the JSON-Schema shapes used by these four schemas ($ref/$defs, enum, anyOf[T,null], type:[T,"null"], array w/ minItems==maxItems as a fixed tuple, object). They are not a general JSON-Schema compiler; extending schema/*.json with a new construct (e.g. a second level of $ref indirection across files, oneOf, allOf) requires extending the generators too.
- [P1] fixtures/chicago-scene.json was authored via a short deterministic Python layout script (grid of 40 buildings in 5 rows x 8 cols, procedurally correct CCW/CW ring winding) rather than typed by hand coordinate-by-coordinate, then verified against scene_graph.json (jsonschema) and round-tripped through the generated Pydantic models; the script was deleted after generating the fixture. It is a hand-authored fixture in the sense required (not fetched from Overpass); geo-ingest will later regenerate it from real data.
- [P1] `make validate` resolves a relative `FILE`/positional-arg path against `$PWD` (the repo root at invocation) into an absolute path before `cd services/bake`, so both `make validate FILE=artifacts/x.3mf` and `make validate artifacts/x.3mf` (per G3's exact command) work when run from the repo root, even though the recipe internally cd's into services/bake per 02/05's literal target shape.
- [P1] On this Windows Git Bash host, bash's `$!`/`kill -0` do not reliably reference real Windows PIDs (verified empirically: a captured `$!` did not match any `tasklist` entry, while the actual uvicorn/npm processes were listening under different PIDs). `make up`'s native path therefore starts each service, then (when `taskkill` is on PATH) discovers the real PID by scanning `netstat -ano` for the service's port and records that PID in `.run/*.pid`; `make down` then `taskkill //PID <pid> //T //F`s it. POSIX hosts (no `taskkill`) keep the plain `$!` capture and `kill`. Verified end-to-end on this host: `make up` (idempotent on rerun) -> both curls 200 -> `make down` frees both ports.
- [P1] Could not verify on this host: `ruff` (both `ruff` and `uv run python -m ruff`) is blocked by a Windows Application Control policy (`WinError 4551`), unrelated to this repo; ruff config is committed and correct, verify in CI or another machine. Docker/`docker compose` is not installed on this host (per project brief); docker-compose.yml and both Dockerfiles are syntactically valid (YAML parses, structure matches 02's spec) but unbuilt and unrun.
- [P1] apps/web pins: next 15.5.24, react/react-dom 19.2.8, typescript 5.9.3 (not npm's newly-tagged 7.x, for eslint-config-next/@typescript-eslint compatibility), eslint 9.39.5 (eslint-config-next 15.5.24's peer range is `^9`, not 10.x, despite npm flagging eslint 9 as past its own support window), tailwindcss/@tailwindcss/postcss 4.3.3, @react-three/fiber 9.7.0 + drei 10.7.8 (peer-compatible with react 19.2.x and three 0.185.1), maplibre-gl 6.6.0, vitest 4.1.11, @playwright/test 1.62.1. `npm run build` and `npm run lint` both verified clean.
- [P1] The "© OpenStreetMap contributors" footer lives in `app/layout.tsx` (wrapping all routes), not only in `page.tsx`, so it stays persistent as web-editor adds more routes; `page.tsx` itself renders the required "ok" text per G1.
- [P1-fix] `gen_py.py` now emits every schema bound it previously dropped: `minimum`/`maximum`/`exclusiveMinimum`/`exclusiveMaximum` become `Field(ge=/le=/gt=/lt=)` and `minItems`/`maxItems` become `Annotated[List[T], Field(min_length=/max_length=)]` (skipped where `minItems == maxItems` already yields a fixed `Tuple`), so the generated Pydantic models reject exactly what jsonschema rejects; FastAPI now enforces the contract's real ranges on POST /scene and POST /bake and publishes them in the OpenAPI schema. Purely additive: no field renamed, removed, or added, so the phase-1 freeze holds.
- [P1-fix] Every generated model now carries `ConfigDict(extra="forbid", populate_by_name=True, serialize_by_alias=True)`. SUPERSEDES the `[P1]` alias line above: `model_dump()` / `model_dump_json()` emit the contract key names `3mf` and `class` with no `by_alias=True`, so the 24 h /scene disk cache, the `<name>.json` bake sidecar and the validator CLI cannot silently write `file_3mf`/`class_`. Passing `by_alias=True` explicitly remains correct and changes nothing.
- [P1-fix] `services/bake/tests/test_contracts.py` gained a schema/model bound-parity suite: 33 out-of-range instances (one per bounded field, both ends where the schema bounds both) asserted rejected by *both* `jsonschema` and the generated model, plus an in-range baseline per schema so the rejection cases cannot pass vacuously, plus four tests that assert default (non-alias) serialization equals the contract JSON (including the full `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` round trip).
- [P1-fix] `make up` no longer adopts a pre-existing listener as its own service: a listener on :8000/:3000 whose PID differs from the one recorded in `.run/<name>.pid` is now a hard error (`error: port <p> already in use by pid <pid> (not started by make up)`, exit 1) instead of being written to the pid file, so `make down` can never `taskkill //T //F` an unrelated user process. A listener whose PID matches the recorded one still reports "already running", so `make up` stays idempotent on rerun.
- [P1-fix] The Makefile catch-all `%: @:` is now scoped with `ifneq (,$(filter validate,$(MAKECMDGOALS)))`, so `make validate artifacts/x.3mf` still works while a mistyped target (`make gaet`, `make tset`) fails with "No rule to make target" instead of exiting 0 - CI/gate typos now fail closed.
- [P1-fix] Added `apps/web/.dockerignore` (node_modules, .next, out, test-results, playwright-report, .env*, *.log) and a root `.dockerignore` (apps/web/node_modules, apps/web/.next, services/bake/.venv, .git, artifacts, .run, **/__pycache__) so `COPY . .` cannot overlay the host's Windows-native native modules (@next/swc-win32-*, lightningcss-win32-*, @tailwindcss/oxide-win32-*) or host build output onto the Linux image, and neither build context uploads node_modules/.venv/.git. Still unverified on this host (no Docker), same caveat as the Dockerfiles themselves.
- [P1-gate] G1 PASS on 2026-08-29: `make up` brought uvicorn :8000 and next dev :3000 up natively; /health 200, / 200. Orchestrator note: never pipe `make up` output through another command (background children hold the pipe); redirect to a file instead.
- [P1] Ring convention for SceneGraph: the first vertex is NOT repeated as the last; consumers close rings implicitly. Exterior CCW, holes CW. geo-ingest emits shapely coords[:-1].
- [P1] stats.height_tag_ratio = count(height_source == "tag") / building_count (0.0 when building_count is 0).
- [P1] OSM highway -> SceneGraph road.class mapping: trunk->motorway, tertiary->secondary, unclassified->residential, pedestrian & footway->path, others verbatim; width_m always comes from the 03 table keyed by the ORIGINAL highway tag (or width/lanes tags).
- [P1] Preset ids and UI labels: chicago-loop "Chicago — Loop", new-york-midtown "New York — Midtown", paris-eiffel "Paris — Tour Eiffel", tokyo-shinjuku "Tokyo — Shinjuku", london-city "London — City", san-francisco-fidi "San Francisco — Financial District". GET /presets returns the six SceneRequest objects (with preset_id); labels live client-side keyed by preset_id.
- [P1] Shared transform math lives in services/bake/app/geom/transform.py (pure Python, no numpy) mirrored by apps/web/lib/transform.ts; web-editor (P4) writes BOTH plus fixtures/parity-scene.json + fixtures/parity-expected.json; mesh-bake (P3) must consume transform.py unchanged. Parity fixture is separate from chicago-scene.json so geo-ingest may regenerate the latter freely.
- [P1] Orchestrator invokes the P2/P4/P3 builder agents with a model override to opus (ultracode mode: correctness over cost); agent definition files keep the 05 models.
- [P4] `scale_mm_per_m` returns PRINT MM PER GROUND METRE, i.e. 04's dimensionless `usable / (span_m * 1000)` multiplied by 1000, because every downstream formula in 04 immediately multiplies by 1000 again; the 04 worked example (1.8 km span, 180 mm plate, frame off) is therefore 0.1 mm/m and both implementations assert it.
- [P4] TS mirrors Python with IDENTICAL snake_case function and field names (`scale_mm_per_m`, `thresholds_ground_m`, `min_wall`, ...) rather than idiomatic camelCase, so `app/geom/transform.py` and `apps/web/lib/transform.ts` can be diffed side by side; no eslint naming-convention rule is enabled so this stays lint-clean, and `contracts.ts` is already snake_case.
- [P4] `transform.py` is stdlib-only and duck-typed via `typing.Protocol` (`ParamsLike`, `BuildingLike`, `RoadLike`, `TreeLike`, `BoundsLike`); it never imports pydantic or the generated contracts, so mesh-bake can pass the real models in unchanged and the module stays trivially mirrorable.
- [P4] `min(0.6, base_thickness/3)` for the engrave depth never binds on the `base/3` branch inside the contract's 2..8 mm base range (2/3 > 0.6), so engraving is always exactly 0.6 mm deep today; the formula is kept verbatim from 04 and the test asserts the constant across the whole legal range.
- [P4] `water_z_mm` returns null when `params.water` is off; `green_z_mm` is always +0.3 because the FROZEN PrintParams has a water toggle but no green toggle. Green is not user-switchable in the MVP.
- [P4] The preview scales by `radius_m_from_bounds(scene.bounds)`, not by the radius slider, so a scene that is stale relative to the slider still renders at its own true scale instead of silently mis-scaling.
- [P4] `building_dropped` approximates 04's post-`buffer(d)` area with a square of the same area grown by `2*d`; a real 2D buffer is unavailable on both sides (stdlib-only Python, no-boolean browser) and this is the cheapest predicate the two implementations can agree on exactly.
- [P4] `select_tree_indices` sorts by (-radius, original index) and returns survivors in ascending index order, so the 2000-tree cap picks the same trees in Python and TS regardless of sort stability.
- [P4] `fixtures/parity-scene.json` is the phase-1 hand-authored Chicago fixture PLUS three appended buildings (a 3x80 m sliver, a 3x3 m shed, a 12x12x210 m tower), three larger trees and a 5x5 m green patch, so the parity dump exercises dilation, the tall/short multiplier split, tree visibility and the sub-detail area drop across all three parameter cases. It is deliberately separate from `chicago-scene.json`, which geo-ingest now regenerates from real Overpass data.
- [P4] `fixtures/parity-expected.json` is generated by `services/bake/tests/test_transform.py` (floats rounded to 9 dp) and asserted equal on every run; regenerate with `FRAMECRAFT_WRITE_PARITY=1 uv run pytest tests/test_transform.py`. The TS side compares ground-metre quantities after conversion to print mm (`diff * scale`), because 0.01 mm is a print tolerance.
- [P4] The preview's minimum-feature approximation is per-footprint only: a footprint whose hydraulic diameter is under `min_wall_ground` is widened by `2*d` on both axes and floored at `min_wall_ground`. It cannot merge neighbouring blocks (that needs a boolean), so the canvas carries "Preview is approximate: the bake merges buildings closer than X m" with X = the real `min_gap_ground`.
- [P4] Sub-millimetre recesses (water -0.5 mm, engraved roads -0.6 mm) are drawn ON the base top in the preview instead of cut into it: cutting needs a boolean the browser must never run, and a surface under an opaque slab would simply be invisible. `lib/transform.ts` still reports the true signed offsets and the bake still cuts them; the on-canvas note says so.
- [P4] Buildings are previewed as the minimum-area oriented bounding rectangle of the footprint (convex hull + rotating calipers), not the real polygon, so one InstancedMesh of a unit box holds 5000 buildings and a height slider rewrites only `instanceMatrix` (measured 0.06 ms for 994 buildings, 1.6 ms for 5000).
- [P4] `nozzle_mm` is exposed as a "Printer" slider even though it is not in 01's editor table: it drives every minimum-feature threshold and therefore visibly changes the preview, so hiding it would make the dilation warnings unexplainable. Range taken from the contract's PARAM_RANGES.
- [P4] Radius and rotation re-generate on pointer/key release (both re-frame the same place); moving the PIN (map click or pin drag) only marks the scene stale and turns Generate into Regenerate, so a stray map click never costs an Overpass query. Preset clicks generate immediately (01/A1).
- [P4] The crop square drawn on the map is the axis-aligned square of side `2*radius` rotated `+rotation_deg` counter-clockwise in the ENU frame (x east, y north), the inverse of the `-rotation_deg` the server applies to the points before cropping.
- [P4] MAPLIBRE WORKER FIX: maplibre-gl 6 resolves its worker with `new URL("./maplibre-gl-worker.mjs", import.meta.url)`; Next's bundler rewrites `import.meta.url` to the document URL, the worker request lands on the app's 404 HTML, and the module worker dies SILENTLY - raster tiles keep working while every GeoJSON source stays permanently empty (this is exactly why the radius circle and crop square rendered nothing). Fixed by copying the worker plus its shared chunk into `public/maplibre/` from `predev`/`prebuild` (`apps/web/scripts/copy-maplibre-worker.mjs`, output gitignored) and calling `setWorkerUrl("/maplibre/maplibre-gl-worker.mjs")`. Any maplibre upgrade must keep both halves.
- [P4] Water/green are merged into ONE BufferGeometry per layer (earcut via `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape`, constant +z normals) instead of one mesh per polygon: a 900 m Chicago crop has 711 green polygons and per-polygon meshes meant ~700 draw calls per frame. Road ribbons reuse their position buffer in place when the triangle count is unchanged, which it is for every scale change.
- [P4] `vitest.config.ts` gained a `resolve.alias` for `@/*` mirroring tsconfig paths so unit tests import the same specifiers the app does; `eslint.config.mjs` now ignores `public/**` (the vendored maplibre worker).
- [P4] The theme is applied by a tiny inline pre-paint script in `app/layout.tsx` (localStorage key `framecraft-theme`, OS preference on first run) and Tailwind v4's `dark:` variant is redefined with `@custom-variant dark (&:where(.dark, .dark *))` so the class toggle - not `prefers-color-scheme` - drives it.
- [P4] Canned `BakeResult` objects exist only in `lib/bake.test.ts`; no app-code path fabricates one. With mesh-bake's `/bake` still absent the UI shows the real error ("/bake failed (HTTP 404): Not Found") rather than a mock, per the no-placeholder rule.
- [P4] Verified on this host against real geo-ingest data (994-building Chicago, 2905-building Paris): 0 `/scene` calls from any PrintParams slider, 1 per preset click, 1 per rotation release. Preview build from the real SceneGraph measures 4 ms end to end (buildBuildings 1.7, buildRoads 2.8, earcut 1.5, instance matrices 0.06), far inside 02's 1.5 s build and 33 ms slider budgets. Absolute frame times could NOT be measured here: headless Chromium falls back to SwiftShader and one idle frame of the Chicago preview costs ~45 ms on its own, so fps must be re-measured on real GPU hardware.
- [P4] Chicago and Paris both preview with 0 trees: 04 only emits a tree whose scaled site radius reaches 0.5 mm, and OSM tree nodes there carry radii well under the ~5.4 m that needs at a 180 mm plate. Preview and bake agree; it is not a preview bug.
- [P4] Running `next build` while `next dev` is up corrupts the dev server's `.next` (chunk 404s, page 500s). `make gate` must not build while `make up` is running; restart dev after a build.
- [P2] Overpass bbox: 03's 15% margin is applied to the rotated crop's actual reach, `half = radius_m * (|cos t| + |sin t|) * 1.15` with `t = rotation_deg mod 90`. That is literally `1.15 * radius_m` for the five presets at rotation 0/90/180/270; without the reach factor the 29 deg New York crop would come back with empty corners (it needs 1.36 x radius before any margin). The bbox is built by projecting the metric square's edges (16 samples per edge, not just corners) back to WGS84, so the UTM edge bulge cannot clip data.
- [P2] `rotation_deg` convention: the geometry is rotated counter-clockwise by `rotation_deg` about the origin, i.e. the compass bearing `rotation_deg` (deg CW from north) ends up pointing +y in the model. New York's 29 deg therefore squares the Manhattan avenue grid to the plate. web-editor's picker overlay must draw the crop square rotated CW by the same angle.
- [P2] Projection runs *before* the seven hygiene steps (every 03 tolerance is metric: 0.25 m simplify, 0.5 m centroid dedupe) and the rotation is folded into the same vectorized numpy/pyproj pass. Rotation is rigid and every hygiene op is rotation invariant, so 03's "rotate, then clip to the axis-aligned square" is preserved exactly; only the clip happens after hygiene.
- [P2] Hygiene step 7 ("union overlapping building footprints that touch") is implemented as: union only footprints whose *interiors* share area greater than `max(0.25 m2, 2% of the smaller footprint)`. Buildings that merely share a wall (a Paris block, 2905 buildings, 303 with courtyards) keep their own heights - merging them into one prism at the tallest member's height would flatten the skyline, and 04's assemble unions them anyway.
- [P2] Merged groups (dedupe step 6 and union step 7) keep the largest-area member's id and geometry footprint, the tallest member's `height_m` + `height_source`, and the minimum `min_height_m`. Emitted ids stay unique per scene: `w<id>` / `r<id>` / `n<id>`, with a `-1`, `-2` suffix when the crop splits one way into several parts.
- [P2] Polygons smaller than 1.0 m2 (after hygiene and again after the crop) and road fragments shorter than 1.0 m are dropped as print-scale noise; 03's step 1 "zero area" test stays literal (`area <= 0`) and is separate from this threshold.
- [P2] Coverage: 03 does not say what `building_count >= 150` with under 4% footprint area is; it is classified `sparse` (only count AND area together earn `good`). All six presets measure `good`.
- [P2] Height inference extensions, all inside 03's rule order: rule 1 also reads `building:height` (same meaning, same `height_source == "tag"`), rule 3 also honours an explicit `min_height` tag, and the rule-4 table adds `garages` (3 m) next to `garage`. `building=no`/`building=false` is not a building. Jitter is `sha1(osm_id)[:8]` mapped uniformly into +/-6%.
- [P2] Water and green are dissolved per layer with `unary_union` after hygiene (an OSM relation and its member ways both come back in one response, so undissolved layers print coincident slabs); buildings are never dissolved that way, they go through steps 6 and 7 so each keeps its own height.
- [P2] Roads are simplified with the same 0.25 m tolerance as polygons, clipped by intersection, and MultiLineString results are split into separate entries. Width is `width` tag, else `lanes * 3.5`, else the 03 table keyed by the ORIGINAL highway tag; `class` follows the [P1] mapping.
- [P2] Emitted coordinates are rounded to 1 mm (3 decimals in meters) with consecutive duplicates removed and no repeated closing vertex; a ring whose rounded form is not a valid `Polygon(ring, holes)` drops its holes, and if it is still invalid the part is dropped (never observed on the six fixtures: 0 invalid out of 16 828 footprints).
- [P2] POST /scene caches: an in-process LRU of 6 SceneGraphs (~14 MB each, so the six presets stay warm; measured 7-12 ms warm, 32 ms from disk, 1.7 s cold from a fixture) plus `artifacts/cache/scene/<sha256-of-request>.json` with a 24 h mtime TTL. The parsed raw Overpass cache holds only 2 responses because a parsed fixture is 35-85 MB in memory.
- [P2] `preset_id` semantics on POST /scene: a request that names a preset AND matches its lat/lon/radius/rotation is served from the committed fixture with the network disabled; a request that names a preset but has edited values is no longer that preset and takes the normal cache-then-network path; an unknown `preset_id` is a 400. Error mapping: `OverpassOffline` -> 503, `OverpassUnavailable` / `FixtureMissing` -> 502 with the reason in `detail`.
- [P2] `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE=1` (set by `services/bake/tests/conftest.py` for the whole suite) makes every Overpass network path raise `OverpassOffline`, so the tests provably run from the committed fixtures; the preset /scene test additionally monkeypatches `overpass._post` to a raising sentinel and asserts it was never called.
- [P2] The six raw Overpass fixtures are committed under their sha1-of-query names (6.9-20.6 MB each, ~76 MB total, none near the 60 MB note threshold) plus `fixtures/presets-index.json` (preset_id -> request, fixture_file, fetched_at, element_count, bytes) so a human can find them. `refresh-fixtures` deletes sha1-named fixtures no preset points at any more (`--keep-stale` opts out) and pauses 5 s between fetches per the Overpass usage policy.
- [P2] `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` is regenerated from the real Chicago fixture at rotation 0 via `python -m app.cli scene --preset chicago-loop --compact --out ...`: 994 buildings, coverage `good`, 1.3 MB of compact (single-line) JSON rather than 2.9 MB indented, since it is machine-generated and regenerable. It still validates against `scene_graph.json` and still round-trips through the generated models, so test_contracts.py stays green.
- [P2] `app/cli.py` registers each subcommand in its own `_add_<name>(sub)` function that attaches a `handler` default; P3 adds `_add_bake`, qa-gate adds `_add_validate`, and `main()` (which also swallows `BrokenPipeError` from `| head`) does not change.
- [P2-fix] Overpass bbox: the sampled ring is built in the ROTATED frame at `radius_m * 1.15` and `LocalFrame.to_wgs84` un-rotates it, which is what produces DECISIONS [P2]'s `(|cos t| + |sin t|)` reach; the old code multiplied the ring by that reach as well, so the fetched window was `1.15 * r * reach^2` (New York 1941 m instead of 1407 m half-extent, ~2x the area and ~2x the timeout risk). `unrotated_reach_m()` documents the invariant and the test asserts the measured half-extent is within 2% of `1.15 * 900 * (cos29 + sin29)`. Only the New York query text changes; its fixture was re-fetched (new sha1 `f41c2522...`, 12.9 MB / 16 998 elements, down from 20.6 MB / 28 130) and `presets-index.json` regenerated. The other five sha1s are byte-identical, and NY's normalized scene is unchanged (2 683 buildings, same height_tag_ratio), i.e. the discarded 40% of the response was all outside the crop.
- [P2-fix] An Overpass HTTP 200 whose body carries a `runtime error` remark (documented for query timeout and out-of-memory) - or any remark next to an empty `elements` list - is a FAILED ATTEMPT, not an empty city: `overpass.fatal_remark()` detects it, `_parse` raises `OverpassUnavailable(switch_mirror=True)` for the timeout/OOM shapes so the loop falls back to the kumi.systems mirror exactly as a 504 does, and the fixture file is written only after `_parse` accepts the body. `_parse` now runs INSIDE the per-attempt `try`, so a proxy's HTML 200 also gets the retry policy instead of escaping on attempt 1. `load_raw` additionally refuses to serve an already-cached error body (raises `FixtureMissing` when the network is off, re-fetches when it is on), because the fixture cache is consulted before the network and has no TTL.
- [P2-fix] A `way` that is the `outer` member of a `building` multipolygon relation is dropped as a standalone building during classification (03 step 6 anticipates that both come back). Old-style OSM tagging repeats `building=*` on that way, the way has no inner rings, and it used to win - through step 6's dedupe below 5% and through step 7's union above it - which printed the courtyard solid (london-city r1439717 Haberdashers Hall was emitted as w100835946, 1 556 m2 with 0 holes, instead of 1 213 m2 with 1 hole).
- [P2-fix] SUPERSEDES the step-6 half of the earlier [P2] "merged groups keep the largest-area member's id and geometry" line: dedupe now compares HOLE-LESS (exterior) areas, so a holed relation still matches its outer way, and the group keeps the geometry AND id of the member with the most interiors (largest area breaks the tie). Step 7's union is unchanged and still keeps the largest-area member's id. Heights still come from the tallest member.
- [P2-fix] Geometry is snap-rounded onto the 1 mm emission grid with `shapely.set_precision(g, 0.001)` (GEOS topology-repairing) at the end of `_clean` and again after the crop, i.e. BEFORE validity is judged, and `_rings_of` now repairs a still-invalid rounded polygon with `make_valid` and emits its largest polygonal part instead of dropping the holes or the part. A hole drafted onto its own exterior used to leave an epsilon sliver that `simplify` preserved and mm-rounding turned into a ring self-intersection, dropping the building (edge contact) or all of its holes (corner contact); it is now emitted as a clean notch. The hole-drop and part-drop fallbacks fire 0 times over all six fixtures (paris-eiffel previously lost 2 courtyards).
- [P2-fix] Tag parsers reject non-finite values (`parse_length_m` and `parse_levels` return None unless `math.isfinite`), and `road_width_m` is clamped to [0.5, 60] m like the height and tree-radius rules, so `lanes=1e308` or a 400-digit `width` can no longer put `inf` on the frozen SceneGraph wire (FastAPI serialised it as `"width_m": null`, which fails re-validation). `main._cache_put` writes the scene cache with `json.dumps(..., allow_nan=False)` so a non-finite value can never be persisted as non-standard JSON.
- [P2-fix] `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` regenerated after the snap-rounding change (identical stats: 994 buildings, `good`, height_tag_ratio 0.111; 956 footprints move by at most 1 mm). Re-running `refresh-fixtures --preset new-york-midtown` also pruned two orphan sha1 fixtures that no preset pointed at any more (the old NY one and `67ba0986...`), which is that command's documented stale-fixture behaviour.
- [P4-fix] SUPERSEDES the [P4] crop-square line: the server applies `+rotation_deg` CCW to the GEOMETRY (`project.py` `to_local`: `xr = x cos - y sin`, `yr = x sin + y cos`) and then clips the axis-aligned square, so the patch of world that prints is that square rotated CLOCKWISE by `rotation_deg` - exactly what [P2] always said the picker overlay must draw. `cropSquareRing` now applies the inverse rotation (`east = x cos + y sin`, `north = -x sin + y cos`); the old CCW overlay only coincided with the printed crop at multiples of 45 deg (New York's 29 deg put the drawn corners at 1.378 x radius in the model frame). `geo.test.ts` now asserts the SW corner swings from bearing 225 to 270 at +45 deg and, for 7 angles including 29, that every drawn corner lands on `(+-r, +-r)` after `to_local`'s `+theta`.
- [P4-fix] `AreaSurfaces` builds its vertex array AFTER `ShapeUtils.triangulateShape`, not before: `Shape.extractPoints()` repeats each contour's first point and `triangulateShape` pops those duplicates IN PLACE (`removeDupEndPts`) before numbering hole vertices off the shortened outline, so the old pre-built array shifted every hole index by one per preceding contour (verified on three 0.185.1: a 100x100 ring with a 20x20 island came out 9800 instead of 9600 with 7 triangles covering the island). `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` has 4 holed water/green polygons, so the preview really did paint islands over.
- [P4-fix] The triangulation is exported from `components/scene/AreaSurfaces.tsx` as the pure `areaTrianglePositions(areas)` and tested there, rather than moved into `lib/preview.ts` as first suggested: `preview.ts`'s stated rule 3 is "no three.js", and the earcut call needs `Shape`/`ShapeUtils`. `AreaSurfaces.test.ts` asserts merged area == ring area - hole area and that no triangle contains a hole centroid, for one hole, two holes and two merged areas.
- [P4-fix] Every `CityPreview` memo is now keyed through the exported `previewDeps` map, which names only primitives read off `params` plus the identity-stable `graph` - never `params` itself and never the `Thresholds` object derived from it. `store.setParam` rebuilds `params` by spread on every write, so the old `thresholds = useMemo(..., [params, scale])` handed the water/green memos a new object on every height-slider tick and re-ran earcut over 711 green polygons, re-allocated a BufferGeometry and re-uploaded it to the GPU per frame of a drag, against 02's "rebuild only the affected instance buffer when a slider moves".
- [P4-fix] `Controls.Slider` commits through `createCommitGate`: `onChange` marks it dirty, release commits only if dirty, and `onKeyUp` is filtered by `isValueChangingKey` (Arrow*/Home/End/PageUp/PageDown, no modifier chords). Tabbing into #radius_m, on into #rotation_deg and releasing Shift used to cost three POST /scene calls with zero value change; at a non-preset location each is a live 6.6-11.5 s Overpass query.
- [P4-fix] The commit is additionally debounced by `COMMIT_DEBOUNCE_MS = 250`, so five quick ArrowRight taps cost ONE POST /scene instead of five (four of which the client aborted without stopping the server's Overpass query). The gate is cancelled on unmount. The alternative short-circuit inside `store.generate()` (skip when the scene is ready, not stale and the request deep-equals) was deliberately NOT added: it would silently turn the Generate button into a no-op on a ready scene, and the gate already removes every no-change request.
- [P4-fix] apps/web has no jsdom/@testing-library, and adding them for these fixes was rejected as out of proportion; `CityPreview.test.ts` and `Controls.test.ts` therefore drive the exact helpers the components wire (`previewDeps.*`, `createCommitGate`, `isValueChangingKey`) and replay React's own `Object.is`-per-dep memo rule, rather than re-implementing the logic under test.
- [P2-gate] G2 PASS on 2026-08-29: 123 ingest tests green; Chicago preset /scene -> 994 buildings, coverage good, height_tag_ratio 0.11, 35 ms cold (fixture) / 8 ms warm.
- [P3] mesh-bake implements the `bake` and `validate` subcommands of services/bake/app/cli.py so that G3 (`make bake-fixture && make validate ...`) can run before qa-gate; qa-gate extends the validator table and adds 3MF structural checks rather than rewriting it.
- [P3] Bake CPU work runs in a worker thread (asyncio.to_thread) from the in-process job registry so GET /bake/{id} stays responsive; progress is reported through BakeResult.progress.
- [P3] `services/bake/pyproject.toml` gains four runtime deps, all needed by code 04 mandates: `scipy` and `networkx` (trimesh's `section`/`body_count`/path traversal are optional-dependency gated and raise `ModuleNotFoundError` without them), `lxml` (trimesh's 3MF reader, i.e. `make validate artifacts/chicago.3mf`), and `fast-simplification` (trimesh's `simplify_quadric_decimation`, 04's triangle-budget remedy). `rtree` is deliberately NOT added: `Path2D.polygons_full` is the only thing that wants it and the validator does its own nesting.
- [P3] `fast_simplification`'s native `_simplify` DLL is blocked on this host by the same Windows Application Control policy that blocks `ruff` (DECISIONS [P1]); `enforce_triangle_budget` therefore catches the import failure and falls back to bisecting `manifold3d.Manifold.simplify` onto the same 1.5M target, which keeps the result manifold by construction. Both branches are exercised by `test_validator_triangle_budget_and_the_decimation_path`.
- [P3] Stage 1 works in ground metres on shapely and converts to print millimetres exactly once, in `extrude.contours_mm`; every threshold, height and Z offset comes from `app/geom/transform.py` unchanged (no function was added to it, so the TS mirror and the parity fixtures are untouched).
- [P3] Merged-block `is_tall` is INHERITED from the contributing footprint that supplied the 80th-percentile height, never recomputed, so 02's "computed once in the SceneGraph" still holds for a synthetic block that has no SceneGraph entity.
- [P3] The area-weighted 80th percentile is defined as the smallest contributing height whose cumulative footprint area reaches 80% of the component's total, ties broken on the original index. No interpolation: the block height is always a height some real building actually has.
- [P3] A preserved tall footprint (over 1.5x the block height) is extruded from `block_top - 0.2 mm`, not from the block top exactly. 04 says "from the block top up"; the extra 0.2 mm is the same overlap 04 already prescribes between buildings and the base and exists so the two solids interpenetrate instead of meeting on a coincident face. The printed shape is identical because the block below is solid.
- [P3] Preserved tall footprints are clipped to the union of the blocks that SURVIVED the final crop and min-feature filters, so a tower whose block was dropped can never be left as a floating solid.
- [P3] 04's hydraulic-diameter dilation under-widens strips (for a strip of width t the hydraulic diameter is 2t, so the rule leaves it `min_wall - t` wide). Rather than drop such a building, `thicken.widen_to_min_wall` repeats the dilation in tenths of a wall until the footprint survives the same erosion probe the Stage 4 validator runs. Chicago: 3 buildings dropped instead of 29, 443 widened.
- [P3] Stage 1 additionally drops any component that vanishes under `buffer(-0.45 * min_wall)` after the final crop (a clipped sliver at the plate edge). That is byte-for-byte the Stage 4 `min_wall` fail rule, so the repair and the gate agree by construction instead of by luck.
- [P3] Every Stage 1 layer is snap-rounded onto a 0.01 mm PRINT grid (`shapely.set_precision`, converted to ground metres by the scene scale), then opened by two grid cells with the components kept SEPARATE (so a hairline limb is judged on its own and dropped), then re-snapped, then stripped of collinear vertices. Reason: OSM geometry arrives on a 1 mm ground grid, which at 1:10000 is 1e-4 mm of print and feeds the boolean engine nanometre slivers. This alone took a Chicago bake from 1931 degenerate faces to 69.
- [P3] Where one layer is differenced out of another the subtrahend is first grown by 0.02 mm of print (`thicken.separate`), so no two solids ever meet on an exactly coincident vertical face. Coincident faces were the single largest source of boolean slivers (~1800 of Chicago's 1931).
- [P3] Layer precedence is water (deepest recess) -> roads -> green, each differenced against the ones before it. An EMBOSSED road is additive so it is also differenced against the water cutter, otherwise the cutter slices the raised ribbon and the T-junctions it inserts on the ribbon's top face retriangulate into zero-area caps. Green always gives way to roads for the same reason.
- [P3] 04 does not close the road layer, but an engraved road is a groove and what prints is its COMPLEMENT: two grooves less than a wall apart leave a ridge of base no nozzle can lay down. The road layer (and the water layer) are therefore closed at `min_wall/2`, and `thicken.merge_recess_ridges` additionally measures every island of the recesses' complement with the validator's own erosion probe and hands the failing ones to the deepest recess (grown by four grid cells first, because such an island can have literally zero area).
- [P3] Trees are also excluded where they would stand in water, not only on a building or a road as 04 says, so a recess cutter can never undercut a cone and leave it floating.
- [P3] `assemble.finalize` runs after the union: drop zero-volume boolean debris (`decompose` + volume filter), `Manifold.simplify(1e-6 mm)`, and if any face under 1e-9 mm^2 survives, weld vertices that agree to 1e-6..1e-3 mm and re-import through manifold3d - accepting the repair only when it returns `NoError` with the same volume to 1e-6 relative. Chicago needs the weld; five of the six presets do not.
- [P3] Subtractive slabs (engraved roads, water) run from their recess depth up to `base_top + 0.5 mm`, which clears every additive surface feature (green +0.3, emboss +0.4) so a recess cuts through them, and never reaches z = 0 (04 caps engraving at `base_thickness/3` and the contract floors the base at 2 mm).
- [P3] The `min_wall` validator counts a slice region as a WALL only if it still holds 70% of its area 0.25 mm higher. 04 models trees as cones and caps the tree radius at 0.5 mm printed, i.e. 04 itself permits sub-min-wall cones; without the persistence test every scene with a tree would fail on its own apex. For an 8-gon cone of slope 1/3 the test excludes exactly the band whose hydraulic width is under 0.94 mm, which is above the 0.72 mm fail threshold, so no genuinely thin vertical wall is excluded.
- [P3] `min_wall` reports the hydraulic width `4*area/perimeter` (the same measure 04 stage 1 uses, so repair and gate speak one language; for a long strip that is twice the caliper width) and FAILS on the erosion probe `buffer(-0.45 * min_wall).is_empty`, which is a true geometric width test. Both must hold.
- [P3] The min-wall probe slices with `manifold3d.Manifold.slice`, not `trimesh.section`, whenever a Manifold can be built from the mesh (always, in practice). `trimesh.section` returns a ring soup, and two solids that touch at a single point - two preserved towers on one block do exactly that - come back as ONE self-intersecting ring; any even-odd nesting of it reads the courtyard beside the pinch as solid and the tower as a 6-micrometre sliver. `Manifold.slice` is exact, keeps holes as holes and is ~5x faster. `validate.section_polygons` keeps the `trimesh.section` path as the documented fallback and a test asserts the two agree.
- [P3] "Watertight ... euler_number consistent" is implemented as `euler_number` even and `<= 2 * body_count` (a closed orientable surface has chi = 2*(B - genus), so equality only when every body has genus 0). Body count comes from `Manifold.decompose()` when the solid is in hand and from `trimesh.body_count` otherwise.
- [P3] The self-intersection check records manifold3d's guarantee plus two independently computed pieces of evidence: every edge shared by exactly two faces with no duplicate triangles (complete, O(F log F)), and a deterministic grid-broad-phase sample of up to 6000 faces / 400k pairs tested for edge-triangle crossings (a spot check, as 04 allows).
- [P3] The 3MF writes vertex coordinates as `%.12f` fixed point, never `%g`. At 6 significant digits trimesh reloaded the Chicago plate as NON-manifold: rounding merged distinct vertices. The `validate` CLI additionally loads with `process=False`, because trimesh's default load pass welds within 1e-8 and would repair the very topology the command exists to judge.
- [P3] Only 3MF core reserved metadata names are written (Title, Designer, Description, Copyright, LicenseTerms, Application, CreationDate); `write_3mf` raises on anything else, since a non-reserved name needs its own XML namespace. `read_metadata` refuses a model part over 512 MB or one carrying a DOCTYPE (billion-laughs) before it parses.
- [P3] A bake whose validators fail writes NOTHING to the download directory: the .3mf/.stl/.json are moved into `artifacts/debug/<job_id>/` alongside every intermediate solid (base, frame, buildings, roads, water, green, trees, final) as STL, and `BakeResult.files` stays null with `error` naming the failing check. 04: "do not silently ship".
- [P3] Sliding `large_scale` to 2.0 on a tall city legitimately fails the `bounding_box` validator (Chicago reaches 66.5 mm against 04's 60 mm ceiling). The bake does NOT clamp the height: clamping would silently diverge from the preview, which is 01's worst failure mode. The failure message names the height and says which slider to lower; a pre-bake guard belongs in the editor.
- [P3] `POST /bake` answers 202 with `{job_id}` (10 hex chars from `secrets.token_hex(5)`), runs the job as an `asyncio.create_task` that takes an `asyncio.Semaphore(2)` and then `asyncio.to_thread`s both the scene build and the pipeline. Task references are held in a module-level set (asyncio only keeps weak references). `GET /bake/{id}` is 404 for unknown and otherwise always 200 - a failed bake is `status: "failed"` with `error`, never a 500.
- [P3] `POST /bake`'s body model (`scene_request`, `print_params`) is declared in `app/main.py`, not in `packages/contracts/`: 02 specifies the endpoint body but the frozen schema set has no `BakeRequest`. `print_params` defaults to the contract defaults so a client may omit it. Nothing in the four frozen schemas was touched.
- [P3] `POST /scene`'s body was refactored into a reusable `main.build_scene(request)` so the bake job resolves a preset, hits the same 24 h disk cache and the same LRU as the preview does; a bake and a preview can never look at different geometry for one request.
- [P3] mesh-bake implements BOTH `app/cli.py` subcommands (`bake` and `validate`), superseding the [P2] note that qa-gate would add `_add_validate`: G3 (`make bake-fixture && make validate ...`) has to run before qa-gate exists. qa-gate should extend the table, not rewrite it.
- [P3] Measured on this host (Windows 11, 32 cores, uv/py3.12), bake only, excluding the scene build: chicago-loop 994 buildings 2.9 s, new-york-midtown 2683 in 2.7 s, paris-eiffel 2905 in 4.5 s, tokyo-shinjuku 5401 in 6.6 s, london-city 2197 in 5.8 s, san-francisco-fidi 2648 in 3.7 s. All six pass every validator at default params. 02's budget is 90 s; the batched tree union never showed up in a profile (0.2-0.7 s of every bake), shapely Stage 1 dominates at 60-70%.
- [P3-fix] SUPERSEDES the [P3] line "`min_wall` reports the hydraulic width 4*area/perimeter ... and FAILS on the erosion probe ... Both must hold": the gate now uses ONE measure for both the number and the verdict, `2 * shapely.maximum_inscribed_circle(region, 0.01*min_wall).length` (GEOS 3.9+, this host has 3.13.1), and fails on `width < 0.9 * min_wall`. The hydraulic clause is gone. It failed a jagged base island whose 4A/P was 0.49 mm while the island was 0.78 mm wide everywhere it mattered (radius 3000 m: "0 of 281 sampled regions are under 0.720 mm" - a failure with nothing failing), and the erosion probe `buffer(-0.36).is_empty` failed a 3-vertex building triangle 0.84 mm across the moment `Manifold.slice` put three T-junction vertices 0.3-0.7 um off its edges (rotation 37 deg). The inscribed circle is exact, insensitive to extra near-collinear vertices and to a jagged boundary. `BakeStats.min_wall_mm` keeps its name and is now a true caliper reading.
- [P3-fix] Width is measured PER APPENDAGE, not per region: `thicken.narrowest_width` lowers a region's inscribed width by the inscribed width of every part the morphological opening at `0.45*min_wall` leaves behind. "Does `buffer(-0.45*min_wall)` leave anything?" is a question about the whole region, so a 0.5 mm wing on a 40 mm block passed both Stage 1 and the Stage 4 gate; chicago-loop shipped 18 such wings over 1 mm long and 1 mm tall (5 of them over 10 mm tall) and tokyo-shinjuku 16. Stage 1 now applies 04's dilation rule to the wing itself (`widen_thin_parts`, after the layer closing) and cuts it where dilation is illegal (`strip_thin_parts`, after the crop and on the additive surface layers). The audit's own `artifacts/audit/thinwalls.py` now counts 0 residues on chicago-loop, chicago at plate 100, chicago at nozzle 0.6 and london-city, and 1 zero-height one on tokyo-shinjuku, down from 29 / 21 / 21 / 16.
- [P3-fix] Stage 1 repairs to a FULL `min_wall` (`MIN_WALL_REPAIR_FACTOR = 1.0`, which is 04's own dilation target) while Stage 4 fails only under `0.9 * min_wall`. The margin is load-bearing, not cosmetic: the repair measures in ground metres on shapely and the gate measures in print millimetres on slices of a boolean result, through a 0.01 mm snap grid and a 1%-of-a-wall inscribed-circle tolerance. Repairing exactly to the gate's 0.9 left regions reading 0.720 mm against a 0.720 mm threshold and left every preset with a handful of 0.68-0.71 mm wings.
- [P3-fix] The opening residue needs two noise filters and both are load-bearing. (a) The opening is grown by `RESIDUE_EDGE_TOLERANCE = 0.02 * min_wall` before the difference: GEOS' offset curve does not reproduce a long straight edge to the last bit, so `poly - opening` carries a hairline rind a few micrometres wide whose AREA over 30 mm of edge (0.043 mm^2) is that of a real wing while its width is that of nothing at all. (b) A residue part must reach `residue_area_floor = 0.25 * min_detail^2`, not `min_detail^2`: GEOS splits one 4 mm x 0.17 mm wing into two 0.23 mm^2 halves in ground metres and leaves it whole (0.46 mm^2) in print millimetres, which straddles the full floor and made the repair blind to a wing the gate then measured (chicago at nozzle 0.5).
- [P3-fix] Every appendage pass is iterated (`APPENDAGE_ROUNDS = 4`, breaking as soon as a round finds nothing, so the extra rounds are free): widening a wing fills a notch and cutting one opens a notch, so a single pass is never enough and a pass that ends by repairing has not verified itself. A single strip left tokyo-shinjuku with a 0.477 mm wing on a block the strip had just cut.
- [P3-fix] `thicken.repair_slice_profiles` repairs thin walls that exist only in a horizontal SLICE. What prints at height z is the union of the solids that reach that high, and two preserved towers (04 stage 1, buildings 5) that overlap by a corner make a 0.5 mm neck above their block that neither footprint has on its own. Sorted by printed top, the solids present at z are exactly a PREFIX of the sorted list, so walking the prefixes checks every cross-section the model can have; each neck gets 04's dilation rule and the patch is given to the shortest solid of the prefix (clipped to the block union when it belongs to a stacked solid, so a patch can never be left floating). This is the only place Stage 1 needs `PrintParams`, which is why `repair_buildings` now takes `params` and `scale`.
- [P3-fix] `_drop_unprintable` (and therefore `finish_layer`) takes `strip_thin`, and it is FALSE for a subtractive layer - engraved roads, water. What prints there is the layer's COMPLEMENT, so a thin limb of the cutter is a harmless groove, while cutting it away leaves a new unprintable ridge of base beside it. Those complements are measured by `merge_recess_ridges` instead, which now also hands over the thin APPENDAGES of an otherwise-fat city block (the wedge where two grooves converge), runs up to 4 passes instead of 2, and measures the complement over the whole BASE PLATE instead of the crop square - a groove near the crop edge leaves a nub hanging off the 6 mm band outside the crop, and clipping the complement at the crop turned that nub into part of a fat island.
- [P3-fix] `checks.WALL_PERSIST_MM` (the look-ahead of the "wall or tapering tip?" test) becomes ONE PRINTED LAYER: `wall_persist_mm(min_wall) = 0.625 * nozzle`, which is exactly the old 0.25 mm at the default 0.4 mm nozzle. A cone's cross-section shrinks by a third of the look-ahead per layer, so a FIXED look-ahead made the band of a tree that got MEASURED start at a fixed 0.51 mm radius - under one wall for every nozzle above ~0.45 mm. That, and not the tree size, is why every scene with a visible tree failed `min_wall` from nozzle 0.5 up; no tree radius can fix it, because every cone tapers through that band. Verified: lone cones of radius 0.55-5.0 mm pass at nozzle 0.1, 0.4, 0.5, 0.6, 0.8 and 1.2, and the widths reported at 0.4 are identical to before to the last digit.
- [P3-fix] `thicken.tree_min_radius_mm(params) = max(TREE_MIN_RADIUS_MM, min_wall / (2*cos(pi/8)))` is a bake-only floor on the printed tree radius, applied in `select_trees` with a warning naming it. 04 caps the tree radius at 0.5 mm printed and says nothing about the nozzle, but an 8-gon of circumradius r is only `2 r cos(pi/8)` wide across its flats, so at the 0.5 mm floor the BASE of the cone is 0.92 mm - under one extruded bead from a 0.5 mm nozzle up. At the default 0.4 mm nozzle the floor is 0.433 mm, so 04's 0.5 mm still binds and nothing changes. `transform.py` and the parity fixtures are untouched: like the "does not intersect a building or a road" half of 04's tree rule this is a drop only the bake can make, and it is never a tree the preview hides and the bake prints.
- [P3-fix] `run_pipeline` refuses a model over 04's 60 mm ceiling BEFORE Stage 1: `bake.predicted_top_mm` maxes `transform.building_top_mm` over the buildings together with the frame top and the tree tops, and raises `ModelTooTallError` (a `BakeError`) naming the height and the sliders, in under a millisecond. 01 allows a 250 m radius and Chicago re-cropped to 450 m reaches 66.5 mm at the DEFAULT parameters, so "zoom into the Loop and bake" was a normal user path that spent seconds building a model only to quarantine it. Every term comes from the same `transform.py` functions the preview draws with, so the guard's number is the height the user is looking at; it is an upper bound on the baked height, since Stage 1's area-weighted block percentile can only lower a tower. The Stage 4 `bounding_box` validator remains the gate: `run_pipeline(..., height_guard=False)` skips only the shortcut, and the quarantine test uses that to keep exercising the full build-then-fail-then-quarantine path.
- [P3-fix] `make validate` on a `.stl` re-indexes the file first (`cli._index_stl_triangle_soup`, an exact `np.unique` on the coordinates the file itself stores). A binary STL has no vertex index, so `trimesh.load(process=False)` hands back three vertices per face and every edge belongs to exactly one face: chicago.stl failed manifold, watertight and self_intersection (206 226 bad edges) while the 3MF of the same model passed. The weld is bitwise, never a tolerance pass, so it re-indexes the soup without being able to close a real crack - which is what DECISIONS [P3]'s `process=False` note exists to prevent.
- [P3-fix] `assemble.finalize` additionally requires the mesh to survive the float32 round trip a binary STL puts it through (`float32_defect_count`: zero-area faces after quantisation, plus vertices that collide under it). One float32 step at a 90 mm coordinate is 7.6e-6 mm, enough to collapse a triangle a nanometre across: Chicago's STL came back with 8 zero-area faces and 4 non-manifold edges while its 3MF (written `%.12f`) was perfect. One 1e-6 mm weld fixes it, costing 8 of 69 258 triangles and leaving the volume unchanged to 1e-11 relative, and `make validate` now passes on the .3mf AND the .stl.
- [P3-fix] NOT DONE, outside this fix pass's file scope and left for web-editor: mirroring the two new bake-only predicates in `apps/web`. The Bake button should be disabled/annotated from `lib/transform.ts` with the same predicted top (max over buildings of `building_top_mm`, the frame lip and the tree cones) against 60 mm, and the preview should hide a tree whose printed radius is under `max(0.5, nozzle / cos(pi/8))` mm. Until then the editor can still offer a bake the server refuses - in 1 ms, with a message naming the sliders.
- [P3-gate] G3 PASS on 2026-08-29: `make bake-fixture` 11 s -> artifacts/chicago.3mf 180x180x34.7 mm, 68,682 triangles, watertight single body, min wall 0.81 mm; `make validate` ALL CHECKS PASS.
- [P5] The bake-side fast-fail height guard (predicted top >= 60 mm) and the nozzle-aware tree radius floor are mirrored in the web editor via additive helpers in transform.py/transform.ts (parity fixture regenerated) so preview, Bake button state and bake agree; this web-editor follow-up runs before qa-gate writes the Playwright smoke test.
- [P5-web] The two bake-only rules from [P3-fix] are now SHARED math, added additively to `app/geom/transform.py` and `apps/web/lib/transform.ts` in one go with `fixtures/parity-expected.json` regenerated: `TREE_SIDES`, `MAX_HEIGHT_MM = 60`, `tree_min_radius_mm(params)`, `tree_visible_for(tree, params, scale)`, `select_tree_indices_for(trees, params, scale)`, `predicted_top_mm(scene, params, radius_m)` and `model_too_tall(...)`. `bake.predicted_top_mm` and `thicken.tree_min_radius_mm` are now thin wrappers over the shared versions (no behaviour change); `thicken.TREE_SIDES` is re-exported from `transform` so `extrude`'s cone and the existing tests still see 8.
- [P5-web] `tree_visible(tree, scale)` KEEPS its two-argument signature (it is 04's literal rule and the committed parity values depend on it); the nozzle floor is a second composable predicate `tree_visible_for` rather than a new argument, so every existing call site and expectation stays valid.
- [P5-web] `transform.predicted_top_mm` takes `radius_m` explicitly and a `SceneLike` of `buildings` + `trees` only (no `bounds`), so the caller passes the radius it already resolved and `transform.py` stays stdlib-only and duck-typed.
- [P5-web] `predicted_top_mm` counts trees with `tree_visible`, NOT `tree_visible_for`: the nozzle floor only ever drops trees, so ignoring it keeps the number an upper bound on the baked height AND keeps it byte-identical to what the server's guard computes. Editor and bake therefore print the same millimetre (verified: Chicago at large_scale 2.0 reads 66.5 mm in the editor and `POST /bake` fails with "would print 66.5 mm tall").
- [P5-web] `MAX_HEIGHT_MM = 60` is duplicated between `transform.py` (which deliberately imports nothing) and `validate/checks.py` (which owns the Stage 4 validator); `tests/test_transform.py` asserts the two constants are equal and that `bake.predicted_top_mm` equals `T.predicted_top_mm` for all three parity parameter sets, so the duplication and the wrapper cannot rot.
- [P5-web] `sceneWarnings` and `bakeBlockReason` now take `PrintParams` and emit a second `block`-level warning, `model-too-tall`: "Model would be X mm tall (limit 60 mm) — lower the building scales or the plate size". Coverage still blocks FIRST, so a 5-building scene is told to enlarge the radius rather than to lower a slider. Bake is disabled from the same string on the button, the banner and inside `store.requestBake`.
- [P5-web] The prediction is memoised through an exported `warningDeps(graph, params)` key (the `previewDeps` discipline from [P4-fix]): graph, plate_mm, frame, base_thickness_mm, small_scale, large_scale, trees - never `params` itself. `lib/warnings.test.ts` walks every key of the frozen PrintParams and fails if a parameter that moves the prediction is missing from the list, or if a listed one does not move it. `CityPreview.test.ts` now asserts a height slider rebuilds ONLY the (arithmetic-only) `height` memo and no geometry layer; `previewDeps.trees` gained `nozzle_mm` because the tree floor is nozzle-aware.
- [P5-web] The predicted height is surfaced in two places rather than in the print-stats card: "Predicted height X mm of 60 mm" under the Bake button (red past the limit, `data-testid="predicted-height"` + `data-predicted-mm`), and "· X mm tall" in the preview HUD chip with a red `preview-too-tall` chip past the ceiling. `StatsCard` still shows only measured `BakeResult.stats`, so a predicted number can never be mistaken for a baked one.
- [P5-web] `lib/preview.ts::buildTrees` filters with `select_tree_indices_for`, and a `preview-tree-floor` chip explains a raised floor in GROUND metres ("A 0.8 mm nozzle drops trees under 9.3 m of site radius"). At the default 0.4 mm nozzle the floor is 0.433 mm, 04's 0.5 mm still binds, and nothing about the preview changes.
- [P5-web] Playwright MCP was NOT available in this session (ToolSearch disabled); the runtime check ran instead through a throwaway `@playwright/test` chromium script in `apps/web/` (deleted afterwards, `apps/web/e2e/` left empty for qa-gate). Chicago Loop at radius 900: 100% -> 34.7 mm (exactly the G3 baked bbox Z) Bake enabled, 150% -> 50.6 mm enabled, 200% -> 66.5 mm Bake DISABLED with banner + chip + reason, zero console errors. The orchestrator's expectation that 200% stays enabled was wrong: [P3] already recorded Chicago reaching 66.5 mm there and the server refusing it, and matching that refusal is the point of this task.
- [P5-qa] `apps/web/playwright.config.ts` starts the two commands `make up`'s native path starts (uvicorn + `next dev`, or `npm run build && npm run start` when `FRAMECRAFT_WEB_MODE=prod`) rather than shelling out to `make up`: Playwright's `webServer` needs a process that stays in the foreground (`make up` daemonises and exits) and spawns it through cmd.exe on this host, where `make` is not on PATH. `reuseExistingServer: true` on both entries, so the config starts NOTHING when `make gate` (or a developer) already brought the stack up.
- [P5-qa] `make gate` runs, in order: `make down` (it owns the stack lifecycle), pytest, `npm run lint && npm test && npm run build`, a chromium-installed check that prints the exact `npx playwright install chromium` hint, then `make up` (output redirected to artifacts/logs/up.log) + `npm run test:e2e` + `make down` ALWAYS, and exits non-zero if any step failed. `next build` deliberately runs BEFORE `make up`: building into `.next` while `next dev` serves it corrupts the dev server (DECISIONS [P4]).
- [P5-qa] `make up` gained `FRAMECRAFT_WEB_MODE=prod` (foreground `npm run build`, then `npm run start`), defaulting to `next dev` so `make up` stays fast. The e2e passes against both; the gate uses whichever is already up.
- [P5-qa] The 3MF container checks (zip parts, `unit="millimeter"`, exactly one `<object>` and one `<build><item>`, `Description` carrying the OSM attribution, XML vertex/triangle counts equal to the loaded mesh) were added to the `validate` subcommand in `app/cli.py`, NOT to `app/validate/checks.py`: 04 stage 4 is a mesh gate and `checks.validate(mesh, params)` never sees a file. A `.3mf` that will not load now prints the container table naming the broken part instead of a traceback; `.stl` behaviour is unchanged. Exit code is 1 on any FAIL, stage-4 or container.
- [P5-qa] The `3mf_attribution` row prints "present (N chars)" instead of the Description text: a Windows console codepage that cannot encode the copyright sign would turn printing the row into a crash.
- [P5-qa] The smoke test bakes with the sliders it moved (plate 200 mm, large_scale 120 %, base 4 mm) and downloads the bake's `<stem>.json` sidecar next to the `.3mf`, so `app.cli validate` judges the file against those parameters. Verified independently that this combination passes every validator (200.000 x 200.000 x 46.613 mm, min wall 0.829 mm). Without the sidecar the validator correctly FAILS `bounding_box` against the 180 mm default - that is the gate working, not a bug.
- [P5-qa] The smoke test nudges rotation +1 deg (asserting exactly one POST /scene, which is the whole point of the rotation control being server-side) and then back to 0 deg before baking, so the bake runs on the fixture-backed preset and the gate's main assertion never depends on a live Overpass query. The +1 deg fetch hits the network once, then the 24 h /scene disk cache.
- [P5-qa] The A2 low-coverage pin is dropped by a real map click, calibrated from the pin and radius-handle markers (two known geographic points) after five clicks of the map's zoom-out control; the target is 41.90/-87.45, ~14 km into Lake Michigan, where Overpass returns 0 elements in 8 s (no coastline way is inside the bbox, so the Lake Michigan relation is not returned either). The test soft-skips ONLY on a 502/503 from POST /scene, never on an assertion.
- [P5-qa] A non-preset `/scene` caches its raw Overpass response as `fixtures/<sha1>.json`, so the smoke test's `afterAll` deletes every sha1-named fixture `presets-index.json` does not list - the same rule `python -m app.cli refresh-fixtures` uses. That also performed the STATUS.md prune: 12 sha1 fixtures -> 6, 137 128 KB -> 67 372 KB, 68.1 MiB freed, with `uv run pytest -q` still green offline (309 passed).
- [P5-qa] A3's 30 fps is MEASURED AND REPORTED, not asserted: the e2e drives a height slider on every animation frame for 2 s and prints the rate (22 fps on 994 buildings), because headless Chromium here renders through SwiftShader where one idle frame already costs ~45 ms (DECISIONS [P4]). Only a catastrophic floor (> 4 fps) is asserted. The provable halves of A3 - zero server calls on a PrintParams change and zero page reloads - are asserted outright.
- [P5-qa] A6 (opens in a slicer) cannot be verified on this host and is reported as PROXIED, never as passed: the proxies are the `3mf_*` container rows, `sits_at_zero`, and `test_a6_proxy_*` (trimesh's own 3MF reader -> one body, watertight, positive volume, bottom at z=0). A step-by-step manual procedure is in docs/handoff/05-qa-gate.md.
- [P5-qa] G4 PASS on 2026-08-29: `make gate` rc=0 - 309 pytest (64.5 s), eslint clean, 125 vitest, `next build` clean, 2 Playwright tests (37.7 s). Measured A1 0.12-0.19 s preset-to-preview, A4 8.8-9.3 s bake-to-download through the UI, and the downloaded 3MF passes `app.cli validate` with ALL CHECKS PASS.
- [P5-fix] A SUBTRACTIVE layer (water, engraved roads) is no longer clipped to the inset crop square when the frame is OFF: `thicken.recess_clip_square` returns `crop` with the frame on and the PLATE square grown by `RIDGE_BRIDGE_CELLS * grid` with it off, so a recess that reaches the edge opens onto the plate side wall instead of leaving a `CROP_INSET_MM` (0.05 mm) RIND of base standing between it and the physical edge. With the frame off `usable == plate`, so the crop edge is 0.05 mm inside the plate's own side wall; chicago-loop at plate 240/256 failed Stage 4 `min_wall` at 0.050 mm and at plate 200 shipped 39 sub-0.06 mm appendages at distance 0.000 from the edge. An EMBOSSED road is additive and still stops at `crop`; green and buildings are untouched; the frame-on default path is byte-identical.
- [P5-fix] `merge_recess_ridges` no longer puts the bridge it just built back through the layer's minimum-feature filters: it regrids (`thicken.regrid_layer`, the half of `finish_layer` that is only `set_precision` + collinear pruning) and keeps it. `_drop_unprintable` was discarding it every pass - a wedge between two ponds is bridged into the *road* layer (the deepest recess) where it is an isolated ~0.1 mm^2 polygon under `min_detail^2` - so all four passes rebuilt the same bridge and all four dropped it, leaving the ridge. A sub-minimum CUTTER polygon prints as a dimple no nozzle reaches, i.e. solid base; dropping it prints as a ridge no nozzle can lay down. The sink keeps its own `dropped_small`/`dropped_thin` counts instead of having them overwritten by the merge's.
- [P5-fix] `checks._min_wall_probe` takes `extra_zs` and `validate()` always adds `checks.recess_probe_zs(params, min_wall)`: one deterministic slice per recess band, at the midpoint of `[band_bottom, band_top - persist]` (NOT the band midpoint - the look-ahead would land exactly on the base top, where the base does not persist and the region is skipped as a tapering tip). 12 random Z over a 40-51 mm model land inside a 0.5 mm water band only by luck, which is why the frame-off rind shipped at plate 200 and failed at 256. A band shallower than one look-ahead cannot be probed this way and is left to the random slices. This immediately caught a REAL pre-existing 0.113 mm ridge in the default frame-on Chicago bake (the bridge bug above).
- [P5-fix] `thicken.snap` degrades instead of aborting when GEOS' overlay raises `TopologyException: Ring edge missing` on the dilate-and-clip step: the polygon is kept snapped and un-opened. The opening is a deburring pass, not a correctness invariant (every caller still runs the minimum-feature filters), and the exception took the whole bake down. Reproduced on chicago-loop at nozzle 0.6 / frame off / plate 256 and confirmed PRE-EXISTING (it reproduces with both this pass's Stage 1 changes reverted); `intersection(..., grid_size=grid)` does not survive it either.
- [P5-fix] `make validate` gained a `bodies` row (`app/cli._single_body_check`, run for `.3mf` AND `.stl`): PASS only at exactly one connected shell. 04 stage 4's `watertight` row deliberately tolerates several bodies (`even euler <= 2 * body_count`) and `3mf_objects`/`3mf_counts` count XML elements, so a second disconnected body written into the SAME `<object>` - `artifacts/audit-tests/corrupt/twobodies.3mf`, the e2e Chicago bake plus a floating 5 mm cube at z=30 - printed `ALL CHECKS PASS` with exit 0. `checks._body_count` is now the public `checks.body_count(mesh, manifold=None)`; no other behaviour changed.
- [P5-fix] The Playwright gate asserts 01's own numbers: `A1_BUDGET_MS = 5_000` and `A4_BUDGET_MS = 90_000` (was 15 s / 120 s, i.e. a preview that regressed 60x still passed). "Warm" is taken literally: the happy path clicks Chicago, then san-francisco-fidi, then Chicago again and times only the third click, polling the HUD building count back to Chicago's - so the timed click provably rebuilt the preview and the cold 12 MB fixture parse of a clean clone is not what gets measured. Measured 0.09 s / 10.3 s. The untimed warm-up clicks get `WARMUP_BUDGET_MS = 60_000`.
- [P5-fix] The A2 low-coverage test may still soft-skip when Overpass is unreachable, but it now reads the 502/503 BODY first and asserts the detail does not contain `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE`: `main.py` maps `OverpassOffline` -> 503, `make up` passes the caller's environment through, and `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE=1 make gate` therefore printed GATE PASS with A2 never exercised and a skip reason ("Overpass is unreachable") that was not what happened. Belt and braces: `make gate` now starts the stack with `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE=` (empty) so the bake API can reach Overpass whatever the caller exported, and fails the gate on ANY skipped Playwright test via a `json` reporter (`artifacts/e2e/results.json`, `stats.skipped`). Verified: `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE=1 make gate` -> rc=0 with 2 passed / 0 skipped and the A2 banner really asserted.
- [P5-fix] Regression tests: `test_bake.py::test_frame_off_recesses_open_onto_the_plate_edge_instead_of_leaving_a_rind` bakes the real Chicago scene at frame=False on plate 200 and 256 and asserts every validator passes AND that `base_top - 0.25` plus every `recess_probe_zs` height holds no appendage under `0.9 * min_wall` (measured with `thicken.narrowest_width`, the gate's own measure); `test_the_recess_slice_catches_the_rind_the_random_slices_missed` monkeypatches the pre-fix clip back and asserts `validate(..., slices=0)` - the recess probe ALONE, no random slice - fails at the 0.05 mm crop inset. `test_validate_cli.py` gained three `bodies` cases (a floating cube in a 3MF and in an STL, plus the row passing on the real bake). 309 -> 315 pytest, 125 vitest unchanged; no test, validator or threshold was weakened, skipped or deleted.
- [P5-gate] G4 PASS on 2026-08-29: `make gate` rc=0 in 166 s — 315 pytest, eslint clean, 125 vitest, next build clean, 2 Playwright e2e (happy path incl. bake -> download -> CLI validate; lake-pin low-coverage path); stack down afterwards.
- [P6] One final polish fixer pass for audit minors (tsc on a test file, favicon 404, stale BakeResult after param change, preset highlight after reload, validator sidecar crash, gate swallowing make down rc) plus a clean-clone `make install && make up` check for A7, then G4 is re-run.
- [P6-fix] `apps/web` gained `npm run typecheck` (`tsc --noEmit`) and `make gate` step 2 now runs `lint && typecheck && vitest && build`. F1 was not just a bad cast: NOTHING in the gate type-checked the project, because `next build` only type-checks the app graph, `eslint .` is not type-aware and vitest transpiles without checking - so a type error in a test file, an e2e spec or any module the app graph does not reach was invisible to `make gate`. The cast in `lib/warnings.test.ts` now takes the `as unknown as` hop and the assertion around it is untouched; FAILURES.md F1 is marked FIXED.
- [P6-fix] The site icon ships BOTH ways, `apps/web/app/icon.svg` and `apps/web/app/favicon.ico`, via the App Router metadata convention. The link tag alone is not enough: the P4 session's own capture (`.playwright-mcp/console-...-15-52-37-562Z.log`) shows Chromium requesting a bare `/favicon.ico` and logging the 404 as a console ERROR on every load. The `.ico` is the same glyph rasterised to a 32x32 PNG-in-ICO (170 bytes) by a throwaway stdlib script (zlib + CRC by hand, no new dependency), deleted after generating the file - the same practice as the P1 `chicago-scene.json` layout script. Verified from a clean clone: both 200, both link tags emitted, `next build` clean.
- [P6-fix] `BakeState` gained `stale: boolean` and the editor retires a finished bake whose inputs moved: `markBakeStale` (terminal phases only, identity-stable so it re-renders nothing when it changes nothing) is applied by `setParam`, `resetParams`, `setPin`, `setRadius`, `setRotation`, `applyPreset` and a successful `generate()`, and `reduceBake` clears it because a response off the wire is current by definition. The RESULT is kept - the stats card still shows the last real bake, retitled "Print stats (previous bake)" - but `bakeDownloadLinks()` returns nothing and the panel says "Parameters changed since this bake - bake again to download a matching file". 01's promise is that the preview and the printed result agree; a download link that outlives its parameters breaks exactly that.
- [P6-fix] `setParam` marks the bake stale on EVERY write, without comparing the new value to the old one. A spurious invalidation costs one re-bake; a missed one ships a file that does not match the preview, and `Controls.createCommitGate` already suppresses no-change writes upstream.
- [P6-fix] The active preset chip comes from the new `store.activePresetId(state)` selector, not from `location.preset_id`: the id is returned only when `scene.graph !== null` (a scene really is loaded) or `presetChosen` (a new flag set by `applyPreset`, cleared by `setPin`). `INITIAL_LOCATION.preset_id` is `chicago-loop`, so reading the id directly lit the Chicago chip on every fresh page load with nothing generated. A preset click still highlights immediately, before its scene arrives; a Generate that FAILS at the initial location now leaves every chip dark.
- [P6-fix] `app.cli validate` treats a broken bake sidecar as a verdict, never a traceback: `_params_from_sidecar` returns `(params, source, error)` and an unusable `<stem>.json` - unreadable, not JSON, no `print_params` object, or values the FROZEN contract rejects - becomes a `sidecar_params` FAIL row reading "sidecar params invalid: <field>: <reason>" plus exit 1, while the mesh is still judged against `--plate-mm`/`--nozzle-mm` if given (else the defaults) and the `parameters` header names which. Overrides are merged BEFORE validation, so `--nozzle-mm 0.4` can repair the one field a sidecar got wrong and the run goes green; an override the contract itself rejects is a usage error on stderr with exit 2, checked before the mesh is loaded. +12 tests in `tests/test_validate_cli.py`, one of them a real subprocess asserting no `Traceback`.
- [P6-fix] `make gate` gained an explicit `[5/5] teardown` step (steps renumbered `[0/5]`..`[5/5]`): the final `make down` still always runs, but a non-zero exit now fails the gate with the tail of `artifacts/logs/down.log`, and a `port_listening()` helper (netstat `LISTENING` on Windows, plus a `curl` probe of `/` and `/health` for portability) fails it naming any port still serving. "Never leaves the stack up" was a comment; since [P1-fix] made a foreign listener on :8000/:3000 a hard `make up` error, a silently-failed teardown surfaced as a confusing failure one run later. Positive control: the helper reports `busy=[ 8000 3000]` with the stack up and `busy=[]` after `make down`.
- [P6-fix] A7 VERIFIED for real on 2026-08-29 from a clean clone outside the tree (`git ls-files -co --exclude-standard | tar` = 134 files, 68 MB): `make install` 16 s, `make up` 16 s -> /health `{"status":"ok"}` + web 200 + /presets, `make bake-fixture` 10 s, `make validate artifacts/chicago.3mf` ALL CHECKS PASS (180x180x34.733 mm, min wall 0.8016), `npm run lint`/`typecheck`/`test` and `uv run pytest -q` (327 passed) green, `make down` frees both ports. NOTHING in the main tree had to change for a clone to work: `apps/web/public/maplibre/*` is regenerated by the `predev`/`prebuild` copy script and `.venv`/`node_modules` by `make install`; no `.env`, no missing fixture, no stale generated file. Caveat: uv/npm/playwright resolved from this host's caches, so 16 s is a warm-cache number. Logs kept at `artifacts/logs/clean-clone/`; the 1.1 GB clone was deleted.
- [P6-fix] `.playwright-mcp/` (Playwright MCP session console logs and page snapshots, 28 files) is now in `.gitignore`. It was tool output, not repo content, and a clone would have carried it.
- [P6-gate] G4 PASS on 2026-08-29 after the polish pass: `make gate` rc=0 in 180 s - 327 pytest (was 315), eslint clean, `tsc --noEmit` clean (new), 140 vitest (was 125), `next build` clean, 2 Playwright e2e; teardown checked, both ports free afterwards. Log: `artifacts/logs/gate-polish.log`.
- [P6-gate] Final G4 re-run by the orchestrator on 2026-08-29: `make gate` rc=0 in 180 s (327 pytest, 140 vitest, lint + tsc + next build, 2 Playwright e2e, teardown checked). A7 verified from a clean clone by the polish fixer (docs/handoff/06-polish.md). Run complete; no gate DEGRADED.
- [V2-P0] v2 run started 2026-08-29 from commit da9ab83 (v1, all gates green). Phase order: Task 0 min-wall investigation first (serial), then contracts v2 + v1 golden (serial, golden generated BEFORE any pipeline change), then parts export ‖ UI redesign, then lettering/ornaments/advisor math ‖ share link + hero picking, then preview integration, then qa-gate wiring, then gates G1-G8. Each phase: builder (opus) -> adversarial auditor (opus, did not write the code) -> fixer if needed.
- [V2-P0] The six agent definitions keep 05's verbatim OUTPUT PROTOCOL block; a "V2 RUN" paragraph above it names the v2 handoff file pattern (docs/handoff/v2-NN-<phase>.md), the temporary unfreeze of the contracts, and the test_v1_compat.py invariant. No seventh agent file: adversarial audits run as general-purpose agents so G0's `ls .claude/agents | wc -l == 6` still holds.
- [V2-P0] Two concurrent agents never edit the same file tree: services/bake work is serialized (parts export before lettering, both touch assemble.py/bake.py/checks.py) while apps/web work runs in parallel with it; the shared transform pair is edited by exactly one agent at a time.
- [V2-P1] Task 0 verdict: H1, the reported bake ran at `nozzle_mm: 0.2`, not 0.4 - `artifacts/265aa8c0ed.json` (radius 1980, plate 180, frame on, 1:23,571) records it, and every 0.4 mm bake on disk measures 0.80-0.88 mm of min wall against that one's 0.400. No factor of two was lost: `min_wall = 2 * nozzle` holds in transform.py/ts, the HUD, thicken's Stage 1 (`MIN_WALL_REPAIR_FACTOR = 1.0`) and checks' Stage 4 (`MIN_WALL_FAIL_FACTOR = 0.9`), and a *passing* bake reporting 0.40 mm is impossible at nozzle 0.4 because the gate fails under 0.72 mm.
- [V2-P1] H3 was refuted by reading the code, not the number: `min_detail_ground` (1 nozzle) at 0.4 mm and 1:23,571 is *also* 9.4 m, so the HUD string alone can never distinguish a halved nozzle from a HUD reading the wrong threshold. Both readings are now pinned by name in `test_the_reported_1_to_23571_bake_reads_18_9_m_at_the_default_nozzle` and its TS twin, so the next person does not have to re-derive the coincidence.
- [V2-P1] 04's three derived thresholds became named functions in the shared pair - `min_wall_mm`, `min_gap_mm`, `min_detail_mm` in `app/geom/transform.py` and `apps/web/lib/transform.ts` - and `thresholds_ground_m`, `tree_min_radius_mm` and `checks.validate` now call them instead of spelling out `MIN_WALL_NOZZLES * nozzle`. Hand-written `2 * nozzle` is zero occurrences outside the helpers now; the multipliers were four chances to drop a two whose only symptom is a plausible-looking smaller number.
- [V2-P1] The HUD's widened clause moved into `preview.ts` as `dilatedNotice(count, thresholds)` (next to `mergeNoticeMetres`), so the exact string - metres and label together - is asserted by vitest rather than living untested inside `CityPreview.tsx`.
- [V2-P1] The Stage 4 threshold is asserted by running the real `checks.validate()` on a bar mesh at five nozzles and reading `report.get("min_wall").threshold`, never by re-deriving `0.9 * 2 * nozzle` in the test; the Stage 1 target is bracketed behaviourally at 0.95/1.05 walls (outside the 1%-of-a-wall probe tolerance) on top of the constant assertions.
