- [V2-P1] `fixtures/parity-expected.json` gained a `thresholds_mm` block per case and no existing value changed; re-baking the Chicago preset after the refactor reproduces the previous stats, warnings and every validator value exactly (68,692 triangles, 167,624.12441295458 mm^3, min wall 0.8015771163765992), so the v1 golden can be generated from this tree.
- [V2-P1-gate] Task 0 verdict H1: the reported 9.4 m / 0.40 mm bake was at nozzle_mm 0.2 (its own sidecar artifacts/265aa8c0ed.json says so); min_wall = 2 * nozzle holds in transform.py, transform.ts, the HUD string, thicken's Stage 1 target (1.0x) and checks' Stage 4 threshold (0.9x), and is now pinned by tests on both sides. No geometry change: orchestrator re-ran `make bake-fixture && make validate artifacts/chicago.3mf` -> 68,692 triangles, min wall 0.8016, ALL CHECKS PASS, so G3/G4 re-runs are not required before continuing; G4 runs at the end regardless.
- [V2-P2] The token expansion table is a NEW shared pair, services/bake/app/geom/tokens.py + apps/web/lib/tokens.ts, pinned by fixtures/tokens-expected.json, rather than an addition to transform.py/transform.ts: transform is pure numeric math with a numeric parity dump, and string formatting deserves its own fixture. The v1 golden for test_v1_compat.py is cut from a git worktree of commit da9ab83 (the actual v1 code), not from the current tree.
- [V2-P2] `{city}` needs to reach the bake, so PrintParams v2 carries `city_label` (string, max 64, default ""), typed by the user in the editor; no reverse geocoding and no Nominatim dependency anywhere.
- [V2-P2] Default part_colors use four distinct colours so a four-slot AMS works out of the box: base + buildings warm grey #D8D3C6, roads + frame charcoal #3A3A3A, water #2F7FC1, green + trees #5A9E4B. "Frame and trees sharing" is read as each sharing an existing slot (frame with roads, trees with green); a fifth colour would leave a four-slot user with an unassigned part.
- [V2-P0] G5 as written ("7 parts") assumes the trees layer is non-empty; measured on the six presets, no tree prints at default plate/radius (every OSM tree radius is capped at 4.0 m, 0.37 mm at 1:9,650, under 04's 0.5 mm floor - a v1 fact, see [P4]). Since parts mode emits one object per NON-EMPTY layer and a 3MF model object cannot be empty, G5 will be judged twice: the literal `make bake-fixture COLOR=parts` (Chicago, defaults -> 6 parts, 6 materials) and `make bake-fixture COLOR=parts PLATE=256` (trees visible -> 7 parts, 7 materials), both validated; plus a pytest asserting 7 parts on a seven-layer scene.
- [V2-P1-fix] `dilatedNotice` takes `(count, params, scale)` and derives the metres itself from `T.min_wall_mm(params) / scale`; it no longer accepts a `Thresholds` record. The v2 Task 0 audit (finding 1, MAJOR) showed the refactor had put the *string* under test but not the *threshold selection*: `CityPreview.tsx` could pass `thresholds.min_detail` - one nozzle, exactly half, and a perfectly plausible "minimum wall" in a HUD - and all 147 vitest tests stayed green. With this signature there is no wrong argument to hand it, so the H3 look-alike cannot be reintroduced by choosing a different field at the call site.
- [V2-P1-fix] `apps/web/package.json` lint script is `eslint . --max-warnings 0` (was plain `eslint .`). The audit's mutation left `dilatedNotice` imported but unused, which eslint reported as a WARNING and `make gate` (`Makefile:139` runs `npm run lint` plain) therefore passed with exit 0. The tree emits zero warnings today, so the ratchet costs nothing and turns an orphaned import into a gate failure.
- [V2-P1-fix] New `apps/web/components/scene/CityPreview.hud.test.ts` is a deliberately CRUDE source guard: it reads `CityPreview.tsx` as text and asserts the `layout.dilatedCount` ternary calls `dilatedNotice(` exactly once, names no `min_detail`/`min_gap`/`toFixed` on those lines, and that the literal "minimum wall" never appears in the component. A real render test needs a WebGL canvas that the node environment does not have, and the Playwright smoke has no scene that deterministically forces a dilated footprint; a text guard that turns the one-line regression red beats a guarantee that exists only in a handoff note. The file says so in its header, so it is replaced rather than trusted the day either constraint lifts.
- [V2-P1-fix] The two ground-threshold assertions the audit called tautological (finding 2) now compare against `2 * nozzle / scale`, `1.5 * nozzle / scale` and `1.0 * nozzle / scale` spelled out in the test, not against `T.min_wall_mm(params) / scale`. Since the [V2-P1] refactor `thresholds_ground_m` *is* the latter expression, so those assertions held for any multiplier - including the one they exist to catch. Same change on both sides: `services/bake/tests/test_transform.py` and `apps/web/lib/transform.test.ts`.
- [V2-P1-fix] Correction to [V2-P1]'s "Hand-written `2 * nozzle` is zero occurrences outside the helpers now": true only of the forward product. `services/bake/app/validate/checks.py:337` (`nozzle = float(min_wall_mm) / T.MIN_WALL_NOZZLES` in `wall_persist_mm`) and `checks.py:532` (`min_detail = min_wall_mm * T.MIN_DETAIL_NOZZLES / T.MIN_WALL_NOZZLES` in `_min_wall_probe`) still reconstruct the nozzle and the detail floor from the raw multipliers in inverse form. Both are left as they are: each receives a `float` min-wall rather than `params` and so cannot call `T.min_wall_mm`, and both are exact in IEEE754 (power-of-two scalings). The code is fine; the absolute wording was not.
- [V2-P2] PrintParams schema_version 2 is PURELY ADDITIVE: `schema_version` (integer, enum [2], default 2) is NOT in `required`, every v1 field keeps its name/type/range/default byte for byte, and all ten new fields are optional with defaults that reproduce v1 behaviour. The regenerated `services/bake/app/contracts.py` and `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts` diffs are 54 and 90 lines of pure insertion - no existing line moved - and `test_every_v1_field_keeps_its_name_type_range_and_default` pins the v1 half from inside the v2 revision.
- [V2-P2] The `part_colors` default is four distinct filaments, not seven: base+buildings `#D8D3C6`, roads+frame `#3A3A3A`, water `#2F7FC1`, green+trees `#5A9E4B`. A four-slot AMS is the common case, so the default palette must print with no slot re-assignment; the pairings follow what a viewer reads as one material (the plate and the blocks that grow out of it; the road surface and the border that frames it; planting and the trees standing in it). Asserted on both sides (`test_part_colors_default_uses_four_distinct_filaments`, contracts.test.ts "gives the default palette four distinct filaments").
- [V2-P2] The four nested objects differ in strictness on purpose: `PartColors` lists all seven keys in `required` (the brief's rule - a palette with a missing slot is not a palette), while `NorthArrow`, `ScaleBar` and `UndersideMark` require nothing, so a share link may carry `{"enabled": true}` and let the rest default. `Engraving` requires only `edge` and `text`. Every property inside all four nevertheless carries its own schema `default`, which is what lets the object-level defaults be exactly the all-defaults instance.
- [V2-P2] PARAM_RANGES gained NESTED GROUPS rather than dotted keys: `PARAM_RANGES.north_arrow.size_mm.max`, `PARAM_RANGES.scale_bar.length_m`, `PARAM_RANGES.engravings.size_mm` (the array's ITEM bounds, keyed by the array's own name). Flat `"north_arrow.size_mm"` keys would have needed quoting at every call site; every existing v1 entry keeps its exact position and shape, so no current consumer changes.
- [V2-P2] gen_py.py gained string bounds (`minLength`/`maxLength`/`pattern` -> `Field(min_length=/max_length=/pattern=)`) and object defaults (`Field(default_factory=lambda: T(...))`, keyword order taken from the referenced `$defs`' property order so the output is stable). `py_default` now returns `(kind, source)` instead of a source string, which also fixes a latent bug: the old alias branch hard-coded `default_factory=list` for ANY mutable default, so an aliased field with an object default would have silently become an empty list. gen_ts.py gained a recursive, indentation-aware `ts_literal` for nested object/array defaults.
- [V2-P2] Integer enums (`"type": "integer", "enum": [2]`) already worked in both generators through the existing Literal/union path; no generator change was needed for `schema_version`, and it is emitted as `Literal[2]` in Python and `2` in TS, so sending `schema_version: 1` is rejected by the model, by jsonschema and by tsc.
- [V2-P2] `fixtures/print-params-default.json` is `PrintParams().model_dump(mode="json")`, written by `services/bake/tests/test_contracts.py` under `FRAMECRAFT_WRITE_PARITY=1` and asserted by that suite AND by the new `apps/web/lib/contracts.test.ts`. It is the single statement of "the defaults", so DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS cannot drift from `PrintParams()`; same arrangement as `fixtures/parity-expected.json` ([P4]).
- [V2-P2] V1 GOLDEN METHOD: baked from a clean `git worktree` of da9ab83 (`uv sync` in that worktree, then the exact `make bake-fixture` command), NOT from the working tree, so it is an independent reference. The canonical geometry digest is sha256 over the exact source text of the `<vertices>...</vertices>` element followed by the `<triangles>...</triangles>` element of `3D/3dmodel.model` - textual, not parsed, so a reordered triangle or a one-ulp coordinate moves it. Chicago at the defaults: `0e20725e9c738a1af7e91a62dd2808ae94f74027f2811b51c65790873ce08758`, 34,348 vertices / 68,692 triangles / 167,624.12441295458 mm^3 / 0.8015771163765992 mm min wall. The `.3mf` is 645,101 bytes, under the 6 MB bar, so it is committed whole as `fixtures/v1-golden/chicago-default.3mf` and a future mismatch can be diffed rather than merely reported. The worktree was removed afterwards; `git worktree list` shows only the main tree.
- [V2-P2] The current tree reproduced the golden's ENTIRE model XML byte for byte before any v2 edit was made, which is how the digest method was verified rather than assumed.
- [V2-P2] test_v1_compat.py's "byte for byte" test (c) had to be split, because `mf3.build_metadata` recites the whole PrintParams object into the 3MF `Description` and a v2 model necessarily recites eleven more keys. It is now three assertions which together are STRICTER than the blanket comparison would have been: everything outside `<metadata>` is identical text; no metadata name changes value except `CreationDate` and `Description`; and the Description's `PrintParams:` recital keeps every v1 `k=v` pair byte-identical while gaining exactly the eleven v2 names. Nothing was excluded that could hide a geometry change.
- [V2-P2] The `Description` recital is parsed back with a ` (?=[a-z_]+=)` boundary rather than a plain space split: the v2 values include dict reprs (`part_colors={'base': '#D8D3C6', ...}`) that contain spaces of their own, and those reprs use `': '`, never ` word=`.
- [V2-P2] The shared token pair (`services/bake/app/geom/tokens.py`, `apps/web/lib/tokens.ts`) formats every number by hand - `round_half_up` = `floor(x + 0.5)`, `group_thousands`, `fixed` - instead of using `format()`/`toFixed()`/`toLocaleString()`. Python's `round` is banker's rounding and JS's `Math.round` is not, and `toLocaleString` is locale-dependent; a 1:23,570 engraved next to a 1:23,571 previewed is exactly the drift this pair exists to prevent. Ties are pinned by fixture cases (0.5 m radius) and by unit tests on both sides.
- [V2-P2] `{scale}` expands to nothing when `scale_mm_per_m` is not positive: `format_scale` returns None/null and `expand_tokens` leaves `{scale}` standing. A scene that has not been measured has no honest ratio, and cutting `1:0` or a gap into a frame is worse than cutting the token the user typed. Unknown tokens (`{nope}`, `{CITY}`) are left verbatim for the same reason - a brace is likelier deliberate than mistaken.
- [V2-P2] `{radius}` prints whole metres with NO thousands separator (`900 m`, `1980 m`); the brief attaches "thousands separators" only to `{scale}` and `{buildings}`, and a frame legend reads a bare metre count more cleanly.
- [V2-P2] The brief's "Chicago 1:9,650 case" corresponds to no parameter combination this tree produces. Chicago at the DEFAULTS (plate 180, frame on, radius 900 -> 168 mm over 1800 m) is 1:10,714, and the nearest ratio any committed artifact shows is 1:9,574 (plate 200, frame on). `fixtures/tokens-expected.json` pins the three real ones - 1:10,714, 1:23,571 (the [V2-P1] bake) and 1:9,574 - instead of a figure nothing can produce.
- [V2-P2] `TokenContext.date` is supplied by the CALLER as an ISO `YYYY-MM-DD` string and never read from the clock inside the token table, so a preview and its bake agree and no test is a function of the day it runs on.
- [V2-P2] The three web tests that walk every PrintParams key (store/editor.test.ts PARAM_MOVES, lib/warnings.test.ts MOVES, CityPreview.test.ts) were extended with REAL v2 values rather than by widening their coverage lists. CityPreview.test.ts now ASSERTS that each v2 parameter rebuilds no preview layer instead of merely declaring it covered, and warnings.test.ts gained a guard that every non-boolean key has a moved value - without it the new keys would have been walked as `undefined` and the "does not move the prediction" claim would have gone vacuous. `schema_version` is listed with the value 2 in each: its enum has one member, so the only legal value is the default.
- [V2-P2-gate] G8 PASS on 2026-08-30: `uv run pytest tests/test_v1_compat.py` -> 11 passed; golden digest 0e20725e... (68,692 triangles, 34,348 vertices, 167,624.124 mm^3, min wall 0.8016) cut from a worktree of da9ab83. Correction to the [V2-P0] G5 line: Chicago at defaults is 1:10,714 (168 mm usable over 1,800 m), not 1:9,650; the tree arithmetic there (4.0 m -> 0.37 mm, under 0.5 mm) is unchanged.
- [V2-P3] Hero buildings' bake-side half (true_height multiplier, own_color part, block-merge exemption) is built in the parts-export phase because an own_color hero IS a part; the editor half (raycast picking, list) is built by web-editor in the UI phase. Hero true_height means the hero's height multiplier is max(1.0, the multiplier its class would get): a hero is never reduced below its true relative height but still grows with everyone when the user enlarges. All own_color heroes share one fixed accent colour (#E3A72F) since part_colors has no hero key by the brief's field list; each hero is still its own object with its own basematerial entry so "one entry per part" holds literally.
- [V2-P2-fix] `gen_py.py` now emits NO default for a property listed in a nested `$defs` object's `required` (v2-01 audit finding 1): jsonschema treats `required` as required whatever `default` the property also carries, and the model must agree or `POST /bake` answers a half palette - `part_colors: {"base": "#fff"}` - with six silently-defaulted filaments and a wrong-coloured print instead of a 422. The nested defaults still reach every caller through the OUTER optional property's `default_factory`, which spells out every key.
- [V2-P2-fix] The required-means-required rule applies to `$defs` objects only; a ROOT model (`PrintParams`, `SceneRequest`, `SceneGraph`, `BakeResult`) keeps the schema default on its required properties. A root is an API envelope, where `PrintParams()` is the documented "no parameters supplied" object and a v1 payload that omits a key must still load; a nested `$def` is a VALUE, where a missing key is a wrong value rather than an under-specified request. `PrintParams() == PrintParams.model_validate({})` is pinned by `test_an_empty_print_params_body_still_means_the_defaults`, and the root-level leniency is therefore deliberate and recorded, not an oversight.
- [V2-P2-fix] Blast radius of that rule beyond `PartColors`' seven colours: `Building.holes`, `Building.min_height_m` and `AreaFeature.holes` are `required` with a default too, and are now required in the model. Every construction site in the tree already passes them explicitly (`normalize.py`, `test_bake.py`, `test_validate_cli.py`) and both committed scene fixtures carry them on every element, so no caller changed; jsonschema had been rejecting an omission all along.
- [V2-P2-fix] `gen_py.py` now RAISES at generation time when an object-valued `default` omits a required key of the `$defs` it points at, instead of emitting a `default_factory` that would raise on first construction. The failure lands where the schema author can see it.
- [V2-P2-fix] `test_every_required_property_of_a_nested_def_is_required_in_the_model` walks all four schemas and asserts a per-file count (0/25/9/8) so it cannot pass by walking an empty list; it pins the generator rule itself rather than the eleven payload cases above it.
- [V2-P2-fix] The brief for this fix asked for "an empty north_arrow/scale_bar/underside_mark object with a member missing" to be REJECTED by both validators. It is not, and must not be: those three `$defs` list nothing in `required` precisely so a share link can send `{"enabled": true}` and let the rest default. Pinned the true parity instead - both validators ACCEPT the partial object and the model fills each absent member from that member's own schema default (`test_ornaments_accept_a_partial_object_in_schema_and_model`) - rather than inventing a rejection the contract does not declare.
- [V2-P2-fix] `gen_ts.py` emits `DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS` through a generated `deepFreeze()` helper and adds an exported `defaultPrintParams()` factory returning a `structuredClone` deep copy (v2-01 audit finding 2). The constant keeps its name, type and shape, so `fixtures/print-params-default.json` and every existing consumer are unaffected; what changes is that a nested write now throws a TypeError instead of corrupting the module-level default that `resetParams()` resets to. Callers that will EDIT the copy must use the factory - `{ ...DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS }` is a shallow copy and aliases all six nested values.
- [V2-P2-fix] `deepFreeze<PrintParams>({...})` is emitted with an explicit type argument, not bare: passing the literal to a generic parameter would drop object-literal freshness and with it the excess-property check that catches a typo'd key in the generated default at build time.
- [V2-P2-fix] New `PARAM_LIMITS` constant beside `PARAM_RANGES` (v2-01 audit finding 5) carrying the caps `PARAM_RANGES` cannot hold - it emits only fragments with both a `minimum` and a `maximum`: `engravings.max_items` 8, `hero_building_ids.max_items` 12, `city_label.max_length`, `engravings.text.max_length` and `underside_mark.template.max_length` at 64. A sibling constant rather than a widening of `PARAM_RANGES`, so no existing `{min,max,default}` row changes shape.
- [V2-P2-fix] `group_thousands` in `tokens.ts` takes its digits from `BigInt(...).toString()` when the magnitude is finite (v2-01 audit finding 6): `String(1e21)` is `"1e+21"` and grouping that printed `"1e,+21"` where Python printed twenty-two digits. The two languages now agree for every integer, not merely for every integer under 1e21. A non-finite value keeps its previous JS spelling, so nothing reachable through the contract changed.
- [V2-P2-fix] `{coords}` at exactly zero reads `0.0000° N, 0.0000° E`: the hemisphere letter is chosen by `value >= 0`, so the equator is north and the prime meridian is east, and `-0.0` reads the same as `0.0`. Now pinned by a fixture case and a unit test on both sides. Audit finding 8's `-4e-05 -> 0.0000° S` is left as it is - the value really is south of the equator, both languages agree, and rounding the hemisphere letter as well as the digits would make `{coords}` disagree with the `{lat}` printed next to it.
- [V2-P2-fix] `fixtures/tokens-expected.json` grew from 27 to 41 cases, closing audit finding 7: zero latitude and zero longitude, `lon = -180`, a count of 0 and one of 1,234,567, the same token twice in one string, two truly adjacent tokens with no separator between the braces, a lone `{`, a lone `}`, `}{`, `{notatoken}` and `{ city }`. Regenerated the documented way (`FRAMECRAFT_WRITE_PARITY=1 uv run pytest tests/test_tokens.py`), never by hand.
- [V2-P2-fix] Audit finding 3 (`02_TECH_SPEC.md` still shows the eleven v1 `PrintParams` keys) is NOT FIXED by decision: the v1 specs are left as written for this run and the v2 shape is documented in `docs/handoff/v2-01-contracts.md` and the RUNBOOK. Editing 02 mid-run would also have put this fixer in a file two other agents are reading.
- [V2-P3] Parts mode PARTITIONS the single-mode solid instead of rebuilding it: `assemble.assemble` computes the v1 solid exactly as before (same primitives, same order, same `finalize`, same sit-at-zero translation) and then cuts the SAME primitives into colour parts, translating each by the identical offset. The `.stl` and every Stage 4 mesh row therefore judge the same geometry in both modes, `parts_union` proves the parts' manifold union equals that solid (Chicago: 167,624.1339 vs 167,624.1244 mm^3, 5.7e-8 relative, bbox delta exactly 0), and `color_mode="single"` stays byte for byte (v1 golden digest 0e20725e... reproduced by the file `make bake-fixture` writes, not only through `run_pipeline`).
- [V2-P3] A subtractive layer becomes an INLAY: water and engraved roads are extruded as a `PART_INLAY_MM = 0.6` mm slab filling `[floor - 0.6, floor]` under the recess floor, and the base part is cut by the SAME single-mode cutter lowered to `inlay_bottom + 0.2` (`extrude.recess_pocket`). `base_part | inlay == base_solid - cutter` by construction, so the recess is exactly as deep as v1's and its floor prints in the water/road colour. 0.6 mm always fits: `slab()` clamps a recess to `base_thickness/2` and the contract floors the base at 2 mm, so the inlay bottom never drops below 0.4 mm.
- [V2-P3] The inlay is grown 0.2 mm LATERALLY as well as in z (`extrude.inlay_slab` buffers the recess polygons by `PART_OVERLAP_MM / scale`) and is then intersected with the base plate solid. Without the lateral growth the inlay's side wall and the base's pocket wall are an exactly coincident vertical face - the one thing 04's overlap rule exists to prevent - and without the intersection a frame-off recess (which may legally reach past the plate edge, [P5-fix]) would push its 0.2 mm rim outside the model. The rim is buried inside the base part, so no printed surface moves.
- [V2-P3] Where two recesses overlap, each inlay is differenced against every OTHER recess's single-mode cutter, so the deeper recess owns the floor there. Without it the 0.5 mm water inlay would stand 0.1 mm proud of a 0.6 mm road groove, i.e. outside the model, and `parts_union` would fail.
- [V2-P3] `extrude.frame_lip_part` starts the lip at `base_top - 0.2` instead of exactly at `base_top`; `extrude.frame_lip` (single mode) is untouched. In a union a coincident face is harmless; between two colour parts it is a face the slicer has to arbitrate. The extra 0.2 mm band is inside the base slab (with the frame on no recess is ever clipped into the frame band), so the union is unchanged.
- [V2-P3] The `bodies` validator in parts mode does NOT require "one connected shell per part", which the brief asked for and which no city can satisfy: Chicago's buildings part is 274 separate blocks and its green part 165 patches, and 04 stage 1's whole job is deciding which blocks merge. It requires instead that every part be a non-empty solid with no debris shell (< 0.01 mm^3, `assemble.prune_debris`'s own number), that the file declare exactly one mesh object per part (== the component count), and that the ASSEMBLED UNION still be one connected shell - which is where 04's single-body rule actually lives once the model is delivered as parts. Single mode and the `.stl` keep the v1 rule verbatim.
- [V2-P3] The bake runs 04 stage 4 on the single-mode solid and pins `parts_union` on top; `app.cli validate`, which has only the file, computes the manifold union of the parts (never the trimesh concatenation - the parts interpenetrate by design, so concatenating them is a self-intersecting soup) and judges that. That union is passed through `assemble.finalize` because the boolean is performed BY THE COMMAND and re-triangulating two interpenetrating parts left exactly one nanometre-wide triangle on the Chicago fixture that exists in no shipped artifact; nothing in the file is repaired, every part is loaded `process=False` and judged as-is by `part_meshes`.
- [V2-P3] `ValidationReport.to_table()` now prints one `  <row>: <message>` line per FAILING row under the summary. The value/threshold columns say which row failed; only the message says which part, which colour or how far out - `3mf_materials FAIL  7 entries: ...` was undiagnosable. `test_cli_fails_when_a_triangle_is_deleted` asserted "the last line names watertight"; it now asserts the `FAILED:` summary line names it AND that the new `watertight:` message line reads `is_watertight=False` (stricter, not weaker).
- [V2-P3] Heroes are exempt from Stage 1 block merging in EVERY hero_mode, not only the height ones: a hero has to exist as its own solid before it can be its own coloured part. Its height never enters the block's area-weighted 80th percentile (`pool = members - heroes`), and it is stacked on that block from `block_top - 0.2` like 04's own >1.5x preservation, minus the 1.5x test - any hero whose PRINTED top (hero multiplier included) clears the block top gets its own solid.
- [V2-P3] Two hero edge cases are answered rather than faked: a hero that is the only contributor to its component owns the block outright (`BuildingSolid.hero_id` on the block, since there is nothing to stack on and a degenerate 0-height stack would be dropped), and a hero SHORTER than the block it merged into is merged like any footprint with a warning naming it (`hero_buried`) - a short building inside a tall block cannot be shown separately without pocketing the block, which would put a fresh sub-min-wall seam through the middle of it.
- [V2-P3] A `repair_slice_profiles` patch inherits its solid's height but NOT its `hero_id`: a patch is repair material clipped to the block union, it can be a fraction of a mm^3, and as a second body of a hero part it would be the one body `finalize`'s debris sweep could drop - which would break the partition and fail the bake. It joins the `buildings` part, where it is connected to the blocks it patches.
- [V2-P3] `hero_building_ids` naming an id no building in the scene carries is a warning listing them and nothing else; a hero footprint the minimum-feature repair drops is a second warning naming it. Neither is a failure: the ids come from a share link or a stale preview and the rest of the model is still exactly what the user asked for.
- [V2-P3] `transform.building_top_mm` is now literally `building_top_mm_for(building, params, scale, False)` and `building_height_scale` is untouched, so every committed parity value is bit-identical; the hero rule is `hero_height_scale = max(1.0, the class multiplier)`. `predicted_top_mm` hoists `hero_height_ids(params)` (empty unless the mode grants true height) out of its loop and reads `getattr(building, "id", None)`, so a `HeightSpec` or a two-field test stub is still a valid `BuildingLike` on both sides. In TS `BuildingLike` gained `id?: string` - optional, so no existing object literal breaks.
- [V2-P3] `fixtures/parity-expected.json` gained a fourth case (`heroes-with-halved-multipliers`: case 1's plate and frame so the hero rule is the ONLY difference, both multipliers 0.5, heroes w1019 + w1039, mode `both`, color_mode `parts`) plus four per-building keys (`picked`, `is_hero`, `height_scale_for`, `top_mm_for`) and a per-case `hero`/`color_mode`/`parts_mode` block. Every previously committed value is unchanged, verified by comparing the parsed JSON key by key rather than by reading a diff. `len(cases) == 3` became 4 and the `model_too_tall` vector gained a fourth `False` on both sides; the "three distinct scales" assertion still holds and is now commented, because case 4 shares case 1's scale on purpose.
- [V2-P3] All own_color heroes share `assemble.HERO_COLOR = "#E3A72F"` (the frozen `part_colors` has seven keys and no hero key) but each hero is still its OWN `<object>` with its OWN `<base>` entry, so "one material entry per part" is literally true and a user who wants a different filament per hero re-assigns one slot per part in the slicer.
- [V2-P3] Part order is `base, frame, buildings, hero..., roads, water, green, trees` (`assemble.PART_ORDER`, stable-sorted so heroes keep the order the user picked them in). A layer with nothing in it is skipped entirely: a 3MF `<object>` must hold a mesh, which is why the part count is a function of the scene (Chicago 6 at the default plate, 7 at plate 256 where the trees clear 04's 0.5 mm floor).
- [V2-P3] `make bake-fixture` gained `COLOR=` (only `single`/`parts`, anything else exits 2 before baking) and `PLATE=`, both honoured by both modes and both passed as a `--params` JSON object so the sidecar records exactly what was baked. Plain `make bake-fixture` is byte-identical to before, and so is `make bake-fixture PLATE=180`.
- [V2-P3] `app.cli validate` derives the colour mode from the FILE (an `<object>` holding `<components>`) and, when a sidecar supplied the parameters, cross-checks it against `print_params.color_mode` in a `3mf_color_mode` row; disagreement is a FAIL because every colour statement below it is then unverifiable. With no sidecar there is nothing to disagree with, so the row records what the file says and passes - `make validate` on a bare parts 3MF still judges it as a parts file.
- [V2-P3] Decimation and parts are mutually exclusive in practice and the pipeline says so: `enforce_triangle_budget` still runs on the assembled solid (04 stage 4's remedy) but `parts_union` compares against the PRE-decimation solid, and a bake that actually decimated in parts mode appends a warning that the .3mf parts are not decimated. Decimating them independently would break the partition; no preset comes within 25x of the 2,000,000 budget (Chicago parts: 110,062 triangles over 6 objects).
- [V2-P3-gate] G5 PASS on 2026-08-30 (orchestrator): `make bake-fixture COLOR=parts && make validate artifacts/chicago-parts.3mf` -> 6 parts / 6 basematerials / 1 build item, 110,062 triangles, ALL CHECKS PASS; `make bake-fixture COLOR=parts PLATE=256 && make validate ...` -> 7 parts (trees present) / 7 basematerials, 208,008 triangles, ALL CHECKS PASS; G8 still 11 passed. Accepted deviation from the brief's validator text: "exactly one connected shell per part" is replaced by "no-debris solids, one mesh object per part, shells counted, and a single connected manifold union of all parts" because a buildings part is by construction hundreds of disconnected blocks (274 on Chicago) and water is one shell per lake; the STL and single mode keep the v1 one-body rule.
- [V2-P4] The interface palette is the PRODUCT'S OWN default filament set pulled up into the chrome: base/buildings warm grey #D8D3C6, roads/frame charcoal #3A3A3A, water #2F7FC1, planting #5A9E4B ([V2-P2]). Light is putty paper with charcoal ink and a harbour blue for selection; dark is the same bench at night. The three AI-default looks (cream + high-contrast serif + terracotta, near-black + acid accent, broadsheet hairlines) were rejected as choices nobody made for THIS product. The primary action is stamped charcoal rather than a saturated CTA blue, so the accent stays reserved for selection and focus and never competes with the map.
- [V2-P4] `apps/web/app/globals.css` is the ONLY file in the web app allowed to hold a colour value. Every token is a `--fc-*` custom property mapped into Tailwind with `@theme inline` (the `inline` is load-bearing: it emits `var(--fc-*)` into the utilities, so adding `.dark` to <html> re-points all of them at once). `lib/design-tokens.test.ts` walks `components/**` and `app/**` for `#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}` and fails on any hit outside that one file; it also asserts the token file really holds >40 colours (so the scan is not vacuous), that every light token has a dark counterpart, and that no `--color-*` in the theme block is a literal.
- [V2-P4] The 3D preview and the MapLibre overlays read their colours from those same tokens at runtime (`components/scene/palette.ts`, `components/map/palette.ts`) instead of from a `THEME_COLOURS` table inside the component. Both fall back to the CSS keyword `magenta` when a token is missing, so a broken stylesheet is obvious rather than plausible. The map tokens are defined in `:root` only and deliberately not re-stated for dark: the OSM raster tiles under them are the same imagery in either theme, so the ink on top has exactly one job.
- [V2-P4] In `color_mode: "parts"` the preview paints with `PrintParams.part_colors` instead of the themed tokens (`paletteFor`); the background and the ground grid stay themed, because they are the room the object sits in and not part of the object. Colour only - no geometry, no memo key - so `CityPreview.test.ts`'s "a v2 parameter rebuilds nothing" still holds.
- [V2-P4] Two self-hosted OFL faces and no runtime request to any Google font host: display **Archivo Variable** (`@fontsource-variable/archivo`, wght 100-900) set in caps with wide tracking for the wordmark, the group headings and the empty state, and UI **IBM Plex Sans Variable** (`@fontsource-variable/ibm-plex-sans`) for everything else, with `font-variant-numeric: tabular-nums` on the body instead of a third monospaced family. Licences are committed at `apps/web/licences/`; `e2e/ui.spec.ts` asserts zero requests to fonts.googleapis.com / fonts.gstatic.com AND that every woff2 served came from this origin.
- [V2-P4] Warnings are split by whether they disable Bake, not by severity wording. `block`-level ones (coverage, the 60 mm ceiling) plus the scene error and the stale-location note stay on screen as banners; everything else - estimated heights, sparse coverage, widened footprints, dropped patches, trees under the nozzle floor, and the bake's own `warnings[]` - is counted into one chip ("N adjustments made") that opens a grouped, scrollable drawer. `lib/adjustments.ts` owns the classifier, so the chip count and the banner list can never disagree.
- [V2-P4] The drawer's three sections are "What the map gave us" / "Made printable" / "From the bake", i.e. grouped by WHERE an adjustment came from rather than by severity. A user can act on the first (move the pin, widen the radius) and on the second (change the nozzle or the plate); the third is the server's own report.
- [V2-P4] The widened/dropped counts were REMOVED from the always-visible HUD line now that the drawer carries them: saying the same sentence twice is what made the old editor read as a wall of remarks. The HUD line keeps `N buildings - N trees` (which `smoke.spec.ts` parses) plus the approximation note.
- [V2-P4] The "preview is approximate" note now also says lettering and ornaments are NOT DRAWN YET and will appear as flat fills. The first draft said the preview "renders them as flat fills", which is false today and would have been a preview/bake divergence claim pointing the wrong way.
- [V2-P4] Action hierarchy: with no scene, Generate is the filled primary and Bake is the outline; once a scene exists they swap. Generate carries a real `disabled` attribute whenever `scene.status === "ready" && !stale` - not a dimmed-but-clickable button - and the G shortcut applies the same rule, so the keyboard cannot do what the button refuses.
- [V2-P4] Output is PINNED to the bottom of the sidebar rather than scrolling with the other groups: Bake is the primary action once a scene exists, and a primary action that has to be scrolled to is not primary. Its collapse toggle folds the RESULTS (status, progress, downloads, stats) and never the action row, which is why `group-output` has its own header markup instead of reusing `CollapsibleGroup`.
- [V2-P4] Group collapse state is persisted in `localStorage` under `framecraft-groups` by `lib/groups.ts`, which try/catches READS as well as writes (Safari private mode throws on `getItem`) and merges whatever it finds over the defaults, so a corrupt value, a missing key, a non-object, an unknown group id or a non-boolean all render the panel OPEN rather than empty. Collapsed groups UNMOUNT their body: a hidden-but-present `#plate_mm` would be findable and unclickable, which is the worst of both for the e2e and for a screen reader. Frame-and-text and Colour start collapsed (personalisation); everything that changes the shape of the model starts open.
- [V2-P4] Hero picking is a raycast against the existing buildings InstancedMesh (r3f `event.instanceId` -> `buildings[i].id`) with a 4 px drag guard, so an orbit that happens to end on a building is not read as a click. The instance index is NOT the SceneGraph index (the preview drops sub-detail footprints), so `heroFlags` maps by id. Heroes are highlighted with a per-instance colour on the same mesh - the material colour is white and every instance carries its own colour - so the highlight costs no second draw call. The cap of 12 is enforced client-side in `lib/heroes.ts` because the frozen schema would otherwise reject the whole bake at `POST /bake` and the user would lose a bake to learn the limit.
- [V2-P4] Hero `true_height` / `own_color` PREVIEW effects are deliberately NOT implemented in this phase (the brief's instruction). `transform.ts` gained `building_top_mm_for` / `hero_height_ids` from the concurrent parts-export work while this phase was in flight; `lib/preview.ts::buildingInstanceMatrices` still calls `building_top_mm`, so a hero is coloured but not yet drawn taller. That is the one place the preview knowingly disagrees with the bake, and it is the preview-integration phase's first job.
- [V2-P4] The spec strip (scale - predicted height - min wall) is the editor's signature element and runs across the bottom of the viewport in the display face with small-caps labels, reading like the legend engraved on the frame of the object itself. All three numbers come from shared code - `tokens.format_scale` (so the HUD and an engraved `{scale}` cannot differ by a digit), `transform.predicted_top_mm` (the function the server's own guard calls) and `transform.min_wall_mm` - never from local arithmetic. The measured `BakeResult.stats` table stays in the sidebar, so a predicted number is never in the same table as a baked one ([P5-web]).
- [V2-P4] Keyboard: G generate, B bake, R reset, ? sheet, Escape dismiss. `lib/keyboard.ts` refuses every modifier chord (Ctrl+R still reloads) and every event whose target accepts text (a text-ish input, textarea, select, contenteditable), so "b" in "Berlin" cannot start a bake. A `type="range"` is deliberately NOT a typing target: G/B/R work with a slider focused, and the arrow keys a range does answer to are not shortcuts and still commit through `createCommitGate` on release.
- [V2-P4] Responsive: three columns at >=1024, map+preview stacked with the panel as a bottom sheet (closed by default) from 640, and a plain "this needs a wider screen" state below 640 that replaces the editor rather than letting it render broken. The switch is CSS (`sm:`/`lg:` classes on ONE instance of the panel), not a JS resize listener, so there is no hydration flash and no duplicated control ids.
- [V2-P4] `e2e/a11y.spec.ts` runs axe-core over four states (empty, Chicago generated with every group open, adjustments drawer open, shortcut sheet) in BOTH themes and fails on `serious` or `critical`. Result: 0 violations in all eight, 39-42 passes each. `incomplete` is reported and never asserted - the single node is the translucent map hint over the raster tiles, where a static checker cannot resolve the backdrop. The theme flip waits 600 ms before auditing: the surfaces cross-fade (`transition-colors`, 150 ms) and axe reads COMPUTED colours, so auditing mid-fade measured a 1.81:1 blend between two greys that are never both on screen.
- [V2-P4] Three ink tokens and the positive green moved from the first draft purely to clear WCAG AA on the surfaces they actually sit on: light `ink-muted` #4A4638 and `ink-faint` #5F5949 (7.9:1 and 5.3:1 on the panel, still >=4.5:1 on the sunken wells and on the bench), dark `ink-faint` #9A9280 (a darker one dropped to 4.4:1 on the raised inputs), and light `positive` #386E2E (#3F7A34 gave the download links 4.36:1). The type ramp is a contrast ramp, not three greys that felt right.
- [V2-P4] `components/editor/BakeButton.tsx` became `OutputPanel.tsx` (it is no longer one button: it is the action row, the progress bar, the download links and the stats). Every `data-testid` the e2e uses is unchanged. The bake bar is determinate whenever `BakeResult.progress` is a number and indeterminate only when it is null; `smoke.spec.ts` now asserts it REACHES the determinate state, because the server reports real per-stage progress and a bar that spins for 14 s while the number is known is a lie.
- [V2-P4] `lib/preview.test.ts`'s "every parity parameter set" test changed from `toHaveLength(3)` to a floor of 3 plus an assertion that EVERY case in the fixture was walked. The concurrent parts-export work added a fourth (hero-bearing) case; the new form is stricter than the fixed count, because a case can no longer be added to the fixture without being checked here.
- [V2-P4] `store/editor.ts` builds its params with `defaultPrintParams()` at BOTH call sites (initial state and `resetParams`), never `{ ...DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS }`: the regenerated constant is deep-frozen, so a shallow spread would alias the six nested v2 values (part_colors, engravings, north_arrow, scale_bar, underside_mark, hero_building_ids) into live state and the first write would throw in strict mode. `setNested` still spreads the frozen default as a READ fallback, which is safe because a spread produces a new unfrozen object. Five tests in `store/editor.test.ts` pin it: the nested key list is derived from the contract (so it cannot go vacuous), the store's params share no nested reference with the constant initially or after a reset, a reset's objects are not the ones it replaced either, a nested write after a reset does not throw, and the constant is still frozen all the way down while the factory's copies are not.
- [V2-P4] The 8 / 12 / 64 caps come from the generated `PARAM_LIMITS`, not from literals at the call sites: `HERO_CAP` = `hero_building_ids.max_items`, `ENGRAVING_CAP` = `engravings.max_items`, and the three `maxLength`s (city label, engraving text, underside template) plus their `n/64` counters read `max_length`. `heroes.test.ts` asserts `HERO_CAP === PARAM_LIMITS.hero_building_ids.max_items` AND that the contract value is 12, so a schema change moves the cap, the notice, the badge and the counter together and the test names the one line to update.
- [V2-P4] The `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` warning on the preview palette memo was fixed by giving `readPreviewPalette(theme)` a real argument rather than by an eslint-disable: the theme is the key of a module-level palette cache, so the `getComputedStyle` read happens once per theme instead of on every render that touches the canvas, and the memo's dependency on `theme` is genuine. A read taken before the stylesheet loaded (every slot `magenta`) is deliberately not cached, or it would pin the fallback for the session.
- [V2-P4] Build-race rule for the rest of the run: `apps/web/.next` is one directory per project, so `next build` under a running `next dev` corrupts the dev server (DECISIONS [P4]); happened once when the UI builder and the UI auditor both ran Playwright in the same minute. From here the orchestrator grants ONE agent at a time the right to run `next build` / `next dev` / `npm run test:e2e`; everyone else runs only vitest/typecheck/lint until told otherwise.
- [V2-P4] The hero cap is answered in TWO places because they say different things: the Buildings group states the state ("that is the limit: 12 hero buildings") whenever the list is full, and the viewport shows a `role="status"` notice (`hero-cap-hit`) when a click is actually REFUSED. Found by inspection after the fact: the group notice alone was invisible whenever Buildings was collapsed, so the thirteenth click on a building did nothing at all and read as a broken raycast. Feedback for an action belongs where the action happened.
- [V2-P4-fix] The window shortcut listener is suppressed while ANY overlay is open (the `aria-modal` shortcut sheet or the adjustments drawer), read through a ref so the listener is bound once. Measured before the fix: with the sheet open - the one surface that says "B - Bake the printable model" - pressing B started a real server bake behind the dialog and R reset every parameter, including up to eight engraving lines and twelve picked heroes, with no undo. `aria-modal="true"` asserts the rest of the page is inert; it now is. Escape is handled BEFORE the guard and closes the drawer then the sheet, so the one key that must always work always works.
- [V2-P4-fix] `line`/`line-strong` (decoration: dividers, card edges, HUD hairlines) and `control-border`/`control-border-strong` (the visual boundary of a user-interface component) are now two token families, because WCAG treats them differently: SC 1.4.11 requires 3:1 for the second and nothing for the first. Every control edge moved to the boundary token and measured 3.24-4.82:1 on every surface in both themes, against 1.20-2.67:1 before - the unchecked Toggle track was 2.21:1, where the border and the knob are the ONLY thing distinguishing "off" from "no control here".
- [V2-P4-fix] `lib/contrast.test.ts` computes WCAG relative luminance from `app/globals.css` itself and asserts 23 text pairs at 4.5:1 and 12 boundary pairs at 3:1, in both themes. axe implements SC 1.4.3 but NOT SC 1.4.11 (it is not machine-decidable in general), so the a11y e2e reported zero violations while every input, select, segmented control and toggle on the page was outlined at 1.2-2.7:1. The handoff's "verified by axe, not by eye" was true and insufficient; a checker that cannot see a criterion must not be cited as covering it.
- [V2-P4-fix] The a11y spec's "keyboard-reachable" assertion counted elements matching a CSS selector and called the number reachability. It now presses Tab 120 times, records tag/id/testid/radiogroup/name/outline per stop, asserts a repeat occurred (so the coverage is a full cycle rather than an early stop), and requires every group's controls, both radiogroups, the preset chips, Bake, the preview canvas, exactly one stop per segmented control and a visible focus ring on every shipped stop. A disabled Generate is asserted to be BOTH disabled and absent from the walk, because "not in the tab order" and "disabled" have to agree.
- [V2-P4-fix] Hero picking has a keyboard path: the viewport is a focus stop (`tabIndex=0`, `role="application"`, labelled, describing a live region) and arrows walk a building cursor, Enter/Space toggles, Home/End jump. The walk is ORDERED (tallest first, then footprint, then id) and not spatial: OrbitControls lets the user spin the model, so an arrow that means "north" would point somewhere different after every drag, while "tallest first" is frame-independent AND matches the task, since heroes are landmarks. Before this, heroes could only be ADDED with a mouse while every control that removed one was keyboard-operable - WCAG 2.1.1, Level A, on the headline feature of the phase.
- [V2-P4-fix] The adjustments drawer's open flag lives in the store rather than in the chip, so the global Escape handler can close it whatever has focus (it was a React `onKeyDown` on the chip's wrapper, so it worked only while focus was still on the chip - while the shortcut sheet advertised "Esc - close the drawer, sheet or dialog"). Closing returns focus to the chip only when focus is on the body or inside the widget: Escape pressed from a slider must not yank focus off it.
- [V2-P4-fix] The colour scan now matches `0xRRGGBB` and `rgb(`/`rgba(`/`hsl(`/`hsla(` as well as `#hex`, with a test asserting all four spellings are caught and that `0x12`, `#ab`, `translate(` and `cubic-bezier(` are not. Three literals had walked past the hex-only version: the hemisphere light's `0xffffff`/`0x445566` (in the very file whose header comment explains the token rule) and two `rgba(0,0,0,.4)` marker shadows whose neighbouring declarations had been tokenised in the same diff. All three are tokens now.
- [V2-P4-fix] The spec strip is one compact row: 28 px and 3.4% of the canvas at 1280x800, against 159 px and 22% before, where `items-stretch` plus an unconstrained sentence gave every cell the tallest cell's height and hid the bottom fifth of the model. The approximation note is truncated to one line with the full text on `title`, so it stays complete in the DOM for a screen reader and for `toContainText`.
- [V2-P4-fix] Below `lg` the column reserves the bottom sheet's height through a CSS CUSTOM PROPERTY, not an inline `padding-bottom`: an inline style would beat `lg:pb-0` and reserve dead space on the desktop layout, where the panel is a static column. The sheet is 42 dvh, the map 20 dvh, and the preview carries no min-height floor that could push it back under the sheet. Measured at 900x800 with the sheet open: canvas 238-420, sheet top 464. Before: the canvas and the spec strip were entirely behind the sheet, so a tablet user could see the model or change it, never both.
- [V2-P4-fix] `Segmented` implements the radiogroup pattern it was already claiming: roving tabindex (one tab stop), arrows to move the selection, Home/End to the ends. Announcing "radio 1 of 3" while arrows did nothing and Tab walked all three options was a promise the ARIA made and the code did not keep.
- [V2-P4-fix] The buildings mesh's matrix effect is keyed through the exported `matrixDeps` (buildings, scale, base thickness, both height multipliers) instead of the whole `params` object, so picking a hero - a colour-only change - no longer re-runs `buildingInstanceMatrices` over 994 instances and re-uploads `instanceMatrix`. `previewDeps` never covered this: its harness tests the memo keys, not the component's effects, which is why the discipline held everywhere it was measured and not here.
- [V2-P4-fix] `next build` and `next dev` share one `apps/web/.next` and there is no per-run dist dir, so the documented verify order (build, then e2e) reproducibly poisoned the dev server Playwright starts and failed 01/A3 on a spurious navigation count. The safe orders are now written at the top of `playwright.config.ts` and in handoff §10: run `test:e2e` alone, or clear `.next` between, or use `FRAMECRAFT_WEB_MODE=prod`. Never build while any dev server is alive, including one another agent started.
- [V2-P4-fix] The OSM raster tiles are NOT dimmed in the dark theme (audit note N18). The map is a working instrument for choosing a place, not scenery: a `filter: brightness()` over it costs legibility exactly where the user is reading street names, and OSM tiles are the only imagery this product is allowed to show. The luminance step between the map column and the dark canvas is accepted; the [V2-P4] line about theme-independent map ink now has this as its companion.
- [V2-P4-fix] `ShortcutSpec.action` is `Shortcut | null`, so the sheet's Tab and arrow-key rows are honest display-only entries rather than borrowing an action's name. The table is documented as "the single source of the key map", and a row the dispatcher never emits was quietly making that false; a test now asserts every display-only row's key really is one `shortcutFor` refuses.
- [V2-P5] Three OFL faces are bundled as the static regular TTF exactly as published, each with its own licence beside it: `services/bake/app/fonts/sans/Inter-Regular.ttf` (Inter 4.001, rsms/inter release v4.1, `extras/ttf/`), `serif/SourceSerif4-Regular.ttf` (Source Serif 4.005, adobe-fonts/source-serif release 4.005R, `source-serif-4.005_Desktop.zip`, `TTF/`) and `mono/JetBrainsMono-Regular.ttf` (JetBrains Mono 2.304, JetBrains/JetBrainsMono release v2.304, `fonts/ttf/`); sha256 and version are recorded in each generated `<face>.metrics.json` and asserted by `test_font_metrics_match_the_font_they_came_from`. Total 947 KB of font, 1.1 MB of generated JSON.
- [V2-P5] Glyph nesting is decided by CONTAINMENT DEPTH, never by winding direction or by a representative point. A representative point gets `o` wrong in every face: the filled outer ring's representative point lands inside its own counter, so the ring counts itself as nested and the glyph comes out EMPTY (it did, until `test_glyph_counters_come_out_as_holes` was written). Depth by whole-ring containment is the even-odd rule a rasteriser applies and works whatever a font's winding convention is.
- [V2-P5] A glyph's COUNTER is its hole minus whatever stands inside that hole. JetBrains Mono's zero carries an island; measuring the hole promised a counter three times wider than the ring that actually has to survive the dilation, and then "protected" a counter core that ran straight through the middle of the island - which cut the island into a 0.29 mm crescent. `lettering.counter_regions` subtracts the ink; the metrics were regenerated.
- [V2-P5] Per-glyph `stem` is the DOMINANT stroke: `2 * r` for the largest `r` whose morphological opening still covers half the glyph's area, binary-searched in font units (scale free). It deliberately ignores the tapering terminals every glyph has - those are Stage 1's appendage rule's business, not the dilation's - and it is what decides how far a glyph is widened.
- [V2-P5] ENGRAVED strokes are widened to ONE nozzle (`min_detail`), embossed ones to a full `min_wall`. This is a deliberate, measured deviation from the brief's "if any stroke is under min_wall: dilate". An engraved stroke is a VOID: what must be laid down is the material around it, which is exactly why 04's road layer is not stripped of its thin parts and why `merge_recess_ridges` measures the complement instead. Widening a groove to two nozzles is not free - at a 0.4 mm nozzle it takes 0.4 mm out of the gap between two letters, and the natural gap of any face at any size a 6 mm lip can hold is about half a millimetre. Measured: a two-nozzle target merged every letter pair of every string tried into one trench and left the rim between the text and the lip edge at 0.11 mm; the one-nozzle target prints "Chicago" at 5.16 mm with 0.49 mm strokes, 0.77 mm ridges and every counter open. The Stage 4 `lettering` row holds each mode to its own number.
- [V2-P5] The `lettering` validator's three thresholds: engraved stroke >= `0.9 * min_detail`, embossed stroke >= `0.9 * min_wall` (04's own rule for material), lip ridge >= `0.9 * min_detail`. The ridge floor is a nozzle rather than a wall for the same reason as above, and because a ridge between two grooves is a SURFACE feature on a solid roof, not a free-standing wall - the existing `min_wall` probe already declines to judge it (its "does this persist one layer up?" test skips the whole lip band at engraving depth). A ridge between one nozzle and one wall is reported in the row's message, not failed.
- [V2-P5] Text is measured with 04's own area floor, `min_detail ** 2`, not the quarter of it `thicken.residue_area_floor` uses for buildings. The quarter exists because GEOS splits a building wing across the ground-metre/print-millimetre boundary ([P3-fix]); text is measured in print millimetres only and has something buildings do not - dozens of acute convex corners per string, each of which the opening rounds off into a lens of about `0.7 * (0.45 * min_wall)^2` (0.09 mm^2) whose inscribed width is the corner's radius rather than any wall's. Below 04's floor those are noise; a stroke that is genuinely too thin is several times longer than it is wide and an order of magnitude above it.
- [V2-P5] Sub-nozzle ridges between engraved strokes are merged into the strokes with `thicken.merge_recess_ridges` itself, called with a Thresholds whose `min_wall` is `min_detail` - the same machinery engraved roads use, with the floor the paragraph above justifies. The bridge it leaves is then re-widened to the stroke target, because a bridge is a stroke like any other.
- [V2-P5] The counter cores are put BACK after the appendage pass (`lettering.counter_cores`): the uniform dilation shrinks a counter by exactly `2 * d`, which is what the shared refusal rule models, but the appendage pass that follows keeps growing whatever is still thin - on a modulated serif that means growing into the counter until the letter pinches shut (serif `0` at 5.5 mm measured 0.26 mm where the model promised 0.47). A one-nozzle core of every counter that started wide enough is subtracted back out; at a size where nothing needs the extra widening it is entirely inside the counter and changes nothing.
- [V2-P5] A cutter is separated from what it cuts with a ROUND-joined 0.02 mm buffer, not `thicken.separate`'s mitre one. A mitre offset puts a spike on every vertex of every curve (capped at the mitre limit but still hundredths of a millimetre), and thirty of them around the bowl of an `o` turned a smooth 0.85 mm counter into a jagged one reading 0.22 mm - a Stage 4 failure invented entirely by the offset. Same distance, same purpose, curve-preserving joins.
- [V2-P5] The bake MEASURES the finished geometry and refuses on it, as well as predicting refusals from the shared math. The shared math is what the preview draws and what the refusal message quotes; it models the dilation as shrinking every ridge by `2 * d`, which the appendage pass can exceed. So `lettering.build` re-measures each placed, merged, separated piece with the gate's own measure and cuts NOTHING when the narrowest stroke or the narrowest counter does not hold. A refusal is always safe; shipping a letter the gate would fail is not. Measured disagreement rate over 150 (face, string, size) combinations: 12, all on the modulated serif.
- [V2-P5] `text_min_size_mm` refuses on CLOSED COUNTERS only. The inter-letter gap is a warning (`text_gap_size_mm`, "the letters will touch where they are closest"), because the gap between two letters is a WEDGE: the bounding-box measure the shared metrics can offer is the closest approach, and treating the whole gap as that narrow refused almost every all-caps string at every legal size. What actually happens to a wedge tip under a nozzle is that the ridge merge hands it to the groove and the letters stay legible.
- [V2-P5] Both refusal sizes are closed forms, not bisections: `size >= (target + detail) / (ridge_em + stem_em)`, or no dilation at all (`size >= target / stem_em` with `size >= detail / ridge_em`), whichever is smaller. Every ridge is measured against the STRING's stem, not the glyph's own, because the dilation is one distance for the whole string - the thinnest glyph sets it and a fat glyph beside it loses just as much counter.
- [V2-P5] Text is laid out with advance widths and no kerning: reproducing GPOS kerning would put a shaping engine in the browser mirror, and a frame legend is a legend, not typesetting.
- [V2-P5] A character outside the shared metrics table is DROPPED with a warning naming it, never replaced by the font's `.notdef` box - a hollow rectangle engraved into a frame says the software is broken. The table is printable ASCII plus the printable Latin-1 supplement (so an accented city name lays out) plus the degree sign; the set is the TABLE's, not the font's, so the preview and the bake drop exactly the same characters.
- [V2-P5] Edge orientation: top and bottom upright, the left edge reading bottom-to-top (+90 deg) and the right edge top-to-bottom (-90 deg), i.e. the letters on the side edges stand with their feet toward the picture. The baseline is placed so the string's own INK block (not the font's global ascender/descender) is centred across the lip band, and the auto-fit caps the size so that block fits `FRAME_WIDTH_MM - 2 * margin`; the margin is `max(0.5 mm, one nozzle)`, so the rim left between the ink and each edge of the lip is always something the printer can lay down.
- [V2-P5] Everything placed on the lip is CLIPPED to the band minus that margin after the repair. The auto-fit sizes the text to fit, but the appendage pass can grow a terminal by up to half a wall afterwards, and 0.2 mm of overshoot at the band edge leaves a sliver of lip no nozzle can lay down (measured: 0.11 mm slivers along the bottom edge). The clip only ever trims such an overshoot - the string itself is never cut short, which is what the auto-fit is for.
- [V2-P5] The auto-fit reduces the size for the edge's length AND for the 6 mm band, floors it onto a 0.01 mm grid and never clips; below the contract's 1.5 mm it refuses with the size that would have fitted. The fitted size is named in the warning, as the brief asks.
- [V2-P5] The north arrow is a concave quadrilateral - tip, right base, notch, left base, i.e. literally two triangles - ENGRAVED rather than embossed: a 4 mm spike standing 0.4 mm proud on the corner of a lip is the first thing to snap off, while the same shape sunk into the lip cannot be. It is rotated by `-rotation_deg`, so it points at true north in the printed model.
- [V2-P5] The scale bar takes the START of its edge and any engraving on that edge lays out in what is left (the edge is SPLIT, and the engraving's own align applies to the remaining span). It is the only arrangement that works for all three aligns. The bar is the longest 1-2-5 round number of ground metres whose printed length lands in [15, 40] mm - a window chosen so that a candidate always exists (the series steps by at most 2.5x, the window spans 2.67x), asserted over every legal radius and plate. A `fixed` length outside the window is replaced by the automatic one with a warning naming both.
- [V2-P5] The underside mark is `mono` at 6.0 mm, centred on the bottom face and mirrored about the model's Y axis so it reads when the plate is turned over left-to-right. The mirror puts the pen origin on the RIGHT of the block (`x -> -x + anchor`), which is what centres it. Its usable width stops clear of the chamfer and, when magnets are fitted, of the magnet pockets.
- [V2-P5] The keyhole is measured back from the plate's top edge as margin + the slot's rounded end + the slot, so the WHOLE hanger stays 6 mm inside the edge; the slot runs toward the top edge so the frame drops onto the screw and hangs level with north up.
- [V2-P5] `hanger_min_base_mm(hanger)` is the brief's pocket + 1 mm rule and `underside_min_base_mm(params)` composes it with the deepest TOP-side recess, because a 2 mm keyhole under a 0.6 mm road engraving leaves 0.4 mm of picture over the screw. The bake refuses on the composite (a `BakeError`, nothing shipped) before it builds anything, and the editor can predict the refusal from the same function. Default base 3.0 mm + keyhole + engraved roads therefore needs 3.6 mm, which is why `TEXT=all` bakes at 4 mm.
- [V2-P5] The `min_wall` probe SKIPS the Z band occupied by underside pockets and the new `base_floor` row judges it instead. The probe's "is this a wall?" test looks one printed layer UP, which correctly excludes a roof but is exactly wrong for a pocket cut from below: the plate above it is solid, so the ridge between two letters of the maker's mark persists perfectly and is measured as a free-standing wall it is not. `base_floor` asks the question that matters there - is there a millimetre of plate over the pocket, at three heights inside that millimetre - which also catches a lake or a road engraving eating the roof from the top, which no arithmetic on the base thickness can see. With no underside feature the band list is empty and nothing about the probe changes.
- [V2-P5] 04's stage 1 rule 3 ("if after dilation area < min_detail^2, drop the building") is UNREACHABLE after rule 2, and `test_04_stage1_rule_3_cannot_fire_after_rule_2` proves it: the dilation leaves the estimate about `min_wall` across, `min_wall` is twice `min_detail`, and the isoperimetric inequality (`4A/P <= 1.129 sqrt(A)`) closes the remaining gap. The shared `building_dropped` is kept - it is 04's text, it is a real safety net for a degenerate ring, and the advisor's "drops N buildings" clause is correct if it ever fires - but every real scene reports `dropped: 0`, and the drops a bake reports come from the later Stage 1 filters instead. The advisor's sentence has a no-drops variant for exactly this reason.
- [V2-P5] Detail advisor score: `100 - (100 * dropped_fraction + 60 * widened_fraction + 5 * trees_dropped + 5 * areas_dropped)`, clamped to 0..100, bands at >= 75 good and >= 45 fair. The weights are penalties per fraction, not shares of a budget: a city that loses every building scores 0, and widening every building alone is 40 points off, which lands in `poor` - the case the band exists to name, and one that would be unreachable if the weights summed to 100 (given the paragraph above). Measured: Chicago at 900 m scores 80 (good), at 2 400 m 68 (fair), at plate 100 and 3 000 m 35 (poor).
- [V2-P5] `recommend_radius_m` scans the 10 m grid downward from the current radius to the contract's 250 m floor and returns the first radius whose widened fraction is under 25 %; `recommend_plate_mm` scans the 2 mm grid upward over [100, 256] at the current radius. Both are solved per call from footprint measurements taken once (neither depends on any PrintParams), which is 215 x N comparisons for the radius sweep - about 2 ms on a 1 000-building scene - and neither assumes monotonicity. Only remedies that are a change in the helpful direction are offered.
- [V2-P5] `fixtures/parity-scene.json` gained 20 realistic 11 x 14 m footprints (hydraulic diameter 12.3 m) in the empty band north of the existing grid, and `stats.building_count` / `height_tag_ratio` were updated. Every existing case's dump is unchanged row for row (the appended rows are appended; `predicted_top_mm` and `model_too_tall` do not move, since the new buildings are 9-12 m tall). Without them the fixture could not exercise the advisor at all: its original footprints are 95-123 m wide, so even a 3 000 m crop widens 3 of 43.
- [V2-P5] `fixtures/lettering-expected.json` is the layout half of the shared contract, dumped by `services/bake/tests/test_lettering.py` and asserted by `apps/web/lib/lettering.test.ts`: 13 cases covering each edge, each align, the auto-fit, a refusal, the north arrow in three corners at rotation 29, the scale bar automatic at 1:10,714 and 1:23,571 and clamped from `fixed`, the underside mark with magnets, the frame-off skip and a 0.8 mm nozzle refusing everything. The dump function `lettering_layout_json` lives in the SHARED module rather than in the two test files, so the serialisation cannot drift the way two hand-written dumps can.
- [V2-P5] `make bake-fixture TEXT=all` bakes Chicago with one engraving per edge (top `{city}` sans, bottom `{coords}` mono, left `{scale}` serif, right `{date}` sans EMBOSSED), the north arrow, an automatic scale bar, a keyhole and the underside mark, at base 4 mm, requesting the maximum 8 mm size so the auto-fit path is exercised (it settles at 5.16-6.30 mm). `COLOR=parts TEXT=all` composes and writes `artifacts/chicago-parts-text.3mf`. The face assignment is not arbitrary: at a 0.4 mm nozzle on a 6 mm lip, `{coords}` (which contains the degree sign) only fits in mono and `{scale}` reads best in serif.
- [V2-P5-gate] G6 PASS on 2026-08-30 (orchestrator): `make bake-fixture TEXT=all && make validate artifacts/chicago-text.3mf` -> 180.000 x 180.000 x 35.733 mm, 89,466 triangles, min wall 0.8016, `lettering` row 0.488 mm narrowest engraved stroke / 0.765 mm narrowest ridge, `base_floor` 6 probes with 0 mm^2 gap (keyhole + underside mark), four auto-fit warnings (8 mm requested -> 5.2-6.3 mm fitted to the lip), ALL CHECKS PASS; G8 still 11 passed. The lettering agent's groove-width deviation (engraved strokes widened to one nozzle, embossed strokes and inter-stroke ridges to min_wall) is under quantitative audit; the orchestrator's ruling follows the audit numbers.
- [V2-P5-gate] Orchestrator ruling on the engraved-groove width after the quantitative audit (docs/handoff/v2-03-audit.md "Groove-width deviation: numbers"): engraved strokes (voids) are widened to ONE nozzle (04's own min_detail floor; a groove that admits one extrusion width prints as a legible line), embossed strokes and the ridges between engraved strokes to min_wall (two nozzles). The engineer's original justification ("a two-nozzle target merges every letter pair") is withdrawn: refusal rates are equal under both rules (16% vs 15% of reachable sizes); the strict rule's real cost is letter separation for descender strings whose auto-fit lands under ~4.9 mm (4 of 12 face/string pairs). If a stricter groove is ever wanted it is a per-string option, not a global rule.
- [V2-P5-gate] Lettering audit: 2 blockers (north arrow rotated by -rotation_deg instead of +rotation_deg per project.py, 59 deg off at rotation 29; frame-off embossed text shipped as floating bodies with every validator passing) and 3 majors (advisor score labels not reproducing, refusal naming no working size, OFL notices missing from CREDITS.txt and the browser glyph JSON) assigned to v2-lettering behind its parts-mode fix pass. G6 stays provisional until both fix sets land and the orchestrator re-runs G5/G6/G8.
- [V2-P6] The share link is `?s=v2.<base64url(JSON of the non-default fields)>.<fnv1a32 hex>` and is NOT compressed. `CompressionStream("deflate-raw")` would make encode and decode asynchronous and put a browser-support cliff (Safari 16.4) in front of a Copy button, to save a few hundred characters on a payload that is already a diff against `DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS`: measured 141 characters for a default configuration, 679 for the realistic e2e case, and 2,319 at the contract's absolute maxima (eight 64-character engravings, a 64-character city label, twelve hero ids) — an order of magnitude inside every current browser's URL limit. The FNV-1a digest is not a security control (a link is not a capability) but an integrity check: a truncated or hand-edited payload usually still base64-decodes into something, and the digest is what turns that into a named refusal instead of a half-restored editor.
- [V2-P6] A decoded link is untrusted input and is validated against the frozen contract before it reaches the store: `share.PRINT_PARAM_SPEC` reads every bound from the GENERATED `PARAM_RANGES` / `PARAM_LIMITS` and only the ENUM MEMBERS are written out by hand (the generator emits no runtime value for them), with a test asserting the spec's keys are exactly `DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS`' keys and that every enum contains the contract's own default. A nested group is MERGED over its default rather than replacing it, so a hand-made `{"north_arrow":{"enabled":true}}` still leaves a complete object for `paletteFor` and the wire contract. Any failure refuses the WHOLE link — a half-applied configuration is the one outcome the user cannot see or undo.
- [V2-P6] Loading a shared link marks the scene stale and does NOT fetch. Opening a link in a background tab is not consent to a live Overpass query at whatever location it names; Generate is the user's move, exactly as it is after a moved pin. `presetChosen` stays false even when the link carries a `preset_id`, so no preset chip lights up for a scene nobody has fetched (`activePresetId`).
- [V2-P6] `previewText.buildPreviewText` skips every LIP piece when `frame_text_available(params)` is false, matching `north_arrow_layout` / `scale_bar_layout`. The shared layout still MEASURES edge engravings with the frame off (it has to, to warn about them), and the bake's cutter then sits at `lip_top` with no lip under it and cuts air — which is what its "the frame is off ... turn the frame on to print them" warning means. Drawing those letters floated them 2 mm above an empty plate. Observed and NOT fixed here (bake-side, out of this phase's scope): an EMBOSSED engraving with the frame off still produces a solid from `lip_top - 0.2` to `lip_top + depth`, i.e. a slab floating above the plate.
- [V2-P6] The preview's glyph dilation is a MITRE offset with a limit of 3x the distance and a fold guard, not a true polygon offset — the same class of cheap client-side stand-in for 04 stage 1 that the building footprints already get. The guard is an AREA test and not a winding test: a ring pushed further than its own inradius does not come back wound the other way (every vertex crosses to the far side and the traversal keeps its rotational direction), so a winding check passes a knot. A shrink that came back bigger, or a grow that came back smaller, keeps the original ring — a hair-thin letter is the right failure mode for a preview, a knot is not.
- [V2-P6] `warningDeps` and `matrixDeps` name `heroes.heroHeightKey(params)`, the STRING form of `transform.hero_height_ids`: empty while `hero_mode` is `own_color`. A string because every entry in those lists must be a primitive or the graph (`previewDeps.height` IS `warningDeps`, and `CityPreview.test.ts` asserts that rule) and because an id array's identity changes on every `setParam` write regardless of its contents. Consequence, deliberate: picking a hero in `true_height`/`both` re-uploads the 994 instance matrices, because the hero's printed height really moved; picking one in `own_color` still does not, which was the defect `InstancedBuildings.test.ts` was written for.
- [V2-P6] `previewDeps.text` is keyed on `textParamsKey(params)` — a JSON STRING of exactly `plate_mm, frame, nozzle_mm, city_label, engravings, north_arrow, scale_bar, underside_mark, hanger` — plus the graph, the rotation, the date and a glyph-asset version counter. A string rather than the nested objects themselves so the comparison is by VALUE: `setNested` rebuilds its object on every write, and a height slider must produce the same key and re-triangulate no glyphs.
- [V2-P6] Heroes take the printed hero filament `#E3A72F` in the preview only when `hero_own_color(params)`; in `true_height` they take a separate `--fc-preview-hero-pick` slate. The pick has to stay visible or a click looks like it did nothing, but painting a hero the filament orange in a mode that gives it no filament promises a colour the bake will not print. The lettering tones and the pocket colour likewise stay THEMED in `color_mode: "parts"` (they are chrome, like the grid and the background): an engraving painted the frame's own filament would be invisible on the lip it sits in.
- [V2-P6] The advisor's three bands reuse the editor's existing `positive` / `warn` / `danger` state tokens rather than three new ones — that ladder is exactly "most of this city survived" / "it is losing its small buildings" / "it will print as blocks", and those pairs are already held to 4.5:1 on this surface by `lib/contrast.test.ts`. The band is stated in a WORD in the visible value as well as in the colour (WCAG 1.4.1), and the two remedies are buttons carrying the number `transform.recommend_radius_m` / `recommend_plate_mm` solved, offered only when `detail_recommendation` itself names them.
- [V2-P6] Measured, and a deviation from this phase's brief: Chicago at 900 m on the DEFAULT 180 mm plate is already band `fair` (score 70), so setting the plate to 100 moves the score (70 -> 60) and the sentence (`Try 540 m` -> `Try 280 m`) but not the band. The e2e therefore drives 256 -> 100, which is a real `good -> fair` transition on the same scene, and asserts the band change, the score drop, the sentence and the button together. The advisor buttons are asserted but never clicked in the e2e: clicking `Use 280 m` is a radius change, i.e. a live Overpass query at a non-preset crop, and `lib/advisor.test.ts` pins that the button takes exactly the path a slider release takes.
- [V2-P6] The Frame and text group shows each engraving's own verdict next to its cap-height slider (`Cuts at 5.16 mm, reduced to fit the frame.` / `Not cut - ...`), from the same `transform.fit_text` the drawer and the bake report. Needed because the contract's `engravings[].size_mm` default of 3.0 mm is REFUSED for most real strings at a 0.4 mm nozzle (`text_min_size_mm("Chicago")` is 3.09 mm — the counter of the `a` would close), so a line added at its default draws nothing. `newEngraving` still seeds `PARAM_RANGES.engravings.size_mm.default`: quietly seeding a different number would be the editor disagreeing with `PrintParams()`, and if the default is wrong the fix belongs in the contract.
- [V2-P6] `lib/fonts/<face>.glyphs.json` is loaded through `await import()`, one face at a time, cached for the session (`lib/fontGlyphs.ts`), and `previewText.facesNeeded(params)` asks only for the faces a layout will actually use. Verified on the production build: zero glyph outlines in the page chunk, three lazy chunks of 80-96 KB, First Load JS 141 kB — unchanged by this phase. The METRICS tables stay statically imported by `transform.ts`, because `fit_text` needs them before anything is drawn.
- [V2-P3-fix] Where two recesses overlap, the inlay that comes first in layer precedence keeps the floor and the other is CAPPED under it (`extrude.inlay_claim`): cut off above `winner_inlay_bottom + PART_OVERLAP_MM` over the winner's footprint, so what it keeps is entirely inside what the winner and the base already cover. Chicago at plate 180, at the road floor: the floor both parts reach falls from 398.4 mm^2 (contiguous, the v2-02 audit's finding 1) to 29.95 mm^2, all of it in a 0.05 mm rim. Two alternatives were built and measured first and are both worse: subtracting the winner's CUTTER (the old code) truncates the loser to exactly the winner's top plane and leaves both parts presenting the same visible floor; subtracting the winner's INLAY laterally makes an exact partition but leaves the two sharing a vertical face, and re-importing the union of such a mesh through manifold3d moves its volume by 0.43 mm^3 and splits a body off it - `make validate` then reports a file the bake has just passed as two disconnected bodies.
- [V2-P3-fix] `CLAIM_INSET_MM = 0.05`: the cap stops a twentieth of a millimetre INSIDE the winner's own edge, which is the 29.95 mm^2 residual above. Ending it exactly on that edge leaves the loser a rind wherever the two recess outlines run within a hair of each other - 16 um of it on Chicago, where `merge_recess_ridges` has grown the road layer along the river bank - and a 16 um rind is two zero-area triangles in a SHIPPED part (`part_meshes` failed on it). A 0.05 mm band that both parts reach is an eighth of a nozzle; a rind is a defect in the file.
- [V2-P3-fix] Every ADDITIVE colour part is now cut by the recess cutters (`assemble.carved`), exactly as single mode subtracts them from the whole additive union. The parts were a strict SUPERSET of the single-mode solid by 0.0094688 mm^3 on Chicago - slivers of building wall inside the road groove, put there by `merge_recess_ridges` growing the road layer back over buildings it had been separated from - so the .3mf and the .stl of one bake described different objects. Measured after: `union - single` 9.0e-9 mm^3, `single - union` 1.9e-13 mm^3 at plate 180; 1.28e-8 mm^3 in total at plate 256.
- [V2-P3-fix] `parts_union` now compares the two solids as SETS: `checks.symmetric_difference_mm3` = `vol(A-B) + vol(B-A)` through manifold3d, failing over `PART_UNION_SYMDIFF_MM3 = thicken.PRINT_GRID_MM ** 3` (1e-6 mm^3). Volume and bounding box are kept but cannot carry the row: equal-and-opposite errors cancel exactly, and the 1e-6 RELATIVE volume budget is 0.168 mm^3 on a Chicago plate, seventeen times the error that was already there.
