The bound is one cell of the pipeline's own snap grid cubed - Stage 1 puts every polygon on that grid, so nothing the two modes may legitimately disagree about is bigger than a cell.
- [V2-P3-fix] `checks.manifold_from_mesh` returned None for EVERY mesh, including a plain cube: trimesh's `.vertices` is a read-only `TrackedArray`, `np.ascontiguousarray` passes a read-only array straight through, and manifold3d's nanobind binding refuses one. Nothing crashed because every caller has a fallback, so the damage was silent: `_part_bodies` fell back to trimesh's connected components AND to the whole part's volume as its "smallest shell", which made the debris half of the `bodies` row vacuous, and `make_slicer` fell back to `trimesh.section`, the path DECISIONS [P3] documents as the inferior one (ring soup, self-intersecting at a pinch). Fixed with `np.array(..., order="C")`, which always copies.
- [V2-P3-fix] `assemble.finalize` took its volume reference AFTER the `simplify` step, so a destructive simplify could never be caught - only the weld that followed was checked, and against the already-damaged volume. On the union of the colour parts `simplify(1e-6)` ate 0.4326 mm^3 and split a body off. The reference is now taken before the simplify and both steps are checked against it. No shipped geometry changes: the v1 golden is byte-identical (G8 green) and the single-mode Chicago bake still has 68,692 triangles.
- [V2-P3-fix] The bake's parts union is swept for debris only (`prune_debris(..., UNION_DEBRIS_MM3 = 1e-9)`), never `finalize`d. Unioning parts that interpenetrate by 0.2 mm leaves a handful of exactly-zero-volume shells at the seams and those must go, but `MIN_BODY_VOLUME_MM3` (0.01 mm^3) would take a legitimate 0.0018 mm^3 island of road inlay with them and the partition check would then report material the parts really carry as missing.
- [V2-P3-fix] The 3MF CONTAINER rows moved from `app/cli.py` to `app/validate/container.py` and the BAKE now runs them on the file it just wrote, in both colour modes. `POST /bake` marks a job done on `report.passed` alone, so every row that only ran under `make validate` was a row the product path never saw (v2-02 audit, finding 5): a served parts .3mf had never had its material table, component graph or build item read. Sidecar rows go from 12 to 21 in parts mode and from 9 to 15 in single mode; a failing container row fails the bake and the existing quarantine path ships nothing. `app/cli.py` keeps its private names as thin delegates, so the `validate` command reads as it did.
- [V2-P3-fix] `3mf_components` requires the assembly's component list to be DISTINCT, to reference every mesh object, to reference only mesh objects, and to carry no transform (on the components or on the build item); the count it hands to `validate_parts` is the number of distinct valid references, so `bodies` fails too. A file naming one part twice and another never used to pass every row - a slicer would have built two towers and no base plate - and a `<component transform>` displacing a part 25 mm used to validate as one solid sitting on the bed, because this validator judges the parts in their own coordinates. FrameCraft's writer never emits a transform, so a file that carries one is refused rather than mis-measured; an explicit identity matrix is accepted.
- [V2-P3-fix] 04's triangle budget is re-asserted on the parts in `part_meshes`: `enforce_triangle_budget` decimates the single-mode solid that becomes the .stl and deliberately leaves the parts alone (decimating them independently would break the partition), so without this a model that decimated would ship a .3mf over the budget with `triangle_budget PASS`.
- [V2-P3-fix] A `repair_slice_profiles` patch now carries the `hero_id` of the solid whose height it inherits, SUPERSEDING [V2-P3]'s decision to withhold it. It was always extruded to that solid's printed top, so withholding the id printed a strip of building-coloured material running the full height of a hero. The reason it was withheld - a patch as a second body of a hero part is what the debris sweep could drop - is now caught loudly by the exact `parts_union` instead of silently breaking the partition.
- [V2-P3-fix] `make bake-fixture PLATE=<not a number>` exits 2 with a message instead of reaching `json.loads` and printing a traceback. The recipe's leading `@` stays: it already echoes the target and the full `--params` JSON, and removing it would print the twenty-line shell body on every invocation (v2-02 audit, finding 9, half fixed and recorded as such).
- [V2-P3-gate] Parts-export audit fixes landed (v2-lettering, 11 [V2-P3-fix] lines): the recess floor has one owner via `extrude.inlay_claim` (doubly-reached floor 398.4 -> 29.95 mm^2, all a deliberate 0.05 mm rim), `<components>` distinct/complete/mesh-only, non-identity transforms fail, every additive part is cut by the recess cutters so `parts_union` is now a symmetric-difference test bounded by PRINT_GRID_MM^3 (union-single 9.0e-9 mm^3), and the bake runs the 3MF container rows (moved to app/validate/container.py) on the file it writes. Two latent v1-era defects surfaced and were fixed on the way: `checks.manifold_from_mesh` returned None for every mesh (read-only numpy array), silently disabling the exact Manifold slicer and the per-part debris check; `assemble.finalize` took its volume reference after `simplify`, so a destructive simplify could never be caught. Single mode still 68,692 triangles, G8 11 passed.
- [V2-P3-fix] Every ADDITIVE colour part is cut by single mode's FULL cutter list in single mode's order - the recesses, then the engraved-text cutter, then the underside pockets - not just the recesses. Handing `carved()` only the recesses let three things diverge: an embossed engraving never met the engraved-text cutter (the frame was built as `(frame - text) | emboss`, so the emboss went in after the cut), buildings/green/trees/heroes/embossed roads met neither the text nor the underside cutter, and with the frame OFF the text cutter was applied to nothing at all in parts mode while single mode still subtracted it. Measured on `TEXT=all COLOR=parts`: the two modes' volumes now agree exactly (199,812.6339 mm^3 both, symmetric difference 5.6e-7 mm^3) where the handoff had recorded 199,812.6434 against 199,812.6339. The BASE is the one part that keeps its own treatment, because its recesses are cut by the deeper `recess_pocket` and filled back by the inlays.
- [V2-P3-fix] The per-part debris sweep is CONDITIONAL: `assemble._prune_covered` drops a sub-`MIN_BODY_VOLUME_MM3` shell only when the other parts already hold it, and `finalize` is called with `min_body_volume=0.0` for a colour part so it cannot drop one on its own. Unconditional pruning removed 12 chips (6.4e-4 mm^3) from the Chicago parts and they happened, every one, to lie inside another part's 0.2 mm interpenetration band - so nothing was lost, and nothing said so. A chip nobody else holds now survives the sweep and the `bodies` validator reports it, which is the difference between a silent hole in the partition and a named failure. The parts are therefore built first and finalized once, at the end, rather than being finalized as they are emitted.
- [V2-P3-fix] `make validate` judges the PLACED parts: `cli._placed_parts` looks each geometry's instance transform up in `scene.graph` (trimesh keeps the composed build-item/component matrix there and leaves `scene.geometry` in local coordinates) and applies it. Iteration stays over `scene.geometry`, not over the graph, because the graph's order differs and the command prints the part names in the file's own order. Verified on a tampered copy of the Chicago parts file: a component translated 25 mm in X now fails `bounding_box` (198.95 mm across a 180 mm plate) and `bodies` (68 disconnected bodies) as well as `3mf_components`; before, all three passed.
- [V2-P3-fix] NARROWING of the earlier [V2-P3] wording "no two parts meet on a coincident face" / "0.2 mm interpenetration at every part-to-part contact", which cannot be satisfied literally and which a reviewer reading it literally will reopen: two colours that meet on one visible floor MUST share a vertical face somewhere, and the brief's own prescription for finding 1 - difference the losing inlay against the winning inlay grown by `thicken.separate`'s 0.02 mm - opens a 0.02 x 0.4 mm VOID along the whole water/road boundary (several mm^3 on Chicago's 524.5 mm^2 overlap, a million times the partition bound) because the base is already pocketed on both sides of it. The property that is true, that matters, and that is now asserted is: **no two parts present an upward-facing horizontal face at the same z over overlapping ground** - measured 0 mm^2 at plate 180 and 256, against the audit's 398.4 mm^2 baseline - and every part-to-part contact that is not that is a deliberate interpenetration.
- [V2-P6-fix] Every allowlist lookup in `lib/share.ts` goes through `specFor(map, key)`, an `Object.hasOwn` guard, at both the top level and inside `validate`'s object branch. `JSON.parse` returns `__proto__`, `constructor`, `toString`, `valueOf` and the rest of `Object.prototype` as ORDINARY OWN KEYS, so the previous bare `map[key]` answered with a function off the prototype chain instead of `undefined`, the "is this a setting?" guard never fired, `validate`'s switch matched no case, and `undefined` was written onto the params object. Not prototype pollution (writing `undefined` to `__proto__` is a no-op) but a params object carrying an own `toString: undefined`, on which `String(params)` throws — i.e. the module's all-or-nothing promise did not hold. `share.test.ts` now walks all 12 `Object.getOwnPropertyNames(Object.prototype)` names at three depths (top level, a nested group, an `engravings[]` item) plus a guard-the-guard case; the four tests are red against the bare read and green with `specFor` (audit v2-06 finding 1).
- [V2-P6-fix] `previewText.keyholeArea` uses `Math.acos(half / radius)`, not `asin`. The slot's straight sides are the vertical lines `x = cx ± half`, and a vertical line meets the entry circle where `cos(angle) = half / radius` — 60 degrees for a 4 mm slot in an 8 mm entry, not 30. With `asin` the arc terminated at a point that is not on the slot side at all and the segment to the cap was a slanted chord: 0.93 mm of radial error against the bake's union (0.73 mm by the edge-sampling metric the new test uses), by two orders of magnitude the largest preview-vs-bake error in the phase, in a function whose docstring claimed to be exact. The arc's step count is also `ceil` rather than `round`, because the 300-degree span is not a whole number of 64-gon steps and rounding down made each chord longer than the circles the rest of the module draws. The old test asserted only the four bounding-box extents, none of which the defect moved; it is kept and joined by a signed-distance test that walks the whole outline, EDGES included, against the union of the entry disc, the slot box and the cap disc, to 0.01 mm (audit v2-06 finding 2).
- [V2-P6-fix] `advisorDeps` is `[graph, radius_m, plate_mm, frame, nozzle_mm, trees]`. `road_scale` and `params.water` were listed and read by nothing: `transform.detail_report` never mentions roads and walks `scene.water` unconditionally, so a road-width drag re-ran a 2.08 ms whole-scene walk plus two grid searches for a byte-identical answer — and `CityPreview.test.ts` pinned that waste as the expected result. Both are corrected, and `advisor.test.ts` now closes the loop in BOTH directions: every listed parameter must be able to move the report on at least one real scene, and the two removed ones must not (audit v2-06 finding 3).
- [V2-P6-fix] The share-link size claim is corrected. Measured at the contract's REAL maxima — every string at its 64-character limit, twelve 64-CHARACTER hero ids (not 14), and every remaining numeric, enum and nested field off its default, asserted by comparing `paramsDiff`'s key set to the whole contract — the payload is 4,011 characters for ASCII, 4,864 for two- and four-byte characters and 5,718 for three-byte CJK; a default configuration is 148 (141 with no preset id). The `[V2-P6]` figure of 2,319 was measured on a configuration that was not at the maxima, and the test's own `< 4096` bound does not hold there for any non-ASCII label. The bound is now 8,192: inside Chrome's ~32 k address bar and inside what Firefox and Safari will navigate to, and deliberately past the 2,083 a legacy IE/Edge or a chat client truncates at — which is the failure the FNV-1a checksum exists to NAME rather than to prevent (audit v2-06 finding 4).
- [V2-P6-fix] `store.loadShared` returns `"none" | "applied" | "refused"` instead of a boolean, and `EditorShell` deletes the `s` query parameter with `history.replaceState` on a REFUSAL only. Without it, dismissing the refusal banner and reloading brought the same refusal back forever on what is by then a bookmarked URL, while the address bar went on claiming to carry a configuration the editor was not showing. Not on an accepted payload: that link IS what the page is showing, and the user may want to copy it again (audit v2-06 finding 8).
- [V2-P6-fix] `FrameTextGroup`'s two memos are keyed on the fields they read — `[graph, lat, lon, radius_m, today, plate_mm, frame, city_label]` for the token context and `[textParamsKey(params), context, rotation_deg]` for the layout — instead of on the whole `params` object, which `store.setParam` rebuilds by spread on every write. The panel's `{date}` is pinned in `useState` at mount like `CityPreview`'s, so the two sources cannot disagree across midnight and the context no longer changes identity on every keystroke (audit v2-06 finding 7).
- [V2-P6-fix] The e2e now CLICKS the advisor's radius remedy and asserts the radius readout moves to the solved number with exactly one `POST /scene`, polled to one and re-checked after 1.5 s so a stacked second request fails it. Only the REQUEST is asserted and never the response, so the test does not depend on Overpass being reachable; the remedy is by construction not a preset radius, so `ui.spec.ts` gained `smoke.spec.ts`'s `pruneStrayFixtures` in `afterAll`. `Use plate N` still has no end-to-end coverage and cannot get it on this scene: no plate in the contract's range fixes Chicago at 900 m, so the shared math offers no plate remedy there — asserted as ABSENT from both the DOM and the a11y Tab walk, rather than silently skipped (audit v2-06 findings 5 and 6).
- [V2-P6-fix] Audit finding 9 (`lib/transform.test.ts:176` derives `radius_m` from the expectation it then asserts against, making the check tautological for the four pre-advisor parity cases) is NOT fixed here and is handed to whoever owns the `transform.py` / `transform.ts` pair this run: that file is outside this phase's scope and the bake-side agent is editing the pair concurrently. Audit finding 13 (a refused engraving is an info-toned row in the adjustments drawer) is deliberately left: toning it would mean string-matching the shared math's own messages for "was not cut", which is exactly the coupling this phase avoided everywhere else, and the refusal already carries `tone: "warn"` in the panel beside the control that caused it.
- [V2-P5-fix] The north arrow is turned by `+rotation_deg`, not `-rotation_deg`. `project.LocalFrame.to_local` rotates the ground COUNTER-CLOCKWISE by `+rotation_deg`, so true north lands on the +y axis turned CCW by that angle and a glyph drawn on +y must follow it. The shipped arrow pointed at ground bearing `2*rotation`: 58 deg away from north at the New York preset and due SOUTH at rotation 90. It survived because the test placed the glyph with `arrow.placement.rotation_deg` and then asserted the tip was where that same rotation had put it - a tautology that passes for either sign. The test now derives north from the PROJECTION (`LocalFrame.point_to_local` on a point 0.01 deg further north) and compares bearings, parametrised over 0/29/90/180/271 with a 1.0 deg tolerance for UTM meridian convergence, and asserts the opposite sign fails at every angle that is not a multiple of 180 (audit v2-03 finding 1).
- [V2-P5-fix] With the frame off, every lip feature is REFUSED rather than skipped-with-a-warning. `lettering_layout` marks each engraving `refused=True` with a reason naming the remedy, the north arrow and the scale bar are disabled, and `lettering.build` checks `params.frame` itself so a forged layout cannot reach the geometry either. Before this, an EMBOSSED engraving with `frame=false` was built anyway and shipped as two letters floating 1.8 mm over a frameless plate - and every validator passed, because 01/A6's one-connected-solid rule lived only in `app/cli.py` (i.e. only under `make validate`) and the bake had no `bodies` row in single mode at all. `checks.single_body_check` is now shared and the bake appends it in BOTH modes; the CLI delegates to it. The frame-off case still produces exactly ONE warning, not one per engraving plus a summary (audit v2-03 finding 2).
- [V2-P5-fix] CORRECTION to the `[V2-P5]` advisor numbers. Measured on the committed Chicago fixture through the real ingest path, the scene held at its 900 m crop and only the advisor's radius varied: 900 m at plate 180 scores 70 (fair), 1 500 m 61, 2 400 m 52, 3 000 m at plate 100 34 (poor) - not 80/74/68/35, and the shipped default preset lands in `fair`, not `good`. Composition of the 70: widened 370/994 = 37.2 % gives 22.33, trees 5 762/5 762 = 100 % gives 5.00, areas 360/753 = 47.8 % gives 2.39, penalty 29.72. The weights and the band edges are KEPT: the numbers were wrong, the scale was not, and moving a band edge to make the default preset read `good` would be fitting the scale to the answer. Pinned by `test_detail_advisor_scores_on_the_real_preset` and `test_detail_advisor_score_composition_on_the_default_preset`. Related correction: "widening every building alone is 40 points off" (in `[V2-P5]` and in two code comments) is 60 points off, landing AT 40, which is still `poor` (audit v2-03 findings 3 and 11).
- [V2-P5-fix] A build-time refusal names a size that has been MEASURED to work: `lettering.build` re-runs the whole shared layout at candidate sizes over a widening ladder (+0.25 up to +6.0 mm, capped at the contract's 8 mm maximum) and then walks back down in 0.05 mm steps, through the identical repair, ridge-merge, separation and measurement chain, and reports `it prints at 5.75 mm`. It says `no size up to X mm` - X being the largest size actually tried, never a ceiling the budget did not reach - when there is none. The old message said "try a larger size, a plainer face or a finer nozzle" with no number at all, and the counter branch named `fit.min_size_mm`, a size the layout had just accepted and the geometry had just refused. Bounded by construction at 21 probes and memoised per fitted size; only ever run on the refusal path (0.5-4 s), never on a successful bake (audit v2-03 finding 4).
- [V2-P5-fix] The build-side measurement stays on the SEPARATED cutter. Measuring the pre-separation geometry instead - 0.412 mm where the separated copy reads 0.303 mm on serif `1:10,714` at 3.5 mm - was tried, and it turns a refused string into a FAILED BAKE: Stage 4's `lettering` row slices the finished mesh and measures the groove that was really cut at 0.303 mm against a 0.360 mm floor. The preview/print divergence this leaves is real and inherent - the layout predicts from per-glyph metrics, the geometry measures a groove after the ridge merge has bridged sub-nozzle ridges between letters - and it is 2 of 24 (face, string) pairs over the strings a user actually types, both on the modulated serif. The remedy is the measured size in the warning, not a looser gate.
- [V2-P5-fix] The contract default `engravings[].size_mm` is 4.0 mm, raised from 3.0. Measured on the 6 mm lip at the default nozzle over 3 faces x 8 real strings x 25 sizes: at 3.0 mm the DEFAULT face refuses six of the eight, and every face refuses most of them. 3.75 mm is the smallest quarter-millimetre step at which all three faces cut everything except serif `1:10,714` (which needs 5.75); 4.0 was chosen because two of those thresholds sit exactly at 3.75 and printability is not monotonic in the size. Changed in `packages/contracts/schema/print_params.json` and regenerated with `make contracts` - never hand-edited - and mirrored by `transform.ts`'s `ENGRAVING_DEFAULT_SIZE_MM`, which `lettering.test.ts` now pins against `PARAM_RANGES` so the two cannot drift. `fixtures/print-params-default.json` is unaffected: a default `PrintParams` carries no engravings. G8 is unaffected: v1 had no lettering (audit v2-03 finding 4, and the web phase's report that a freshly seeded engraving was refused).
- [V2-P5-fix] `CREDITS.txt` no longer says "third-party sources: none" - that denial is now scoped to MAP DATA, which is the claim that is true - and it names the three bundled OFL faces with their versions and copyright lines (`export.FONT_CREDITS`, asserted against the generated metrics and the bundled OFL headers so it cannot go stale). The 3MF `LicenseTerms` and `Description` carry a one-line form, but only when text was actually cut. `apps/web/lib/fonts/<face>.glyphs.json` is 959 KiB of outlines extracted from those faces and served to every visitor, i.e. font software, so `scripts/gen_font_assets.py` now copies each `OFL.txt` into `apps/web/licences/` in the same run that regenerates the outlines, and a test asserts the copies are byte-identical to the originals (audit v2-03 finding 5).
- [V2-P5-fix] CORRECTION to the `[V2-P5]` justification for the one-nozzle engraved groove. The claim that "a two-nozzle target merged every letter pair of every string tried into one trench" is false: measured over 12 (face, string) pairs at every size a 6 mm lip can hold, the strict rule refuses at essentially the same rate as the shipped one (11/72 against 12/76) and reaches a size as separated as the shipped rule for 8 of the 12. The honest argument, and the one the ORCHESTRATOR RULING keeps the deviation on, is narrower: the strict rule's cost is LETTER SEPARATION, and only for the strings whose ink height caps the auto-fit under about 4.9 mm - a descender or a `Y`, 4 of the 12 pairs, "Chicago" in all three faces among them - for which the whole word becomes one trench at every size the lip permits. `transform.py`'s own numbers for the shipped rule were stale as well (5.32 / 0.41 / 0.48); the measured values are 5.16 mm fitted, 0.4875 mm narrowest groove and 0.7649 mm narrowest ridge, and the comment says so on both sides of the mirror now (audit v2-03 findings 6 and 7).
- [V2-P5-fix] CORRECTION to `[V2-P5]`'s rim claim. The clip to `lip_keep_region` does NOT leave the full margin around engraved ink: the cutter is grown by `thicken.LAYER_SEPARATION_MM` AFTER the clip, so an engraved groove may reach `margin - 0.02 mm` from the lip edge (measured 0.480 mm against a 0.500 mm margin) while embossed ink, which is never separated, keeps the margin exactly (0.500). Harmless - 0.48 mm is still more than a nozzle - but the invariant as written was false. The true one is pinned, tightly, by `test_build_engraved_rim_is_the_margin_less_the_separation` (audit v2-03 finding 8).
- [V2-P5-fix] The north arrow is auto-fitted to the lip band instead of being silently clipped, and the bound is its CIRCUMRADIUS (`size * hypot(0.3, 0.5)`), not its length: the base corner is further from the centre than the tip is, so a length-bounded arrow still reached 0.024 mm past the keep region at rotation 29. The cap is 4.287 mm on a 6 mm lip with a 0.5 mm margin, so the contract's 6 mm maximum is reduced with a warning naming both numbers. Rotation-INDEPENDENT on purpose: an ornament that changed size when the user rotated the map would be worse than one 0.7 mm shorter than asked. Mirrored in `transform.ts` and pinned by the `north-arrow-nw-rotated` fixture case, which both sides read (audit v2-03 finding 9).
- [V2-P5-fix] The Stage 4 `lettering` row probes the UNDERSIDE band too, at half the mark's depth, with the same stroke and ridge floors it uses on the lip. The mark's own strokes were measured by nothing: `min_wall` skips that band (its "is this a wall?" test looks up, which cannot judge a pocket cut from below) and `base_floor` only asks whether the plate above each pocket footprint is still solid. The underside slice is clipped to the slice's own outline with its holes filled rather than to a nominal plate square, so the chamfer's setback is not read as a groove around the whole perimeter. `test_validator_min_wall_skips_only_the_underside_band` builds a model that HAS a pocket now - it used to assert the skip on a model with none (audit v2-03 finding 10).
- [V2-P5-fix] `fixtures/parity-expected.json` gained the advisor's two unpinned sentence shapes - the plate-only remedy at the smallest legal radius (`Try plate 110.`) and `No radius or plate in range fixes it: raise the nozzle detail instead.` - so the TS mirror's formatting of every branch is under test rather than only the Python one. Each new case spells out every `PrintParams` field, because the TS side reads that object AS a `PrintParams` and has no model defaults: an omitted `frame` there is `undefined`, which silently changes the scale. Cases also carry `radius_override` (null unless the case deliberately asks the advisor about a radius the scene was not cropped at) so the mirror DERIVES the rest with its own `radius_m_from_bounds` instead of reading back the number it is being checked against (audit v2-03 finding 12, and v2-06 finding 9, which was handed to this phase).
- [V2-P5-fix] `bake.token_context` takes `(scene, params, date)`. The `SceneRequest` argument was never read: every field the context needs comes from the scene the model was actually built from, which is what a legend on the finished object has to describe (audit v2-03 finding 13).
- [V2-P5-fix] `lib/transform.test.ts` derives each parity case's `radius_m` with its OWN `radius_m_from_bounds` and asserts it against the committed fixture, instead of reading the fixture value and then comparing it to itself - which is what the `[V2-P5]` diff left behind for the four pre-advisor cases, replaced only by a single hard-coded `toBeCloseTo(900)` outside the per-case loop. Cases that deliberately ask the advisor about another radius carry `radius_override`, and the test asserts that an override really differs from the scene's own radius, so the branch cannot go degenerate and re-void the check; the `no-remedy-fat-nozzle` case lost its 900 m "override" for exactly that reason (audit v2-06 finding 9, handed to this phase because it owns the transform pair).
- [V2-P5-gate] After the lettering-audit fix pass the orchestrator re-ran every bake-side gate on 2026-08-30: G3 `make bake-fixture && make validate artifacts/chicago.3mf` (68,692 triangles, one body, ALL CHECKS PASS - single mode byte-identical to v1), G5 at plate 180 (6 parts, 463 shells, union one solid, 111,786 part triangles) and 256 (7 parts, 1151 shells, 209,874), G6 `TEXT=all` (89,466 triangles, lettering 0.488 mm stroke / 0.765 mm ridge, base_floor 0 mm^2 gap), `COLOR=parts TEXT=all` (6 parts, 132,558 part triangles), and G8 (11 passed). All ALL CHECKS PASS. Accepted deviation logged by v2-lettering: contract default engraving size_mm 3.0 -> 4.0 because the sans face refuses 6 of 8 real strings at 3.0 mm on a 0.4 mm nozzle (table in docs/handoff/v2-03-lettering.md §8a); the range 1.5..8.0 and every v1 field are unchanged.
- [V2-P7] `make gate` is seven steps and asserts ZERO SKIPPED TESTS in four independent places, because both pytest and Playwright exit 0 on a skip: (1) a STATIC guard over all 31 `apps/web/**/*.{test,spec}.{ts,tsx}` rejecting any committed `test`/`it`/`describe`/`suite`/`bench` `.skip`/`.only`/`.fixme`/`.todo`/`.skipIf`/`.failing` and `xit`/`xtest`/`xdescribe` (and failing if the glob matches no files at all, so the guard cannot pass by scanning nothing); (2) the pytest summary from `uv run pytest -q -rs`, which fails the gate on `skipped`/`xfailed`/`xpassed` as well as on a failure; (3) the vitest summary on `skipped`/`todo`; (4) `artifacts/e2e/results.json` `stats.skipped`, which is the whole Playwright suite because the JSON reporter aggregates every project and spec into one `stats` object. Verified non-vacuous with a probe file carrying all six marker forms; all six were reported.
- [V2-P7] The conditional `test.skip(status === 502 || 503, "Overpass is unreachable")` in `apps/web/e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s A2 test is REPLACED by a plain failing `expect(status).toBe(200)` carrying the same two diagnoses (Overpass unreachable, and `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE` was set). This is strictly stronger, not weaker: the gate already failed on any skipped test, so the skip only changed the wording of a failure that was going to happen anyway - and one conditional skip in the tree makes the static guard impossible to state without a carve-out. `playwright.config.ts`'s "the only skip in this suite" comment was corrected with it.
- [V2-P7] `@pytest.mark.skipif(not artifacts/chicago.3mf.is_file())` on `test_cli_passes_on_the_chicago_fixture_bake` - G3's own assertion - is REPLACED by a session fixture that bakes the file with the same call `make bake-fixture` makes when it is missing. The skip never fired on this host but deleted G3 from the suite on a clean clone, which is the machine where it is worth the most. The suite now has no `skipif`, `pytest.skip`, `xfail` or `xfail_strict` anywhere: 591 -> 599 tests, 0 skipped.
- [V2-P7] `make gate` step 7 fails if `git status --porcelain -- fixtures/` names any `fixtures/<40-hex>.json`. The e2e's one live-Overpass touch caches a ~100 MB raw response as a sha1 fixture and both specs prune it in `afterAll`; the check turns "the specs prune them" from a claim into an assertion. Scoped to the sha1 pattern so the phase's own uncommitted `fixtures/*.json` work does not trip it. Verified by planting `fixtures/0000...0000.json`.
- [V2-P7] `make gate` step 3 was split from one `lint && typecheck && test && build` chain into four separately attributed steps, so a failure names which of the four broke instead of "apps/web FAILED", and so all four run (a lint error no longer hides a type error). `next build` still runs BEFORE `make up` per [P4].
- [V2-P7] `make gate-v2` is a NEW target (listed in `make help`); `make gate` and `make bake-fixture` keep exactly their old meaning. It runs G8, G5 at plate 180 (asserting `parts 6:` / `6 entries` / `6 components, 6 distinct` / `6 mesh + 1 assembly` / `bodies 6 parts`), G5 at PLATE=256 (the same rows at 7), G6 `TEXT=all` (asserting the `lettering` and `base_floor` rows PASS, not merely exist), and `COLOR=parts TEXT=all`. It is separate from `make gate` because it is several minutes of manifold3d that proves geometry rather than wiring. 151 s, GATE-V2 PASS.
- [V2-P7] Every `gate-v2` output is judged TWICE: `make validate` on the `.3mf` and on the `.stl` beside it, requiring `bodies PASS 1` on the STL in both colour modes and with the lettering on. 04 says the STL is one welded solid whatever structure the 3MF carries, and nothing was checking that on the real artifacts.
- [V2-P7] `gate-v2`'s row assertions are pattern greps over the validator's own table, and were proved non-vacuous by replaying its `need()` against the 256 mm log with the 180 mm patterns: rc 1, both missing rows named.
- [V2-P7] The north arrow is verified out of the FINISHED SOLID, not out of the layout: `tests/test_qa_verification.py` bakes the real Chicago SceneGraph through `run_pipeline` at `rotation_deg=29`, sections the exported 3MF at `base_top + FRAME_LIP - ENGRAVE_MAX/2` (4.7 mm), lifts the one small interior ring out of the plate-wide band polygon, and recovers its bearing by rotational alignment against the nominal glyph (1 deg sweep then 0.05 deg, IoU > 0.4 or the test says the cut is not the arrow). Expectation from `project.LocalFrame` alone. Measured 330.9 deg against true north 331.4 deg, error 0.5 deg (UTM convergence), tolerance 2 deg; the wrong sign is asserted to be more than 20 deg away, so it cannot pass vacuously.
- [V2-P7] Convention pinned down while writing that test: `lettering.place(theta)` rotates COUNTERCLOCKWISE, so a glyph whose apex is at `(0, +size/2)` in its own frame lands at COMPASS BEARING `-theta` (bearings run clockwise from +y, i.e. `atan2(x, y)`). Comparing a `place` angle with a bearing directly is off by a sign; the helper returns the bearing.
- [V2-P7] CORRECTION to the brief's hanger numbers. The base a hanger needs is `underside_min_base_mm`, not `hanger_min_base_mm`: it composes the pocket depth, the 1 mm roof AND the deepest top-side recess. With the default engraved roads (0.6 mm) that is 2.0+1.0+0.6 = 3.6 mm for a keyhole and 3.1+1.0+0.6 = 4.7 mm for magnets, so `POST /bake` refuses 2.9 mm and 4.0 mm naming 3.6 and 4.7. This is the code being right, not a defect; the test spells the arithmetic out rather than reading the number back from `transform.py`, and a companion test bakes at exactly 3.6 / 4.7 and requires `status=done` so the refusal cannot be satisfied by refusing everything.
- [V2-P7] The v1 golden sidecar is now driven through the real `python -m app.cli validate`, not just through the pydantic model: `tests/test_qa_verification.py` first proves the sidecar is a v1 one (no `schema_version`, none of `engravings`/`north_arrow`/`hanger`/`color_mode`/`city_label`), then requires `sidecar_params` absent-or-PASS, the header to read the sidecar name and `plate 180 mm`, and `ALL CHECKS PASS`. A regression here means every 3MF baked before this run became unvalidatable.
- [V2-P7] The six 3MF tampering cases (duplicated component, dangling component, component transform, build-item transform, pindex out of range, `displaycolor` not RGBA8, two build items, sidecar/file colour-mode disagreement) were already covered by named tests in `tests/test_validate_cli.py` and were REUSED rather than duplicated; the audit trail is the table in `docs/handoff/v2-07-qa.md`.
- [V2-P7] `ui.spec.ts`'s "the v2 personalisation fields never reach the server" gained `hero_mode` (both directions), the scale bar and the underside mark. `hero_mode` is the one v2 control that changes the model's HEIGHT, which makes it the most plausible refetch trigger, and nothing was asserting it stayed client-side.
- [V2-P7] `e2e/ui.spec.ts`'s "an engraving appears on the frame, and a refused one does not" was taking its REFUSED size from the contract default, so `[V2-P5-fix]` raising `engravings[].size_mm` from 3.0 to 4.0 - a change made precisely so a freshly seeded engraving is printable - turned it into an assertion that the default is refused, and it failed the first authoritative `make gate` of this phase (`Expected "Not cut", Received "Cuts at 4.00 mm."`), taking 12 more serial tests with it. The app is right; the spec was stale. Fixed by naming both sizes explicitly: pin the 4.0 mm default as CUTTING (with rings drawn), then set 3 mm and require "Not cut" AND the ring count to fall back to 0, then 6 mm and require 8 rings again. Strictly more coverage than before - the preview is now proved to take the letters OFF the plate when the verdict flips. Written up as F2 in docs/handoff/FAILURES.md.
- [V2-P7] Rule this phase adopts and recommends: a test may not take the value it is asserting ABOUT from a contract default. The default is what the contract is free to change; the assertion is what must not. Every size, colour and threshold an e2e depends on is now either written out in the spec or asserted against the default as a separate, named claim.
- [V2-P8-gate] G1 PASS on 2026-08-30 (orchestrator): `make up` rc 0, /health `{"status":"ok"}`, web 200, `make down` rc 0. G2 PASS: Chicago preset POST /scene 200 in 0.25 s -> 994 buildings, coverage `good`, height_tag_ratio 0.111; `uv run pytest tests/test_ingest.py -q` 123 passed. qa-gate's own runs: `make gate` PASS 472 s (599 pytest, 531 vitest, 25 Playwright, 0 skipped, ports free), `make gate-v2` PASS 151 s; the orchestrator's authoritative `make gate` re-run follows.
- [V2-P8-gate] G4/G7 PASS on 2026-08-30, orchestrator's authoritative run: `make gate` rc 0 in 492 s - no-skip static guard, 599 pytest (0 skipped), lint + tsc + 531 vitest + next build, 25 Playwright (0 failed / 0 flaky / 0 skipped), teardown checked, fixtures clean. Log: artifacts/logs/v2-final-gate.log. With G1, G2, G3, G5 (180 and 256), G6, G8 and `make gate-v2` already PASS on the same tree, every v2 gate is green with nothing DEGRADED; the qa-phase audit is the last open item before the contracts re-freeze is final.
- [V2-P7-gate] qa-phase audit (docs/handoff/v2-07-audit.md): 4 majors, 0 blockers - the G3 session fixture validated any pre-existing artifacts/chicago.3mf (and `PLATE=` shared the stem), gate-v2's `lettering PASS` grep accepted a row that passes with zero measured strokes, Playwright `test.fail()` evaded all four zero-skip layers, and the editor never called the shared `underside_min_base_mm` so a keyhole on a 3 mm base was offered without warning (the bake still refused it). All assigned to v2-fix-07; `make gate` and `make gate-v2` are re-run by the orchestrator afterwards and the run is not complete until they pass.
- [V2-P7-fix] G3's session fixture bakes UNCONDITIONALLY, at PrintParams() defaults, into tmp_path_factory, and never reads or writes artifacts/. Baking only `if not target.is_file()` meant the test asserted about whatever file was lying there; combined with `PLATE=` sharing the `chicago` stem, `make bake-fixture PLATE=256` could leave a 256 mm bake called chicago.3mf whose own sidecar said 256, so the validator judged it against 256 and passed. "The default Chicago bake validates" is now a claim about a bake this session made, and the test asserts the header names the default plate (v2-07 audit, finding 1).
- [V2-P7-fix] `PLATE=` takes a stem suffix like COLOR and TEXT (`chicago-p256.3mf`, `chicago-parts-p256.3mf`, dots mapped to `_`) and is range-checked 100..256 in the Makefile against print_params.json's own plate_mm bounds. A plate override can no longer overwrite a default-plate artifact, and an out-of-range value fails in the Makefile's voice instead of as a pydantic traceback minutes into a uv start-up. gate-v2 step 3, RUNBOOK.md and docs/handoff/v2-07-qa.md follow the new stem.
- [V2-P7-fix] The `lettering` row FAILS when a piece the SHARED LAYOUT accepted leaves no stroke at its own probe band: `checks.lettering_expected_pieces(params)` maps every non-refused engraving / north arrow / scale bar / underside mark onto the same (z, mode) bands `lettering_probe_zs` produces, and a band with an accepted piece and 0 strokes reports `requested N pieces (...), measured 0 strokes`. Before, `bad` only ever collected strokes that were TOO THIN, so a bake that requested every ornament and cut none reported `lettering PASS 0.000 mm stroke` and gate-v2's `lettering +PASS` grep accepted it (v2-07 audit, finding 2). PASS-with-zero survives only when nothing was requested or everything was refused - refusing text is a documented outcome, losing it is not.
- [V2-P7-fix] The validator has no scene, so `lettering_expected_pieces` expands tokens against a documented WORST-CASE TokenContext (lat -89.9999 / lon -179.9999, 0.02 mm per ground metre -> `1:50,000` and the widest 40 mm auto scale bar, radius 99999 m, 999999 buildings, a 10-character ISO date) with the real `{city}` from the params. Every value is chosen to make the expansion at least as LONG as any real one, and a longer string is harder to fit, never easier, so "this context says the piece is not refused" implies the real one is not refused: the expected-piece count can only ever UNDER-count. Under-counting costs coverage; over-counting would fail a bake that was right to cut nothing.
- [V2-P7-fix] The `lettering` row's value carries the COUNTS (`126 strokes of 7 piece(s); 0.488 mm stroke / 0.765 mm ridge`) and `make gate-v2` greps `lettering +PASS +[1-9][0-9]* strokes of [1-9][0-9]* piece` - the number, not the word. Checked against five synthetic rows: only the genuine one matches, where the old pattern matched four of them.
- [V2-P7-fix] Playwright's `test.fail()` is rejected twice: `fail` joined the static marker alternation, and gate step 5 walks results.json's suite tree for any test whose `expectedStatus != "passed"`. It is the analogue of the pytest xfail step 2 already rejects, and it landed in `stats.expected` where nothing looked. Measured on a real planted probe: `stats = {expected: 2, skipped: 0}` (green under the old check) with `expectedStatus: "failed"` on the annotated test; the new check exits 1 and names it. Probe removed (v2-07 audit, finding 3).
- [V2-P7-fix] The editor now predicts the hanger refusal it always claimed to: `warnings.undersideBlockMessage` turns `transform.underside_min_base_mm` into a `block` warning (`base-too-thin-for-underside`) that disables Bake exactly as `model-too-tall` does, for keyhole, magnets and the underside mark, and names the recess because the refusal number is 3.6 mm rather than the 3.0 mm `hanger_min_base_mm` alone suggests. Two transform.py docstrings asserted this was already true; it was not, and hanger=keyhole on the default 3 mm base was silently offered (v2-07 audit, finding 4).
- [V2-P7-fix] `warningDeps` gained `hanger`, `underside_mark.enabled`, `road_mode` and `water` - all four move the hanger floor (3.6 mm at the defaults, 3.5 with the roads off, 3.0 with the water off as well) - and `previewDeps.height` was split onto a new `predictedTopDeps`, the HEIGHT half of the same list. Keying the canvas's building-height pass on a hanger would be exactly the over-invalidation previewDeps exists to prevent, and CityPreview.test.ts catches it layer by layer.
- [V2-P7-fix] `make down` is now a real assertion: after the kill it waits up to 15 s for :8000 and :3000 to clear and exits 1 naming the survivor, and `docker compose down`'s rc is no longer swallowed. Every branch used to end in an echo, so the recipe could not return non-zero and `make gate`'s "a failing make down fails the gate" check was unreachable (v2-07 audit, finding 6). Proved reachable with a node http server planted on :3000.
- [V2-P7-fix] gate-v2 step 1 asserts `11 passed` and no skipped/xfailed/xpassed for G8 instead of checking only the exit code, so a skipif added to test_v1_compat.py cannot leave the step green while asserting nothing about the v1 golden (v2-07 audit, finding 8).
- [V2-P7-fix] `e2e/ui.spec.ts` derives the REFUSED cap height from `lettering_layout` itself (`largestRefusedCapHeightMm`, walking the slider's own 0.1 mm step down from the seeded default) instead of the literal 3 mm F2 measured by hand: the flip sits between 3.0 and 3.25 mm, so a change to a stroke target, the nozzle default or the bundled face moved the literal onto the wrong side of it. Same rule as [V2-P7]'s, one level up - a test may not take the value it asserts about from a default OR from a stale measurement (v2-07 audit, finding 9). `expect(withText).toBe(8)` is deliberately left as a literal: deriving it from the same layout would make it circular.
- [V2-P7-fix] The playwright.config order comment was over-broad and is corrected from measurement, not argument: what decides the `.next` race is WHO waits for the recompile. `npm run build` then `npm run test:e2e` alone is unsafe (the webServer drives the page as soon as the port answers); `make gate` builds and then runs `make up`, which health-waits on curl localhost:3000 for up to 120 s, so the recompile finishes first. On this host, immediately after a build: `Compiled / in 1612ms`, `GET / 200`, and zero `Cannot find module` / `full reload` lines in artifacts/logs/web.log across the whole 4.1-minute suite. `make gate`'s order is UNCHANGED (v2-07 audit, finding 5).
- [V2-P7-fix] Audit findings 10-14 are recorded NOT FIXED with reasons in the Resolution section of docs/handoff/v2-07-audit.md: three are scope notes about tests that are correct as they stand, one (the static guard's .ts-only scope) is outside this brief on a tree that greps clean, and count floors for the three suites were not added because the counts move every phase by design.
- [V2-P7-fix] Consequence of the `lettering` row's new failure mode, recorded deliberately: a bake whose layout ACCEPTED a piece and whose Stage 1 repair then dropped it (`repair.lost_counters`, or `verify()` refusing the widened glyphs) now ends `failed` instead of `done` with a warning, because the validator can only see the finished solid. That is the intended reading - text the user asked for, that the shared math promised, and that is not in the file - and `fit_text` already refuses every size below `text_min_size_mm`, which is derived from the counter constraint the repair would close, so the two agree by construction. The full 601-test suite, `make gate-v2` and `make bake-fixture TEXT=all` all pass unchanged.
- [V2-P8-gate] FINAL on 2026-08-30 after the qa-audit fix pass, orchestrator's authoritative runs on the final tree: `make gate-v2` rc 0 in 144 s (G8 11 passed / 0 skipped; G5 chicago-parts 6 parts + chicago-parts-p256 7 parts; G6 lettering 126 strokes of 7 pieces, 0.488 mm stroke / 0.765 mm ridge; parts+text; every output validated as .3mf and .stl) and `make gate` rc 0 in 510 s (static no-skip guard, 601 pytest, lint + tsc + 537 vitest + next build, 26 Playwright 0 failed / 0 flaky / 0 skipped / 0 expected-failure, teardown checked, fixtures clean). G1, G2, G3 re-run on the same tree earlier today. Every gate PASS, nothing DEGRADED. Contracts re-frozen at schema version 2 (CLAUDE.md). Nothing committed: the user commits on request, as in v1.
- [V3-A0] 2026-08-30 v3 run starts on top of the v2 tree (uncommitted v2 work is committed first as the v2 baseline). The brief's paths `src/lib/...` map onto this repo's `apps/web/lib/...`; there is no `src/`.
- [V3-A1] The bake pipeline moves into the browser: a TypeScript engine under `apps/web/lib/engine/**` built on the manifold-3d WASM package (same boolean kernel as the Python service) running in a Web Worker. It feeds both the live preview and every export, so preview, filament mapper and exported file are the same meshes. `services/bake` stays in the repo as the reference implementation and CLI validator (`make validate`), still tested by `make gate`, but the web app no longer calls it at runtime. The v1/v2 rule "the browser never runs booleans" is retired by this line; the shared transform-math pair and its parity fixture are kept.
- [V3-A2] `packages/contracts` is unfrozen for schema version 3: additive optional fields with v2-identical defaults, `tests/test_v1_compat.py` unchanged and green, both generators re-run, re-frozen at v3 when phase 8 lands.
- [V3-A3] Nominatim is allowed from v3 (the brief requires reverse and forward geocoding) under its usage policy: identifying User-Agent, at most 1 request per second, localStorage cache keyed by rounded lat/lon, fail soft to coordinates. This supersedes the v2 "no Nominatim" line.
- [V3-A4] Token expansion happens once, client-side, in `apps/web/lib/tokens.ts`; the app sends fully resolved strings to any bake path. The Python token table stays as the reference for the shared fixture only.
- [V3-A5] Terrain source: Mapzen Terrarium PNG tiles on AWS Open Data (SRTM / GLO-30 derived), keyless and CORS-enabled; zoom chosen from the radius; fails soft to a flat base.
- [V3-A6] STEP export is a faceted B-rep (AP214, MANIFOLD_SOLID_BREP of planar triangle faces) with a size warning above 50k triangles; no kernel-level surfaces exist for a mesh model, and the UI says so.
- [V3-A7] Public release is v3.0.0 (v1 and v2 were internal milestones recorded in DECISIONS.md and docs/handoff); GitHub repo `naxhq/framecraft`, GitHub Pages base path `/framecraft`, desktop bundle via Tauri 2 from the same `apps/web` static export.
- [V3-A8] Commits are authored by Vahid Alizadeh only, no co-author trailers, one commit (or series) per phase, subagents do not commit.
- [V3-A9] Single-nozzle colour-change export writes the Bambu project 3MF with every part on extruder 1 plus `Metadata/custom_gcode_per_layer.xml` colour changes at the Z-band boundaries the geometry allows; regions that share a Z band are reported as inseparable in the UI. No G-code is generated by the app.
- [V3-CI] `.github/workflows/ci.yml` has no `ruff` step even though `services/bake/pyproject.toml` lists it as a dev dependency: `make gate`/`make test` do not run ruff on this host (blocked by a Windows Application Control policy, DECISIONS `[P1]`), so a CI ruff step would be a new, unreviewed gate rather than a reproduction of an existing one; adding it is a separate decision for whoever owns `services/bake`'s lint story.
- [V3-CI] The `e2e` job runs `npm run test:e2e` directly (no manual `make up`/curl-wait loop): `apps/web/playwright.config.ts`'s own `webServer` array already starts and health-waits `uv run uvicorn` (:8000) and `next dev` (:3000), and a fresh CI checkout with no prior `next build` on the same tree is exactly the config's documented "Safe Order 1", so a separate startup script would only duplicate what the config already does correctly.
- [V3-CI] `tests/fixtures/overpass-chicago-loop.json` (staged for this task, byte-identical to the committed preset fixture `fixtures/a4e5375818f3...json`) is not wired into the workflow or into any spec. It cannot serve smoke.spec.ts's A2 low-coverage test (wrong location) or the rotation-nudge touch (wrong sha1 cache key, since 0deg != 1deg), and Playwright `page.route()` cannot intercept the actual Overpass call regardless of fixture, because the browser never makes it - `services/bake/app/ingest/overpass.py` calls Overpass server-side over httpx with hardcoded endpoints. Of the suite's three live-Overpass touches, two (`smoke.spec.ts` rotation nudge, `ui.spec.ts` advisor-radius click) already assert only that the request fired, never that it succeeded, so they need nothing; the third (A2) requires a real successful response by design (v2-07 audit deliberately made this a hard failure, not a skip) and is left as a genuine live-network dependency in CI, matching what a native `make gate` already requires. I fetched and then deleted a same-purpose fixture for A2's exact lat/lon after confirming the sha1 cache key depends on the browser's actual click-to-lat/lon resolution (only guaranteed within 6 px by the test itself), which I cannot reproduce without running Playwright. Full reasoning in `docs/handoff/v3-00-ci.md`.
- [V3-CI] The uploaded failure artifact is `artifacts/e2e/**` + `artifacts/logs/**`, not `playwright-report/`: `playwright.config.ts`'s `reporter` is `[list, json]` with no `html` entry, so no `playwright-report/` directory is ever produced by this config (the gitignore entries for it predate the JSON reporter). `playwright.config.ts` itself was left unmodified rather than adding an `html` reporter to match the literal ask, per this task's explicit "do not modify application code except an e2e spec" boundary.
- [V3-P1c] PrintParams.schema_version becomes `{"enum": [2, 3], "default": 3}`. Default 3 (not 2) does not break tests/test_v1_compat.py: schema_version is not a v1 field, so it only widens the "keys the model adds" set that test already enumerates explicitly (extended here, not weakened), and it never enters the byte-identical geometry comparison.
- [V3-P1c] Every new schema_version 3 `$defs` object (Place, Regions/RoadRegion/WaterRegion/ParkRegion/RailRegion, RegionSlots, RegionColors, Tint, Gradient, Colour, CustomProfile, Terrain, TypeDefaults, Heights, Bridges, HeightExaggeration, HeroAuto, Tiling, ShadowGap, Matting, Separate, Texture, FrameStyle, HangerMagnet) declares no `required` list, matching v2's NorthArrow/ScaleBar/UndersideMark (partial-share-link-friendly) rather than v2's PartColors/Engraving (all-required). Every leaf still carries an explicit schema `default`, so this is additive-safe and keeps `test_v1_compat.py` green without touching `REQUIRED_DEF_PROPERTIES` (still 9: PartColors 7 + Engraving 2).
- [V3-P1c] `regions`'s four per-region placements (roads/water/parks/rail) are four separate `$defs` (RoadRegion/WaterRegion/ParkRegion/RailRegion), not one shared `RegionPlacement` reused four times, because each region needs a different `depth_mm`/`proud_mm` default. A shared `$def` would need to leave those leaves default-less (since three different defaults can't share one schema property default), which would make them `Optional[float] = None` in the generated Pydantic model - silently allowing `None` past every bound check. Four small `$defs`, each with its own explicit defaults, keeps every leaf non-optional and bounded.
- [V3-P1c] `printer_profile` (enum) and `custom_profile` (object) are two separate top-level PrintParams fields, not one nested under the other, per the literal brief phrasing ("`printer_profile`: enum ... ; `custom_profile` {...}"). `custom_profile` is populated regardless of `printer_profile`'s value; the bake/engine side chooses which one to read.
- [V3-P1c] `packages/contracts/gen_py.py`'s `py_default` was generalized into a recursive `py_value_expr` (dict values recurse through their `$ref`'d class, list values recurse through `items`) so a default can nest arbitrarily deep (`FrameStyle.shadow_gap` is itself a nested-model default; `Colour.gradient.slots` is a non-empty scalar-list default). The prior implementation only supported an empty list default and a single level of scalar dict values; extended rather than special-cased per this task's generator-extension allowance. All four schema files' existing defaults (scalars, empty lists, one-level dicts) render byte-identical to before.
- [V3-P1c] `packages/contracts/gen_ts.py`'s `numeric_range_entries`/`limit_entries` were rewritten from one-level lookups into general recursive tree walks (with a `seen` cycle guard) so `PARAM_RANGES`/`PARAM_LIMITS` surface bounds/caps at any `$defs` depth (`PARAM_RANGES.regions.rail.width_m`, `PARAM_RANGES.frame_style.shadow_gap.width_mm`, `PARAM_LIMITS.colour.gradient.slots.max_items`), not just one level. `range_literal` moved to module scope so the new recursive renderer can call it. v1/v2 output (one level deep) renders identically.
- [V3-P1c] `fixtures/print-params-default.json` and `fixtures/lettering-expected.json` were regenerated with `FRAMECRAFT_WRITE_PARITY=1` after the schema_version default moved to 3; the diff in both is purely additive (new v3 keys/defaults plus the changed schema_version value) with no change to any v1/v2 value, warning, or lettering layout result - confirmed by diffing before committing.
- [V3-P1c] `services/bake/tests/test_v1_compat.py`'s two literal "keys the model adds beyond v1" sets were extended with the fourteen v3 top-level names (place, regions, colour, printer_profile, custom_profile, export_target, terrain, heights, bridges, height_exaggeration, hero_auto, tiling, frame_style, hanger_magnet), per this task's explicit authorization to extend (not weaken) a test that asserts an exact v2 field set. All eleven G8 tests stay green; the v1 golden geometry digest is untouched.
- [V3-P1c] TypeDefaults' six per-building-type heights, Tint.seed and CustomProfile.change_gcode carry no minimum/maximum/maxLength: the task brief specified defaults for these but no range, so none was invented. They are absent from PARAM_RANGES/PARAM_LIMITS by design, not omission.
- [V3-P1c] apps/web files outside this task's ownership (lib/advisor.ts, lib/share.ts, lib/warnings.ts, store/editor.ts, components/scene/CityPreview.ts) have "covers every key of DEFAULT_PRINT_PARAMS" guard tests that now fail because PrintParams gained fourteen top-level keys; left untouched per the explicit apps/web file-ownership boundary for this task and flagged to the requesting agent for the owning agents to extend, mirroring the pattern already applied in apps/web/lib/contracts.test.ts.
- [V3-P1c] Corrected per orchestrator note: `terrain.exaggeration` was a duplicate of the existing top-level v1/v2 `terrain_exaggeration` field (range 0.0-3.0, default 1.0, unchanged) and has been removed from the `Terrain` $defs object. `terrain` is now `{enabled: false, smoothing: 1 (int, 0..5)}` only; the exaggeration slider for terrain, once built, reads/writes `terrain_exaggeration` directly. Regenerated contracts; fixed the two OUT_OF_RANGE_CASES/example references in services/bake/tests/test_contracts.py and the PARAM_RANGES assertion in apps/web/lib/contracts.test.ts; regenerated fixtures/print-params-default.json and fixtures/lettering-expected.json. `apps/web/store/editor.test.ts:108` and `apps/web/lib/warnings.test.ts:78` still construct `{enabled: true, exaggeration: 2.0}` against the old shape and now fail `npm run typecheck`; both are outside this task's apps/web file ownership and were flagged to the requesting agent rather than edited here.
- [V3-P1] The lettering token fix (phase 1) landed while contracts v3 was still being extended concurrently: `store/editor.test.ts` and `lib/warnings.test.ts` were fixed here to `{ enabled: true, smoothing: 3 }` for `terrain`, matching the corrected (exaggeration-free) shape noted just above by the contracts agent, closing the gap that note flagged.
- [V3-P1] `{city} {lat} {lon} {coords} {scale} {radius} {date} {buildings}` grew five tokens: `{country} {state} {neighbourhood} {author} {hero}`. `lib/tokens.ts` stays a pure FORMATTER (it never decides what a token says, only how); the CALLER resolves the values (`store/editor.ts` for city/place, a new `lib/geocode.ts` for the reverse-geocode source). `services/bake/app/geom/tokens.py` gained the same five formatters, with defaulted (never-populated-by-`bake.py`) dataclass fields, purely so `expand_tokens` stays byte-for-byte identical between languages for the shared `fixtures/tokens-expected.json` fixture; `services/bake/app/bake.py` (out of this task's file authorization: only `tokens.py` + its test + the fixture) was not touched, so the server-side `TokenContext` never actually carries real country/state/neighbourhood/author/hero values today — harmless, because rule below sends only fully-resolved text.
- [V3-P1] `{hero}` formats the COUNT of selected hero buildings ("3"), not a building's name, empty when none are picked. `SceneGraph.Building` (frozen contract: id/ring/holes/height_m/height_source/min_height_m/is_tall) carries no `name` field, so a literal hero name cannot be resolved without a contract change, which is out of this task's authority ("do not change contracts"). This is the one place the brief's literal wording ("hero name") could not be honoured as written; flagged to the team lead as a real, deliberate deviation rather than invented text.
- [V3-P1] `lib/tokens.ts` gained `resolve_text(text, ctx) -> { text, tokens: [{token, empty}] }` alongside the unchanged `expand_tokens`. TS-only, no Python mirror and no parity-fixture entry: it is a UI-diagnostic layer (which token in a line had nothing to say) that only `lib/resolvedOutput.ts`, the Issues badge and the "Resolved output" panel need: by the time text reaches `POST /bake` it is already fully resolved (see the client-side-expansion rule below), so the server never needs to make this same judgement.
- [V3-P1] `lib/resolvedOutput.ts` (new) is the single source of truth for "what actually gets cut": one `ResolvedLine` per configured engraving line and the underside mark, `status: "cut" | "skipped"` and a `cause`/`reason` when skipped. Three callers share it: `lib/warnings.ts`'s new `letteringWarnings()` (Issues badge: one warn-level entry per empty line/mark naming the exact `{token}`, one info-level entry for the whole frame when it is off), `components/editor/OutputPanel.tsx`'s "Resolved output" panel (rule: nothing that bakes may be missing from the list), and `lib/bake.ts`'s `resolveParamsForBake()` (what is actually sent). `letteringWarnings` is deliberately NOT folded into `sceneWarnings(graph, params)` itself — it needs a `TokenContext` neither `graph` nor `params` alone supply, and nothing in it is ever `block` level (an empty line is omitted from the bake, never a refusal), so `bakeBlockReason` does not need it.
- [V3-P1] Token expansion happens ONCE, client-side, in `lib/bake.ts`'s `resolveParamsForBake(params, ctx)`, called from `store.requestBake` right before `POST /bake`: every engraving's `text` and the underside mark's `template` are replaced with their fully-resolved strings; a line that resolves empty is REMOVED from the array outright (never sent as `""`); every engraving is removed outright when the frame is off; an underside mark that resolves empty is sent `enabled: false` rather than with empty text. This is the only way `{country}/{state}/{neighbourhood}/{author}/{hero}` carry real text at all, since `services/bake/app/bake.py`'s `TokenContext` never learns those values (previous entry) — and it also means a raw un-expanded template literally containing `{country}` etc. would be silently blanked by the server's own `expand_tokens` if it ever reached it, which is exactly why it must not.
- [V3-P1] Root cause of the reported bug, confirmed: `{city}` was bound to `PrintParams.city_label`, which nothing ever wrote to. Fix, in priority order: (a) a preset's own city name (`lib/presets.ts`'s new `PRESET_CITY_NAMES`/`presetCityName()`, client-side, no network: "Chicago", "New York", "Paris", "Tokyo", "London", "San Francisco" — the city alone, `PRESET_LABELS`already carries the neighbourhood for display); (b) a debounced Nominatim reverse geocode (`lib/geocode.ts`) for a location that is not a preset; (c) the user's own typed text in the Place name field, which always wins from the moment they type and survives both a later pin move and a later preset click. The store tracks this as `placeDetect: { status, source, detectedCity, overridden }`, deliberately named apart from `PrintParams.place` (the unrelated v3 wire field for country/state/neighbourhood/author) to avoid confusing the two.
- [V3-P1] There is no "MODEL" group in this tree's `lib/groups.ts` (seven groups: location/scale/buildings/surface/frame/colour/output); the brief's "Place name field in the MODEL group" maps onto `LocationGroup`, which already owned the pre-existing "City label" field this bug report is about — renamed to "Place name" in place, `id="city_label"` UNCHANGED (existing e2e (`ui.spec.ts`, `share.spec.ts`) select it by that id). A "reset to detected" button appears only when the detected value differs from the current field value; the field's placeholder (never its value) falls back to `{coords}` formatting when nothing has been detected yet or detection failed, so a user always sees SOMETHING without FrameCraft ever silently writing raw coordinates into `city_label` as if they were a chosen name.
- [V3-P1] The Author field is bound to `params.place.author` — the ALREADY-FROZEN v3 wire field (contracts landed `place: {country, state, neighbourhood, author}` on `PrintParams` concurrently with this phase) — not a disconnected client-only value as the brief's literal wording ("persisted in localStorage") implied before that field existed. `params.place.author` is the live, shareable, bake-reaching value; `framecraft.author.v1` in localStorage is only ever read once, on mount (`store.initAuthor()`, called from `EditorShell`), to PREFILL a fresh session or a `resetParams()` — it never overwrites a real value already present (e.g. from a shared link). `country`/`state`/`neighbourhood` have no dedicated override field (the brief specifies one only for city and author): a fresh geocode result always overwrites them in `params.place`, with no user override to protect.
- [V3-P1] Geocoding is triggered from `EditorShell` (always mounted, unlike a `CollapsibleGroup` body which unmounts when collapsed) via a `useEffect` watching `(lat, lon, preset_id)`, calling `lib/geocode.ts`'s `scheduleReverseGeocode`, NOT from inside `store.setPin`/`setRadius`/`setRotation` themselves. Two reasons: (1) `store/editor.test.ts` asserts those three actions never touch `fetch`, by design (01 step 4's "no server call on slider change" — Nominatim is a different network call than `/scene`, but the same discipline was kept for the same reason: a store action's own effects must stay deterministic and test-observable without a timer); (2) a debounced `setTimeout` started inside a zustand action risks firing during an unrelated LATER test if not perfectly cancelled, which a React effect's synchronous cleanup avoids. `preset_id !== null` skips scheduling entirely — a preset never needs Nominatim.
- [V3-P1] `apps/web/e2e/smoke.spec.ts` is deliberately NOT given a Nominatim route mock even though its one real pin drop (dropping a custom pin on Lake Michigan) now schedules a reverse geocode: that file's own docstring says "nothing here is mocked; a route interception would defeat the purpose of a gate," and `lib/geocode.ts` fails soft on every path (a live call, if the CI sandbox allows it, either succeeds harmlessly or times out after 5 s into a `null` that is never asserted on by that spec) — so leaving it real is both correct to the file's stated intent and safe. `e2e/lettering.spec.ts` (new, for this phase) DOES route-mock Nominatim, per this task's explicit instruction, even though its own flow never leaves the Chicago preset and so never actually calls it: the mock is defensive against a future edit to that file adding a pin drop.
- [V3-P1] `EngravingsEditor.tsx`'s per-line "Not cut" reason is now a DISPLAY-LAYER override, not a change to the shared math: `transform.fit_text` already refuses an empty resolved string with a generic reason (`"the ${edge} engraving is empty"`, unchanged, not owned by this task) and that string still flows into `lib/adjustments.ts`'s `textNotices` (Issues badge, "repair" group) exactly as before. This task ADDS a more specific, token-named reason ("Line 2: the {city} token has no value.") computed independently via `resolve_text`, which replaces ONLY the inline text shown next to that one control when it is more specific than the shared math's own reason; with the frame off, the inline reason is the brief's required exact copy, "Turn on Frame to engrave the edges." — reused verbatim as `EngravingsEditor`'s own `engravings-frame-off` Note (new `data-testid`) rather than the pre-existing, broader `FrameTextGroup`-level `frame-off-notice` (reworded here to cover only the north arrow and scale bar, so the two notes stop saying almost the same thing twice when the frame is off).
- [V3-P1] Fixed, in the same pass (already touching these files for this phase, not scope creep into other agents' phases): the "every non-boolean/every key" contract-exhaustiveness guard tests in `store/editor.test.ts` (`PARAM_MOVES`, `nestedKeys()`), `lib/warnings.test.ts` (`MOVES`), and `components/scene/CityPreview.test.ts` (`V2_TEXT_MOVES` gained `place`; the hero-pick test's expectation moved from `["height"]` to `["height", "text"]`, since `hero_building_ids` now also feeds the `{hero}` token through `previewText.textParamsKey`) — all of which had gone red from the schema_version-3 field additions landing concurrently, independent of this task. `CityPreview.test.ts`'s OWN broader "covers every key" test (13 fields still missing: bridges/colour/custom_profile/export_target/frame_style/hanger_magnet/height_exaggeration/heights/hero_auto/printer_profile/regions/terrain/tiling), and the same-shaped failures in `lib/advisor.test.ts` and `lib/share.test.ts`, are NOT lettering's fields and were left for whoever wires those v3 groups into the preview/advisor/share paths (flagged to the team lead).
- [V3-P1] Per the team lead's follow-up ruling, took the remaining three guards rather than leaving them for another agent (nobody else was editing `apps/web` at the time). `lib/advisor.test.ts`'s `MOVES` gained the same fourteen v3 entries as `warnings.test.ts`/`store/editor.test.ts` (the advisor does not read any of them, so this only keeps the exhaustiveness walk non-vacuous). `lib/share.ts`'s `PRINT_PARAM_SPEC` gained one `FieldSpec` per v3 group (nested per leaf, bounds/enums read from `PARAM_RANGES`/`PARAM_LIMITS`, unbounded where the contract itself declares no range: `TypeDefaults`, `Colour.tint.seed`, `Colour.gradient.slots` items, `CustomProfile.change_gcode`), plus the `hanger` enum's two new members and `Engraving`'s (`mode: +inlay`, `edge: +underside` -- a NEW `ENGRAVING_EDGES` list, since `ScaleBar.edge` does not gain "underside" and must not share `EDGES` with `Engraving.edge` any more). `share.test.ts`'s `randomParams()`/`maximal()` generators were extended to cover all fourteen groups (a new `randomV3Params()` helper) and a new explicit "round-trips a v3 payload" test was added per the ruling; the pre-existing "stays inside a browser URL" bound moved from 8 kB to 16 kB, with the comment's measured numbers updated (6,828 ASCII / 9,218 CJK now that the v3 groups roughly double the field count, still comfortably under Chrome's ~32 k limit) -- not a weakened test, a corrected one: the OLD 8 kB bound was measured before the v3 groups existed and this file's own docstring says the number must be the real, current maximum. `components/scene/CityPreview.test.ts` gained a `V3_ENGINE_MOVES` bucket asserting all thirteen NON-`place` v3 groups (the ten the ruling calls "geometry-affecting" plus `printer_profile`/`custom_profile`/`export_target`) currently rebuild NOTHING -- the browser engine that will actually read them (`docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` phase 2+) has not landed, and `previewDeps`'s own discipline (DECISIONS [P4-fix]) is that a dep list names only primitives a function DEMONSTRABLY reads, so forcing these ten into an existing `previewDeps.*` list today would be real, wasteful over-invalidation for zero behavioural effect, not a fix. One deliberate deviation from the ruling's literal wording: `place` stays a `previewDeps.text` dependency (in `V2_TEXT_MOVES`, not `V3_ENGINE_MOVES`), because it is NOT inert -- `{country}`/`{state}`/`{neighbourhood}`/`{author}` are live engraving tokens this same phase wired up (`lib/previewText.ts`'s `textTokenContext`/`textParamsKey`), verified by a passing `rebuiltBy("place", ...) === ["text"]` test; removing it would reintroduce a stale-token bug the phase's own acceptance criteria checks for. Final state: `npm run typecheck`, `npm run lint` (0 warnings) and `npx vitest run` all green, 640/640 tests across 30 files, zero pre-existing failures remaining.
- [V3-P2-E1] `packages/contracts/schema/scene_graph.json` was NOT extended with the requested name/landmark/rail/bridge/layer fields, despite the task brief asking for them as "additive optional SceneGraph fields... regenerate contracts." Verified directly against pydantic v2 (not assumed): `services/bake/tests/test_contracts.py::test_scene_graph_dumps_fixture_verbatim_without_by_alias` does `SceneGraph(**fixture).model_dump(mode="json") == fixture` with no key filtering (unlike the analogous PrintParams tests, which filter to the original example's keys); any new Optional field on Building/Road/SceneGraph is emitted as `null` by `model_dump` even when absent from the input, so regenerating `contracts.py` would break that pinned test for every existing SceneGraph fixture, in a test file this task does not own and is not authorized to edit. Instead `apps/web/lib/engine/osm/types.ts` defines `EngineBuilding`/`EngineRoad`/`Rail`/`EngineSceneGraph` as structural supersets of the frozen contract types (assignable anywhere a plain `SceneGraph` is expected). Flagged for whoever owns `test_contracts.py` to promote into the frozen schema (extend that one test the same way the print_params tests already filter).
- [V3-P2-E1] `lib/engine/osm/**` has no new npm dependency, including no `manifold-3d` (already a project dependency): a hand-rolled 2D polygon toolkit (`geometry.ts`) does orientation, Douglas-Peucker simplify, snap-to-grid, ring assembly from line segments, Sutherland-Hodgman convex-window clipping and a Greiner-Hormann polygon union/intersection, because pulling manifold-3d's WASM into the ingest path would add an async init to a function documented as pure and risked the 1.5 s ingest budget for no benefit ingest doesn't need (no CSG/genus/watertightness questions arise at this stage). `tmerc.ts` hand-implements the WGS84 ellipsoidal UTM Kruger n-series to order n^6 (Karney 2011, the same series PROJ's `etmerc` uses) rather than a `proj4`-style dependency; validated directly against `pyproj`-computed reference coordinates (not assumed correct) to sub-micron agreement in both hemispheres and at rotation, so it round-trips through the 1 mm SceneGraph emission grid identically to the Python service in every case checked.
- [V3-P2-E1] `sha1.ts` is a ~90-line hand-rolled synchronous SHA-1 (cross-validated against `node:crypto` for a range of inputs including unicode and multi-block padding, plus the NIST test vectors), not `crypto.subtle` (async everywhere, complicating the jitter/cache-key call sites) and not `node:crypto` (Node-only, would break the browser/worker build this engine actually ships in).
- [V3-P2-E1] Real, measured parity on the committed Chicago Loop fixture (16k elements) against `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` (the Python `/scene` reference): buildings 992 vs 994 (99.8%), roads 5439 vs 5443 (99.9%), water 41 vs 42, green 709 vs 711, trees 5762 vs 5762 (exact). First ten buildings sorted by id: ids match Python's exactly; 8 of 10 net footprint areas (ring minus holes) are bit-exact or within 1e-6 m, one (`r1870546`, a large ring) differs by ~0.06 sq m out of ~26000 (Douglas-Peucker vs GEOS's topology-preserving simplify picking a different near-tolerance vertex), and one two-part relation (`r17460539`/`r17460539-1`) splits into the same pair of areas as Python but the TS and Python pipelines assign the id suffix to different parts (both parts individually correct as a set). Literal 1e-6 m parity on every one of ~1300 building candidates is not achievable without reimplementing GEOS bit-for-bit; `normalize.test.ts` pins the actual achieved numbers (not the aspirational ones) so a regression is caught, with the specific known exceptions documented inline.
- [V3-P2-E1] Two real bugs found and fixed while chasing that parity, both regression-tested: (1) `intersectSegLine`'s line-intersection formula had a sign error in the denominator, making `clipConvex` (Sutherland-Hodgman) return wildly wrong points for any polygon that actually crossed the clip window boundary (caught because it collapsed the Chicago building count from ~994 to 656; `geometry.test.ts`'s clip regression test pins the exact case that caught it). (2) A naive bounding-box-overlap-area fallback for `intersectionArea` (when neither ring is convex) badly over-merged adjacent, non-overlapping footprints in `unionOverlapping` (165 spurious merges out of 1341 real candidates vs Python's real 88); replaced with an inclusion-exclusion estimate via the same Greiner-Hormann union `unionPair` uses (`area(a)+area(b)-area(a union b)`), which brought it to 91 vs 88.
- [V3-P2-E1] `dissolveRings` (used for the water/green "unary_union all overlapping-or-touching pieces" step, and for combining a multi-outer-member building relation's parts) merges on EITHER genuine interior-area overlap OR a shared vertex on the 1 mm emission grid, not area-overlap alone: `unary_union`'s real semantics dissolve polygons that merely touch at a shared parcel boundary node (common where OSM landuse/leisure areas are subdivided along shared ways), unlike 03 step 7's building merge (`unionOverlapping`), which deliberately uses "overlaps"/"covers" (interior area only) so wall-sharing buildings stay separate, per 03's own stated intent. Confirmed by measurement: without the touch check, the Chicago fixture's green-space dissolve stopped 35 pairs short of Python's real merge count (870 vs 835 groups); with it, 834 vs 835.
- [V3-P2-E1] Performance: the Chicago fixture's committed water data includes one `natural=water` relation (Lake Michigan) whose assembled ring has 47,431 vertices, even though only a sliver falls near a 900 m crop. Two fixes were needed to hit the 1.5 s budget (initial cost ~2.6 s in `dissolveRings` alone, then ~1.6 s after the first fix moved the cost into `simplifyRing`): (1) water/green rings (and their holes) are pre-clipped to a padded square (radius times 1.3, matching the Overpass fetch margin) before the O(n^2) pairwise dissolve stage runs, shrinking the 47k-vertex ring to ~264 vertices before any pairwise geometry executes; the exact, final crop still happens later in `croppedParts`. (2) `simplifyRing`'s farthest-pair split (used to avoid simplifying away a ring's closure seam) is O(n^2) exact for rings up to 500 vertices (matches GEOS closely enough for the pinned building-area parity above) and an O(n) approximation (farthest pair among the four bbox-extreme points) above that; buildings and roads never approach 500 vertices, so this only affects large natural features, never the pinned building-area parity. Measured: 496 ms warm for the full 16k-element fixture, well under the 1.5 s budget.
- [V3-P2-E1] `EngineStats.height_fallback_counts` (tag/levels/default) is tallied over the FINAL, emitted building list (post dedupe/union/crop), matching `height_tag_ratio`'s own accounting, not over the pre-dedupe candidate list; the two must sum to `building_count` for the status-bar summary to make sense, and a first draft that counted per-candidate did not (caught by `normalize.test.ts`'s internal-consistency check, which independently recomputes the distribution from `scene.buildings` and compares).
- [V3-P2-E1] `presets.ts`'s six `EnginePreset.cityName` values are read directly from `apps/web/lib/presets.ts`'s existing `PRESET_CITY_NAMES` (imported, not duplicated), per that task's explicit "read it; do not edit it" instruction; ids/centers/radius/rotation are pinned against `services/bake/app/ingest/presets.py`'s six frozen presets (`presets.test.ts`).
- [V3-P2-E1] `overpass.ts`'s mirror list, backoff (500 ms/1 s/2 s) and 60 s per-attempt timeout are new for this browser engine, not carried over from `services/bake/app/ingest/overpass.py`'s own retry policy (03 only specifies the query text and a two-endpoint mirror pair with a 2 s/4 s backoff); the query TEXT and bbox math are pinned byte-identical to the Python builder (sha1 `a4e5375818f309940313e0ac08b8ebb88c615f9e` for the Chicago Loop preset, matching the committed fixture's own filename), per the task's explicit instruction.
- [V3-P2-E3] Bambu Studio recognises a project as its own by the `Application` metadata alone: `_handle_end_metadata` (bbs_3mf.cpp) sets `m_is_bbl_3mf` only when the value starts with `BambuStudio-`, and parses the rest as the generator version; `BambuStudio:3mfVersion` is read into `m_version` but no longer flips the flag (the assignment is commented out). Without a parseable generator version the loader sets `dont_load_config` and the GUI shows "The 3mf is not from Bambu Lab, load geometry data and color data only" (Plater.cpp, the `load_config && config_loaded.empty()` branch). FrameCraft therefore writes `Application` = `BambuStudio-02.00.00.00` and `BambuStudio:3mfVersion` = `1`. 2.0.0.0 is the lowest version that skips every legacy migration in the GUI loader (bed translation below 1.5.9, prime-tower rewrite below 2.0.0) and is not newer than any 2.x install, so neither the "saved with a newer version" dialog nor the CLI's version refusal (`CLI_FILE_VERSION_NOT_SUPPORTED`, a major/minor comparison in BambuStudio.cpp) can fire.
