- [V3-P2-E3] The Bambu project follows Bambu Studio's own split layout, not an inline one: `3D/3dmodel.model` holds one assembly `<object>` of `<component p:path="/3D/Objects/object_1.model" objectid=N>` entries, `3D/Objects/object_1.model` holds one mesh `<object>` per region with an empty `<build/>`, and `3D/_rels/3dmodel.model.rels` lists the sub-model, because the reader discovers sub-models only through that rels file (it derives `3D/_rels/3dmodel.model.rels` from the start part and reads every 3dmodel relationship in it). Production-extension `p:UUID`s follow Bambu's scheme (`%08x` of the backup id, or of `index + (id << 16)`, plus the fixed suffixes) so a project re-saved by Bambu Studio keeps the same identities.
- [V3-P2-E3] `Metadata/project_settings.config` always carries `printer_settings_id`, `print_settings_id`, `filament_settings_id` and `nozzle_diameter`, even for a third-party printer: the Bambu Studio CLI dereferences all four without a null check right after loading a Bambu-flavoured project (BambuStudio.cpp, the `is_bbl_3mf` block after `Model::read_from_file`), so a project missing one is an access violation rather than an error. For the Bambu rows the values are the machine preset's own `default_print_profile` / `default_filament_profile` (names verified in resources/profiles/BBL.json: `0.20mm Standard @BBL X1C` for P1S and X1C, `@BBL A1`, `@BBL A1M`, `@BBL H2S`; `Bambu PLA Basic @BBL P1S 0.4 nozzle`, `@BBL X1C`, `@BBL A1`, `@BBL A1M`, `@BBL H2S`). A third-party row (`prusa-*`, `ender-3`, `custom`) gets FrameCraft-named presets that the GUI resolves to its current selection, plus `printable_area`/`printable_height` from the profile and no `printer_model` (the GUI's `is_bbl_vendor_config` then falls through to `check_project_config`, which passes for one nozzle).
- [V3-P2-E3] `Metadata/custom_gcode_per_layer.xml` is written in the layout the Bambu Studio reader actually parses (`_extract_custom_gcode_per_print_z_from_archive`): root `<custom_gcodes_per_layer>`, one `<plate>` with `<plate_info id="1"/>`, one `<layer top_z type="0" extruder="1" color extra="" gcode/>` per change and `<mode value="SingleExtruder"/>`; not the PrusaSlicer `<custom_gcode_per_print_z><code print_z .../>` form the task brief quoted, which that reader returns from without reading (`if (main_tree.front().first != "custom_gcodes_per_layer") return;`). `type` 0 is `CustomGCode::ColorChange` (CustomGCode.hpp). The `gcode` attribute is informational (the reader ignores it when `type` is present; the emitted command is the printer preset's `change_filament_gcode`), so the profile's `change_gcode` is also written as `change_filament_gcode` in project_settings.config for non-Bambu printers only; Bambu machines keep their system AMS sequence.
- [V3-P2-E3] Single-nozzle colour planning (`colorchange.ts`): a region is separable by height when its Z range overlaps no region on a different slot (touching within 1e-3 mm does not count); bands of separable regions on the same slot merge; a change is emitted at the top of the first layer at or above each band start (`floor(z / layer_height) + 1` layers, default 0.2 mm), starting from the slot of the lowest band. Inseparable regions are reported with the regions they conflict with and print in whatever colour is loaded. Every part is written on extruder 1 and `filament_colour` lists only the starting colour.
- [V3-P2-E3] The generic 3MF follows `color_mode`: `single` merges every region into one object (one item, no materials), `parts` writes `<basematerials>` plus one mesh object per region and one assembly object, exactly as `services/bake/app/export/mf3.py` does, so `app/validate/container.py`'s rows accept either. `<basematerials>` is core-spec 3MF (section 5.1), not the materials extension, so no `m:` namespace is declared. Non-reserved metadata is namespaced `framecraft:` (attribution, generator, scale, lat, lon, radius_m, rotation_deg, preset_id). Every format translates the model so min x/y sit at 0 and the base underside at z = 0; the Bambu build item additionally carries a translation that centres the object on the profile's plate.
- [V3-P2-E3] STEP (AP214, faceted B-rep per [V3-A6]) welds vertices per region by exact coordinate and shares `EDGE_CURVE`s between the two faces that use them, so each region is one `CLOSED_SHELL` under its own `PRODUCT` named after the region; zero-area triangles are dropped and counted. The size policy is a returned note (never a refusal) above `triangleWarnLimit` (50 000 by default; an option so the test can exercise it without a 50k-triangle mesh), quoting the entity count and byte size.
- [V3-P2-E3] The Bambu CLI check is written as one always-running vitest, not `describe.skipIf`: `make gate` step 1 and the CI workflow reject any `skipIf` marker textually and step 3 rejects a runtime skip, so a skipped suite would fail exactly the gate it was meant to protect. When bambu-studio.exe is absent the test asserts the structure of the file it would have handed over and logs that the executable did not run. When present it runs two probes derived from BambuStudio.cpp: loader acceptance (the project as the SECOND input file yields `CLI_FILELIST_INVALID_ORDER`, -4, which the CLI only reaches after `Model::read_from_file` parsed the file and flagged it as a Bambu project; a generic 3MF in the same position yields 0 as the control) and the full `--export-3mf` re-export, whose preserved part names and extruders are asserted whenever the CLI produces result.json.
- [V3-P2-E3] Bambu Studio 2.8.1.55 on this host crashes with an access violation (BambuStudio.dll+0x15e3c3e in the Windows Application Error log) on `--export-3mf`, `--export-stl` and `--info` of EVERY Bambu-flavoured project given as the first file, including a project the same executable had exported itself a minute earlier from FrameCraft's generic 3MF, with or without `--load-settings`/`--load-filaments`/`--datadir`; non-Bambu 3MF and STL inputs export with return_code 0. The return_code-0 round trip the task brief asked for is therefore not achievable on this install and the test reports the crash instead of counting it as a pass. Acceptance was verified two other ways: the CLI loader probe above (-4), and opening the two-cube project in the Bambu Studio GUI, which loaded it as a project (printer Bambu Lab P1S, process 0.20mm Standard @BBL X1C, four filament slots in the file's colours) with no "Load 3mf" dialog; the only dialogs were Bambu's own "New version" and "Configuration update" prompts.
- [V3-P2-E3] `apps/web/scripts/bake-cli.ts` imports the engine dynamically from `lib/engine/{engine,index}.{ts,js}` and accepts `runEngine`, `bake`, `buildEngineResult` or a default export, so it typechecks and reports a clear error while the engine is still being built; it writes the export, a `<stem>.json` sidecar in the Python bake's shape (`attribution`, `license`, `generator`, `created_at`, `print_params`, `bake_result`, `scene_stats`, `validation: null`, `timings_s`) and `CREDITS.txt`.
- [V3-P2-E2] The browser engine's Stage 1 repair works in PRINT MILLIMETRES on Clipper2 (`CrossSection`), not in ground metres on shapely as `services/bake/app/geom/thicken.py` does. The conversion is exact - the map scale is a single positive factor, so `min_wall_ground * scale` and `min_wall_mm` are the same length - and it removes a whole class of unit bugs in a module that has no shapely to fall back on. The two decisions that depend on ground metres (04's dilation rule and its drop rule) still go through `lib/transform.ts` in ground metres, so the preview's widened/dropped counts and the engine's cannot differ.
- [V3-P2-E2] Regions PARTITION the model: their interiors are disjoint and they touch on shared faces. This is the opposite of the reference implementation's parts mode, which deliberately interpenetrates its parts by `PART_OVERLAP_MM = 0.2` to keep manifold3d off coincident faces. Two reasons for the reversal: a multi-material slicer given two parts that claim the same extrusion has to arbitrate which filament wins, and the brief's own acceptance criterion is a pairwise intersection under 1e-3 mm^3. Measured on Chicago, the largest pair overlap is 5.9e-4 mm^3 (buildings vs roads) and the rest are at 1e-12. Manifold's union of two solids meeting exactly on a face returns ONE body, verified directly, so nothing is lost by not overlapping.
- [V3-P2-E2] `regions.building_skirt_mm` (default 0.3 mm) is the depth buildings reach BELOW the base top, and the base is carved by the building solids themselves so the two are exact complements. It replaces 04 stage 2.3's `base_top - 0.2` overlap, which cannot be used when the buildings are a separate region. Cutting the base with a prism rebuilt from the union of the footprints (the first version) left 0.35 mm^3 of base and buildings claiming the same material, because Clipper2's union of a set of outlines is not the same VERTEX LIST as the outlines themselves; cutting with the very solids that go into the region makes the complement exact by construction.
- [V3-P2-E2] A preserved tall footprint (04 stage 1 step 5) starts exactly on its block's roof plane instead of 0.2 mm inside it, for the same partition reason, and it is clipped to ITS OWN block rather than to the union of every block. A tower whose footprint spilled over into a shorter neighbour used to be extruded from its own block's roof over ground that ends millimetres lower: two bodies of 85 mm^3 and 112 mm^3 floating in mid air on the Chicago plate.
- [V3-P2-E2] Every extrusion plane is snapped to a 2^-12 mm grid (`solid/manifold.ts:snapZ`). An extrusion is built from 0 to `height` and translated by `z0`, so its top face lands at `z0 + (z1 - z0)`, which is NOT `z1` in binary floating point - and a one-ULP gap between a tower and the roof it stands on is a gap, which manifold reports as two bodies. On a 2^-12 grid every plane under 64 mm needs 18 bits of mantissa, so the round trip is exact in float32 and in double alike. A plane can move by up to 122 nanometres.
- [V3-P2-E2] Surface layers are subtracted from each other with NO separation, where `thicken.py` holds them 0.02 mm apart (`LAYER_SEPARATION_MM`) to keep the welded solid off coincident vertical faces. Here the layers are separate regions that are SUPPOSED to meet, and the 0.02 mm gap is a 0.02 mm rind of base standing between two pockets: an unprintable wall the separation itself created, measured at 0.016 mm on the Chicago plate before this changed.
- [V3-P2-E2] A surface region's solid spans `[base_top + proud_mm - depth_mm, base_top + proud_mm]` and the base is carved by the same footprint from that underside up past the base top. The underside is clamped twice: never above `base_top - 0.02` (or the region would float on the surface with nothing holding it) and never below half the base thickness (`extrude.slab`'s own clamp, so a groove always has a floor).
- [V3-P2-E2] Surface precedence is buildings, then water, rail, roads, parks. Buildings first is 04 stage 1's own rule ("roads must not tunnel through buildings"); water first among the surfaces is the reference implementation's order (`thicken.repair_scene`); rail before roads because a level crossing belongs to the railway.
- [V3-P2-E2] `road_mode` decides whether the roads region exists at all ("off" removes it); `regions.roads.proud_mm` decides where it sits, so a negative value is an engraved road and a positive one is embossed. When the two disagree (mode "emboss" with a negative offset) the placement wins and a `road-placement-conflict` finding says so with a one-click fix, rather than either setting silently overriding the other.
- [V3-P2-E2] A hero building the user picked ALWAYS becomes its own `hero_building` region, whatever `hero_mode` says, because the regions are the colour partition and a hero left inside `buildings` could never be given a filament. `hero_mode` keeps its v2 meaning: `true_height`/`both` raise it, `own_color`/`both` give it its own slot and colour. Without `own_color` the hero region is emitted carrying the buildings region's slot and colour, so it prints in the same filament it would have if it had never been split out.
- [V3-P2-E2] Boolean debris is pruned per region at 0.01 mm^3 (`assemble.MIN_BODY_VOLUME_MM3`) and discounted in the assembly body count at 1e-9 mm^3 (`assemble.UNION_DEBRIS_MM3`). The brief's "no debris under 1 mm3" is NOT used as a floor: the smallest LEGAL building block at a 0.4 mm nozzle is `min_detail^2 * 0.6 mm` = 0.096 mm^3, so a 1 mm^3 floor would delete real buildings. 119 exactly-zero-volume shells appear at the seams where regions meet on coincident faces; they belong to the measurement union and to no shipped region.
- [V3-P2-E2] The minimum wall is measured on the UNION of the regions, not per region. Inside a filled pocket a thin feature is a colour edge, not a wall: a 0.25 mm ridge of base between a road groove and a park is flanked by material on both sides and prints fine, while the same ridge in the reference implementation's single welded solid was a free-standing wall (which is why `thicken.merge_recess_ridges` exists there and not here). Per-region thin features on Chicago: roads 0.60 mm, parks 0.53 mm, base 0.25 mm; the union measures 0.847 mm against a 0.8 mm minimum.
- [V3-P2-E2] Widths are measured by morphological opening (`solid/measure.ts`), since Clipper2 has no maximum-inscribed-circle query. Three settings, each with its own reason: the gate opens with 64-segment round joins and a 99 % area rule (a 16-gon disc is 2 % narrower across its flats than the circle it stands for, which is exactly the difference between "0.795 mm, fails" and "0.801 mm, passes"); the repair opens with 16 segments, where a coarse probe only widens a wing slightly more than it had to; and the measurement SATURATES at 1.5x the minimum wall, because every question it answers lives inside that range and each doubling of the ceiling costs another opening of a 40 000-vertex slice.
- [V3-P2-E2] A region is kept only if a disc of a FULL minimum wall fits inside it (erosion at `min_wall / 2`), matching `thicken.MIN_WALL_REPAIR_FACTOR = 1.0`, and a footprint that fails is grown in tenths of a wall up to six times before it is dropped (`thicken.widen_to_min_wall`). 04's `4A/P` alone is not enough: for a `w` by `L` strip with `L >> w` the hydraulic diameter converges on `2w`, so the formula reports a 0.4 mm wing as 0.8 mm wide and the widening pass leaves it exactly as it found it. `4A/P` is kept as the cheap pre-filter in front of the real measurement.
- [V3-P2-E2] The appendage passes clip their growth to the region they live in (the crop square for buildings, the lip's keep region for text) INSIDE the loop rather than after it, so the next round can finish the job inwards. Clipping only at the end left 12.7 mm^3 of buildings inside the frame region, a partition failure rather than a rounding one; clipping without re-running the drop rule left a 0.797 mm wall on the crop edge.
- [V3-P2-E2] A text piece's stroke is measured with a 0.5 area ratio, not the 99 % the structural probe uses: every glyph has tapering terminals and a join or two narrower than its stem, and a 99 % rule measures those instead of the stroke the nozzle lays. 0.5 is the same ratio the committed metrics were generated with (`gen_font_assets.py`'s `stem_area_ratio`), and the two agree: 0.426 mm measured on the finished groove against 0.427 mm predicted by `transform.text_stroke_mm` for "Chicago" at 5 mm in Inter. A piece that is under the target EVERYWHERE is refused separately, by the reference implementation's own erosion probe applied per connected piece.
- [V3-P2-E2] `Engraving.edge = "underside"` (new in schema 3) is laid out inside `solid/lettering.ts`, not through `transform.lettering_layout`: `transform.edge_axis` throws on any edge but the four frame ones, and `lib/transform.ts` is shared with the preview and not this task's to change. The underside lines use the underside mark's own placement rule (mirrored about the anchor so they read when the plate is turned over) and stack in a column below the mark, and they are refused when the base is too thin to carry the pocket, on the same arithmetic `transform.underside_min_base_mm` uses.
- [V3-P2-E2] `Engraving.mode = "inlay"` cuts exactly the pocket an engraving would and emits a solid filling it in the `lettering` region. Pocket and fill come from the same `CrossSection` and the same two Z planes, so the frame and the lettering regions partition what the frame alone used to be: measured, the frame loses exactly what the lettering region gains, to three decimal places.
- [V3-P2-E2] The rail layer is read defensively from an optional `rail` key on the SceneGraph (`solid/roads.ts:railWays`). `packages/contracts/schema/scene_graph.json` is frozen without one and the ingest phase carries railways as an additive extra key, so the rail region appears the moment ingest emits it and is simply absent until then, with no contract change and no crash on an older scene.
- [V3-P2-E2] `EngineInput` gained two optional fields, `rotationDeg` and `date` (both additive, both defaulted): the north arrow has to be turned back by the SceneRequest's rotation to keep pointing at true north, and `{date}` cannot be `new Date()` inside the engine or no fixture could pin it. `bake()` also gained an options argument with an `onSolids` hook, which is the seam the partition test measures through: a `RegionMesh` is a float32 rendering of a solid built in double, and measuring the partition on re-imported meshes measures the mesh format instead of the geometry.
- [V3-P2-E2] `RegionMesh.positions` is a `Float32Array` by the frozen engine contract, so an exported region is a float32 rendering of a double-precision solid. On Chicago the exported regions overlap by 6.5 mm^3 out of 172 000 (four parts in 100 000, sub-micrometre everywhere, almost all of it between the base and the floor of the road grooves) while the solids themselves overlap by 1e-12 mm^3. Whoever writes the exporters should know the number is not zero.
- [V3-P2-E2] The height ceiling is `min(04's 60 mm, custom_profile.max_height_mm)`. 04's 60 mm is a product rule the editor, the reference bake and every existing warning already enforce; the per-printer plate and height table is the printer phase's (`lib/printers.ts`), and until it exists `custom_profile.max_height_mm` is the only printer number the contract carries.
- [V3-P2-E2] The engine builds the plain frame profile and the keyhole and magnet hangers. `frame_style`'s profiles, corners, shadow gap, matting, separate part and textures, and the `cleat` and `easel` mounts, are reported as `frame-style-unbuilt` and `hanger-refused` findings rather than silently ignored, so a user who selected one is told it did not happen.
- [V3-P2-E2] A `TerrainSampler` displaces the plate's top surface for real (refine to a 3 mm grid, then `warp` through `transform.terrain_z_mm`), and emits a `terrain-not-draped` info finding saying the buildings, roads and water are still placed on the flat base top. Draping them is the terrain phase's work; a half-built feature that says so is better than one that quietly produces a wrong model.

[V3-P2-E4] `scripts/bake-cli.ts` cannot run under `tsx`: `manifold-3d`'s package.json exports map declares only an `"import"` condition (no `"require"`), and `tsx` globally shims Node's CJS `Module._resolveFilename` as part of enabling TS support, so ANY resolution of `manifold-3d` (static or dynamic, anywhere in the import graph) is routed through that shim and fails with `ERR_PACKAGE_PATH_NOT_EXPORTED`, regardless of how the importing code is written. Reproduced directly against `manifold-3d` alone, independent of any FrameCraft code. `apps/web/package.json`'s `bake:cli` script now runs `vite-node` (new devDependency, matching the resolver vitest itself already uses successfully; `tsx` removed, nothing else used it) instead of `tsx`; confirmed against the Chicago fixture (109,098 triangles, matching `docs/handoff/v3-02-solids.md`'s own numbers exactly).

[V3-P2-E4] Two build fixes were needed for the worker to actually load `manifold-3d`'s WASM under `next build`/`next dev` (verified end to end in a real Chromium: Chicago preset -> real Overpass ingest -> real WASM bake in the worker -> populated stats card -> Bake -> Blob download, zero console/page errors): (1) `apps/web/next.config.ts` gained a webpack `IgnorePlugin` for `node:` scheme imports, client compilation only -- `manifold-3d`'s emscripten glue branches on `ENVIRONMENT_IS_NODE` and only then does `await import("node:module")`, correct and dead in a browser but still something webpack's client target has to compile around ("UnhandledSchemeError"). (2) `lib/engine/solid/manifold.ts`'s `loadManifold()` now passes a `Module.locateFile` override (root-relative `/manifold/manifold.wasm`) outside Node, the same bug class `copy-maplibre-worker.mjs`'s own docstring describes for the MapLibre worker (`import.meta.url` inside a worker chunk resolves to the chunk's own `/_next/static/...` URL, which does not serve `manifold.wasm` next to it); `scripts/copy-manifold-wasm.mjs` (new, wired into `predev`/`prebuild` beside the maplibre copy) puts the real file at that path. Node's own path (vitest, `bake:cli`) is untouched and still resolves the package's own `import.meta.url`-relative logic directly.

[V3-P2-E4] `RegionMeshes.tsx` replaces every approximate instanced/flat-fill preview layer (base, frame, water, green, roads, buildings, trees, lettering) the moment `state.engine` is fresh (`status:"ready" && !stale`); while a newer engine job is computing, the instanced v1 preview stays on screen (never a flicker to empty) with a small "Updating model..." badge. `InstancedBuildings` gained a `hidden` prop (fully transparent material, shadow casting off) rather than being unmounted once RegionMeshes is showing: three.js's `Raycaster`/`Mesh.raycast` do not consult `.visible` or material opacity (verified directly against `node_modules/three/src/core/Raycaster.js`), so an invisible instanced mesh is exactly as clickable as a visible one, which is what keeps hero-picking (click and the keyboard cursor) working once the fused, per-building-identity-free RegionMesh is what is actually seen. `components/scene/palette.ts` and the `color_mode`/`part_colors` v1/v2 fields were deliberately left untouched (still drive the instanced fallback's colours and `generic-3mf`'s single/parts default): the new `params.colour` region-slot/colour table added to `ColourGroup.tsx` is additive, not a replacement, given the size of the blast radius touching `palette.ts` would have (CityPreview.test.ts, InstancedBuildings.test.ts, share.ts, transform.ts, generic3mf.ts all read one or the other today).

[V3-P2-E4] The gate command the brief names (`npm run bake:cli` on the committed Chicago fixture + `fixtures/print-params-default.json`, `--target generic-3mf`, then `make validate`) does not pass today, and the cause is in `lib/engine/solid/**` (E2's files), not this integration. Measured directly: single mode (the params fixture's own default `color_mode`) fails `degenerate_faces` (1,302 faces under 1e-9 mm^2), `bodies` (463 disconnected instead of 1) and `min_wall` (0.025 mm); `color_mode:"parts"` is the architecturally correct check per `services/bake/app/validate/checks.py:validate_parts`'s own design (a part MAY be multi-body, only the assembled union has to be one solid) but still fails it -- the validator's OWN re-union of the exported parts comes back as 7 bodies, and the `base` region alone carries 1,106 degenerate faces. The reference Python CLI baking the IDENTICAL Chicago fixture in the same session passes cleanly (68,692 triangles, `degenerate_faces 0`, `bodies 1`, ALL CHECKS PASS), so this is not the validator being stricter than the reference pipeline tolerates. `RegionMesh.positions` being frozen as `Float32Array` (`[V3-P2-E2]`) is documented to leave a small, nonzero seam overlap on re-import, but four figures of degenerate faces in one region is larger than plain float32 rounding of genuinely-tiny double-precision slivers would explain on its own, which points at a specific triangulation or repair-pass artifact in the solid engine rather than only the frozen contract's own known tradeoff. Not fixed here: it is E2's geometry kernel, this task does not have the domain context to patch it safely, and `make gate`'s new step (wired as specified, not weakened) is the mechanism that will show whether a later change to `lib/engine/solid/**` closes the gap. Reported to the orchestrator directly.

[V3-P2-E4] Follow-up to the previous entry, since the diagnosis is more precise than first measured. `color_mode:"single"` cannot pass `services/bake`'s `bodies` check for ANY multi-region bake, independent of geometry quality: the engine partitions regions on purpose (`[V3-P2-E2]`) and `generic3mf.ts`'s single mode only CONCATENATES them (`common.ts:mergeRegions`, "no welding, no boolean"), so base/buildings/frame/roads/water/parks are never topologically joined even when they are geometrically flush -- proven on a hand-built 4-building synthetic scene with zero degenerate faces, still 8 disconnected bodies in single mode. `color_mode:"parts"` on the SAME small scene passes `make validate` cleanly end to end (`ALL CHECKS PASS`, union of the 5 parts is 1 body, 0 degenerate faces) -- confirming `validate_parts()`'s design (a part may be multi-body, only the assembled union has to be one solid) is genuinely satisfiable by this engine's architecture, not merely closer. `make gate`'s new browser-engine step and the CI `web` job therefore both bake with a new `fixtures/print-params-parts.json` (the default fixture with `color_mode` overridden to `"parts"`, otherwise identical), not `fixtures/print-params-default.json` as the brief's literal text names -- using the literal default would make the step permanently, unfixably red regardless of any future improvement to `lib/engine/solid/**`, which defeats its purpose as a regression gate. What remains open, and is what that gate step actually now measures, is scale-specific: on the full Chicago fixture, PARTS mode still fails (`bodies`: union is 7 not 1; `degenerate_faces`: 47 file-wide, 1,106 in the `base` region alone; `min_wall`: 0.365 mm against 0.72 mm) even though the identically-scoped small scene is clean, which is consistent with a triangulation or repair-pass artifact that only shows up on complex, heavily-carved geometry -- still `lib/engine/solid/**`'s (E2's) to diagnose.

[V3-P2-E4] Follow-up: the E2 fix landed while this run was still open (RegionMesh.positions widened to Float64Array end to end, plus a real `EngineResult.merged` -- an actual manifold3d union, not a concatenation -- that `lib/engine/export/common.ts:placeMerged` now feeds to `generic3mf.ts`'s single mode and `stl.ts`). Re-measured on the identical Chicago fixture + `fixtures/print-params-parts.json`: `degenerate_faces` 1,106+47 -> 0, `bodies` (union) 7 -> 1 (PASS), `part_meshes` now PASS. Only `min_wall` still fails (0.561 mm against 0.72 mm, one sampled region out of 420, down from two regions and a much narrower 0.365 mm before) -- a real, comparatively small thin-wall gap rather than a precision/topology artifact, and still `lib/engine/solid/**`'s to close. `make gate`'s browser-engine step and the CI `web` job are unchanged (still `fixtures/print-params-parts.json`, still expected to fail until that last check clears) but are now one check away from ALL CHECKS PASS rather than four.

[V3-P2-E4] `EngineResult.params` was echoing `input.params` verbatim, so every export's persisted `print_params` (the 3MF metadata, the sidecar JSON) still showed a raw `{city}`-style token instead of the resolved string `resolvedText` already reported for the same line -- a regression from removing `lib/bake.ts`'s old client-side `resolveParamsForBake` (which used to do exactly this substitution before `POST /bake`) without replacing what it did. Fixed with `engine.ts:resolveParamsEcho`, run on `ctx.resolvedText` right before the final return: an engraving whose line resolved to `status:"cuts"` is echoed with its resolved text, one whose line was `"skipped"` (frame off, an empty token, a refusal) is dropped from the echo rather than left showing the unresolved token, mirroring the removed function's own rule. Matches `solid/lettering.ts`'s own id scheme (`engraving-<i>` counted within frame-edge engravings only, `underside-<i>` counted within underside-edge engravings only, `underside-mark` for the separate underside template) without importing its private split, so an engraving mixing frame edges and the underside is not misindexed. Caught by `e2e/lettering.spec.ts`'s sidecar assertion; fix verified against that test directly (was failing `Expected: "Chicago", Received: "{city}"`, now passes) and against the full `lib/engine` vitest suite (`npm run typecheck` clean, no new failures; the two pre-existing `engine.test.ts` min-wall failures reproduce identically on HEAD before this change and are `[V3-P2-E2]`'s open min_wall gap, not this fix).

[V3-P2-E4] The `a11y.spec.ts` "dark editor is clean in every state" failure the earlier `E2E_BUDGET_FACTOR=3` sanity run hit (state reverting to the empty preview between the Chicago-generated and adjustments-chip steps) did not reproduce against a fresh `next dev` server, isolated with axe-core injection included and excluded, or in a direct Playwright re-run of the same test. Treated as a stale `.next`/dev-server artifact from mid-session file edits during that sanity run, not a product bug; no app code changed for it.

[V3-P2-E4] `protocol.ts:runBakeJob` is now single-flight: a `bake` message that arrives while one is already running does not queue behind it for its own full run, it overwrites a single `queuedBake` slot, so N requests made while the worker is busy cost at most one MORE full bake after the one already under way, not N of them run back to back. A bake cannot be preempted mid-flight (unchanged, still documented on `runOneBake`), but nothing was stopping the WORKER from grinding through every now-irrelevant intermediate id one after another once free -- each of which `client.ts`'s supersede had already rejected the caller's promise for, so running it to completion was pure waste. Found via `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s happy path: the A3 frame-rate proof (driving `small_scale` on every animation frame for ~1.5-2 s, a real `setParam` -> `scheduleEngineJob` on each event) left a full Chicago-scale (992-building) bake running in the worker, and the very next step (a rotation commit, a genuine location change per `osm/overpass.test.ts`'s own bbox-rotation math) queued its ingest+bake behind it; a second rotation nudge right after compounded the backlog further, observed exceeding 60 s and climbing before this fix. Covered directly by a new `lib/engine/protocol.test.ts` (id 2, requested while id 1 is running, is superseded before the worker ever starts it: zero `bake-progress`/`bake-done`/`bake-error` for it, ever; a second test pins that sequential, fully-awaited requests all still run, so coalescing only ever drops a request something superseded, never a legitimate one). `smoke.spec.ts`'s two rotation-commit waits now use `WARMUP_BUDGET_MS` (scaled by `E2E_BUDGET_FACTOR`, matching every other WebGL/WASM-heavy wait in the file) instead of an unscaled literal, and both are timed and logged (`log("A6 ...")`/`log("A6b ...")`) the same way A1/A4 already were, rather than picking a number without measuring it first.
- [V3-P2-E2] `RegionMesh.positions` is a `Float64Array`. manifold3d works in double and the JS binding only exposes a float32 mesh (`getMesh()`), so the doubles are read out through `warpBatch` - a warp that changes nothing, whose callback receives the internal vertex array in `getMesh()`'s own order (verified index for index against the float32 array, agreeing to 4.5e-7, which is exactly float32 rounding; a mismatch falls back to the widened float32 positions). Float32 is not good enough to ship: one step at a 90 mm coordinate is 7.6 nanometres, which collapsed 1088 faces of the Chicago base that measure fine in double, and made two regions sharing a face appear to overlap by 6.5 mm^3.
- [V3-P2-E2] `EngineResult.merged` is the single welded solid, and it is NOT the union of the finished regions. That union crosses a shared face wherever two regions meet and leaves zero-area slivers and zero-volume shells behind (measured: 40 faces under 1e-9 mm^2 that no mesh repair could remove, and 463 bodies in the exported file). `merged` follows the reference implementation's own recipe instead (`assemble.assemble`): union the ADDITIVE primitives, then subtract only the visible part of each recess - the groove between the region's top face and the base top. It is the same set (a region fills exactly the pocket it carved, so carving and refilling below the groove is the same as never carving there), 4 000 triangles lighter, one body, and it cleans to zero degenerate faces. The single-object 3MF, the single-body STL, and OBJ and STEP in `color_mode: "single"` write it; parts mode writes the regions.
- [V3-P2-E2] A pocket is grown by `POCKET_GROW_MM` (2 micrometres) and the region solid is extruded from THE SAME grown section. The growth is what stops two abutting pockets from sharing a vertical wall, which is where zero-area triangles come from (721 in the Chicago base with exact pockets, 602 of them from the buildings/roads boundary alone; 9,929 in the exported file). Sharing the section between the pocket and the solid is what stops the region from sitting clear of the base, which the reference validator reads as thin regions and disconnected bodies when it unions the colour parts. The price is that two regions whose footprints abut interpenetrate by 4 micrometres over whatever Z range they share: 4.0 mm^3 of 172,577 on the Chicago plate, 0.0023 %, against the reference implementation's own deliberate 0.2 mm.
- [V3-P2-E2] Two alternatives to that growth were built and measured, and both are worse. A COLLAR (grow the pocket only where it meets a neighbour, so the fit is exact elsewhere) makes the pocket outline carry a two micrometre hairline with the neighbour's whole boundary: 10,467 zero-area faces. Growing the solid and then subtracting the neighbours (an exact partition AND an exact fit) leaves the same hairline in the solid: the buildings region went from 0 to 527. Z-BANDING the cutters (each band's footprint the 2D union of every layer that reaches that deep, so the boundary between two layers is dissolved by Clipper2 before anything is extruded) removes the vertical coincidence between layers but not between bands, because a band's footprint is nested inside the next one's and their walls coincide wherever no new layer joins: 4,972 faces.
- [V3-P2-E2] `solid/mesh.ts` repairs the exported mesh in double precision, because no boolean kernel meets 04 stage 4's "zero faces under 1e-9 mm^2" on its own and the JS binding cannot do what the reference does (`assemble.finalize` welds and re-imports through manifold3d; `Manifold.ofMesh` takes a `Float32Array`, so any round trip through it re-quantises the model). Two defects, two exact repairs: a CAP (two coincident vertices) is welded, and a NEEDLE (three collinear vertices, a T-junction) is removed by splitting the neighbour across its long edge - `(v0, v2, x)` becomes `(v0, v1, x)` and `(v1, v2, x)`, which leaves every other edge paired and moves no vertex at all. Every pass is a transaction: a pass that leaves the mesh no better, opens an edge, or moves the volume is discarded and the last good mesh is kept.
- [V3-P2-E2] The mesh repair works to 1e-7 mm^2, two decades above the gate's 1e-9. Writing a vertex as a decimal string moves it, and moving a vertex moves the area of every face that uses it, so a face measuring 4e-9 in memory can land under the threshold in the file - which happened, once, on the Chicago plate. `engine.test.ts` asserts on the mesh rounded exactly as `generic3mf` writes it, which is the property that actually matters.
- [V3-P2-E2] A pinch point - an outline that touches itself, which a chain of 2D booleans leaves behind - extrudes into a pair of zero-area triangles joining two coincident-but-distinct vertices. They cannot be welded (the two vertices belong to two sheets of the surface and merging them makes the mesh non-manifold) and they cannot be split. They are prevented instead: `manifold.cleanSection` opens every section by `DEBURR_MM` (10 nanometres) before extruding it, which took the Chicago parks region from 26 such faces to none and moved the layer's area by less than a thousandth of a square millimetre in 1549. The reference does the same thing at a thousand times the radius (`thicken.DEBURR_GRID_CELLS`).
- [V3-P2-E2] 3MF vertices are written at 12 decimals, matching the reference writer (`app/export/mf3.py`, `%.12f`), not the 6 the generic writer used. Six quantises a coordinate to a micrometre, which is enough to move two faces a boolean made exactly coincident onto different grid points; on the parts export that alone took the union's zero-area face count from 1,107 to 74.
- [V3-P2-E2] The min-wall measurement is now the reference validator's, question for question: per connected REGION of a slice rather than per slice (a whole-slice area rule cannot see one narrow island among 1200 regions, which is how a 0.56 mm tower passed unnoticed); the widest disc that fits (`inscribedWidthMm`, an erosion, twice GEOS' maximum inscribed circle) rather than an area ratio, which bottoms out at zero the moment a region has a thin tail; probed at half a wall so "not thin" means a full minimum wall fits, matching `MIN_WALL_KEEP_FACTOR`; and only regions that survive one printed layer upward are judged (`WALL_PERSIST_PER_NOZZLE`, the reference's own 0.625 nozzles), because a 0.1 mm tall ridge of base between two grooves is a bump on a solid surface, not a wall. The measurement saturates at the minimum wall.
- [V3-P2-E2] A preserved tall footprint (04 stage 1 step 5) is judged by the same minimum-wall rule as every other solid after it is clipped. It is clipped twice - to the crop square, then to its own block - and either cut can leave a strip too narrow to print: the reference validator found exactly one on the Chicago plate, 0.57 mm wide and 9.6 mm long, sitting on the crop edge, and it was the single reason the single-mode file failed `min_wall`. A tower that fails is not dropped from the model; its footprint is still part of the block below, it just stops being a solid of its own.
- [V3-P2-E2] Parts mode does not reach ALL CHECKS PASS and the gap is structural, not a tuning problem. Every individual part is manifold, watertight and free of degenerate faces (`part_meshes` PASS), the assembly structure and materials are correct, and the union IS one body (`bodies` PASS). What fails is the mesh the validator BUILDS by unioning the six parts: six solids meeting along a boundary as intricate as a road network cannot be unioned without slivers at the seam. The reference implementation does not try - its colour parts interpenetrate by 0.2 mm and are cut out of one welded solid. Reaching PASS means adopting that construction and giving up the disjointness the phase 2 brief specifies and the filament mapper and per-region exporters rely on; the interpenetration was swept from 0 to 50 micrometres and the union's sliver count moves between 59 and 10,467 without reaching zero. Flagged to the team lead rather than decided here.
- [V3-P2] Parts-mode construction ruling (2026-08-30): the browser engine's regions may interpenetrate the base (and buildings the base through the skirt) by a fixed 0.2 mm at every seam, derived from the one welded solid the way the Python parts mode does it. A disjoint partition of touching solids satisfies every per-part check but the reference validator's union of those parts grows seam slivers (74 degenerate faces, 0.018 mm walls on Chicago) that no interpenetration sweep removed; slicers want a small overlap at multi-material seams and hate coincident faces. `merged` stays the welded solid for single mode, totals and estimates; per-region volumes are reported as built.
- [V3-P2-E2] RULING (team lead, superseding the flush-partition entries above): the regions INTERPENETRATE at every seam by `PART_OVERLAP_MM` = 0.2 mm, the reference implementation's own `extrude.PART_OVERLAP_MM`, and they are derived from the one welded solid the way the Python parts mode is. The brief's "distinct, non-welded, watertight solids" is satisfied by separate BODIES; a small overlap is what a slicer wants at a multi-material seam (no micro gaps after discretisation) and coincident faces are what it cannot handle. Concretely: a surface region is extruded from its footprint grown 0.2 mm, from 0.2 mm below its pocket floor; the frame lip starts 0.2 mm inside the plate (`extrude.frame_lip_part`); a building interpenetrates through `regions.building_skirt_mm` and the base is no longer socketed for it at all; a lettering inlay reaches 0.2 mm deeper into the lip than its pocket. Every one of those extras lies inside material `EngineResult.merged` already has, so the union of the regions IS the welded solid - measured at 172 575.08 mm3 in ONE body against the welded 172 462.83 mm3, a 0.065 % boolean-rounding difference.
- [V3-P2-E2] Per-region volumes are reported AS BUILT and therefore double-count the seam overlap: 181 391.00 mm3 across the six Chicago regions against 172 462.83 mm3 welded, +5.18 %. Any total, filament estimate or price must come from `EngineResult.merged.volumeMm3`, never from a sum of `RegionMesh.volumeMm3`. The largest single seam is base against roads at 3 695 mm3 (a 0.2 mm collar and floor under 11 300 mm2 of road), and the frame's is exactly checkable in closed form: `(180^2 - 168^2) * 0.2` = 835.0 mm3, which is what the test asserts.
- [V3-P2-E2] With that construction BOTH exports read ALL CHECKS PASS against the reference validator on the Chicago fixture. Single mode: min_wall 0.8746, degenerate_faces 0, bodies 1, watertight euler=2, 58 978 triangles. Parts mode: min_wall 0.9096, degenerate_faces 0, `bodies` 6 parts / 463 shells / union 1, `part_meshes` 6 parts / 100 278 triangles each manifold, watertight and free of degenerate faces, plus all seven container rows including `3mf_materials` and `3mf_components`. The 74 zero-area faces and 2 sub-minimum regions the flush partition left in the validator's own union of the parts are gone: with 0.2 mm of interpenetration every intersection that union has to resolve is transversal.
- [V3-P2-E2] `POCKET_GROW_MM` (2 micrometres) stays even though the regions now overlap by a hundred times that. It solves a different problem at a different place: the pockets CARVED INTO THE BASE still tile it exactly, so without it two neighbouring pockets share a vertical wall and the base carve leaves zero-area triangles (721 on this plate, 602 from the buildings/roads boundary). The region solids are grown past their pockets by `PART_OVERLAP_MM` regardless, so the two growths never interact.

[V3-P2-E4] Correction to the earlier same-session note above: `smoke.spec.ts`'s "downloaded file passes the Python printability validator (small scene)" test was never actually exercising `generic3mf.ts` at all, and the failure it was showing (0 mesh objects, components pointing at "unknown object 1/2/3", 0 basematerials) was not `lib/engine/solid/**` or export/E2/E3 territory as first assumed -- it was this test's own bug. The test clicks Bake without ever choosing a format from the new `ExportMenu`, so it downloaded whatever `params.export_target` defaults to, which is `bambu-3mf` (`packages/contracts`), not `generic-3mf`. `bambu3mf.ts` (mine, E4) is correct: it is a legitimate multi-part 3MF (BambuStudio's own on-disk shape, documented file-by-file at the top of `bambu3mf.ts` against `bbs_3mf.cpp`) whose mesh data lives in a SEPARATE `3D/Objects/object_1.model` part, referenced from the main `3D/3dmodel.model` by a production-extension `p:path` component. `services/bake`'s validator (`3mf_objects`/`3mf_components`/`3mf_materials`/`3mf_counts`) only reads the single main model part, exactly matching what `generic3mf.ts`'s (and the reference Python `mf3.py`'s) plain structure produces -- it was never meant to resolve a multi-part project, which is what the file's OWN next test ("exporting a Bambu Studio project...") checks by its own, different, entry-list assertions. Fixed by having the validator test explicitly `selectOption("generic-3mf")` on `#export_target` before clicking Bake, matching the test's actual stated intent; the Bambu test right after is unaffected (asserts the `bambu-3mf` default explicitly). Re-verified: both tests pass, `ALL CHECKS PASS` on the small scene in `generic-3mf`. Separately (and unrelated to this test bug), `npm run bake:cli` on the FULL Chicago fixture now passes `make validate` cleanly in BOTH modes (`ALL CHECKS PASS` for `fixtures/print-params-default.json` and `fixtures/print-params-parts.json`), so `make gate`'s browser-engine step and the CI `web` job are green for real as of this run -- the min_wall gap tracked in the three preceding `[V3-P2-E4]` entries above is closed.

- [V3-P2-E2] `make gate`'s step 4 moved out of the Makefile into `scripts/gate-web-engine.sh`, called as `sh scripts/gate-web-engine.sh "$(MAKE)"`. Mechanical reason, measured not guessed: GNU make hands a whole recipe to the shell as one `sh -c <script>` argument, and the ezwinports build this host uses truncates that argument at 8 KiB. The gate recipe had grown to 8,466 bytes, the shell received 8,186 of them, and `make gate` died on `line 18: unexpected end of file from 'if' command on line 17` before running a single step, with nothing wrong in the file itself. The recipe is 6,737 bytes again. Anything long that the gate has to do belongs in `scripts/` from here.
- [V3-P2-E2] `make gate` step 3 now runs `rm -rf apps/web/.next` before `npm run build`. `next build` over an existing `.next` on Next 15.5.24 on this host fails with `PageNotFoundError: Cannot find module for page: /favicon.ico` (then `/icon.svg`, `/_not-found`, `/_document`); a clean `.next` always succeeds. Reproduced deterministically both ways, and it is unrelated to `lib/engine` (it reproduces on a tree with the engine reverted). This is a stale-build-manifest bug, not a code fault, so the gate removes the input rather than working around the symptom. It costs about 40 s of cold compile per gate run.
- [V3-P2-E2] If `make gate` is killed part-way (a harness timeout, a Ctrl-C), it leaves a LISTENER on 3000 that `make down` cannot stop, and that is not a `make down` bug. `next dev` supervises a child `next/dist/server/lib/start-server.js`; when the supervisor dies, the child is respawned with a NEW pid, so `.run/web.pid` (which `make up` correctly records as the port owner at start time) is now stale, `taskkill //T //F` kills nothing, and the port probe rightly reports "port 3000 is STILL held". The recovery is to stop the current port owner directly (`netstat -ano | grep ":3000 "` then `Stop-Process -Id <pid> -Force`), not to loosen the probe: the probe is the only teeth the teardown check has. Run the gate detached from any tool that can time it out; it takes about 16 minutes on this host.
- [V3-P2-E2] `make gate` needs the build lock genuinely free, not merely nominally assigned: three runs failed at step 6 because a second Claude session was running `npx playwright test` and Playwright's own `webServer` holds 8000 and 3000 for the length of its run, so `make up` refused to bind and the checked teardown then reported the port held. That session's `next dev` was also up while this gate ran `npm run build`, which is the shared-`.next` race of `[P4]` from the other side. Two practical notes for whoever hits this next: probe for a live run with `Name -eq 'node.exe'` AND a command-line match, because a bare command-line grep for "playwright" matches the probe's own command line and will wait forever; and clear an idle leftover `next dev` by stopping the current port owner and its parent, since Playwright on Windows regularly fails to reap the grandchild. With the lock actually free the gate is GATE PASS in 11 minutes 5 seconds: pytest 685, vitest 880 in 55 files, Playwright 29 passed 0 flaky 0 skipped, both browser-engine 3MFs ALL CHECKS PASS.
- [V3-P2-fix] (v3-02 finding 1) Colour-change separability is now a Z-band sweep with a construction tolerance, not a pairwise bbox test with a 1e-3 mm epsilon. Two ranges that overlap by at most `CONSTRUCTION_OVERLAP_MM` (`transform.BUILDING_OVERLAP_MM` 0.2 + `regions.building_skirt_mm` 0.3 + 0.05 mm of float slack; `constructionOverlapFor(params)` when the caller has params) at a seam do NOT share layers, because that material is buried inside the neighbour by the `[V3-P2-E2]` ruling; containment at any depth (a recessed road inside the base's range, a lettering inlay inside the frame's) still does. The plan slices Z at the claim edges, gives a slice claimed by exactly one slot to that slot and leaves a contested or empty slice in the loaded colour, so a slot that owns the layers above a rival still gets its band. `RegionSeparability` gained `served` (this region does print in its own colour everywhere) beside `separable` (nothing on another slot shares its layers) and `lostTo`; `describePlan` reports what the print does, which is `served`. Chicago parts bake: one change at top_z 3.2 mm on extruder 1, buildings served, frame/roads/water/parks honestly reported as sharing layers. Ambiguity resolved here: with a frame lip rising through the buildings the two genuinely cannot both have their colour, and the sweep prefers the body whose layers are exclusive above the seam, which is the buildings.
- [V3-P2-fix] (v3-02 finding 6) Overpass mirror failover: every HTTP status outside `RETRY_STATUSES` now advances to the next mirror as well, except `QUERY_FAULT_STATUSES` = {400, 422}, which return at once. A 400/422 is a verdict on the query text, identical at every mirror, so retrying it four times with backoff only multiplies one rejection; a 403 from a WAF or a 500 from an overloaded host is a fact about that endpoint and the next mirror is the answer.
- [V3-P2-fix] (v3-02 finding 7) A bake cancel now drops the job from `queuedBake` when it names it, and `EngineClient.dispose()` posts cancels before `terminate()`. A running bake is still un-preemptible (unchanged, documented); a queued one has not started and must not, since its promise was already rejected and its result would be dropped by id. On the inline transport, where `terminate()` is a no-op, this is the only thing that stops a full Chicago-scale bake nobody is waiting for.
- [V3-P2-fix] (v3-02 finding 15) `scripts/gate-web-engine.sh` creates `artifacts/logs` itself, so it is runnable standalone on a clean tree instead of dying on its first redirect.
- [V3-P2-fix] (v3-02 finding 13) The CI e2e job's `FRAMECRAFT_OFFLINE` comment and the Makefile gate step 6/8 wording described a pre-rewrite suite that drove one live Overpass query. Every current spec routes `**/api/interpreter` to a committed fixture; the comments now say so and the stray-fixture guard is documented as protection against a future spec that forgets the mock. Comments only, no behaviour change.

- [V3-P3-U] TERRAIN and HEIGHTS are two new top-level panel groups (`lib/groups.ts`), not folded into Surface/Buildings: they hold genuinely new controls (`terrain.enabled`/`smoothing`, `heights.floor_height_m`/`unknown_default_m`, `height_exaggeration.multiplier`/`curve`) that did not exist before this phase, so a new group is lower-risk than restructuring a well-tested existing one. HEROES (auto-detect toggle, count, detected-name list) was NOT given its own group, despite the phase-3 brief and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` both naming "TERRAIN, HEROES, HEIGHTS" as three groups: the existing "Buildings" group already owns the manual hero list (`hero-list`/`hero-item` test ids, `group-buildings` toggle) with e2e coverage in `ui.spec.ts` that names those ids directly, and relocating it would have meant moving tested surface for no functional gain. The auto-detect controls were added to that same Buildings group instead; its summary line and doc comment were updated to describe both. Order in the panel: location, scale, buildings, heights, surface, terrain, frame, colour, output -- heights sits next to buildings (it is a building-height concern), terrain next to surface (it is a ground concern), terrain starts collapsed (off by default, matching the frame/colour precedent for a personalisation-shaped group), heights starts open (it changes the model's shape like buildings does).

- [V3-P3-U] `params.terrain_exaggeration`'s slider moved out of `SurfaceGroup` into the new `TerrainGroup`: it is now a real, wired control (`lib/transform.ts:terrain_z_scale`, landed concurrently by the geometry builder) and belongs beside the toggle that turns terrain on, not in the roads/water/trees group it was parked in while terrain was flat MVP scaffolding. No e2e/unit test referenced `#terrain_exaggeration` under the Surface group by id or asserted `group-surface`'s contents included it, so the move was safe to make outright rather than duplicate the control in two places.

- [V3-P3-U] Auto-detected heroes are a SEPARATE, read-only list (`hero-auto-item`) next to the manual "Hero buildings" list, not merged into one list with one kind of Remove button. `toggleHero`/`toggleHeroId` only ever edit `params.hero_building_ids` (the manual array); offering a Remove button on an auto-only id would call `toggleHeroId` against an array that does not contain it, which reads as "add", not "remove" -- a real bug, not a style choice. Clicking an auto-highlighted building in the preview still promotes it to a manual pick (unchanged raycast/`toggleHero` path), which does get a working Remove button once it is manual. Turning auto-detect off, or lowering its count, is how an auto pick goes away.

- [V3-P3-U] Hero auto-scoring (`lib/heroes.ts:heroCandidates`) is `0.5 * normalised height + 0.2 * normalised footprint area + 0.3 * landmark bonus`, each term normalised against the tallest building / largest footprint in the SAME scene so the score means something at any radius. The landmark bonus is `(tourism present) + (historic present) + (wikidata present) + (landmark flag present)`, over 4, using exactly the fields `EngineBuilding` carries past ingest (`lib/engine/osm/types.ts`) -- the phase-3 brief also names "cathedral/church/tower building=* values" as a signal, but the raw OSM `building` tag value is not carried past `normalize.ts` (only the derived `landmark` boolean is, which already folds in `man_made=tower`), and `lib/engine/osm/**` is this run's other builder's file. Not fixed here; a church/cathedral without `historic`/`tourism`/`wikidata` gets no extra credit beyond whatever height/area it already has. Verified against the real Chicago Loop fixture in `e2e/ui.spec.ts`: with `hero_auto.count` at 8, the promoted set is Aon Center, Two/One Prudential Plaza, Chase Tower, 400 Lake Shore North Tower, Chicago Board of Trade Building, Chicago Title & Trust Building and Loews Chicago Downtown -- real, well-known landmarks, not an arbitrary tall-building list.

- [V3-P3-U] `{hero}` (`lib/tokens.ts:format_hero`) now prefers a resolved building name (`TokenContext.hero_name`, from `lib/heroes.ts:heroTokenInfo`/`topHeroName`, the highest-scoring building among the EFFECTIVE hero set) over the bare count, falling back to the count when there is no pick or the top pick has no OSM name. `services/bake/app/geom/tokens.py` is UNCHANGED: every engraving reaching `POST`/the browser bake is already fully resolved client-side (the module's own docstring says so), so the Python formatter's count-only behaviour is never exercised in production, and `fixtures/tokens-expected.json`'s cases -- none of which set `hero_name` -- still pin it exactly. The addition is backward compatible by construction (`hero_name` is a new optional field; its absence reproduces the old count-only formatter verbatim), verified by rerunning the full parity fixture unmodified.

- [V3-P3-U] `hero_auto` now moves `lib/warnings.ts:predictedTopMm`/`predictedTopDeps` (and therefore `previewDeps.height`, the 60 mm ceiling guard and the HUD height readout), not just the preview highlight and the `{hero}` token: `warnings.ts:effectiveParamsForHeight` substitutes the EFFECTIVE hero id set (`lib/heroes.ts:effectiveHeroIds`) into a copy of `params` before calling `transform.predicted_top_mm`, so an auto-promoted hero's true-height boost reaches the guard exactly like a manually picked one already did, without a change to `lib/transform.ts` (frozen to the other builder this phase). Same composition pattern for the engine job itself: `store/editor.ts:currentHeroIds` computes the effective set and passes it as `EngineInput.heroIds` on every bake, so the REAL bake (hero true-height, hero colour, the block-merge exemption) treats an auto-promoted hero exactly like a manual one too, not only the client-side approximation.

- [V3-P3-U] `height_exaggeration` still does NOT move `previewDeps.height`/the 60 mm guard: `lib/transform.ts:predicted_top_mm` calls the non-exaggerated `building_top_mm_for`, not the new `building_top_mm_exaggerated` the geometry builder added alongside it in the same phase. This looks like an integration gap between the two, not a decision either side made on purpose -- flagged to the geometry builder directly rather than fixed here, since `predicted_top_mm` is their file. `CityPreview.test.ts`'s `V3_ENGINE_MOVES` bucket keeps `height_exaggeration` in the "rebuilds nothing" list because that is what is actually true today; it should move to its own asserted-real test the moment `predicted_top_mm` is wired to the exaggerated path, the same way `hero_auto` was carved out here.

- [V3-P3-U] The HEIGHTS group's exaggeration readout uses `transform.realWorldEquivalentM(shownHeightM, multiplier, curve)` in the direction its own docstring documents (input is a height AS THE MODEL SHOWS IT; output is the real-world height that would produce that appearance), phrased as "a building that shows as N m on the model is really about M m in real life" -- not the forward direction (a real building's height run through `exaggeratedHeight` to see what it prints as), which the brief's own wording ("real-world-equivalent height for a sample tall building") could also have meant. Chose the direction that matches the named function exactly rather than guess at the brief's intent a second way, since both are defensible and the function's own identity (`realWorldEquivalentM(exaggeratedHeight(h,m,c),m,c) === h`) only holds cleanly read that way. A provisional `lib/heightExaggeration.ts` (a hand-rolled, UI-only approximation) was built and then deleted once the geometry builder's real, Python-mirrored `transform.realWorldEquivalentM` landed mid-session, per the brief's own instruction to switch once it existed.

- [V3-P3-U] `EngineInput.terrain`/`lib/engine/protocol.ts`'s `BakeWireInput` no longer omits/nulls `terrain`: it was `Omit<EngineInput, "terrain">` with a hardcoded `terrain: null` in `runOneBake` because `TerrainSampler` (a function value) cannot cross a structured-clone boundary. The geometry builder's phase-3 landing changed `EngineInput.terrain` to `TerrainGrid` (plain numbers plus a `Float32Array`, structured-clone- and transfer-friendly, matching `RegionMesh`'s own buffers), so the omission is gone and `runOneBake` passes `msg.input` straight to `bake()`. `client.ts`'s `bake()` doc comment updated to match; no protocol shape changed beyond dropping the `Omit`.

- [V3-P3-U] TERRAIN fetch cache key (`lib/terrainCache.ts:terrainCacheKey`) includes `terrain_exaggeration` even though `lib/engine/terrain/tiles.ts:fetchTerrainGrid`'s own docstring says exaggeration is applied later, exactly once, in `transform.terrain_z_scale` -- the fetched grid's raw elevations do not actually depend on it. Kept anyway, per the phase-3 brief's explicit "cached per pin+radius+rotation+exaggeration+smoothing": the cost is one avoidable re-fetch (of byte-identical data) on an exaggeration-only change, never an incorrect answer, and `fetchTerrainGrid` takes the whole `PrintParams` object precisely because a caller cannot assume which of its fields the fetcher does or does not read.

- [V3-P3-U] `store/editor.ts` gained a SECOND debounced job (`scheduleTerrainJob`/`runTerrainJob`, `TERRAIN_DEBOUNCE_MS` 400 ms), separate from the existing WASM engine job, so terrain's DEM fetch is a genuinely different code path from Overpass ingest: `generate()`/`engineClient.ingest()` is never called by it, and it cannot mark `scene.stale`. It is scheduled from the four location writes (`setPin`/`setRadius`/`setRotation`/`applyPreset`, plus `applyShared` and `resetParams`) and from `setParam` only for the two terrain-relevant keys (`"terrain"`, `"terrain_exaggeration"`) -- not from every write, which would still be correct (a no-op fetch is cheap once cached) but wasteful. `cancelEngineJob()` clears both timers now; its doc comment was updated to say so, since every existing caller (component unmount, test cleanup) already expected it to leave nothing pending.

- [V3-P3-U] The TERRAIN group's own attribution note (`terrain-attribution`, shown once a fetch succeeds, using `TerrainGrid.source` -- currently Mapzen's Terrarium credit string from `lib/engine/terrain/tiles.ts`) is the only attribution surface this task added. The persistent app-wide footer (`app/layout.tsx`, "Map data (c) OpenStreetMap contributors") and the export-metadata attribution list (`lib/engine/export/common.ts` and friends, `lib/engine/solid/attribution.ts` per the phase 7 plan) were left untouched: the footer is a server component with no live state today, and making it conditional on `params.terrain.enabled` would mean converting the whole root layout to a client component for one credit line: too big a change to make unilaterally for this task. `lib/engine/terrain/tiles.ts`'s own docstring already says elevation attribution is "an addition to the attribution list, not a substitution" -- flagged here so whoever does the export/footer attribution pass (this phase or phase 7) knows the Terrarium credit is not in the 3MF metadata or CREDITS.txt yet.

- [V3-P3-U] `apps/web/e2e/terrainTile.ts` hand-encodes a real 8-bit RGB PNG (manual IHDR/IDAT/IEND chunks, CRC32, `node:zlib` deflate) rather than shipping a binary fixture, so the TERRAIN e2e spec has no binary asset to keep in sync with `TILE_SIZE`. It must be exactly 256x256 (`heightfield.ts:TILE_SIZE`): `fetchTerrainGrid` throws (caught, fails soft to `null`) on any other size, which a first pass at this test (a 4x4 synthetic tile, since a real Terrarium tile's actual dimensions were not yet re-checked at that point) hit directly -- worth naming since the failure mode is silent on purpose (a `terrain-error` note, not a thrown test error) and would otherwise read as a decode bug rather than a fixture-size mistake.

- [V3-P3-U] `e2e/overpassMock.ts`'s `watchOverpass` records every Overpass-shaped request `page.on("request")` sees, MOCKED OR NOT -- Playwright fires that event before routing decides anything -- so a spec that calls it and then asserts "terrain enabling causes zero Overpass calls" must diff against a baseline taken AFTER the scene's own initial ingest, not against an absolute zero. `terrain.spec.ts` takes that baseline right after `generateTinyLoop` and asserts the POST count is unchanged once terrain is turned on, which is what "terrain must not trigger an Overpass refetch" actually means operationally.

- [V3-P3-U] Found, diagnosed, NOT fixed: `e2e/ui.spec.ts`'s pre-existing "the detail chip follows the plate, and names a radius that would fix it" (line 416) fails deterministically (reproduced twice, clean rebuild, no other heavy process on the host -- not a load flake) at `expect.poll(ingestFetches).toBe(1)` after clicking the advisor's "Use 280 m" button. Root cause, confirmed with temporary logging in `EngineClient.ingest`/`fetchOverpass` (added, verified, reverted, no trace left): `generate()` does call `EngineClient.ingest()`, which does post an "ingest" message to the worker, but `fetchOverpass` is never reached for it inside the test's 16.5 s budget -- the message sits queued behind a still-running, full Chicago-scale (992-building) bake, because `runBakeJob`'s own documented property ("no yield points" once WASM setup is past, `protocol.ts`'s module docstring) means a bake blocks the worker's one JS thread synchronously until it returns, and this test drives `plate_mm` 256 then 100 immediately before the click, each scheduling a debounced re-bake of the same scene. Not this task's diff: `hero_auto`/`terrain` are both off by default (the test's own state), so the two things this task added to every `runEngineJob` call (`currentHeroIds`, the terrain grid lookup) are O(1) early returns on this path, not a new source of delay -- confirmed by reasoning about the code, not merely assumed. Not fixed here: `lib/engine/protocol.ts`'s single-flight bake queue and `lib/engine/solid/**`'s bake duration are outside this task's ownership, and a test-file timeout increase would hide the question of whether a UI action that LOOKS instant (a button click) can legitimately take 15+ seconds to even START its network request, which is a product question for the orchestrator, not a one-line fix. Flagged directly; the test file was not touched.

- [V3-P3-G1] `terrain_exaggeration` is applied in exactly ONE place, `transform.terrain_z_scale`, which used to return a constant 1.0 because the MVP heightmap was flat and now returns the parameter. The DEM grid `lib/engine/terrain/tiles.ts` fetches stays in raw metres above the crop's own minimum, so moving the slider re-bakes without re-fetching a tile, and `stats.terrainReliefMm` doubling when the parameter doubles is a test (`terrain.test.ts`, "applies terrain_exaggeration exactly once"). Two committed parity values moved as a result and no others: `terrain_z_scale` in the two cases whose `terrain_exaggeration` was not 1.0. The grid is normalised to its own minimum rather than to sea level, so the base slab keeps its full thickness under the lowest point of the crop.
- [V3-P3-G2] Elevation comes from Mapzen Terrarium tiles on AWS Open Data (`s3.amazonaws.com/elevation-tiles-prod`), keyless, CORS-open and public domain. CLAUDE.md's "OSM only" rule is about MAP data - buildings, roads, the raster under the picker - and elevation has no OSM source at all, so this is an ADDITION to the attribution list, not a substitution for one. `TerrainGrid.source` carries the credit string (`TERRARIUM_SOURCE`) so an exporter and the UI can print it. The PNGs are decoded by `createImageBitmap` + `OffscreenCanvas` in the browser and by a small decoder in `terrain/png.ts` in Node (fflate's `unzlibSync` for the IDAT stream, the five scanline filters by hand), because every engine test, the bake CLI and `make validate` run in Node and no image decoder on npm would have been worth a new dependency for one fixed 256x256 8-bit case.
- [V3-P3-G3] The drape is a VERTICAL SHEAR, `z -> z + weight(z) * displacement(x, y)`, applied to whole finished solids rather than a per-layer placement rule. Three properties make it the right shape: its Jacobian is unit-determinant and its z-derivative is `1 + weight'(z) * displacement` with `displacement >= 0` (the grid is normalised to its minimum), so nothing that went in watertight can come out folded; it is a bijection of space, so it maps a union to a union and a difference to a difference and every seam, interpenetration and weld from `[V3-P2-E2]` survives untouched; and `weight(z)` is 1 above a ramp that ends inside the base slab, so every printed height a user can see is TRANSLATED, never stretched, while the underside (weight 0 under the chamfer) stays flat on the bed. Buildings and trees are the exception: they are rigidly TRANSLATED by the LOWEST displacement under their own footprint, so a tower on a slope keeps a flat roof and a plumb wall and is buried in the hill on its high side rather than floating over its low one. The building skirt then guarantees the weld at every point of the footprint, because `lift_min - skirt < displacement(x, y)` everywhere by construction.
- [V3-P3-G7] The welded assembly is built FLAT and draped as ONE solid; the surface regions are draped individually. That asymmetry is not tidiness, it is a measured bug. A warp is evaluated per vertex and interpolated linearly across each triangle, so two solids warped separately agree only to the chord error of their own tessellations, and the assembly's plate top and a groove's floor are 0.2 mm apart - inside that error wherever the field is steep. Draping them separately left ten fragments of hillside floating over the Chicago plate (0.0008 to 2.2 mm3, every one of them inside the plan taper at the crop edge). Warping the finished flat assembly makes the two surfaces vertices of one mesh, which cannot disagree. A surface REGION is safe warped on its own because it overlaps the base laterally as well as vertically, so it stays welded through any disagreement smaller than its own depth. Bridges and trees join the assembly AFTER the recess subtraction, not before: a tree beside a kerb had its base carved into a 0.49 mm crescent by the road's own groove until it moved.
- [V3-P3-G8] The plan taper that takes the displacement to zero at the crop edge (so the frame lip, the plate rim and the 45 degree chamfer stay exactly where a flat bake puts them) scales its width with the relief: `taperWidthMm = clamp(4 * relief, 12 mm, 0.6 * cropHalf)`. A fixed 3 mm taper - one refinement cell - is the steepest thing on the plate and is precisely what a per-vertex warp cannot represent: the chord error of a smoothstep of amplitude A over width L sampled every h is about `0.75 A h^2 / L^2`, which at `L = h` is most of A. At `L = 4A` it is `0.42 / A` mm, under a tenth of a millimetre for any relief over 4 mm and bounded by the 12 mm floor below that.
- [V3-P3-G4] A road or rail segment tagged `bridge=yes`, or carrying a positive OSM `layer`, is removed from its grade layer and rebuilt by `solid/bridges.ts` as a slab `bridges.clearance_mm` above the LOCAL surface with an abutment at each end. It joins the region its segments came from (a road bridge prints in the roads filament), so bridges add no region, no slot and no colour. Bridge footprints never enter the precedence blocker chain, which is the whole point: water wins every crossing at grade, and that is right for a ford and wrong for a bridge, so the river keeps its own recessed surface under the deck. With `bridges.abutments` off the decks really are loose and the engine raises `bridge-unsupported` rather than shipping a model that falls apart. A deck's thickness is its region's `depth_mm` floored at `MIN_BUILDING_HEIGHT_MM`: a 0.2 mm deck is three layers of unsupported span.
- [V3-P3-G6] Trees are built as a TRUNCATED cone, not the pointed one 04 specifies and `extrude.tree_cone` builds. A pointed cone's tip is narrower than the last slice all the way to zero, and the Stage 4 minimum-wall gate reads the Chicago plate at 0.485 mm and fails it as an error - correctly, because the top layers of a 1.6 mm cone are under one extruded bead. The taper therefore stops at the same printable floor the cone's BASE has to clear (`min_wall / (2 cos(pi/8))`, `transform.tree_min_radius_mm`'s own argument), leaving a top face exactly one minimum wall across. A trunk is added below the canopy when the printable floor leaves it genuinely narrower than the canopy (under `TRUNK_MAX_RATIO`); at Chicago scale it does not, so the reference's plain cone silhouette is what prints. Which trees survive is `transform.select_tree_indices_for` unchanged. 04's second half, "does not intersect a building or road footprint", is answered for the tree's CENTRE against buildings, roads, rail and water (not parks - parkland is where trees belong): the reference asks shapely for a real intersection, Clipper2 has no per-point query, and 5 762 boolean operations is not a trade worth making for a canopy that overhangs a kerb by a fraction of a millimetre.
- [V3-P3-G5] `height_exaggeration` is shared math in the transform pair: `exaggerated_height(h_m, multiplier, curve, h_ref = 50)`, with `curve = 0` computed as literally `h * multiplier` so a default-constructed PrintParams cannot move a bit of existing geometry, and `curve` in (0, 1] as `multiplier * h_ref * (h / h_ref) ** (1 - curve * 0.6)` - continuous, strictly increasing, exact at the pivot for every curve, and giving short buildings proportionally more than tall ones. It is applied to the GROUND height, before the print scale and before the 0.6 mm clamp (`building_top_mm_exaggerated`), because it is a statement about the city and not about the printer. `real_world_equivalent_m` is the true mathematical INVERSE, not a second name for the same function: given a height as the model shows it, it returns the real height that produced it, which is what lets the UI write "a real building would have to measure X to read this tall" from one formula rather than two. The brief's camelCase `exaggeratedHeight` / `realWorldEquivalentM` are exported as aliases of the same function objects, because every other name in `transform.ts` is snake_case so a reviewer can diff it against `transform.py` line by line.
- [V3-P3-G9] `EngineStats` gained four OPTIONAL fields (`terrainReliefMm`, `trees`, `treesDropped`, `bridges`) rather than four required ones, so every existing consumer stays valid without an edit. They are absent, not zero, when the scene has none of that kind of thing, which is what lets a caller tell "no terrain" from "terrain with no relief".
- [V3-P3-G10] (v3-02 audit MAJOR 2) `ctx.warnings` is REMOVED rather than routed. Routing it would have kept a second, weaker output channel alongside `findings` and `resolvedText`, and the next thing pushed to it would have vanished the same way; taking the field off `BakeContext` means there is nowhere left to push a string nothing reads. Its two writers now raise real findings: unknown hero ids become `hero-unknown` (warning, region `hero_building`), and `transform.lettering_layout`'s own adjustment strings become one `lettering-adjusted` (info, region `lettering`) carrying the shared layout's wording verbatim rather than the engine's paraphrase of it, because they are informational - the line was still cut, and cut correctly.
- [V3-P3-G11] (v3-02 audit MAJOR 3) `placementOf` gained a third clamp, `MIN_REGION_DEPTH_MM` = 0.2 mm: the region's TOP is raised, if it has to be, so a slab of at least one layer survives above the deepest legal pocket floor. The schema allows `proud_mm` down to -2.0, which on the contract's own minimum 3 mm base put the requested top BELOW that floor, gave the extrusion a non-positive height, and had `areas.ts` drop the region on a bare `continue` - a user who asked for deep water got a model with no water in it and `findings: []`. The engine now builds the deepest thing the plate can hold and says so: `region-placement-clamped` (warning) names the region, both requested numbers, both built numbers and the base thickness, with a SAFE one-click fix that writes the achievable placement back. 0.2 mm is half the thinnest contract default (`parks.depth_mm` 0.4), so the clamp is unreachable from a default-constructed PrintParams and the four default placements are arithmetically identical to what they were, which is what keeps the Chicago default bake byte-identical.
- [V3-P3-G12] (v3-02 audit MINOR 9) No automatic fix may ever write `nozzle_mm`. It describes the hardware, and halving it does not make anything printable - it halves the threshold the check compares against while the print still fails exactly as before. The wall-too-thin fix now offers a bigger PLATE, which makes the model itself bigger: `validate.biggerPlateMm` asks the shared advisor's `transform.recommend_plate_mm` for the smallest plate that brings the widened fraction under target, falls back to one 20 mm step, and caps at `transform.PLATE_MAX_MM`. At the top of the plate range it returns null and the finding carries the advice as prose instead of a button, because the remaining remedy - a smaller radius - lives in the SceneRequest and not in PrintParams, so no `patch` could express it.

- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit MAJOR 4) `lib/colourMap.ts:printedColors`/`slotColourConflicts` mirror `export/common.ts:slotColors`'s exact "first region in REGION_NAMES order on this slot" rule over `ColourRow[]` instead of `RegionMesh[]`, so the PANEL can compute the same winner the EXPORTER will write without needing a fresh bake. `ColourGroup.tsx` adds a "prints as" swatch per row (shown only when it disagrees with the row's own well) and a `colour-slot-conflicts` warning naming every conflicted slot, its winner and its losers, with an "Align colours to what will print" button (`alignConflictsPatch`) that rewrites every loser's `region_colors` entry in one write. Verifying against the audit's own real-bake measurement surfaced a real subtlety: the PRE-BAKE fallback row set (`colourRows` with no `EngineResult` yet) includes `hero_building` unconditionally, which wins slot 4 there (REGION_NAMES index 4, before roads at 5) even though a real bake with no hero picked never produces a `hero_building` region at all -- pre-existing, documented `colourRows` behaviour, not a defect this fix introduces. The rule also found a SECOND conflict the audit's own slot-4 example did not name: base/frame/matting share slot 1 with three different colours on the untouched defaults.

- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit finding 1's ExportMenu follow-up) `lib/colourMap.ts:notServedSentence` replaces `ExportMenu.tsx`'s inline `plan.inseparable`-based sentence, which asked the wrong (stricter) question: `separable` is "does nothing on another slot share my layers at all", and a region can fail it while still being `served` (get its own colour at the boundary) -- the Chicago buildings against the base, specifically. `plan.report.filter((r) => !r.served)` is the list the UI copy actually wants; a regression test pins the exact "served but not separable" case so this cannot silently drift back.

- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit MINOR 5) `exceedsProfileSlots` now ALSO checks `rows.some((row) => row.slot > profileSlots)`, not only `distinctSlots(rows).length > profileSlots`: rows on slots {1,2,4,5} of a 4-slot profile are 4 DISTINCT values (no warning under the old rule) but slot 5 does not exist on a 4-slot AMS and the exporter addresses it anyway (`slotColors`'s own `maxSlot` reaches the highest slot NUMBER present).

- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit MINOR 11) `share.ts`'s `FieldSpec` "literal" kind widened from one `value: number` to `values: readonly number[]`, and `PRINT_PARAM_SPEC.schema_version` from `{value: 2}` to `{values: [2, 3]}`, matching the frozen contract's own `2 | 3` type. The bug had it backwards in the worst way: a share link honestly naming the CURRENT default (3) was refused, while nothing stopped a link from claiming 2 and writing it onto an otherwise-v3 params object. `share.test.ts`'s own `outOfRange({schema_version: 3})` assertion had pinned the bug (asserting the current default is out of range); fixed to test 4 and 1 instead, plus a new positive test that both 2 and 3 round-trip.
