- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit MINOR 12) Restored `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`'s Chicago-scale validator assertion, cited by the audit as closed by DECISIONS [V3-P2-E4] -- but re-running it against the LIVE tree (not the audit's own snapshot) shows it is open again and worse (`min_wall` 0.039 mm vs the 0.72 mm floor, `degenerate_faces` 50, `bodies` 12 disconnected; the closing entry had 0.561 mm / 0 / 1). `lib/engine/solid/**` was under active concurrent edit at the same time this was measured (base.ts/buildings.ts/roads.ts/areas.ts modified, new bridges.ts/drape.ts/trees.ts), which is the more likely explanation than a second independent regression; flagged to that builder with the full validator output rather than guessed at further, since diagnosing WHICH concurrent change is responsible needs `lib/engine/solid/**` context this task does not have. The new test (generic-3mf, color_mode parts, full Chicago, mirroring `make gate`'s own `fixtures/print-params-parts.json` combination) is left in place and RED, not weakened or skipped, because that is the honest state of the tree it ran against; the happy path's own bambu-3mf download stays a structural-only check regardless of this, since the reference validator's `trimesh` loader cannot resolve Bambu's multi-part structure at all, a permanent format limitation unrelated to this gap.

- [V3-P3-U] (v3-02 audit MINOR 14) `CityPreview.test.ts`'s `V3_ENGINE_MOVES` bucket comment already got the exact reword this finding asks for, incidentally, while landing the phase 3 `hero_auto` UI work (`docs/handoff/v3-03-ui.md`) -- before this audit note was read. It no longer says "the engine has not landed"; it says the twelve remaining groups still rebuild nothing in THIS file's sense because `previewDeps` describes the client-side approximate preview only, which the real engine job (reruns on every `setParam` regardless of key via `scheduleEngineJob`) does not feed. `hero_auto` itself was carved out into its own asserted-real test in the same pass, for an unrelated reason (`lib/warnings.ts`'s effective-hero-id composition), which happens to be a live instance of exactly the staleness this finding warns could be reintroduced.

- [V3-P3-U] `EngineClient` runs ingest and bake on two entirely separate `Worker` instances (`ingestTransport`/`bakeTransport`), never one shared between them, fixing a real product defect: `runBakeJob`'s own documented "no yield points" property means a worker running a bake is unresponsive to ANY other message posted to it -- including an ingest -- until the bake returns, so a UI action that should be instant (the detail advisor's "Use N m" button, a pin move) could sit queued behind a still-running, full-Chicago-scale bake for however long that bake took (measured exceeding 15 s in a real browser). `worker.ts`/`protocol.ts` needed no change at all: both already dispatch by message kind, and a worker dedicated to one kind now simply never receives the other -- the fix is entirely in which worker `EngineClient` posts to, not in what a worker does once a message arrives. Constructor changed from one `Transport` parameter to `EngineClientTransports { ingest?, bake? }`; the zero-arg `createEngineClient()` callers (`store/editor.ts`, the only one) are unaffected. Transport-level error handling is now scoped per worker: an ingest-worker crash no longer incorrectly rejects a bake in flight on the unrelated bake worker, and vice versa. The alternative the team lead also offered -- terminate and respawn the bake worker whenever a newer ingest or bake supersedes a running one -- was not taken: it would have discarded genuinely useful in-flight bake work every time an unrelated ingest arrived, and repeatedly paid WASM reinitialisation cost the separate-worker design never incurs at all (the ingest worker never receives a "bake" message, so it never loads manifold3d).
- [V3-P3-U] `lib/engine/client.supersede.test.ts` (new): proves the client-level half of the fix above -- that `EngineClient.ingest()` never waits on a bake promise, running or superseded-while-running -- through the in-page fallback transport (no real `Worker` exists in vitest) and a mocked, artificially SLOW `bake()` (a real `await`ed `setTimeout` delay, not a synchronous CPU spin, so the single-threaded test runner can still observe the race between the two promises). The WORKER-level half (a real `Worker` running manifold3d genuinely cannot process a second message until it returns, which Node cannot reproduce at all) is proven instead by `e2e/ui.spec.ts`'s pre-existing "the detail chip follows the plate, and names a radius that would fix it", left completely unmodified and now passing in ~9 s against a real browser (was timing out past 15 s). A separate file from `client.test.ts`, not a new describe block in it: `vi.mock("./engine", ...)` is file-scoped but still replaces `bake()` for every test in whichever file calls it, and `client.test.ts`'s own "really runs a bake end to end" tests need the real engine.

- [V3-P3-U] Follow-up to the v3-02 audit MINOR 12 entries above: the geometry builder found and fixed the real cause. `services/bake`'s `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` (what every prior `lib/engine/solid/**` test baked) carries no `bridge`/`layer` tags at all; the SAME location ingested live through the app's own Overpass path has 780 elevated ways in the Loop, so the deck/abutment construction's coincident-face and near-tangency defects were invisible to the whole existing test suite, not merely untriggered by one fixture. Re-run of `docs/handoff/v3-02-audit.md` finding 12's restored assertion, both directly (`npm run bake:cli`) and through the real `e2e/smoke.spec.ts` UI -> download -> CLI pipeline: `min_wall` 0.7275 mm (a real if narrow margin above the 0.72 mm floor), `degenerate_faces` 0, `bodies` 6 parts / 491 shells / union 1. ALL CHECKS PASS.

- [V3-P3-U] `ColourGroup.tsx`'s "prints as" swatch (MAJOR 4's fix) is a `<span>` carrying `aria-label`; a plain span's implicit role is `generic`, which the ARIA spec does not permit an accessible name on at all -- axe's `aria-prohibited-attr` (serious) on all four disagreeing swatches in the default colour table. Fixed with `role="img"`: a colour swatch conveying visual-only information is exactly what that role is for, and `img` does support a name via `aria-label`. Caught by `e2e/a11y.spec.ts`'s full run (all four states, both themes), which now passes with the swatches visible (Chicago generated, colour group open).

- [V3-P3-U] A real bug found and fixed while investigating a supposed regression in `e2e/share.spec.ts`'s "a copied link restores the whole editor in a fresh browser": the restored hero-item read "unnamed building" instead of the picked landmark's real name. Root cause, confirmed by running the test directly with tracing rather than assumed: at the point the test's own design checks the restored hero (BEFORE clicking Generate, which `store/editor.ts:applyShared`'s own contract never fires automatically -- verified against the SAME test's step 4, "opening a shared link fetched a scene by itself" must stay false), there is genuinely no `SceneGraph` for `lib/heroes.ts:heroDisplayName` to resolve a name from -- not a bug in the lookup, a fact about what data exists at that moment. `heroDisplayName`'s "unnamed building" fallback was written for "we have a scene and this building genuinely has no name tag"; showing the SAME string for "we have never looked, because there is nothing to look in yet" is a different situation reading as an identical, and wrong, one. Fixed at the real cause: `BuildingsGroup.tsx`'s hero rows now show the RAW building id (the one thing the share link actually carries, and what a manual pick showed before hero names existed) whenever there is no scene at all, and fall through to `heroDisplayName` (name, or a real "unnamed building") the moment one exists -- which the same test now also asserts explicitly, re-running Generate and confirming the restored hero resolves to the IDENTICAL name the original browser context showed for the same building. The test's own `heroId` capture was changed from reading the row's visible text (which, since phase 3's hero auto-detect, is a resolved NAME once a scene exists, not an id -- the test predates that feature) to reading the Remove button's `aria-label`, which has always spelled the id out verbatim regardless of what the row displays.
- [V3-P3-G13] The committed `fixtures/chicago-scene.json` cannot exercise phase 3's elevated geometry at all: it comes from the Python service, which carries no `bridge`/`layer` tags and no rail layer. The scene the APP bakes, built by `lib/engine/osm`, has 780 elevated ways in the Chicago Loop, and every bridge defect below was invisible on the committed fixture and immediate on that one. `apps/web/scripts/bake-cli.ts` therefore grew `--overpass`, and `engine.test.ts` now bakes the ingested scene as well as the committed one. Five separate defects, each found by measuring rather than by reasoning, each a variation on "a boolean across a coincident or near-tangent face leaves zero-area triangles": (1) a deck ribbon at exactly `min_wall` measures 0.794 mm because a 16-gon join's flats are 2 percent narrower than its diameter, so a deck is built at `DECK_MIN_WIDTH_FACTOR` = 1.25 of a wall - a deck IS the wall, where a grade road is a groove and the wall around it is base; (2) an abutment disc of the ribbon's half width intersects the ribbon in a lens that tapers to nothing, 0.393 mm; (3) a square clipped back to the deck shares the deck's own walls, 720 degenerate faces; (4) a square flared to 1.2 of the deck width leaves its walls 0.1 mm from the deck's at a junction, 167; (5) taking the deck's shape and growing it put the footing's wall back on the buildings the deck had been cut against, 80 faces and eleven zero-volume shells in the clearance band. What works is a DIAMOND - a square turned 45 degrees, so its edges can never be parallel to a street on a north-south grid, which is what the Loop is - of the deck's nominal width, clipped to the crop and to nothing else, with its foot at `abutmentFootMm` rather than the obvious `base_top - 1 mm` (which is 2.0 mm on a 3 mm base and exactly `areas.solidBottomMm` for the grade roads: two formulas landing on the same plane, and the last degenerate face on the plate).
- [V3-P3-G13b] A deck is held `PART_OVERLAP_MM` CLEAR of the buildings rather than cut flush against them or cut flush and grown back. It is the one layer that is raised AND shares a Z range with the towers it passes, so both of the constructions the rest of the engine can choose between are wrong for it: cutting flush leaves coincident vertical faces (460 degenerate faces in the validator's union of the parts), and cutting flush then growing back by the seam overlap - what every recessed region does through `areas.fittedSolid` - leaves the grown lip hanging in the air wherever the building is shorter than the deck, which measures 0.205 mm and fails the minimum wall. A 0.2 mm air gap between a viaduct and a wall gives neither, and is below the print resolution that would make it visible.
- [V3-P3-G14] A deck that cannot be grounded is DROPPED and counted, never shipped floating. `groundedOnly` decomposes the finished bridge solid and keeps only bodies whose lowest point is below the base top, which is exactly "has an abutment": a slab starts at `base_top + clearance` and a column starts below `base_top`. One `decompose` and a bounding box each is the cheapest connectivity question that can be asked. On the Chicago Loop one of the 780 ways ends inside a building footprint and is reported through `bridge-unsupported`. A segment missing from a viaduct is a smaller lie than a slab floating a millimetre over a river, and it is the difference between a model that validates and one the reference validator fails on `bodies`.
- [V3-P3-G15] `drapeSolid` simplifies at `DRAPE_SIMPLIFY_MM` (one nanometre) after warping. `refineToLength` splits every edge of an already-intricate mesh, and where an existing edge was already shorter than the print grid the split leaves a sliver the warp then flattens further. The tolerance is a thousandth of the smallest deliberate feature in the engine (`POCKET_GROW_MM`) and four orders below the print grid, and `simplify` keeps a subset of the existing vertices and moves no surface further than that, so nothing a printer or a validator can measure moves. The one consequence a test can see is that a region and the assembly, simplified separately, may disagree about a vertex by half a nanometre; `terrain.test.ts` asserts to that tolerance rather than to an arbitrary six decimals.
- [V3-P3-G16] The Stage 4 min-wall gate measures a DRAPED model differently from a flat one, and it has to. Two changes, both live only when `ctx.terrain` is non-null, so no flat bake samples a single extra height and the committed golden's numbers cannot move. (1) `sliceHeights` picks feature PLANES - the mouth of a groove, the floor of a recess, just under the base top - and draping smears every one of them over a band as tall as the relief, so a fixed set of heights samples each feature at one arbitrary point of its own range. `drapedSliceHeights` walks the union of those bands at a 0.05 mm pitch, capped at `MAX_DRAPED_SLICES` because each slice is an erosion of a 40 000-vertex section. (2) The bigger gap was the width rule itself: the engine measured `inscribedWidthMm`, the widest disc that fits in a slice region, while the reference validator reports `thicken.narrowest_width`, which is that number LOWERED by the inscribed width of every appendage the `0.45 * min_wall` opening leaves behind. On a flat bake the two agree because Stage 1 strips or widens every thin appendage in 2D and there are none left to find; on a draped bake a level slice is an OBLIQUE cut through those same footprints and grows wings the 2D repair never saw. `narrowestWidthMm` mirrors the reference rule, behind the same cheap `4A/P` pre-filter `widenThinParts` uses, and `residueParts` was exported from `repair.ts` so the gate and the repair cannot disagree about what an appendage is. Measured on a 60 m-relief Chicago: the engine reported 0.800 mm (saturated, no finding) and now reports 0.096 mm across 2 regions, against the reference validator's 0.146 mm - stricter than the validator, which is the safe direction. The draped bake costs 8.5 s against 6.0 s flat; the flat path is untouched and still byte-identical.
- [V3-P3-G17] The `wall-too-thin` finding carries a COUNT and a terrain-aware remedy. "0.096 mm somewhere" cannot tell one sliver at the crop edge from every groove on the plate, so `MinWallReport.thinRegions` counts them the way the reference validator does, once per thin region per slice. When terrain is active the finding says why a level slice through a hillside reads narrower than the model is built, and its one-click fix HALVES `terrain_exaggeration` (safe, reversible, and the only remedy that attacks the mechanism) rather than offering a bigger plate, which helps but does not. The optional repair-time mitigation the brief allowed - scaling `min_wall`/`min_gap` by a slope factor when terrain is on - was deliberately NOT taken: the wings are produced by the oblique CUT, not by the footprint the repair works on, so widening footprints does not remove them, and shipping an unvalidated threshold change on the draped path would trade a measured limitation for an unmeasured one.
- [V3-P4] Ceiling ruling (2026-09-01): the model height ceiling is the active printer profile's max height (resolveProfile), per the brief's "drive that ceiling from the profile". The v1 flat 60 mm cap survives as the DEFAULT custom profile's max_height_mm (contract default changed 250 -> 60), so default behaviour and every existing test hold, while picking a real printer honestly raises the ceiling ("of 250 mm" on a P1S). The sidecar carries max_height_mm and the Python reference validator's bounding-box row follows it (default 60).
- [V3-P4-U] `lib/warnings.ts:heightCeilingMm` is currently `Math.min(T.MAX_HEIGHT_MM, resolveProfile(params).maxHeightMm)`, an INTERIM implementation, not the [V3-P4] ruling above (which this agent had not yet seen when it shipped): the contract's `custom_profile.max_height_mm` default is still 250 on disk (`packages/contracts/schema/print_params.json`, `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts`, `services/bake/app/contracts.py`, all checked, none regenerated) at the time of writing, so a straight `resolveProfile(...).maxHeightMm` replacement would raise the DEFAULT ceiling from 60 mm to 250 mm for every session that never opens the Printer group -- exactly the regression [V3-P4] itself rules out by dropping that default to 60. `min()` reproduces today's exact behaviour (`min(60, 250) === 60`) and is safe to ship regardless of when the contract regenerates, but it will keep clamping every NAMED profile's ceiling to 60 mm even after the contract lands, which is wrong per the ruling ("picking a real printer honestly raises the ceiling"). Flagged to the orchestrator; `min()` -> straight `resolveProfile(...).maxHeightMm` is a one-line flip in `heightCeilingMm` plus two test expectations in `warnings.test.ts`'s `heightCeilingMm` describe block, held until `custom_profile.max_height_mm`'s default actually changes. `lib/engine/solid/validate.ts:maxHeightMm(ctx)` (p4-engine's file) has the identical `Math.min(T.MAX_HEIGHT_MM, custom)` pattern and needs the same flip, so the UI's block/readout and the engine's own `exceeds-height` finding cannot disagree about which model is too tall; p4-engine was told directly.
- [V3-P4-U] `store.applyFinding`/`applySafeFindingFixes` call `lib/engine/audit/fixes.ts`'s real `applyFix`/`applySafeFixes` (p4-engine's file, landed mid-task) rather than a local reimplementation: this agent had already written one (a shallow `{ ...params, ...patch }`, since every fix patch on `AuditFinding` is a self-contained partial `PrintParams`) before discovering `fixes.ts` existed, and dropped it in favour of the real one the moment it was found -- deep-merge over nested objects (`tiling`, `colour`, ...), a `FORBIDDEN_FIX_KEYS` guard (no fix may ever write `nozzle_mm`), and a `FixChange[]` report the UI did not have to invent its own version of. `lib/issues.ts` re-exports both functions rather than wrapping them, so there is exactly one implementation in the tree.
- [V3-P4-U] The Issues badge (`components/editor/IssuesBadge.tsx`) is ADDITIVE next to `AdjustmentsChip`, not a replacement or a merge into one widget, despite the phase 4 brief's "the Issues badge stays the single warnings surface" line. Reason: `AdjustmentsChip`'s category grouping (site/repair/bake) is named directly by three existing e2e specs (`a11y.spec.ts`, `lettering.spec.ts`, `ui.spec.ts`) and its own vitest suite (`lib/adjustments.test.ts`); folding severity-grouped, fix-capable engine findings into that same component and drawer would mean rewriting its grouping scheme and every one of those call sites, which is out of this task's remaining budget and risks weakening tests that currently pass to make a rewrite fit. `IssuesBadge` is the new, real surface for everything actionable (severity chips, fix buttons, "Auto-fix all safe issues", built on `lib/issues.ts:mergeIssues`, which DOES implement the brief's merge rule -- client `SceneWarning`s folded with engine `AuditFinding`s, engine wins a shared id); `AdjustmentsChip` keeps the passive remarks it already owned. Two floating badges, not one. Flagged to the team lead for a follow-up consolidation if a single literal widget is still wanted.
- [V3-P4-U] `lib/printers.ts:profileApplyPatch` writes the SMALLER of a named profile's two bed dimensions into `plate_mm` (a square print), clamped to `plate_mm`'s own `PARAM_RANGES` (not the profile's raw numbers, which can exceed it -- the H2S's 340 x 320 mm bed against a 256 mm contract ceiling). Applied once, on `printer_profile` selection itself (`store.setPrinterProfile`), never re-applied on a later `plate_mm`/`nozzle_mm` move; selecting `custom` writes only the id, restoring nothing.
- [V3-P4-U] Of the seven export targets, only `bambu-3mf`/`color-change-3mf` are tile-aware today (`lib/engine/export/bambu3mf.ts`, one plate per tile, p4-engine's work); `generic-3mf`, `stl`, `stl-parts-zip`, `obj` and `step` still export the whole assembled model in one piece regardless of `params.tiling`. `components/editor/ExportMenu.tsx`'s tiled-export note says exactly this (checked against every exporter file, not asserted from the brief's own "one multi-plate Bambu project or a zip of per-tile files" wording, which does not hold for the STL/OBJ/STEP targets as shipped) rather than promising a per-tile zip for formats that do not produce one; a per-tile STL/OBJ/STEP export is real future work, not a placeholder claimed here.
- [V3-P4-U] `lib/warnings.ts:predictedTopDeps` (== `previewDeps.height`) now also lists `params.printer_profile` and `params.custom_profile?.max_height_mm`. Neither moves `predicted_top_mm` itself -- only `heightCeilingMm`, what the number is compared AGAINST for the OUTPUT panel's ceiling text, the HUD tone and the preview's too-tall pill, moves with them -- so this is the one honest exception to "every dep here is something the prediction reads": `warnings.test.ts`'s own closed-set check only asserts the direction that matters (a dep that DOES move the prediction must be listed) and does not fail on an extra, honest one. `CityPreview.test.ts`'s `V3_ENGINE_MOVES` "rebuilds nothing" bucket had `printer_profile`/`custom_profile` moved out into their own asserted-real test (`rebuilds only the predicted height...`), mirroring the existing `hero_auto` pattern in the same file.
- [V3-P4-U] A real, pre-existing layout bug, found only by actually running `e2e/print.spec.ts` against a real browser rather than trusting a static review: `ParamPanel.tsx`'s right-sidebar layout is `<flex column> [header] [scrollable group list, flex-1] [Output section, shrink-0]`, and `shrink-0` really does mean shrink-0 -- if the Output section's own content needs more height than the sidebar has, it does not compress, it overflows the sidebar's bottom edge and squeezes the scrollable group list to a visible height of zero, making every group's toggle unclickable (`getByTestId('group-printer-toggle')` / `getByTestId('group-colour-toggle')`, both: `<section data-testid="group-output">...intercepts pointer events`, a 300 s Playwright click-retry storm before it gives up). Confirmed to already exist before this phase's own additions (`StatsCard` and the resolved-output panel alone are enough to trigger it on the default 1280x720 viewport once a real bake has produced content), and made measurably worse by phase 4's own additions (`EstimateCard`, the Issues badge feeding an extra adjustments-style chip) stacking more content into the same unbounded section. Moving `EstimateCard` inside the existing `showResults` collapse (so "hide results" can reclaim the space) was necessary but insufficient, because `showResults` defaults to true -- the failure reproduces in the product's own DEFAULT state, not only after a user expands something. Real fix: `OutputPanel.tsx`'s results `<div id={resultsId}>` (bake status, the estimate card, the resolved-output panel, the stats card -- everything that can grow) is now `max-h-[45vh] overflow-y-auto`, scrolling internally once it is taller than that; the action row, the predicted-height line and the block-reason note stay OUTSIDE the cap, so Bake is never itself scrolled out of reach. Verified: `e2e/print.spec.ts`'s four tests, previously failing with exactly this symptom, now pass in 25.8 s total (were timing out at 300 s each beforehand).

- [V3-P4-E1] The printability audit is ONE catalogue, `lib/engine/audit/rules.ts`, and `auditPrintability` assembles the whole of `EngineResult.findings`: the solid pipeline raises what needs a live WASM handle (is this region a manifold, how many bodies has it, did the lettering refuse a line) and this module raises everything measurable from the finished meshes, then orders the list error-first and de-duplicates it. Rulings inside it: the OVERHANG limit is 50 degrees FROM VERTICAL, a constant and not a parameter (a plumb wall is 0 degrees and a bridge is 90; 45 to 55 is where Bambu Studio, PrusaSlicer and Cura all default, and the audit's job is to describe the model rather than to let the model be re-described until it passes); downward faces whose highest vertex is within 0.5 mm of the bed are not counted, because the plate is under them; the finding is a warning above 2 percent of the model's surface area and an info above 0.5, both area-weighted, and it carries NO fix because no parameter removes an overhang - the detail names the causes present in this model (bridge decks, terrain, tree canopies, a floating frame) instead. `exceeds-profile-plate` judges the model against the ACTIVE profile's raw bed with no margin (a 180 mm model on a 180 mm bed does print, and saying otherwise would be false); the 5 mm per side margin appears only in the REMEDY, where it buys the tile a skirt. The remedy is a smaller plate while that costs under a quarter of the model's size, and `tiling` beyond that (`PLATE_SHRINK_LIMIT` 0.75), so both branches are reachable and the choice is the one a user would make.
- [V3-P4-E2] The estimate is rough and says so: `EstimateResult.isEstimate` is always true and `caveat` is the sentence the UI shows beside the figures. Constants: PLA at 1.24 g/cm3, 1.75 mm filament, 0.2 mm layers, 8 mm3/s of flow (a 0.4 nozzle at 0.2 mm layers and 100 mm/s), 4 s of fixed cost per layer, and travel as plan area over 40 000 mm2/s. The one calibration knob is `MATERIAL_FRACTION` = 0.55, the extruded volume as a fraction of the model's own: a slicer lays perimeters and sparse infill, not a solid, and this is what puts the default Chicago plate at 3 h 32 min inside the brief's 3 to 6 hour window (172 463 mm3 of model, 117.6 g, 39.4 m of filament, 174 layers). Volume comes from `EngineResult.merged` and never from the sum of the regions, which double-counts every 0.2 mm seam (+5.18 percent on this plate); per-SLOT volumes are summed from the regions, because there is no other way to attribute volume to a filament, and then scaled by the single factor that makes them add up to the merged volume. How the volume is spread between layers cannot change the total, so it is not modelled.
- [V3-P4-E3] `fix.safe` means two things and both are required: the fix cannot make any other finding worse, AND it throws away nothing the user asked for. The second half is not pedantry - "Auto-fix all safe issues" applies these without asking, and silently deleting someone's trees or bridges is not something to do unasked, however cleanly the result validates. So the island fix ("turn off the tree markers") and the text fix ("set this line to 3.10 mm") are offered and never applied automatically, while the slot clamp and the terrain-exaggeration halving are safe. No fix may ever write `nozzle_mm` at any depth (`[V3-P3-G12]`); `applyFix` refuses such a patch with a `skipped` entry rather than throwing, because a patch arriving from a stale share link must not take the editor down, and `rules.test.ts` sweeps every rule's patch for it. A text refusal only offers a size when growing the line WOULD cut it, which is answered by re-running the same shared layout that refused it at the candidate size rather than guessed: a line that "does not fit the edge even at the smallest legal 1.5 mm" also reports a `min_size_mm`, and a button that made it bigger would fail in exactly the same way with a longer error.
- [V3-P4-E4] Tile joints are PRISMATIC IN Z, so tiles mate by being lowered onto each other, which is the only assembly direction a plate lying flat has. A dovetail is a trapezoid in PLAN (6 mm at the neck, flaring 1.5 mm a side, 5 mm deep, reaching 0.5 mm back into its own tile so the union with the tile is transversal rather than a coincident face); it locks the pair against being pulled apart in either direction along the bed, which is how a wall-hung plate is loaded. A pin is a cylinder on the cut normal, 4 mm across but CLAMPED to fit inside the base slab with 0.6 mm of material above and below - a 4 mm pin does not fit a 3 mm base, and a pin that broke out through a face would be a hole rather than a joint. The male key always belongs to the lower-indexed tile of a seam (west of a vertical cut, south of a horizontal one), so no tile is all sockets. The brief's "two per edge metre" for pins is honoured as a density with a floor of two per seam and a ceiling of eight; at this product's sizes (a seam is 90 to 170 mm) the density never binds and the floor always does, which is right - two keys is the smallest number that stops the tiles rotating about a single one. Dovetails get three times the pin density for the same reason. Tiles are labelled by grid reference, column letter from the WEST and row number from the NORTH, so "A1" is the top-left tile seen from above; the index is engraved mirrored into each tile's underside through the same `fit_text` / `glyphAreas` / `repairText` machinery every other piece of text goes through, at the underside mark's own size, face and depth.
- [V3-P4-E5] A tiled bake is written one of two ways, and which one is decided by what the receiving software can do with a second bed. A Bambu Studio project holds many PLATES in one file, so a tiled bake for a Bambu printer is one .3mf with one plate per tile. Everything else - a third-party printer, a generic 3MF, an STL, a colour-change project (whose plan is for ONE printed object) - becomes a zip of per-tile files named by grid reference plus CREDITS.txt. The plate positions are Bambu Studio's own arithmetic, read off `src/slic3r/GUI/PartPlate.cpp`: `plate_stride_x/y = size * (1 + 1/5)`, origin `(col * stride_x, -row * stride_y)`, `cols = compute_colum_count(n)` from PartPlate.hpp. That arithmetic is load-bearing and not cosmetic: NOTHING in the 3MF assigns an object to a plate. `PartPlateList::reload_all_objects` walks the plates and gives each instance to the first one whose build volume its bounding box intersects, so an object is on plate N because it stands on plate N's patch of the world; the `<plate>` blocks in `model_settings.config` carry the plate's settings and identify ids and move nothing. The single-plate file is byte-identical to what the writer produced before the multi-plate rewrite, verified against `git show HEAD:...bambu3mf.ts` on the real Chicago bake and pinned by a SHA-256 in `export/tiles.test.ts`.
- [V3-P4-E6] An interior cut is MOVED to the cleanest line within 3 mm of where the grid puts it. A cut through a city is not a cut through a block of material: it passes through grooves, kerbs and walls, and where it lands a hair from one of them it leaves a rind of plate thinner than the nozzle can lay - measured, the nominal centre line of the Chicago plate landed 0.33 mm east of a street groove's wall and the reference validator failed the tile on it. `snapCut` scores candidate lines by their distance to the nearest boundary the model has at the heights the minimum-wall gate samples, and takes the one closest to nominal among those that clear a full wall. Three millimetres is 3 percent of a 90 mm tile, too little to unbalance the grid and more than the width of the features it has to clear. A key's TIP is a cut face too and gets the same treatment.
- [V3-P4-E7] Snapping is not enough and cannot be: scanning every line within 3 mm of the Chicago centre, the BEST of them still shaved 2.4 mm2 of material into fins under the nozzle width (the nominal line shaved 25). So what the cut leaves too thin is measured and REMOVED, and the removal asks exactly the question the reference validator asks - `checks.min_wall`, which is a region's inscribed width lowered by the width of every appendage the `0.45 * min_wall` opening leaves hanging off it. Three wrong answers preceded it, each worth recording. (1) `section - opening(section)` marks every CORNER of every building, because a disc cannot reach into a right angle; shaving them removed 2 000 mm3 of the plate and left razor edges measuring 0.004 mm. (2) An opening built from POLYGONAL discs does not reproduce a straight edge, so the same difference carries a hairline fringe along every edge in the band - tens of thousands of strips a few nanometres wide, and 57 000 degenerate faces once subtracted. `repair.residueParts` avoids both: it offsets with MITRE joins, which are exact on straight edges, and applies the repair's own area floor, and using it means the removal and the Stage 1 repair cannot disagree about what a wing is. (3) The cutter must NOT be clipped back to the section: clipping puts its wall exactly on the model's wall wherever the sliver reached the surface, and a boolean across a coincident face leaves zero-area triangles (2 523 of them, measured). It is grown 0.15 mm past the material instead, so every face of it crosses the model transversally. Two further properties the final version needs: a whole island the cut left behind is its own slice region and is under the residue noise floor, so it is caught by a separate per-component test; and the passes must be CUMULATIVE across bands, because the cutter is one prism through the whole model - a shape found at one height is removed at every height, and what it leaves behind at ANOTHER height is where the next thin edge appears. The search runs entirely in 2D on bands around the cut lines (the model is sliced once, not once per tile), which took the 2x2 Chicago bake from 56 s to 7.8 s. The cost of the whole scheme is stated to the user as an info finding, `tile-seam-trimmed`, with the volume in it.
- [V3-P4-E8] `solid/validate.ts`'s height ceiling now comes from `printers.resolveProfile` rather than from `custom_profile` alone, so a named printer's own usable height is honoured instead of ignored; `audit/rules.ts` adds `exceeds-profile-height` for the band between a profile's limit and 04's own 60 mm product ceiling. The anonymous "the model is not one connected piece" finding is replaced by `islandReport`, which decomposes the assembly once (a cost the connectivity check was paying anyway), calls the largest body the model, attributes each loose one to the region it shares the most volume with, and lets `audit/rules.ts` say which region came away, how much of it there is, and whether it even reaches the bed.
- [V3-P4-E9] The model's Z ceiling is the ACTIVE printer profile's usable height, `resolveProfile(params).maxHeightMm`, and it is NOT `min`ed with 04's 60 mm figure (team lead's ruling, raised by p4-ui). The ceiling is a property of the machine: a P1S has 250 mm of gantry and refusing a 90 mm model on it because the reference implementation's product ceiling says 60 would be wrong. Every existing number holds because the contract's own `custom_profile.max_height_mm` default becomes 60 (p4-ui's schema change) and `printer_profile` defaults to `custom`, so a default-constructed PrintParams still resolves to 60. Three places had to agree and now do: `solid/validate.ts:maxHeightMm` (the `exceeds-height` finding's number), the editor's own readout and warning, and the reference validator's bounding-box row. The last one is reached through the FILE: `export/common.ts` writes the resolved ceiling into the bake sidecar as `max_height_mm`, and `services/bake/app/cli.py:_max_height_from_sidecar` reads it back and passes it to `validate(..., max_height_mm=)`, which defaults to `MAX_HEIGHT_MM` when it is absent, unreadable or not a positive number - so a file with no sidecar, or one written before this existed, is judged exactly as it always was. `audit/rules.ts`'s own `exceeds-profile-height` was DELETED rather than kept alongside: once `validate.ts` measures against the profile there is one ceiling and one finding, and two rules reporting the same millimetre with different wording is how a user learns to distrust both.
- [V3-P4] Orchestrator landed the custom_profile.max_height_mm 250 -> 60 contract change directly (schema $def and parent default object, make contracts, FRAMECRAFT_WRITE_PARITY fixture regen, test expectations) after the grant to p4-ui kept racing its message queue while the engine side had already shipped the straight resolveProfile ceiling, leaving a live default-ceiling divergence (UI 60, engine 250). pytest 198 and vitest 26 green on the contracts suites.
- [V3-P4-U] p4-ui side of the above, closed out: `lib/warnings.ts:heightCeilingMm` flipped from the interim `Math.min(T.MAX_HEIGHT_MM, resolveProfile(params).maxHeightMm)` to the straight `resolveProfile(params).maxHeightMm`, matching `validate.ts` and the ruling, the moment `apps/web/lib/contracts.ts`'s regenerated `custom_profile.max_height_mm` default (250 -> 60) was observed on disk. `warnings.test.ts`'s `heightCeilingMm` describe block and `printers.test.ts`'s one stale `toBe(250)` assertion updated to match (now reads the live contract default rather than a literal); `e2e/print.spec.ts`'s P1S test now asserts "of 250 mm", the brief's own original wording, and passes end to end (25.8 s for the four `print.spec.ts` tests, `FRAMECRAFT_WEB_MODE=prod`, verified against a real browser, not assumed). Full `npx vitest run`: 1167 passed, 0 failed. `npx tsc --noEmit`/`npm run lint`: clean.
- [V3-P4-U] `ExportMenu.tsx`'s tiled-export note, corrected on p4-engine's own review: it originally treated `bambu-3mf` and `color-change-3mf` alike ("one plate per tile") and everything else as "the whole model, not split". Neither half matched `lib/engine/export/index.ts:exportForTarget`'s real dispatch (`[V3-P4-E5]`), which had grown a per-tile ZIP path for every target since this note was first written: the single multi-plate FILE route is `target === "bambu-3mf" && profile.vendor === "bambu"` alone (a colour-change project is a plan for ONE printed object and cannot become several plates of one; `bambu-3mf` chosen on a non-Bambu profile is meaningless as a multi-plate file), and every other combination -- including `color-change-3mf` on a real Bambu printer -- is a zip of per-tile files, one file per tile. The note now reads the same condition the dispatcher itself branches on (`resolveProfile(params).vendor === "bambu"`) rather than guessing a second time, and its wording matches the real bake's own `ExportOutput.notes[0]` text verbatim so the predictive copy and the post-bake note can never read differently. `e2e/print.spec.ts`'s tiling test now selects a real Bambu printer (P1S) before asserting the single-file wording, since the default `custom` profile's tiled export is a zip regardless of `export_target`.
- [V3-P5-C] `lib/tint.ts:hashUnit`'s FNV-1a fold originally XORed the seed into
  the initial hash state AND prefixed it onto the text being hashed; for
  short building ids the seed digit's own char code cancelled the initial XOR
  on the loop's first iteration, so `seed=7` and `seed=8` produced the SAME
  draw (caught by `tint.test.ts`'s "differs across ... seeds", not by eye).
  Fixed by folding the seed in only through the text (`${seed}|${id}`), never
  also into the initial state.
- [V3-P5-C] The contrast checker (`lib/contrastCheck.ts`) compares PRINTED
  colours (the slot winner, `lib/colourMap.ts:printedColors`), never the raw
  `region_colors` entry, AND skips any adjacent pair that shares one filament
  SLOT outright: two regions on the same slot print identically by
  construction (a single-filament choice already surfaced by
  `slotColourConflicts`'s own "Align colours to what will print" warning),
  and flagging it as a "contrast" problem would tell the user to fix
  something no colour choice can fix without giving the pair its own slot.
  FrameCraft's own frozen `colour.region_colors` default relies on the
  same-slot skip for base/frame/matting (all slot 1) but still carries one
  GENUINE clash outside it: `buildings` (slot 2) and `base` (slot 1) share
  the identical nominal hex `#D8D3C6`, inherited from the v1/v2 seven-part
  `part_colors` table's "the base shares with the buildings" pairing, which
  made sense there (no slot indirection) but is a real, different-slot clash
  under v3's own metric. Left as-is (a schema-frozen contract default, not
  this task's file to change) and documented rather than silently exempted;
  `contrastCheck.test.ts`/`palettes.test.ts` assert it explicitly instead of
  assuming a clean baseline.
- [V3-P5-C] `colour.preview_theme` (dark/light) is scoped to a
  `data-fc-viewport-theme` attribute on the canvas's own wrapper div
  (`app/globals.css`'s `[data-fc-viewport-theme="..."]` rules), never to
  `.dark` on `<html>` -- that class is the APP's own theme (`ThemeToggle`),
  a separate switch. Only four tokens are viewport-scoped
  (`--fc-viewport-bg/sky/bounce/grid`, read by
  `components/scene/palette.ts:readViewportPalette`); every other preview
  colour (building/road/water fills in the pre-bake instanced fallback)
  still follows the app theme, matching the brief ("background and ground
  shading only"). No exporter or engine code path reads
  `params.colour.preview_theme` at all -- it is not part of `EngineResult`,
  `RegionMesh`, or any exporter's input type -- so "exported bytes never
  change with it" holds structurally, not by convention; pinned by
  `colourMap.test.ts`'s "preview-theme isolation" suite (regionColor/
  regionSlot/colourRows identical across both values) and
  `e2e/colour.spec.ts`'s stats-card comparison across a live theme flip.
- [V3-P5-C] Building tint (`colour.tint`) and the height gradient
  (`colour.gradient`) are preview/OBJ-only by construction: neither reaches
  any print-path export target (`bambu-3mf`, `generic-3mf`, `stl`,
  `stl-parts-zip`, `color-change-3mf`, `step`), only `obj` (its MTL groups
  carry per-instance colour). `lib/tint.ts:tintIsPreviewOnly` gates the
  standing note honestly against the ACTIVE `export_target`, not a blanket
  "always show" -- `lib/warnings.ts:tintPreviewOnlyWarning` feeds it into the
  Issues badge as one `info` entry. Pending `EngineResult.buildingTints`
  reaching real geometry from p5-frame's mesh work (the type landed
  concurrently with this task, `BuildingTint[]`), the fast instanced preview
  (`InstancedBuildings.tsx`) reads it when present and falls back to this
  module's own deterministic per-building draw otherwise, so tint shows
  immediately on toggling it rather than waiting on a bake either way.
- [V3-P5-C] Playwright's `.fill()` on a native `<input type="color">` does
  not reliably drive a React-controlled value in this app: the DOM attribute
  updates but the visible value snaps back on the next render because the
  event that reached React was not the one `fill()` fired (or the update
  raced the very next re-render). A first fix drove the swatch through a
  small helper (native `HTMLInputElement.prototype` value setter plus real
  bubbling `input`/`change` events) instead; this WAS reliable across
  several standalone local runs, including the Blueprint and custom-palette
  tests that still use edits reached the same way through a button. It was
  NOT reliable inside a full `make gate` run under real system load: the
  contrast-checker test hung the full scaled budget with the panel still
  showing the pre-edit colour, while every other test in the same file (and
  suite) passed -- a genuine environment-dependent gap in the raw-dispatch
  path specifically, not a product defect (`colourRows`'s own live-result
  preference, unchanged, explains the LATENCY before a real edit shows, not
  an edit that never lands at all). `e2e/colour.spec.ts`'s contrast-checker
  test now seeds a one-off custom palette straight into `localStorage`
  before the page loads (`page.addInitScript`) and applies it with the same
  reliable button click the other two palette tests already use, so nothing
  in this suite drives a native colour input directly any more; the helper
  was deleted rather than left unused. Separately, still true and worth
  keeping: `ColourGroup.tsx`'s swatches are sourced from `colourRows`, which
  prefers the live `EngineResult` the moment one exists
  (`[V3-P2-integration]`'s own rule) -- so ANY colour edit, by a real user or
  by the e2e suite, only reaches the visible swatch once the debounced
  rebake THAT EDIT ITSELF scheduled has actually landed, never on the state
  write; every colour-panel e2e assertion waits for the stats card to clear
  its "(previous computation)" label first rather than reading a swatch
  immediately after an edit.

- [V3-P5-F1] Frame profiles are STACKED RINGS, not tapered extrusions. A `frame_style.profile` is a cross-section swept around the rectangle, and the only construction that sweeps one correctly at a corner is a stack of `CrossSection` rings at graduated inset: offsetting a ring mitres its own corner, and it works for a rounded corner as well as a square one. manifold3d's `extrude(..., scaleTop)` was the alternative and cannot be used here, because it scales about the ORIGIN and therefore moves a ring's inner edge exactly as much as its outer one, which is not what any of these profiles does. Curved and sloped profiles use `PROFILE_SLABS = 8` slabs, each inset by the profile at its OWN TOP so the stack is inscribed inside the true surface and never proud of it; `stepped` is two slabs by definition and `plain` is one. `plain` + `square` keeps the pre-phase-5 code path verbatim (one ring, one extrusion), so a default bake is byte for byte what it was: Chicago single and parts, 172 463 mm3, 100 278 triangles, min_wall 0.8746 / 0.9096, ALL CHECKS PASS, every number unchanged. The profiles themselves: `chamfer` cuts a 45 degree face over the top 30 per cent of the lip height (0.6 mm of 2 mm); `stepped` is an outer tier at 60 per cent of the height over the outer half of the width, which is the literal reading of the brief and leaves the full-height flat top on the INNER half; `bevel_in` slopes the inner edge at 45 degrees over `min(height, 0.6 * width)`; `bullnose` rolls the outer top edge over a quarter circle of radius `min(height, width/2)`; `ogee` is a raised cosine S over 40 per cent of the width; `floating` lifts the inner 1 mm of the lip 0.4 mm off the plate and turns the shadow-gap channel on. The frame's flat TOP FACE (`frameTopFaceSection`) is now what lettering, ornaments and texture are clipped to, instead of the full 6 mm band: a profile that eats into the band would otherwise carry ink over its own void. The shared layout still sizes text to the full band (`transform.edge_band_mm` knows nothing about profiles), so a profile that narrows it raises `frame-text-band-narrowed` with both numbers rather than silently clipping a letter.

- [V3-P5-F1b] The mesh cleaner's weld is now a LADDER (`solid/mesh.ts` `WELD_LADDER_MM = [1e-6, 1e-5, 1e-4]`), each rung tried from the ORIGINAL mesh and the first that clears every degenerate face kept. Measured cause: the chamfer + rounded-corner bake left exactly one triangle of 1.3e-10 mm2 at the frame's rounded inner corner whose longest edge is 3.8e-5 mm, which is real geometry to a 1 nm weld and nothing at all to a printer, and 04 stage 4 requires zero faces under 1e-9 mm2. The reference implementation welds on a ladder for the same reason (`assemble.WELD_DIGITS`). Every rung is still subject to the existing acceptance test (closed, oriented, volume preserved to a nanolitre), and a mesh that arrives with no degenerate face still returns on the untouched early path, so the default bake cannot move.

- [V3-P5-F2] Frame corners map onto three DISTINCT geometries through the offset join type, with `square` staying exactly what it was: `square` is the sharp rectangle (built from plain rectangles, no offset at all, so the default is byte-identical); `mitred` offsets by `corner_radius_mm` with Clipper2's `Square` join, which cuts the corner back at 45 degrees, which is what a mitred frame corner looks like; `rounded` uses the `Round` join at the same radius. The BAND keeps its area under all three, because what a treated outer corner takes off the outside its matching inner corner gives back inside, which is exactly right: the frame stays 6 mm wide the whole way round, and what moves is the corner itself (measured in a 1.5 mm probe at the plate corner: 2.25 mm2 sharp, 0.83 mitred, 0.55 rounded). The PLATE outline is left square in every case (`base.ts` untouched): a rounded frame therefore sits on a square plate with a small flat shelf at each corner, which reads as deliberate, and rounding the plate as well would need its 45 degree elephant-foot chamfer, which is built by a uniform scale about the origin, to be rebuilt for a change nothing in the brief asks for. The shadow gap and the matting band follow the frame's corner style with the radius travelling in with the edge, so a rounded frame's channel and mat stay concentric with it.

- [V3-P5-F3] The city crop gives way to the frame's neighbourhood: `solid/context.ts` subtracts `transform.frame_content_inset_mm(params)`, the shadow gap's width plus the matting's, from `content_extents_mm`. Zero at the defaults, so nothing moves for an ordinary bake; on the ogee + gap + matting run it took the Chicago crop in by 7 mm, which shows as 602 merged buildings instead of 720 and 162 507 mm3 instead of 172 463. `content_extents_mm` ITSELF is untouched and the new helper is TS-only: the reference service does not build v3 frame styling, and leaving the shared function alone is what keeps `fixtures/parity-expected.json` meaning what it meant. The shadow gap is refused, with both numbers, when its depth would breach the base floor the whole pipeline shares (`context.MAX_POCKET_FRACTION`, half the base), which is the same guard a surface region's placement is clamped by rather than a new one. Matting is its own region, `matting`, already in the contract's colour tables; it stands `proud_mm` above the base top and reaches `PART_OVERLAP_MM` into the plate like every other region.

- [V3-P5-F4] A SEPARATE frame is lifted clear of the plate by the mount's own `tolerance_mm` rather than resting on it. Two bodies that touch on a coincident plane fuse in the union, and then "separate" would mean nothing in a single-object export; a real gap makes the file honestly describe two parts, and the frame's first layer still prints onto the plate below it. Consequences, all deliberate: the welded model becomes 2 bodies in single mode; `solid/validate.ts:expectedBodies` derives that count FROM THE PARAMETERS and excuses exactly one body per expected region in `islandReport`, so a bake that came apart any other way is still a `floating-island` error with its volume in it; and the reference validator's `bodies` row reads 2 against 1 for such a file, which is the documented exception in `docs/handoff/v3-05-frame.md` (making it PASS there needs an `expected_bodies` sidecar field and a reader in `cli.py`, the phase 4 `max_height_mm` pattern, in files this phase does not own). Mating: `snap` is a 0.8 x 0.8 mm ridge on the base perimeter under the middle of the frame band with the matching groove in the frame underside grown by the tolerance on every flank and at the tip; `magnet` is `hanger_magnet.count` pockets PER SIDE (2 by default, so 8 in all), each `thickness_mm / 2` deep, so one magnet spans the joint, which is the only arrangement that fits between a 2 mm lip and a 3 mm plate with a millimetre of roof left in each. A magnet wider than the band minus a full minimum wall each side is REFUSED with the largest that fits named, not quietly shrunk: measured, a 6 mm magnet in a 6 mm frame broke through both faces of the lip and left 0.11 mm slivers, which the reference validator failed on.

- [V3-P5-F5] Face texture is cut into the frame's FLAT TOP FACE only, one nozzle wide (`min_detail_mm`) at a pitch of at least two nozzles: a groove one nozzle wide at a one nozzle pitch is not a texture, it is a missing top face. Each DIRECTION is built and clipped to the face on its own before the two are unioned. Parallel grooves never cross, so one direction is a union of disjoint rectangles that Clipper2 does in linear time, and the crossed pattern is then formed from two sets already cut down to the 6 mm band; building all of them at once asks the kernel to resolve every crossing over the whole plate, 318 x 318 of them at a 0.8 mm pitch, which took the WASM heap out of bounds. `brush` mitres its rails on the square's diagonals, which is the mitre a real frame is cut on; `dots` are generated only where the band can hold them (8 281 over the whole plate against about 1 000 that survive the clip). The element cap is 2 000, counted before any boolean runs, and a pattern past it is dropped with the count in the finding. Texture gives way to TEXT: the keep-out boxes come from the shared lettering layout, the same source `solid/lettering.ts` cuts from, grown by 1.0 mm, so a groove never runs through a letter and the two can never disagree about where the ink is.

- [V3-P5-F6] The cleat wedge and the easel leg print INSIDE their own pocket, one `LOOSE_PART_CLEARANCE_MM` (0.2 mm) clear on every face and sitting ON THE BED at z = 0. Every other placement is worse: beside the plate puts the model over its own bounds and fails 04's bounding-box row; in the assembled position the part hangs below z = 0 and drags the whole model off the bed; anywhere on top of the plate it lands on the city. A part 0.2 mm ABOVE the bed was tried first and is wrong for a different reason: the pocket opens downward onto the build plate, so a part hanging over it would print in mid air. The cleat slot is undercut at exactly 45 degrees, which is what makes it a cleat and what makes it print without support (every layer overhangs the one below by one layer height), and it runs edge to edge, because a wedge printed inside an undercut slot can only leave sideways. The wedge's staircase is lifted by the clearance in Z as well as inset in Y: with the two staircases sharing slab boundaries, the wedge's step face and the cavity's step face were coplanar over a 0.11 mm strip the length of the slot, and that strip welded the wedge to the plate so the whole model came out as one body. The easel's socket leans 20 degrees from the underside normal, stepped over the same 8 slabs, so the leg PROPS the plate instead of standing square under it. Both mounts are charged for their pocket by the shared `hanger_min_base_mm` / `underside_pockets` pair, mirrored in `transform.py` because `lib/warnings.ts` blocks a bake on that number: both want a 4.10 mm base at the default recesses, so both are refused with the measured numbers on the contract's 3 mm default and both were validated at 5 mm.

- [V3-P5-F7] A height gradient splits the buildings by EQUAL COUNT, not equal height: the band boundaries come from the building height distribution, so every band is populated. Equal-height bands on a real city put nine tenths of the plate in the bottom band and one tower in the top one, which is a gradient nobody can see. Band 1 keeps the region name `buildings`, so a one-band gradient and no gradient at all produce identical regions; bands 2 and up are `buildings_band_N`, added to `REGION_NAMES` as `DERIVED_REGION_NAMES` alongside `cleat`. The cap is 8 bands (`GRADIENT_MAX_BANDS`), with a finding naming the count when more slots are supplied: a band is a filament slot AND a region of its own, and the contract's 16 is past what any printer this targets can address. Band N takes `colour.gradient.slots[N-1]` for its slot and, until the UI supplies per-band colours, a colour interpolated between the buildings colour and the hero colour by `(N-1)/(bands-1)`. The split changes no geometry at all: same triangles, same volume, same merged solid, one region more.

- [V3-P5-F8] `colour.tint` is DATA, never geometry: the print path ignores it entirely, because a printer lays down whatever is in the slot. The engine emits `EngineResult.buildingTints` (id, colour, plan centroid) and the COLOUR comes from `lib/tint.ts:tintedColor`, the editor's own function, called rather than reimplemented, so the shade the preview paints on a building and the shade the OBJ gives it are one number from one place. The centroid is the part only the bake knows and is what `export/obj.ts` matches a mesh BODY to a tint by (union-find over the triangle list; the engine's meshes are indexed, so index adjacency is geometric connectivity). OBJ is the only target that can carry a colour per body, so it is the only one that changes: each buildings-family region becomes one `o` group per body, with the shades bucketed to at most 32 materials by LUMINANCE, in equal-count groups represented by the colour in the middle of each. Quantising the sRGB cube instead was tried and is wrong: a tint set is a narrow band around one colour (12 degrees of hue, 0.12 of lightness), and a cube grid coarse enough to fit 32 buckets flattened 289 shades to 4. With the tint off the OBJ bytes are exactly what they were.

- [V3-P6] Search radius by result type follows the brief's own table exactly: city/town 1500 m, suburb/neighbourhood/quarter 900 m, building/amenity/house 400 m, anything else (a road, an unclassified feature, no `addresstype` at all) defaults to 900 m rather than to the narrowest or widest option, matching the neighbourhood case rather than guessing either extreme (`lib/geocode.ts:radiusForResultType`).
- [V3-P6] A picked search result binds the place name through `applyGeocodeResult` (source "geocode"), not `setPlaceName` (source "user override, wins forever"): a search pick is not a stronger claim on the Place name field than a pin drag-and-settle is, and using the SAME path both take means a later reverse-geocode for the same coordinates can still reconcile it, exactly as it already does after a manual drag.
- [V3-P6] Forward and reverse geocoding share ONE module-level 1 request/second queue (`lib/geocode.ts:enqueueGeocodeRequest`), not two independent ones: Nominatim's usage policy is a budget on the CLIENT, not on which endpoint is called, so a search box typed into while the pin happens to be settling still spends from one shared allowance.
- [V3-P6] The share-link payload's version tag now names the PAYLOAD FORMAT ("v2" plain base64url JSON, "v3" raw-deflated JSON via fflate's `deflateSync`/`inflateSync`), not `PrintParams.schema_version` -- those two numbers were already different by this phase (v3 landed optional schema_version-3 fields onto what was still a "v2" link format). `encodeShare` always writes v3; `decodeShare` reads both v2 and v3, so a link already sitting in someone's chat history from before this phase keeps working. The checksum is computed over the DECOMPRESSED JSON text in both cases, so `checksum()` itself needed no change and a truncated/tampered v3 payload is caught either by `inflateSync` throwing on a broken stream or, more rarely, by the checksum disagreeing after a successful-but-wrong inflate -- both refusals are accepted as equivalent by the test suite, since compressed bytes make "still base64-decodes into something" far less likely than it was for plain base64.
- [V3-P6] No server-side share-id backend exists, by design (this product has no backend at all past Overpass/Nominatim passthroughs): a share link that would exceed `SHARE_LINK_LENGTH_LIMIT` (8000 characters) is not shortened or stored anywhere, it is refused by the Copy-link UI with an explanation and a "save a project file instead" affordance (`lib/project.ts`), which has no length ceiling at all. This is the deviation the original v2 comment's "stored share id fallback" line implied and this phase does not build.
- [V3-P6] `lib/share.ts:parsePrintParams` is factored out of `decodeShare` so `lib/project.ts:parseProject` can validate a project file's `params` object through the exact same `PRINT_PARAM_SPEC` table and bounds -- a project file and a share link can never disagree about what a setting value is allowed to be, and a change to one validator is a change to both by construction.
- [V3-P6] A project file (`.framecraft.json`) carries the WHOLE `PrintParams` object, never a diff against the default the way a share link does: a file on disk is meant to keep meaning the same thing after a future default changes, not to be replayed against "whatever this build currently defaults to". Loading a project DOES trigger an immediate re-ingest (`store/editor.ts:applyProject` calls `generate()`), unlike restoring a share link, which deliberately stays stale for the user to Generate themselves: opening a `.framecraft.json` is an unambiguous "load this design" action with no background-tab-navigation ambiguity a URL has.
- [V3-P6] Undo/redo history (`store/history.ts`) is wired by SUBSCRIBING to the editor store and diffing `location`/`params` by object reference across each notification, rather than by touching every one of `store/editor.ts`'s setters individually. Since zustand fires a subscriber once per `set()` call regardless of how many fields that call touched, this is what makes "palette apply / project load / share restore / auto-fix-all each count as ONE step" true for free, with no special-casing per action. Only `location` and `params` are ever diffed; `scene`, `engine`, `bake`, `placeDetect` and every transient UI flag are invisible to it by construction, which is what keeps a geocode result or a live engine job out of the history the brief asks to exclude.
- [V3-P6] The brief's "the existing 'N adjustments made' chip opens the history list" is read as a naming shorthand, not a literal instruction to repurpose `AdjustmentsChip`: that component is a settled, tested surface for an unrelated concept (`lib/adjustments.ts`'s site/repair/bake grouping of model-building remarks), and `IssuesBadge` already established the precedent for exactly this situation (`[V3-P4-U]`) -- a new, additive chip (`components/editor/HistoryChip.tsx`, `useHistoryStore`'s own `open` state), same visual language and drawer pattern, but NOT living beside `AdjustmentsChip`/`IssuesBadge` in `CityPreview`'s own top-left stack: those two are gated on a generated scene existing (`CityPreview` returns `PreviewEmpty` before that and never reaches their render branch), while a parameter change is recorded into history from the very first slider move, before Generate has ever run. `HistoryChip` therefore renders in `EditorShell`'s header instead, next to the shortcuts button and the theme toggle, where it is reachable regardless of scene state -- found by an e2e run (`e2e/workflow.spec.ts`) failing with "element(s) not found" for a scenario that deliberately never generates a scene, not assumed.
- [V3-P6] Undo/redo is exempt from `EditorShell`'s "nothing else acts while an overlay is up" rule: Ctrl+Z with the shortcut sheet, the Issues drawer or the Adjustments drawer open still undoes, because none of those overlays is a reason the last change should become unreachable. It IS refused while focus is inside a typing target (`lib/keyboard.ts:shortcutFor`, checked first, same discipline as every other shortcut), so it never hijacks a text field's own native undo.
- [V3-P6] Undoing a change never itself fetches: `applyHistorySnapshot` marks the scene stale (which is what re-enables the Generate button) ONLY when the restored location actually differs in value from what is on screen, and reschedules the debounced WASM engine job unconditionally either way (never a network call). Stepping back across a run of pure-parameter edits therefore never re-triggers Overpass; stepping back across a pin move, a radius change, a rotation change or a preset pick does mark the scene stale, exactly like any other location change, and still leaves Generate as the user's own move.
- [V3-P6] `store/history.ts`'s editor-store subscriber wraps its own `record()` call in try/catch: zustand's `subscribe` notifies listeners via a plain `Set.forEach`, uncaught inside the library, so one throwing listener stops every listener registered after it in the SAME notification from running at all -- including, for a listener registered late (as `initHistory`'s effect-based one necessarily is relative to every already-mounted control's own zustand subscription), React's own re-render hooks for components that mount later still (e.g. a collapsed group's body, first opened mid-session). A bug in the history recorder must therefore never be allowed to propagate, or it silently freezes reactivity for whatever mounts after it, not merely lose one undo step.
- [V3-P6] `components/editor/HistoryChip.tsx`'s step count reads `useHistoryStore`'s `cursor`, not `entries.length - 1`: `entries` never shrinks on undo (undo only moves `cursor` backward, so a redo branch stays available), so `entries.length - 1` is the HIGH-WATER MARK of every step ever recorded, not "how many changes are currently applied" -- the two are numerically identical at the tip of history (which is why an initial "3 changes" assertion passed) and diverge the instant a real undo runs, at which point the chip kept reporting the pre-undo count forever. Found by `e2e/workflow.spec.ts` (a raw-keydown diagnostic proved the shortcut dispatched correctly and `useHistoryStore`'s own state moved; only the CHIP's own derived display was wrong), not assumed from reading the code.

- [V3-P7-A1] `EngineInput.attribution` is IGNORED, additively deprecated rather than removed. It was going to be "the attribution strings the UI hands the engine", which is the one thing that field must never be: a caller that can supply the attribution is a caller that can supply an empty one, and every route into `bake()` (the worker, `lib/bake.ts`, `scripts/bake-cli.ts`, a test) would then be a route to an unmarked model. `solid/attribution.ts` composes the strings from its own constants, the bake's `PrintParams` and the bake's date, and reads nothing from the input. The field stays declared, documented as deprecated and discarded, so every existing caller still typechecks; it is deleted in a later phase.
- [V3-P7-A2] `underside_mark.enabled: false` silences the USER's line and nothing else. The v2 underside mark stops being a mark of its own: `solid/ornaments.ts` no longer cuts it, `solid/attribution.ts` owns the whole underside block, and the user's `template` is APPENDED under the mandatory text at a cap height capped by the mandatory line's own (so a one-word personal line can never dominate the credit above it). The user's line keeps the resolved id `underside-mark`, because `engine.resolveParamsEcho`, the OUTPUT panel and the reference validator's `underside_probe_zs` all read that id; the mandatory block is `attribution-underside`. `lettering.buildLettering` is told how much of the underside the block takes (`undersideReserveMm`) and stacks the user's own `edge: "underside"` engravings below it, so the two can never be laid out on top of each other.
- [V3-P7-A3] The deep underside mark is 0.6 mm deep where the plate can carry it and clamped where it cannot, never under 0.4 mm, because it is not optional and the contract's own defaults cannot carry 0.6. The ordinary underside-pocket guard is `depth + HANGER_MIN_ROOF_MM (1.0) + deepest recess <= base_thickness`; on the default 3 mm plate with the default 1.5 mm water recess that leaves 0.5 mm, so the default bake cuts 0.5 mm and emits an `info` finding with the numbers in it. Below 0.4 mm the roof requirement itself gives way to `DEEP_MARK_MIN_ROOF_MM` (0.6 mm, three layers at the commonest layer height); the contract floors `base_thickness_mm` at 2 mm and clamps every recess at half the base, so that branch can never produce a mark under 0.4 mm and can never breach the plate.
- [V3-P7-A4] The mandatory text is laid out to SPAN the plate rather than to be comfortable: one line at whatever cap height fills the available width, wrapped to words only when a single line would fall under 3 mm. On the 180 mm default that is one line, 5.83 mm tall and 174.7 mm wide, which is 97 per cent of the plate against the 60 per cent the brief asks for. Sanding it out therefore takes material off the whole underside rather than off a corner. A layout that came out under 60 per cent (a plate small enough that the text has to wrap several times) is an `info` finding, not a refusal.
- [V3-P7-A5] The frame inner-wall mark is cut on ALL FOUR walls of the opening, not one. "A sanding block cannot reach it" is true of one wall; "the whole frame profile has to be recut" is true only of four, and the brief's requirement is that removal be costly. The exposed wall is the topmost run of `frame.profileSlabs` sharing the top slab's inner inset, clipped to material above the base top: 2.00 mm exactly for the plain lip, which is why the skip threshold is 2 mm. A `bevel_in` profile (sloped inner face), a `floating` profile (1.0 mm undercut over the bottom 0.4 mm) and a SEPARATE frame (lifted by its mount tolerance) all report under 2 mm and fall back to the frame-off path: a second underside mark, turned 90 degrees, against the west edge, with the first mark moved north and narrowed so the two can never touch. Each wall cutter is INTERSECTED with the lip's own footprint before it is used: it overshoots into the opening so its near face is not coincident with the wall it cuts, and the opening is exactly where the city is (buildings are cropped flush to that same plane), so without the clip the attribution would be engraved into any building standing against the frame.
- [V3-P7-A6] No attribution mark is ever refused for being small. `transform.fit_text` refuses below `TEXT_MIN_SIZE_MM` and `lettering.repairText` refuses a stroke under the nozzle, both correctly, for a line the USER chose: a groove that will not print is a promise the editor broke. A provenance mark is the opposite case. A 2 mm frame wall holds about 1.8 mm of cap height and a plate edge holds 1.2 mm, both of which put the strokes under one 0.4 mm nozzle by construction, and a groove too fine to print is still a groove in the mesh and still says where the model came from. So the marks take their size straight from the surface, are cut whatever it is, and are reported with an `info` finding naming the size, the nozzle and the words "may not resolve on the print". For the same reason the small marks are NOT dilated to a printable stroke: at 1.8 mm the dilation that reaches 0.4 mm closes every counter and turns the line into a row of lozenges, which is not a mark at all. The dilation is applied to the big underside mark, and only while it does not close a counter.
- [V3-P7-A7] Glyph sections for the marks are re-flattened to the asset's own 0.02 mm chord tolerance before they are extruded (`manifold.cleanSection` at `flatten_tolerance_mm`). `lib/fonts/*.glyphs.json` is flattened for the contract's LARGEST legal engraving size (8 mm), so a curve cut at 1.2 mm carries six times the vertices its own chord error needs, and each of them becomes four triangles in the extrusion and then propagates through the subtraction. It is worth 9 860 triangles on a default Chicago bake and moves no point further than the flattening already had. The deburring half of `cleanSection` earns its place separately: a glyph outline pinches where two curves meet almost tangentially, and a pinch point extrudes into a zero-area side triangle.
- [V3-P7-A8] The marks' Z bands are DECLARED and carried to the validator rather than measured around. `BakeContext.markBands` is filled as the marks are laid out, echoed on `EngineResult.attributionBands`, written into the export sidecar as `attribution_bands`, and read back by `app/cli.py` exactly the way `max_height_mm` is (`[V3-P4-E9]`). Three consumers, three different reasons. (1) `measure.measureMinWall` must not judge the UNDERSIDE band: a letter counter on the floor of a 0.5 mm pocket is an island in a horizontal slice taken inside that pocket and it persists upward perfectly, because the plate above it is solid, so the "is this a wall?" test calls a 0.44 mm counter a 0.44 mm wall. It is the underside of a plate that is 2.5 mm thick there. The reference validator already excluded that band for that exact reason (`_min_wall_probe`'s `skip_bands`); the engine's own probe did not, and a default bake with `underside_mark` on could report a `wall-too-thin` error that was not one. (2) `tiling.sliverHeights` must not judge ANY of the bands, and for a harder reason: a sliver found at one height is cut out of the whole model at every height, so harvesting a letter counter as a sliver punches a hole clean through the tile. Excluding them took the 2x2 Chicago tile loss from 1.35 per cent to 0.45 per cent. (3) `checks.validate` excludes them from the structural `min_wall` row and judges them by a new `attribution` row instead. That row is not a rubber stamp: it fails when a declared band is over 2.5 mm tall, when there are more than four of them (the two caps together put a hard 10 mm ceiling on everything the row lets min_wall skip, and an ABSOLUTE ceiling is the right kind: a flat plate with a frame is 5 mm tall, so a share-of-height rule would fail the same three legitimate bands it passes on a city with towers), when a band lies outside the model, or when a band has no material at its middle, so an exclusion can never be widened to hide a real thin wall. It does not try to read the glyphs, because a validator cannot tell an engraved credit from a scratch and pretending otherwise would be worse than saying nothing; that the marks are cut is checked by `resolved_text` in the sidecar and by `attribution.test.ts` measuring the volume each one removes.
- [V3-P7-A9] `mesh.collapseNeedles` is added as the last rung of `cleanMesh`: it welds the two ends of ONE degenerate triangle's shortest edge and touches nothing else. The whole-mesh weld cannot always do that job, because it is all-or-nothing at its epsilon (the rung coarse enough to close a 1.4e-5 mm needle in a Chicago tile also merges pairs a boolean meant to keep apart elsewhere on that tile, the mesh comes back open, and the acceptance test correctly throws the whole rung away), and `splitNeedles` cannot do it when the needle's long edge is met by two triangles on the far side, because then no single neighbour holds the twin to split. It is subject to the same acceptance test as every other repair here plus one of its own: closed, oriented, same volume to a nanolitre, strictly fewer degenerate faces, AND no more connected bodies than the mesh came in with, or the mesh is handed back untouched. The body guard is not optional and counts bodies the way `trimesh.body_count` does (faces joined across edges shared by exactly two faces), because a pinch is closed, oriented and volume-preserving and is still three objects where there was one: with a naive vertex-adjacency count the Chicago plate left the exporter as three bodies while the solid it came from was one. It is also OPT IN, off by default, and `solid/tiling.ts` is the only caller that asks for it: the weld ladder clears every degenerate face an untiled bake produces on its own, at a coarser rung than this repair would let it reach, and running it everywhere made the ladder stop one rung early on a mesh that welded to one body only at the next rung. This was found because the frame inner-wall mark moves `tiling.snapCut`'s chosen seam and the new seam clipped a groove mouth badly; the tiled path was already fragile to any added frame geometry (a knurled frame with NO attribution left 17 degenerate faces across the four tiles), and this fixes that case too.
- [V3-P7-A10] Every exporter writes the same five-field provenance block, in the same order, from one function (`export/common.provenanceEntries`): author, licence, generator, source, generated. One string in one place, because a licence notice that says a slightly different thing in each of four files is not a notice, it is four claims. `place.author` also becomes the 3MF `Designer` and the STEP author when it is set, because that is what those fields mean and what a reader shows; blank leaves the generator's own name, which is what every file said before. `author` is written even when it is empty, so the block is uniform and a reader can look for it. `framecraft:generator` was removed from its stand-alone position in both 3MF writers because the block repeats it and the core spec requires metadata names to be unique inside one element. The single-plate Bambu byte-hash moved for exactly this change and was re-pinned after diffing the unzipped `3D/3dmodel.model` entry by entry.
- [V3-P7-A11] A frame-OFF Chicago bake at the committed fixture fails the reference validator's `min_wall` row (0.167 mm, five regions, at every height through one merged building block) and, at plate 256 mm, its `degenerate_faces` row as well. Both were reproduced with the attribution marks disabled and are byte-identical with and without them, so neither belongs to this phase: they are a pre-existing defect in the frame-off crop and merge path, where the city runs to the plate edge and the block scale changes. Left as found and reported rather than fixed here, because the remedy is in the building repair and would move the DEFAULT bake's geometry, which several committed goldens pin. The frame-off attribution path is validated instead on a synthetic scene that is otherwise clean (`ALL CHECKS PASS`, two declared bands, two underside marks).
- [V3-P6] `e2e/workflow.spec.ts`'s undo/redo test drives boolean/numeric controls only (plate size, the frame toggle, the water toggle), never a text field: `.fill()` on `#city_label` was observed, reproducibly, to occasionally leave the field committing TWO edits instead of one (a "Bergen" typed once landing in the store as "BergenBergen") under this session's specific load (many concurrent headless Chromium + Next dev + uvicorn processes on one shared host, started and torn down repeatedly across a long diagnostic run) -- a genuine input-timing artifact, not a `store/history.ts` bug (confirmed across six rounds of instrumentation: the store's own `cursor` and every `record()` call were provably correct throughout; the extra edit was a real, distinct THIRD `record()` call for `city_label`, not a stale read). Text-field `.fill()` is exercised plenty elsewhere in this same file (search, project, share); only THIS specific test, whose whole point is exact step-counting, avoids it.
- [V3-P6] A group's collapsed/open state (`lib/groups.ts`) persists to `localStorage` and therefore SURVIVES `page.reload()` within one browser context: an e2e test that opens the Frame group, saves a project, reloads the page and then needs the Frame group open again must check `data-collapsed` first (`openGroup`, mirroring `e2e/lettering.spec.ts`'s own helper) rather than blindly clicking the toggle a second time, which would CLOSE an already-open group instead.
- [V3-P6] Two real a11y regressions found only by `make gate`'s `e2e/a11y.spec.ts` (not by unit tests or by reading the code): `SearchBox.tsx`'s combobox wrapper and its `<input>` pointed `aria-controls`/`aria-owns` at `search-results-list` even while that listbox is unmounted (conditionally rendered only while `open`), which is `aria-valid-attr-value` (serious, axe) -- an ARIA reference attribute must name an id that actually exists in the DOM. Fixed the same way every other disclosure in this app already does (`AdjustmentsChip`, `IssuesBadge`, `HistoryChip`): `open ? "search-results-list" : undefined`.
