Separately, `SearchBox`'s own `<input>` (no visible `<label>`, placeholder does not count) and `OutputPanel.tsx`'s hidden file input (`#load-project-input`, never skipped by the accessible-name sweep for being hidden -- `INPUT` elements are checked regardless of `offsetParent`) both had no accessible name at all; both got an explicit `aria-label`.
- [V3-P8-C] The repository licence is MIT (LICENSE, Copyright (c) 2026 Vahid Alizadeh): the code is a tool that processes ODbL data, not a derivative database of it, so a permissive code licence and the ODbL map-data obligations coexist; README and LICENSE_AND_ATTRIBUTION.md state the split explicitly (code MIT, map data ODbL, models are Produced Works carrying attribution).

- [V3-P8-D1] The web app is a static export (`output: "export"`), and ONE build-time knob, `NEXT_PUBLIC_BASE_PATH` ("" for the domain root and the desktop app, "/framecraft" for GitHub Pages), controls sub-path hosting: it feeds `basePath`/`assetPrefix` in `next.config.ts` and `apps/web/lib/basePath.ts`, which prefixes the two runtime-constructed public URLs Next cannot rewrite (the MapLibre worker URL in `LocationPicker.tsx`, and the manifold WASM fetch). The WASM case is handled by a fetch shim (`installWasmBasePathFetchShim`, installed by the engine worker and the inline fallback) that rewrites EXACTLY the one root-relative URL `lib/engine/solid/manifold.ts`'s `MANIFOLD_WASM_PUBLIC_PATH` names, because that module sits in `lib/engine/solid/**`, which is frozen to a concurrent fixer for the whole of P8; the shim is a no-op at the root, in Node, and for every other request. Verified in a real Chromium against the exported tree served at `/` AND at `/framecraft` (root-404ing everything outside the prefix): full Chicago bake from the mocked Overpass route, 2.24 MB 3MF downloaded, zero same-origin 4xx, zero console errors.
- [V3-P8-D2] `output: "export"` retires `next start`, so `FRAMECRAFT_WEB_MODE=prod` (playwright.config.ts and `make up`) now serves the exported `out/` tree through `apps/web/scripts/serve-static.mjs`, a dependency-free static server whose `--base` flag mounts the tree under a sub-path ONLY (a request outside it 404s), which is what makes a root-absolute URL bug reproducible locally instead of first appearing on Pages. `next dev` and every e2e assertion are unchanged.
- [V3-P8-D3] The Tauri 2 desktop shell (`apps/desktop`) stays out of the web bundle entirely: `tauri.conf.json` sets `withGlobalTauri`, and `apps/web/lib/platform.ts` talks to `window.__TAURI__.core.invoke` behind an `isTauri()` gate, so no `@tauri-apps/*` JS package is ever imported and the Pages bundle is byte-identical in behaviour. Export bytes cross the IPC boundary as base64 (Tauri's JSON IPC would serialise a Uint8Array as a number per byte); the Rust side is two commands, `save_export` (native save dialog + write) and `cache_dir` (app data dir for future offline caches). Anchor downloads are inert inside wry, so `OutputPanel`'s download links preventDefault under Tauri and route through `lib/bake.ts:saveDownloadFile`.
- [V3-P8-D4] `.github/workflows/release.yml` creates the GitHub Release FIRST (one `gh release create --generate-notes` job) and the tauri-action matrix jobs only attach to it by tag, so the generated notes are never overwritten by whichever platform finishes last. Signing is secrets-driven and optional: with no secrets configured every artifact is built unsigned. The static web build (root base path, `*.map`-stripped, though this build config emits no source maps) is attached as a self-hosting zip.
- [V3-P8-R] `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` and `RUNBOOK.md` rewritten for the v3 reality (fully client-side static app; `apps/web/lib/engine/**` on manifold-3d WASM in two Web Workers, one per job kind; `services/bake` as reference implementation and CLI validator only; contracts schema version 3): the v2 texts described a server-side bake ("the browser never runs booleans") that `[V3-A1]` retired. Verification numbers in RUNBOOK section 5 are read from the latest full gate log, `artifacts/logs/v3-06-gate-2.log` (pytest 697, vitest 1382 in 75 files, Playwright 48, browser-engine files ALL CHECKS PASS in both modes), and every path, flag and target named was checked against the tree (Makefile, `apps/web/scripts/bake-cli.ts`, `.github/workflows/*.yml`) rather than carried over from the plan. Known-limitations lists carry the open items the handoffs left (loose-part `bodies` row, frame-off Chicago min_wall, tint scope, faceted STEP, unsigned installers) instead of omitting them.
- [V3-P7-fix-1] The frame-off Chicago `min_wall` failure of `[V3-P7-A11]` is NOT the crop: it is `repair.residueParts` disagreeing with `thicken._residue`. Both are the same opening at `0.45 * min_wall`, but GEOS simplifies every buffer input at `0.01 * distance` (JTS `BufferOp.SIMPLIFY_FACTOR`) and Clipper2 offsets exactly what it is given, so a needle in the eroded ring - three vertices spanning 5 µm - fires a MITRE spike bounded only by `RESIDUE_MITRE_LIMIT` and lays it straight across the wing the opening exists to isolate. Measured on the failing block: the engine's opening escaped its own component by 0.199 mm2 against GEOS' 0.053, found 0 residue parts where GEOS found one of 0.0595 mm2, and reported 3.3651 mm where the reference validator reported 0.1667 mm. A wing nothing can see is a wing `widenThinParts` never widens. `RESIDUE_SIMPLIFY_FACTOR = 0.01` simplifies the eroded ring first and the two agree again. `thicken._opening`'s final clip back to the component is deliberately not mirrored: the residue is `component - tolerated`, so material outside the component cannot change it, and the clip cost about a second of Clipper2 work against a 15 s bake budget while moving no validator number on any of the six bakes in `docs/handoff/v3-07-fix.md`.
- [V3-P7-fix-2] `thicken.merge_recess_ridges` had never been ported to the browser engine, and its absence is the plate-256 half of `[V3-P7-A11]`. `areas.mergeRecessRidges` ports it between the surface layers' repair and their extrusion, with three departures, each measured: it judges the GROWN POCKETS rather than the raw footprints (the base is carved with the pocket, and `POCKET_GROW_MM`'s two micrometres are what pinch the base to a hairline where a groove runs along a block's edge - the 0.30 mm lobe that leaves is invisible to a probe of the section); a WHOLE island is absorbed only when no building stands on it (a cutter reaches from its floor up past the base top and a building only reaches `building_skirt_mm` down, so absorbing the island a block stands on took `bodies` from 1 to 2, a block floating 0.3 mm over the groove floor), while a wedge is always taken; and it runs only with the frame OFF.
- [V3-P7-fix-3] That frame-off gate is a SCOPING decision and not a geometric one, and the frame-on plate is not clean. Measured on the complement of the recessed pockets: frame-on 180 carries 38 islands that hold no full wall and 13 thin wedges (one of which persists a printed layer upward), frame-on 256 carries 45 and 30. The reference merges all of them unconditionally; doing that here moves the committed default golden, which the brief for this fix rules out. Left for the team lead.
- [V3-P7-fix-4] `measure.measureMinWall`'s flat path stays as it was - it measures only a region that VANISHES under the erosion probe, and measures it with `inscribedWidthMm` - and the comment that claimed Stage 1 leaves no thin appendage in 2D is replaced by the counter-example that disproves it (a merged block holding a 3.37 mm disc and carrying a 0.17 mm wing, which the gate saturated on at 0.80 mm while the reference validator failed the file at 0.1667 mm). Two closures were implemented and measured before being backed out: measuring every persisting region by `narrowestWidthMm` takes the default bake from 6.0 s to 16.2 s against `engine.test.ts`'s 15 s budget (and doing the residue probe first as the pre-gate does not help, because the probe is the cost - four offsets per region per slice - not the width search behind it); removing the `4A/P` pre-filter inside `narrowestWidthMm`, which the reference does not have and which returns early for exactly the fat-region-with-a-wing case, costs the same and additionally turns three draped fixtures from clean into `wall-too-thin` at 0.158 mm, findings that may be real and that nobody has judged. Both need one cheaper appendage probe and are one piece of work.
- [V3-P7-fix-5] The frame-on default bake is NOT byte-identical after this and could not be, because `residueParts` is the shared code path: merged triangles 93 746 -> 93 762, region triangles 135 106 -> 135 130, volume 172 324 -> 172 335 mm3 (+0.006 %), parts shells 463 -> 462. That delta is the same class of wing being widened on the frame-on plate, which is the repair doing what it was always meant to do. Every number the reference validator judges is unchanged (min_wall 0.8746 single / 0.9379 parts, degenerate 0, bodies 1, bounding box, ALL CHECKS PASS in both modes) and every committed golden passes as written - `frame.test.ts`'s plain-lip constant, `synthetic.test.ts`'s empty-scene ring, `export/tiles.test.ts`'s Bambu byte hash and `engine.test.ts`'s volume against the reference. No test was weakened, skipped or deleted.
- [V3-P7-fix-6] Plate 256 is improved and NOT closed, and the two residuals are written up in `docs/handoff/FAILURES.md`. `degenerate_faces` 7 in single mode is not a frame-off defect at all - the frame-ON 256 bake fails the same row with 6 - and `mesh.collapseNeedles` on the merged mesh was tried and did not clear it (the count stayed at 7, so `cleanMesh`'s acceptance test rejects the repair), so the rung was backed out rather than left in as dead configuration. `min_wall` 0.2998 in parts mode is one 0.46 x 0.44 mm lobe hanging off a block's corner through a 4.6 µm pinch that `grownPocket`'s own 2 µm growth creates where a groove runs along that block; four repairs were built and measured and none closes it (bridging fixes single mode and leaves the block's corner overhanging in the parts union; holding the bridge clear of the buildings removes exactly the bridge that fixed it; measuring the printed plan instead gives the same 0.298; widening the neck rather than carving it is worse at 0.253 and does not converge in four passes). The remaining lever is to hold `grownPocket` clear of the building footprint, which would remove the pinch at source and bring back the 602 zero-area faces at the buildings/roads boundary that `POCKET_GROW_MM` exists to kill - a trade that touches every bake and needs measuring on its own.
- [V3-P7-fix] Orchestrator rulings on the two open calls (2026-09-02): mergeRecessRidges stays frame-off-gated for v3.0.0; the reference merges unconditionally but doing so moves the default golden days before the tag, so the frame-on unification is a recorded follow-up, not a release blocker (frame-on passes every validator row as is). The plate-256 residuals (one 0.46 x 0.44 mm lobe in frame-off parts, 6 to 7 degenerate faces at 256 in BOTH frame states) ship as documented known limitations in FAILURES.md and the RUNBOOK; four candidate repairs were built and measured without closing them, the default plate is 180, and the engine's own audit now measures with the corrected probe so the app warns honestly.
- [V3-A2-close] Contracts re-frozen at schema version 3 on 2026-09-02; CLAUDE.md updated (pipeline description, freeze bullet, boolean-engine bullet, ports).
- [V3-P8-gate] The engine bake time-budget unit tests (15 s) flaked under full-suite parallelism on a host measuring the bake at 13.7 to 13.9 s (fixer-p7 timed it four times with and without its change; the 15 s budget dated from a 6.6 s idle-host baseline). Raised to 30 s scaled by E2E_BUDGET_FACTOR with the intent documented in the test: an order-of-magnitude regression pin, not a host-noise trap. Same class of correction as the share-link 8 to 16 kB bound and the e2e budget factor.
- [V3-P7-fix2-1] The `[V3-P7-fix]` frame-off repair shipped a wall-clock regression that every Node-side check missed and the e2e preview stalled on, and it is not in the geometry: `measure.measureMinWall` had its two per-region filters SWAPPED. Both are `continue` filters, so the set they pass through is identical either way and no measured number moves; their costs are not. The erosion probe offsets ONE region and costs 0.04 ms; the persistence test intersects that region with the whole unsimplified slice one printed layer above, which on a Chicago plate is tens of thousands of vertices and costs 4.2 ms whatever the region is. Running persistence first made every region pay it. Measured on the smoke test's own parameters (Chicago fixture, plate 200 mm, base 4.0 mm, large_scale 1.2, engrave, frame on): 1930 regions over 15 slices, 1930 persistence intersects costing 8162 ms of an 8427 ms `measureMinWall` and a 12.57 s engine bake, against 1269 erosions costing 56 ms and zero width searches. Restoring the committed order takes the same bake to 4.40 s against the pre-fix tree's 4.61 s. In the browser the same regression left `EngineClient`'s serial bake queue - a running bake cannot be preempted, so a superseded job waits it out ([V3-P2-E4]) - unable to land a first `EngineResult` inside `smoke.spec.ts`'s 90 s stats-card budget after the A3 slider stress, which is the "Updating model..." forever that `smoke.spec.ts`, `lettering.spec.ts` and `a11y.spec.ts` all failed on. The order is now documented as load-bearing for the clock at the call site.
- [V3-P7-fix2-2] That ordering is pinned by `lib/engine/solid/measure.test.ts`, and it is pinned by COUNTING rather than by timing: a wall-clock bound on a synthetic scene would pin the same property and would be exactly the kind of host-noise trap that produced `[V3-P8-gate]`. The test builds a slab carrying 196 pillars far too fat to be thin anywhere plus one strip under half a wall wide, patches `intersect` on the CrossSection prototype, and asserts the persistence test ran at most once per sampled slice - i.e. only ever on a region the erosion probe already called thin - while still finding the strip. It also asserts the counter saw at least one call, because manifold's binding wraps the embind class and `wasm.CrossSection.prototype` is NOT what a section inherits from, so patching that object is a silent no-op and the test would otherwise pass vacuously (the prototype is read off an instance instead). Verified against the defect: with the two filters swapped it reads 1387 intersects against a bound of 15.
- [V3-P7-fix2-3] `[V3-P8-gate]`'s premise is void and `engine.test.ts`'s time budgets are restored to the committed 15 s, unscaled. The 13.7 to 13.9 s that justified doubling them was not host noise: it was `fix2-1`'s swapped filters, i.e. the very defect the raised budget then let through to the e2e. With the order restored the default Chicago bake measures 6.63 s on this host (5.70 s on the pre-fix tree; the +0.93 s is `RESIDUE_SIMPLIFY_FACTOR` genuinely finding and widening more wings, per `[V3-P7-fix-5]`), and the full 77-file, 1387-test vitest suite passes under exactly the full-suite parallelism that was said to flake. A budget raised to accommodate a regression is a weakened test, and this one hid a shipping defect for a whole phase.
- [V3.1-O1] This run is FrameCraft 3.1.0. Handoff notes use the brief's names (`docs/handoff/v3-NN-<phase>.md`); because the v3.0 run already owns `v3-02-audit.md`, this run's audit files are `v3-NN-<phase>-audit.md`, and STATUS.md indexes both runs.
- [V3.1-O2] Commits stay authored by Vahid Alizadeh only, with no Co-Authored-By, session or generator trailers, per the author's standing rule in CLAUDE.md; the orchestrator commits per gate-green phase and pushes to main as the v3.0 run did, because V3-4 (deployed cold load) and V3-8 (CI wall-clock) are only measurable on GitHub Pages and GitHub runners.
- [V3.1-O3] Vocabulary scope for Task 3: "bake" leaves every user-facing string, TypeScript identifier, make target, test name, script name and document. The Python reference package directory `services/bake`, its `/bake` HTTP routes and the `BakeResult` wire schema keep their names: they are reference-implementation internals and a frozen contract identifier, renaming them changes nothing the author sees and would churn CI, uv config and the v1 golden path. Retired make targets keep a deprecation alias that prints the new name and runs it.
- [V3.1-O4] Contract additions land with the feature that claims them, not all at once: schema_version 4 opens with Task 10's optional SceneGraph `name`/`osm_id`/`kind`, `object_overrides` arrives with Task 11 and `labels` with Task 12. Reason: the V3-1 matrix test fails any PrintParams field no stage moves, so a field must never exist ahead of its implementation between waves.
- [V3.1-O5] Escalations to Fable subagents: the Task 1 pipeline core (an explicit stage graph replacing `engine.ts`'s single traversal, touching the engine, protocol, worker, client, store and preview, well over fifteen files) and Task 12 Tier A glyph-on-surface geometry (novel geometry through the repair and min-wall gates). Everything else runs on Opus 5.
- [V3.1-O6] Layout state (panel sizes, collapsed sides, maximized region, expanded settings sections) is carried by the project file and the permalink but lives OUTSIDE PrintParams: it is not a print parameter, it must not hash into any pipeline stage, and it must not count as a "change from default" in the settings diff.
- [V3.1-P1-1] Pipeline design rulings (orchestrator, from docs/handoff/v3-01-inventory.md; full text in docs/handoff/v3-01-pipeline-design.md): one resolution, the preview renders the exact export solids and no reduced-tessellation twin exists; two-phase delivery inside one job (every region mesh is finished and streamed before assembly, merged, measure, validate, islands, tiling and audit run); progressive per-region posting with a main-thread region hash map; a one-generation cache of stage outputs holding live WASM handles in the worker; fetch and normalise become stages of the same worker (the second worker goes away) so heights.* re-normalises from the cached Overpass response; exporters move into the worker; cancellation is cooperative at stage boundaries; findings are stage outputs concatenated in stage order.
- [V3.1-P1-2] The V3-1 matrix test exempts exactly three PrintParams fields, each with a compensating test: schema_version (payload metadata), colour.preview_theme (a viewer setting moved to the viewport HUD, echoed in the sidecar) and part_colors.* (v1 colour block, migrated into colour.region_colors at parse time when region_colors is absent; the seven wells leave the UI). Every other leaf field must move both preview and file; colour.palette does so by being written into the sidecar and the 3MF metadata.
- [V3.1-P1-3] Location (lat, lon, radius, rotation, preset) is the fetch stage key and never a live trigger: it marks the scene stale and waits for the Preview action. Every PrintParams write schedules a live incremental run after PIPELINE_DEBOUNCE_MS = 80 (was 400) and supersedes a run in flight at its next stage boundary.
- [V3.1-P1-4] `packages/contracts/gen_ts.py` and `gen_py.py` additionally emit PRINT_PARAM_LEAF_PATHS (every leaf of print_params.json as a dotted path, arrays as `engravings[].text`), generated, never hand-edited; the pipeline registry's claims are checked against it, so a schema addition without a stage claim fails the graph test.
- [V3.1-P3-1] The `make bake-fixture` deprecation alias is permanent, not transitional: `05_AGENT_TEAM.md` (frozen) defines gate G3 as `make bake-fixture && make validate artifacts/chicago.3mf`, so removing the alias would silently delete a frozen gate. Same for `npm run bake:cli`. Recorded from v3-03-vocabulary-audit.md finding 12.
- [V3.1-P3-2] Released CHANGELOG entries (3.0.0, 1.0.0) keep the vocabulary of the release they describe; the Task 3 rename is documented under Unreleased only (audit finding 5 restored the three rewritten lines).
- [V3.1-P9-1] The location type-ahead uses Photon (photon.komoot.io), which is OSM-based and built for autocomplete; Nominatim keeps only the single reverse geocode after a pin drop through the existing 1 rps queue, because its usage policy discourages autocomplete. Both are attributed in the search popover and the footer; the OSM credit is unchanged. Policy: no request under three characters, 250 ms debounce, abort on keystroke, LRU 50 for 10 minutes, 429 blocks for max(30 s, Retry-After) with no retry, 5xx retried once after 2 s, everything else reads as unavailable with a coordinate fallback.
- [V3.1-P9-2] A decimal pair whose both numbers are legal latitudes (`-87.6233, 41.8827` is not; `41.88, 12.49` is) is offered as two rows, latitude-first then longitude-first, as openstreetmap.org does; a pair whose first number exceeds 90 in magnitude yields exactly the longitude-first row. Coordinates are parsed locally by a tokenizer and never fetch.
- [V3.1-P9-3] Picking a search result moves the pin, applies the result-type radius rule, binds the place name through applyGeocodeResult and marks the scene stale; it never calls the network-costly Preview by itself (the old code called generate() on every pick).
- [V3.1-P14-1] The required CI path is five parallel jobs on ubuntu-latest / Node 22 (lint-typecheck, unit, pytest, build-and-validate, e2e-smoke), each with timeout-minutes 10 so the ten-minute budget is asserted, not hoped. The smoke subset is the three @smoke-tagged tests (preview, two export targets with a download and a validator verdict); the happy-path test is deliberately excluded because it alone measured 2.2 minutes locally and projects to 5 to 8 minutes on a GPU-less runner, and it runs in nightly.yml's full suite instead. What a green fast path does not prove is written in RUNBOOK section 7.
- [V3.1-P14-2] Cache hit rates are written to the step summary on every run; a warm miss (an entry with the same key created before the run started) fails the job only on a push to the default branch and warns elsewhere, because new branches, evictions and cache-API outages are legitimate first misses. The next build cache reports but never fails: its key is the lock hash plus the branch, not a source hash, so it stops evicting the npm, uv and Playwright entries.
- [V3.1-P14-3] desktop-build.yml is a reusable workflow with no permissions block of its own; release.yml grants contents: write on the calling job, nightly.yml calls it under contents: read, so a nested-escalation validation failure cannot occur and desktop builds run only on tags and on the nightly schedule.
- [V3.1-P1-5] Stage keys hash content digests, not keys, for data-only outputs, and pin an input's generation only when the output references its handles; `base` is keyed on named part digests (`lettering#base`, `ornaments#base`, `attribution#base`, `hangers#base`, `frame-cutters#base`, `buildings#socket`) that are content hashes of the cutter meshes. Reason: without them a frame-edge text change re-carved and re-finished the plate (1.15 s to the preview against the 400 ms target); with them it is 118 ms. The mechanism is exact (same mesh, same solid) and the alias rule keeps the one-generation cache safe.
- [V3.1-P1-6] `toRegionMesh` writes every mesh in canonical order (vertices by coordinate, triangles by lowest vertex). Reason: manifold3d orders output triangles by original mesh id, which depends on allocation history, so a warm incremental run and a cold run of the same parameters differed in byte order; now they are byte-identical and a region hash stands for its mesh.
- [V3.1-P1-7] `surface-parks` repairs the parks layer, merges the base ridges and extrudes all four surface layers; `surface-water`, `-rail` and `-roads` are the repaired footprints only. Reason: the ridge merge's sink is the last recessed layer that was built, known only after every layer's repair; extrusion is milliseconds, the repairs are the cost.
- [V3.1-P1-8] `lettering` does not depend on `attribution` (the underside reserve is a pure layout both compute), so the registry order and the resolved-text order stay lettering, ornaments, attribution.
- [V3.1-P1-9] `fetchTerrainGrid` returns the raw grid stamped `TerrainGrid.smoothing = 0`; the `terrain` stage applies `terrain.smoothing` and gates on `terrain.enabled` (a grid passed to `buildModel()` or the CLI is authoritative). Reason: the two leaves are claimed by a worker stage and applied once, and a smoothing change becomes a stage re-run rather than a refetch.
- [V3.1-P1-10] `context` claims `frame_style.profile` as a keyed claim (floating or not). Reason: the crop inset depends only on whether the frame is floating; keying on the profile string would rebuild the whole model for a plain-to-chamfer change.
- [V3.1-P1-11] `colour.region_colors.attribution` is claimed by no stage: no stage produces an attribution region solid (the marks are cuts). Either a later task gives the field a solid to colour or it joins the matrix exemptions; the graph test lists it as measured, not ruled.
- [V3.1-P1-12] The run modes are `scene`, `preview`, `full` and `export`; `scene` exists for the compat `EngineClient.ingest` and never loads the WASM kernel. The `rotation` extra carries the SceneRequest's rotation to the lettering, because a `buildModel()` call has no SceneRequest.
- [V3.1-P1-13] Ruling on [V3.1-P1-11]: `colour.region_colors.attribution` joins the matrix exemptions as the fourth and last entry. The mandatory attribution marks are engraved cuts by design ([V3-P7-A2..A5]) and never a body, so no colour can reach a file; the field stays in the contract for v3 payload compatibility, its colour well leaves the settings panel (Task 2), and a test pins that no control writes it. `colour.region_slots.attribution` stays claimed by the audit's slot rule. `scale_bar.length_m` is not exempt: it is read whenever `scale_bar.length_mode` is `fixed`, so its matrix probe sets the mode with it.
- [V3.1-P1-14] Pipeline core measured on the Chicago fixture (docs/handoff/v3-01-pipeline.md section 4): to preview after a lettering string 118 ms, north arrow 75 ms, frame profile 355 ms, building colour 312 ms, hanger 210 ms (all under the 400 ms target); road mode 2053 ms, plate 2937 ms, heights 3983 ms (2 s target missed; the lever is the mesh repair ladder in finish-* and merged, assigned to Task 7). Cold 7.0 s to done, 2.5 s to the last region. Memory flat over 20 warm runs (313 handles before and after).
- [V3.1-O7] Escalation: the adversarial audit of the Task 1 pipeline core runs on a Fable subagent, because judging a 71-stage one-generation cache for aliasing, use-after-delete of WASM handles, findings completeness after partial re-runs and byte parity of seven exporters is the kind of correctness analysis the brief reserves for Fable; an Opus audit that missed a cache aliasing bug would cost more than the escalation.
- [V3.1-P2-1] `regions.rail.width_m` never reached geometry: `osm/normalize.ts` fills every rail way's `width_m` from a per-type table (rail 5, light_rail 4, subway 5, tram 3, else 4 m) and `solid/roads.ts:railWidthGroundM` used the parameter only as a fallback that never fired. Ruling: the parameter is authoritative for every rail ribbon, exactly as its schema description says ("ground width of the rail ribbon before the minimum-feature clamp, metres"); the per-type table stays as SceneGraph data. At the unchanged default of 6 m this widens rail grooves on rail-bearing scenes (the committed Chicago fixture has no rail layer, so the gate fixture and the goldens do not move); recorded as a Fixed entry in the CHANGELOG. Implemented in the Task 7 geometry wave.
- [V3.1-P2-2] `frame_style.lip_depth_mm` (default 0.4, range 0 to 3, no description) was resolved into `FrameStyle.lipDepthMm` and read by nothing. Ruling: it is the sight-edge rebate on the frame lip's inner top edge, a step `lip_depth_mm` deep and `FRAME_SIGHT_EDGE_MM` = 1.0 mm wide, 0 meaning a flat lip; the lettering layout keeps clear of the rebate and the inner-wall attribution band starts below it. The unchanged default therefore adds a 1.0 by 0.4 mm inner step to every default frame; golden numbers that pin the frame are updated with this line as the reason. Implemented in the Task 7 geometry wave; until then the two matrix probes stay red, which is what a red gate is for.
- [V3.1-P2-3] The V3-1 matrix runs its export probes with `force: true` through the export stage: several probes deliberately drive the model past a Stage 4 row (a doubled large_scale past the height ceiling, a 120 mm custom bed under a 180 mm plate) and `export/gate.ts` refuses those files by design; the gate has its own tests and the matrix needs the bytes a passing model would have produced. The two dead-field probes (`regions.rail.width_m`, `frame_style.lip_depth_mm`) stay red until [V3.1-P2-1] and [V3.1-P2-2] are implemented.
- [V3.1-P2-4] `colour.region_slots.attribution` is probed, not exempt: no stage builds an attribution solid, so the leaf's only effect is the slot table the `slot-beyond-profile` finding restates in its one-click fix, which reaches the file through the sidecar's findings block. That is a thin effect; if a later wave finds it not worth keeping, the leaf joins the exemptions and [V3.1-P1-13] grows a fifth entry.
- [V3.1-P2-5] `hanger_magnet.*` sizes the SEPARATE FRAME's magnet mount (`solid/frame.ts`), not the wall hanger, which uses the frozen `MAGNET_D_MM` and `MAGNET_DEPTH_MM`; the schema description says otherwise and is corrected at the re-freeze (description only; no name, type or default changes).
- [V3.1-P1-15] A failing export ships nothing (pipeline audit fix; the author's proposed number P1-13 was taken). The export stage reads validate's findings and refuses, by finding id, when any Stage 4 row (not-manifold, floating-island, exceeds-plate, exceeds-height, wall-too-thin) is at error; the refusal is a PipelineStageError at stage export whose detail.blocking lists the findings, and no files event is posted. ExportRequest.force (CLI --force) writes the files anyway for debugging, with the findings still in the sidecar; the UI never sets it. Warnings never block.
- [V3.1-P1-16] Stage content digests hash {output, channels}, so a stage whose output is unchanged but whose findings, resolved text or mark bands moved still invalidates audit and export; region-ready carries each region's hash and `removed` names every region the model lost, including ones streamed by a superseded run (pipeline audit blockers 1 and 2, pinned by a 14-step aliasing sequence in incremental.test.ts and the client tests).
- [V3.1-P1-17] An export request never supersedes a run: it queues behind the run's done, streams no regions, posts done with a null result and transfers the file bytes; done re-sends the merged mesh and tile meshes only when their hash changed (knownMergedHash / knownTilesHash), and the compat build passes knownSceneHash so the scene is never echoed back (pipeline audit majors 3 to 7).
- [V3.1-P7-1] Binary STL is written on a float32 grid, so `export/stl.ts` hardens the PLACED mesh for float32 before writing (`mesh.hardenForFloat32`: a needle whose three vertices agree to under one float32 step at that coordinate is split at its T-junction; no vertex moves, no face is dropped); the 3MF, written in decimal, and the default Chicago mesh are unchanged. Reason: the Chicago `stl` failed the validator's degenerate_faces row with exactly one face whose 6.2e-7 mm2 area survives in double and vanishes on the float32 grid after the +90 mm placement.
- [V3.1-P7-2] Tiling: `thinPart` no longer drops whole components under 0.01 mm2 before asking whether a wall fits (the validator has no such floor, so a 0.00535 mm2 needle the sliver cutter stranded read 0.037 mm), and `buildTile` prunes every tile solid with `pruneDebris` at the shared 0.01 mm3 after the cut (a 0.000458 mm3 water splinter was a second body). All four tiles of the Chicago 2x2 pass; seam trim +6.42 mm3.
- [V3.1-P7-3] The reference Python STL writer (`services/bake/app/export/stl.py`) gained `separate_float32_pinches`, mirrored by `mesh.separateFloat32Pinches` in the engine: two distinct vertices at exactly one point, both joined to one neighbour, hand that edge to four faces after the STL's unavoidable weld, which `assemble.finalize`'s `float32_defect_count` could not see because it subtracts the double-unique count first. The second vertex moves one float32 step into its own material; nothing is welded, no face is added or dropped. Paris 1 pinch, Tokyo 1, London 2, the other presets 0. The Python side was edited because it is the only place the defect can be closed for the reference pipeline's files; the validator is untouched.
- [V3.1-P7-4] The nightly preset matrix (`scripts/ci-preset-matrix.sh`) exercises the Python reference pipeline on the six committed Overpass fixtures; the browser engine's preset coverage is the export matrix plus build-and-validate on Chicago. A browser-engine run over all six presets (`export:cli --overpass`) is added to the nightly in the Task 8 wave so both pipelines are judged on every preset.
- [V3.1-P2-6] A control never ships ahead of its effect: the Lip depth slider is removed in the Task 2 commit and re-added by the Task 7 geometry wave together with the sight-edge rebate of [V3.1-P2-2]. Apply-palette, merge-to-slots and align-colours patch only the ten colourable regions and leave colour.region_colors.attribution untouched, pinned by a test that exercises the real setters (settings audit blockers 1 and 2).
- [V3.1-P2-7] Matrix strengthened after its audit (docs/handoff/v3-01-matrix-audit.md): preview assertions 119 SPECIFIC / 5 echo-only (the ruled sidecar-echo leaves), export 123 SPECIFIC / 1 (colour.region_slots.attribution, which [V3.1-P2-4] accepts). `force` is a per-probe flag carried by exactly the two probes that trip exceeds-height (large_scale, custom_profile.max_height_mm); every other probe exports through the shipped gate, superseding the blanket force of [V3.1-P2-3]. The two dead-field probes pin the ruled semantics (rail groove width equals the parameter on a narrowing case that a fallback, a max or a sum all fail; a 1.0 mm wide rebate floor at lip top minus lip_depth) and stay red until the Task 7 geometry wave.
- [V3.1-P7-5] `separateFloat32Pinches` (TS) and `separate_float32_pinches` (Python) are transactional like the rest of cleanMesh, with one stated deviation: the volume acceptance bound is max(cleanMesh's relative noise bound, the sum of shift times incident area), because the relative bound alone rejects a legitimate one-float32-step move on a 10 mm cube while accepting the same step on a plate; topology is asserted on the caller's own indices. `fixtures/pinch-parity.json` pins the TS/Python mirror to 0.0 difference and caught a real divergence in the Python groups counter. A face that float32 hardening cannot clear is reported as a `float32-degenerate` warning that reaches the sidecar, the CLI and the Issues badge; the reference validator remains the judge of the row.
- [V3.1-P7-6] STL hardening probes before it welds: a float32 collision scan over the placed vertices, one weld rung only when a candidate exists; 25 ms when nothing is found (was 1235 ms at plate 180 and 6175 ms at 256 for a weld that welded nothing).
- [V3.1-P1-18] Per-triangle building identity on the real solids: every building extrusion carries a distinct manifold original id, and `solid/owners.ts` attributes each shipped triangle of the buildings, band and hero meshes to its building (centroid lookup against the union's runOriginalID runs, with an on-face fallback for pieces the weld split), emitted as `RegionMesh.triangleOwner` indexing `RegionMesh.owners`, transferred with the positions and covered by the region hash. Chicago: 289 owners, 100 % coverage, 8 to 15 ms per finish. This restores per-building tint on the actual meshes after the approximate layer was deleted, and gives Tasks 10 and 11 exact picking without proxies.
- [V3.1-P1-19] The runner queues the terminal events (done, files, cancelled, error) and posts them after the `engine.build` span closes, so a run's own perf row rides on the message the worker drains; before this the row was one message late and absent for the last run, which is why e2e/perf.spec.ts:87 went red once the store stopped running two jobs per preview.
- [V3.1-O8] Correction to the record: `[V3.1-P1-18]` describes the ENGINE half of per-building identity only. The audit of the interrupted work (docs/handoff/v3-06-interrupted-audit.md, C1) found no consumer: nothing under `components/scene` reads `triangleOwner`, `owners` or `buildingTints`, the old consumer `InstancedBuildings.tsx` was deleted with it, and `lib/warnings.ts` still promised tint in the preview. Per-building tint was therefore absent from the preview between the integration commit and its restoration. Lesson recorded because two agents' self-reports each described a half as if it were the whole: a claim that a feature reaches the user is only accepted from a test that reads the user-visible surface.
- [V3.1-P7-7] The sight-edge rebate of [V3.1-P2-2] and the mandatory inner-wall attribution mark coexist: the rebate is cut 1.0 mm wide by `lip_depth_mm` deep into the lip's inner top edge, the lettering band and the north-arrow corner are sized to the 5 mm flat face that remains, and the wall mark starts BELOW the rebate, so `WALL_MARK_MIN_HEIGHT_MM` becomes `FRAME_LIP_MM` minus the default rebate depth (1.6 mm) rather than the literal 2.0 mm of [V3-P7-A5]. That keeps A5's classification of bevel_in, floating and separate at the default and keeps the mark on all four inner walls; the alternative (holding 2.0 mm literally) would have removed the wall mark from every default frame, which the run's attribution invariant forbids. Default frame volume moves 9159.31 to 8900.74 mm3 (rebate 270.40 mm3, wall cut 15.85 instead of 27.69 because the mark is fitted to the shorter wall).
- [V3.1-P7-8] The lip-rebate matrix probe queries the ring in the rebate's own x band (`minAbsX = innerHalf + 0.5`) rather than counting every vertex on the plane, because the attribution glyphs on the inner wall occupy the same z. That is a narrowing toward the field's own physical consequence, not a loosened tolerance: the probe still requires the ring absent at depth 0, present and exactly 1.00 mm wide at the default, and the volume line unchanged. The reason is stated in the probe's comment so it cannot later be read as a weakened assertion.
- [V3.1-P10-1] Contracts advance to schema_version 4 (enum [2,3,4], default 4), additive as always: no PrintParams field is added, and `Building`, `Road` and `AreaFeature` gain optional non-nullable `name`, `osm_id` and `kind`. `gen_py.py` derives "optional, no default, not nullable" and emits a wrap serializer that omits an unset key, so a v1, v2 or v3 SceneGraph still dumps verbatim and the pinned fixture test needed no relaxing. The Python reference deliberately keeps emitting a pre-v4 SceneGraph (docs/handoff/v3-10-objects.md says why).
- [V3.1-P10-2] Object names cost 15.8 per cent of the Chicago SceneGraph (1229.7 to 1423.7 kB), cut by two lossless sparse rules (`osm_id` only where the entity id carries a part suffix, `Road.kind` only where it differs from `class`). The cap is `SCENE_NAME_BUDGET_BYTES` = 96 000, chosen so a scene up to twice Chicago's name density (47.2 kB) loses nothing; overflow drops the smallest footprints first, counts into `EngineStats.names_dropped` and surfaces as the `names-over-budget` warning.
- [V3.1-P10-3] Hover identity is exact for buildings (the per-triangle owner map; a merged block answers as "Merged block") and nearest-entity in plan space for roads, water and green, which have no per-triangle identity. Measured: a centreline point names its own road 5438 of 5439 times; at a ribbon edge the lookup names a road covering the point 99.1 per cent of the time but the exact source way only 59.1 per cent, because 47.9 per cent of those points lie under more than one ribbon and the union carries no identity to separate them; an interior area point names its own polygon 749 of 750 times; 0.09 ms per lookup. The popover states nothing it cannot support.
- [V3.1-P7-9] Ten matrix probes were written against a rebate-free lip and are re-read, not relaxed, now that [V3.1-P2-2] has landed: the seven pocket-extent probes filter their plane query to the flat face, because the default engraving depth and the default rebate depth are both 0.4 mm and therefore share a z plane, so `pocketFloor` was measuring the rebate floor's corners instead of the lettering pocket; and the two size probes assert the relationship (a larger `size_mm` yields a strictly larger fitted cap height, equal to the band-limited maximum for the 4 mm band, 5.41 mm for a 7 mm request and 3.43 mm for the arrow) rather than a lowered constant, so a regression that stopped honouring the field still fails. Every changed reading is tabulated with its before and after in docs/handoff/v3-07-perf.md and in the matrix's own audit trail. The alternative of keeping the 5 mm band would have put ink on the rebate, against the ruling.
- [V3.1-T6-1] A skeleton may be shown only while a request is genuinely in flight AND there is no previous value to show. A first build shows one; every rebuild keeps the previous figures on screen, dimmed and labelled as the previous computation, because a superseded number is a better answer than a grey bar; the idle, error and completed states carry none. Enforced by shape, not by name: ActionBar.test.tsx scans the rendered markup of the estimate card, the stats card, the results panel and the bar in all three settled states for a data-testid ending -skeleton or the fc-pulse keyframe, and e2e/actionbar.spec.ts scans the whole live page in the same three states (gate V3-2).
- [V3.1-T6-2] A cancelled export reads as cancelled, not as a refusal. The store cannot tell them apart, since cancelPipeline leaves pipeline.error null (a cancel is not an error) and requestExport then falls through to "The engine could not build a model", so the bar classifies from the TRANSITION: a run that stopped running without a new result and without an error was stopped by somebody. A cancelled export gets its own quiet surface, names the stage it stopped at, and keeps the previous export's files and Blob URLs. exportStatusLabel's failed case reads "No file written", which is true of every failed export ([V3.1-P1-15]); the sentence explaining why belongs to the bar. store/editor.ts:requestExport still writes the misleading string and its owner should retire it.
- [V3.1-T6-3] The optimistic "Fixed" mark belongs to its row and is retired when the row is. Proven end to end by an in-page recorder that logs every state the fix button passes through, because an expect.poll over the same transient passes with the defect present and therefore asserts nothing.
- [V3.1-T6-4] The export writer's findings reach the Issues badge, closing the third clause of [V3.1-P7-5]: IssuesBadge folds exportState.findings in behind the live list, deduplicated with the live row winning (the export's list opens with a copy of the model's own, so the other direction would let a stale measurement overwrite a current one) and only while the export is not stale.
- [V3.1-T6-5] The control row's box is an invariant, measured: everything that can change size renders after the controls, and e2e/actionbar.spec.ts records the action row's rectangle on every animation frame across a run, a refusal and a finished export, asserting one distinct value. Unreachable branches in the bar's own state were removed rather than left as untestable claims.
- [V3.1-P10-4] Per-building tint reaches the pixels through the owner map: `buildGeometry` writes each triangle its building's colour into the same expanded colour attribute the recess bands use. Two colour models share that buffer: untinted it stays a multiplier on the region's `colorHex`, so every existing region renders through the byte-identical path; tinted it holds the absolute colour against a white material, because a per-building colour is not a multiple of the region's and a multiplier would divide by zero on a dark region. Colours go through `new Color(hex)` for the sRGB to working-space conversion three does not apply to attributes. Verified on a real engine build (3 buildings, 3 owners, 56 shipped triangles all owned, 3 distinct colours), not only in a unit test. Consequently `lib/warnings.ts`'s sentence, "tint affects the preview and the OBJ export only", is now exactly true and was left alone.
- [V3.1-P10-5] Picking is proxy-free: a click on the buildings region maps `faceIndex` through `triangleOwner` to a building id, with the same 4 px press-to-release slop so an orbit that ends on a building is not a pick, and a merged block or unattributed triangle is refused rather than promoted to a neighbour. `BuildingPickProxies` and its props are deleted, which removes the first named exception from docs/handoff/v3-01-integration.md and tightens the viewport rule: no PrintParams object crosses the canvas boundary at all. The deleted layer's invariant is replaced by one asserting picking goes through the owner lookup.
- [V3.1-P7-10] The engine's remaining cost was not geometry: `cleanMesh` was 2586 of 5126 ms on a Chicago export (2031 of it on the merged solid, across all three weld rungs), and the cost was string-keyed grids and edge maps. Sort-and-sweep pair finding in `weld`, a needle-only edge index in `splitNeedles`, typed-array `openEdges`, and `pruneDebrisCounted` counting bodies off the mesh instead of a second decompose, all proven byte-identical against the previous code on 56 region and option cases. Rebuilt-from-primitives, double decompose, per-polygon Clipper2 and the drape were measured and ruled out rather than assumed. Result: export engine 5126 to 2797 ms, CLI wall 5470 to 3170 ms (42 per cent, against the Task 7 target of 40), region phase 1142 to 525 ms, merged 2031 to 314 ms.
- [V3.1-P7-11] Task 7 latency after the work, measured on a machine that was not quiet (all three runs are in docs/handoff/v3-07-perf.md): the five cheap changes reach the preview in 63 to 163 ms against the 400 ms target; `road_mode` 1133 ms and `plate_mm` 1574 ms against the 2 s target; `heights.floor_height_m` 2359 ms (best of three 2215) MISSES it, and the reason is stated rather than hidden: `normalise` alone is 492 to 570 ms of that figure and lives in `lib/engine/osm`, outside this wave's ownership, with the remainder in Clipper2 and one manifold boolean. Closing it needs the ingest path optimised, which is left as the named follow-up.
- [V3.1-P8-1] Task 8 measured, and reported without a claimed win it did not measure (docs/handoff/v3-08-siteperf.md). Confirmed: the service worker takes a return visit past the cache lifetime from 3 184 699 B to zero SUBRESOURCE bytes (corrected by [V3.1-P8-7]: the document is network-first and is still fetched every visit; CDP reports 0 for anything the worker answered, so the original 0 B figure counted the HTML as saved when it is not); the bundled preset asset takes the Chicago ingest step from 5 496 ms live to 184 ms, and one live run in three produced no preview at all in 163.8 s across four attempts, reproducing the baseline's pathology. Not claimed: an end-to-end win, because the medians overlap at about 17 s on this host where the engine build dominates. First-load JS is 293 kB against the baseline's 259 kB, and the note states plainly that twenty commits of features moved it and nothing in Task 8 could have; the chunk split is kept for time to interactive, not for bytes.
- [V3.1-P8-2] `print.spec.ts:111` hung forever rather than failing: it read the locator twice (`count()` then `isDisabled()`), and `isDisabled()` carries no timeout of its own, so a row retired by the debounced rebuild between the two calls waited until the test's own 180 s died. Replaced by one synchronous DOM read, which the action bar's recorder then built on. A Playwright assertion that can hang instead of failing is a defect in the test, not flakiness in the app.
- [V3.1-P7-12] The lip probe reads its ring in the plate's CORNER squares (`cornerRingAt`, both axes) rather than the single-axis band I first ruled: the east and west walls' attribution glyphs share the rebate's x, so a `minAbsX` filter alone would have dropped the 84 mm corners and measured the rebate's width as zero. The corner reading keeps every discriminating line and adds one (the default floor must be gone at depth 0). The seven pocket readings band the frame read to the flat face (max of the absolute coordinates at least 85.25 on the 180 mm plate) with their thresholds unchanged. The size probes assert over 4 and under 7 with the fitted 5.41 mm to two decimals, and the arrow asserts 3.43 equal to its own band maximum before and 2 under it after. Every comment names the shared 0.4 mm plane and the attribution glyphs, so none of it can later be read as a loosened tolerance. Matrix: 132 probes green, KNOWN_DEFECTS empty.
- [V3.1-P8-3] The webpack cache-group split is kept and its justification corrected. Measured on two worktrees of af02c98 differing only in that block, three runs each: first-load JS is 293 kB in BOTH arms, so the split buys nothing there; it is worth 9 334 bytes and three requests on a cold load and 8 594 bytes on disk. The claim the comment leaned on could not be reproduced, because webpack's deterministic module ids already keep vendor chunks on their content hashes when a module is added; next.config.ts now carries the table and withdraws the claim. Reverting would add three files and 9 kB to every cold load to remove a claim rather than a cost, which is the wrong trade.
- [V3.1-P11-1] An override region (`override_1` and its siblings) takes its filament slot and colour from its own `object_overrides` entry, with the parent layer's slot and colour as the fallback; the stage declares `object_overrides` as the claim that moves it. The rejected alternative was declaring `colour.region_slots.override_N` in the contract, which would have added four fields no control writes and no user can set, so the V3-1 matrix would owe them probes it could not honestly satisfy and the graph test would assert over fiction. The schema description that a per-object slot "moves the object into an override region of its own" is what invited the wrong reading and is corrected at the re-freeze.
- [V3.1-P13-1] Project files are `.framecraft`, envelope version 4, plain JSON: a maximal project measures 19 907 bytes and gzip would save 17.8 kB, which is not worth losing a file that is readable, greppable and diffable. Version 3 envelopes and the legacy `.framecraft.json` name both load, migrate silently and say so once. Object overrides, surface labels and hero selections are print parameters, so `params` carries them whole; an `extras` passthrough keeps a future build's unknown top-level block from being deleted on the next save. Only `.framecraft` is registered with the OS, because no platform binds a double extension.
- [V3.1-P15-1] `apps/web/package.json`'s version is the one source of truth, feeding the bundle constants and a stamping step for the three desktop manifests, with drift asserted as a test failure. With no git the commit reads "source build" and never an invented sha; `SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` and two pin variables are honoured for reproducible builds. One About dialog serves web and desktop, because the desktop renders the same bundle and a second native window would be a second answer free to drift. The copyright holder is NAXHQ across the licence, README, both package manifests, `Cargo.toml`, the Tauri publisher and copyright fields, the footer and the About dialog; the MIT terms are untouched.
- [V3.1-P8-4] The service worker is tested by running its shipped bytes: `lib/serviceWorkerScript.test.ts` loads `public/sw.js` in a `node:vm` sandbox and exercises all five policy rows, the caps, the warm-up, `activate` and the sub-path, six of which fail against the pre-fix worker. `e2e/siteperf.spec.ts` brings its own origin (the shipped worker in a temp tree served on an assigned port) so it needs neither the dev server nor a built `out/`, which nightly never produces, and asserts a return visit at transferSize 0 with every response served from the worker. Registration is observable through a `data-fc-sw` marker, so the spec fails if the app stops calling it. Two worker defects closed on the way: the stale-while-revalidate refresh is held open with `waitUntil`, and the stable-URL cache carries the build id so it cannot run a deploy behind, while the content-addressed caches deliberately do not.
- [V3.1-P8-5] The duplicate 541 470 byte `manifold.wasm` came from a dead `new URL("manifold.wasm", import.meta.url)` branch in the emscripten glue; a client-only `emit: false` rule stops the second write. Measured on a controlled pair of builds from identical sources: 51 files and 5 960 875 B to 50 files and 5 419 405 B, exactly one copy. Brotli precompression saves 18.3 per cent against gzip on the self-hosted path but takes the release zip from 1 935 172 to 4 964 854 bytes, so it stays wired to the zip job only.
- [V3.1-P8-6] The service worker's registration decision is a named reason, not a boolean: `registrationDecision(env)` returns "on" or one of "off:tauri", "off:dev", "off:insecure", "off:unsupported", "off:killed", written to the document as `data-fc-sw`, with `shouldRegister` reduced to a comparison against it so the two cannot drift. The reason is what makes the desktop gate testable: under `next dev` the app registers no worker whatever happens, so a test that only observes the absence of a worker passes with the Tauri gate deleted; asserting the reason is exactly "off:tauri" proves the gate instead. A test that cannot fail when the feature is removed is not evidence, and this is the second time this run that principle decided a design (see [V3.1-T6-3]).
- [V3.1-P8-7] Two measurement corrections found by probing a spec before trusting it. `page.waitForFunction` does not await an async predicate: it resolves on the first poll because a Promise is truthy, measured at 14 ms for `async () => false` against a 3 s timeout, so three waits in the new site-performance spec were silent no-ops and now use `expect.poll`; no other spec in `e2e/` uses an async predicate. And the "0 B return visit" figure is a CDP artefact: `encodedDataLength` reports 0 for anything a service worker answered, the document included, while the document really is fetched every visit because it is network-first. Resource Timing separates them: cold 90 991 B of subresources plus a 1 495 B document, return visit 0 B of subresources plus the same 1 495 B document. The saving is the whole subresource weight and none of the HTML, and that is what the spec now asserts.
- [V3.1-P15-2] Every exporter's provenance now reads `FrameCraft ${appVersion()}` from the single version source, and the six asserting tests (not four: `solid/attribution.test.ts` carried two more literals, in the provenance list and the sidecar block) assert against the constant plus a shape check `/^FrameCraft \d+\.\d+\.\d+/`, which is stronger than the old literal because it fails when the version stops resolving or stops being a version and cannot pass by the writer agreeing with itself. `lib/version.ts` resolves lazily and `export-cli.ts` sets the variable before the engine's modules parse, verified by running the real CLI: the generic 3MF reads `FrameCraft 3.1.0` in both `Application` and `framecraft:generator`. The reference validator pins the OSM glyph line and not the version, so no provenance row moves. Under vitest the version resolves to the unbuilt placeholder by design, so the tiles golden will not move on future release bumps.
- [V3.1-P15-3] The tiles golden's move was attributed by experiment rather than assumption: re-running the whole test with the version pinned to 3.0.0 reproduces the previous hash exactly, which proves the version stamp moved one metadata value and Tasks 11 and 12 moved the rest, since `object_overrides` and `labels` joined PrintParams and every 3MF carries PrintParams in its Description. All three hashes and the method are in the test comment.

- `[V3.1-O9]` **The no-em-dash rule binds authored prose, not pinned identifiers.** A scan
  found 24 em dashes left in `apps/web/{components,lib}`: 17 are source comments, one is the
  generated header of `lib/contracts.ts`, one is the `[−–—]` character class in
  `lib/coordinates.ts` that normalises a pasted coordinate (deleting it would break the
  paste path), and six are the preset display labels (`Chicago — Loop` and its five
  siblings, duplicated in `lib/presets.ts` and `lib/engine/osm/presets.ts`). The rule is
  applied to every document, decision line and UI string this run authors or rewrites. It is
  NOT applied retroactively to the preset labels: their text is asserted in pinned fixtures
  and goldens, and rewriting six separators to satisfy a typographic preference would put a
  frozen-fixture churn in front of the remaining correctness work. Recorded so the next run
  can make the change deliberately with the fixtures in the same commit. The v2-era handoff
  notes keep their em dashes too: `docs/handoff/` is a historical record, not live prose.

- `[V3.1-O10]` **A stale build lock is taken over, not broken, and never over a live process.**
  `artifacts/build.lock` still named `w3close2`, an agent killed by an account usage limit
  fourteen hours earlier, while an unrelated Playwright suite ran under pid 60236 against
  the static server on :3000. The lock file is therefore worthless as a signal and the
  running pid is the only real one. Ruling: an agent that finds a holder whose owner is dead
  rewrites the holder file with its own name and timestamp rather than removing the
  directory, so the next arrival still sees a live holder; and it waits on the actual
  process regardless of what the file says. The screenshot capture in particular waits,
  because the suite it would race carries wall-clock perf assertions and a corrupted
  measurement is worse than a late screenshot.
- `[V3.1-O11]` **Task 12 is not shippable as an engine half.** `lib/engine/solid/labels.ts`,
  the `labels` stage and `print_params.labels` all exist while nothing in `components/` or
  `store/` reads or writes `labels`, so by `[V3.1-O8]` the feature reaches no user and is not
  claimed anywhere: not in the README, not as a roadmap line, not hedged. The label agent's
  remaining budget goes to the store path, the viewport gizmo (sharing `lib/labelAnchor.ts`
  with the engine so the two cannot drift) and an end-to-end test that asserts a manipulated
  label in both the preview and the exported file. A smaller Tier A that reaches the user
  beats a larger one that does not.

- `[V3.1-O12]` **The build lock serialises builds and the shared server, not every Playwright
  run.** w6-labels3 was about to idle behind w7-swfix's lock while holding its own static
  server on :3012, its own freshly built `out/` and an `--output` directory in scratch. That
  run contends for nothing the lock protects, so it was told to go without taking it. The
  lock covers exactly two things: a `next build` writing `out/`, and contention for the
  shared server on :3000. A fully isolated run takes neither the lock nor a wait.
- `[V3.1-O13]` **The `out/` tree on :3000 is stale and is not a valid measurement or capture
  target.** It was built at 03:53, before Tasks 4, 5, 10, 11 and 12 landed, so a screenshot
  taken from it would show an interface that no longer exists and a latency read against it
  would measure a superseded build. w7-readme caught this and captured from a throwaway
  `next dev` instead, which is the right call and is why the v3.1 screenshots show the real
  current UI. Every agent measuring or capturing from here on builds its own tree.
- `[V3.1-O14]` **An undeclared stage read is fixed by declaring the input, never by loosening
  the strict-claims Proxy.** The Proxy is what makes the per-stage memoization sound: an
  undeclared read means the cache key does not cover everything the stage depends on, so the
  cache can serve a stale result after a settings change. If declaring the input honestly
  costs latency, both numbers get reported and the slower correct one wins.

- `[V3.1-P7-13]` **The last latency target is met, and the cause was digest granularity, not
  arithmetic.** Changing `heights.floor_height_m` cost 2668 ms to preview on this host today
  and now costs 206 to 223 ms across three runs; to done went 4787 ms to 2025-2133 ms.
  `normalise` claimed `heights.*` correctly, but the scene's own digest was every reader's
  cache key, so a building-only change re-ran the four surface stages, bridges and trees,
  none of which read a building, and their fresh handles then dragged base, sit and every
  ground region behind them. The fix is part digests (`normalise#ground`,
  `repair-buildings#footprint`, 5-10 ms and 2-4 ms) with the six ground stages keyed on
  them, and a part-keyed stage is handed a scene view whose other layers throw on read, so a
  part digest cannot go stale in silence. Separately the ingest's 646 ms was 508 ms of
  water/green dissolve and under 5 ms of height rules, so normalise now projects once per
  fetch output and applies heights per run, byte-identical on Chicago and New York across
  five rule sets. All eight Task 7 targets are now met; non-invalidating changes re-measured
  at 66-152 ms against the 400 ms target.
- `[V3.1-P7-14]` **Today's road_mode and plate_mm readings are not trusted and must be taken
  again on a quiet host.** They read 2127-2822 ms and 3104-3228 ms today, before and after
  w7-perf3's change alike, against 1133 ms and 1574 ms recorded in v3-07, on a host carrying
  twelve to sixteen node processes all session including several orphaned by killed agents.
  A number that moves when nothing touching it moved is measuring the host, not the app. The
  v3-07 figures stand as the record until a quiet-host re-measurement replaces them, and
  that re-measurement is a release gate item, not an optional tidy-up.

- `[V3.1-P15-4]` **A chrome control with no parameter is classified out of scope, not given a
  fake catalog row.** T13/T15 added `DesktopProjectOpener`, `SiteFooter` and `AboutDialog`
  under `components/editor`, and the control catalog's exhaustive partition classified them
  as SCANNED by default, which failed two of its own assertions. The tempting fix, adding
  `CONTROLS` rows for `about-button` and `about-dialog-close`, is forbidden: a catalog row
  must name `PrintParams` leaves that a pipeline stage claims, and those two buttons open and
  close a dialog without writing anything, so the rows would have been exactly the inert
  controls this run bans. They take `OUT_OF_SCOPE_FILES` entries with stated reasons, which
  is the test's own designed mechanism and follows the existing `ShortcutSheet` and
  `AdjustmentsChip` precedent. Nothing was weakened: the exhaustiveness assertion, the
  reason-length floor and the "no row for a control nothing renders" check all still run.

- `[V3.1-P7-15]` **A latency figure produced by a stage serving stale output is not a
  latency figure.** `repair-buildings` reads `height_exaggeration` through
  `T.building_top_mm_for` for the tower-stacking decision but never declared it, so changing
  `height_exaggeration.multiplier` used to preview in 95-107 ms across 35 stages while
  `repair-buildings` quietly served a result computed for the previous multiplier. With the
  input declared it costs 225-536 ms across 37 stages, the upper figure taken under a
  concurrent vitest run, and the ground still stays cached through
  `repair-buildings#footprint`. The change is a slowdown on paper and a correctness fix in
  fact, it sits far inside the 2000 ms invalidating budget, and the old number is struck from
  the record rather than kept as a better-looking one. This is a different bug from the
  heights miss, which was over-invalidation rather than a key that failed to cover a read,
  but both are the same invariant: the cache key must cover everything the stage touches.
  `heights.floor_height_m` is unaffected at 267-279 ms under the same load.

- `[V3.1-P13-2]` **The OSM credit becomes one source, or a test that fails when the copies
  diverge.** The T13/T15 audit found `© OpenStreetMap contributors` written as four
  independent literals (`lib/version.ts`, `lib/engine/solid/attribution.ts` which is what gets
  engraved, `lib/engine/export/common.ts` which is what goes into file metadata, and
  `components/map/LocationPicker.tsx`), with only one pinned by a test, and `version.ts`
  documenting its copy as the one the exporters use when they never import it. Attribution
  surviving in the metadata and the engraved marks is a stated correctness invariant of this
  project, so four unpinned copies mean it can stop being true in three places silently. One
  source, or an assertion that all four are identical. A comment asking future readers to keep
  them in sync is not a mechanism.
- `[V3.1-P13-3]` **Four Rust unit tests that have never executed are not coverage.** `cargo`
  appears in no Makefile target, script or workflow, and `tauri build` does not compile
  `#[cfg(test)]` code, so the desktop open-with contract has no execution evidence anywhere
  and the audit's account of it is a code read. `cargo test --lib` is wired into a workflow
  this run. On this host Smart App Control blocks fresh executables inside the repo tree
  (os error 4551), so `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` points into the scratchpad to run them locally first;
  a step is not wired in until it has been seen to pass.
- `[V3.1-P13-4]` **A leading hyphen in a filename is a file, not a flag.** A project named
  `-notes.framecraft` was silently skipped by the desktop launch argument filter and could not
  be double-click opened. Filtering by position or an explicit terminator costs nothing, so
  this is fixed rather than documented as a trade.

- `[V3.1-P11-2]` **Task 11 reaches the user; the gap was the evidence rung, not the feature.**
  The reach audit ran the eleven `object_overrides` leaves through the matrix harness and read
  the written 3MF rather than the sidecar: `hidden` takes the model from 19509 to 9079 mm3,
  `height_scale` 0.5 gives 14345, `hero` 9079, `slot`/`color` produce an `override_1` part,
  `width_scale` 762 to 1475, `road_mode` moves the road out of `roads`, `tint` adds one entry
  to `buildingTints`. Every one of those is a measured effect, so no leaf may take a matrix
  exemption, and those figures are the expected values the probes are written against. Two
  probes need fixture work first: `fixture.ts:area()` omits the id on every water and green
  polygon, so no test scene has one for `raise_mm` to act on, and `osm_id`/`layer` need a base
  row that bears an effect.
- `[V3.1-P5-4]` **The settings search must cover every group, because that is what it claims.**
  `settingsSearch.ts:50` excludes the `output` group, so pressing `/` and typing "format"
  finds nothing, while the control's own help says it searches "across every group" and the
  README says it "finds any control by name". The rule this run has applied throughout is that
  a control does what it says; the fix is to include the group, not to soften two pieces of
  copy to match a narrower search.
- `[V3.1-P15-5]` **A version stamp nothing reads on a real page is unproven.** The build stamp
  and the About dialog are exercised only through `renderToStaticMarkup`, where `buildInfo()`
  always returns `0.0.0-unbuilt`, so nothing checks that a built page shows a real version and
  no spec opens the dialog. The footer's OSM, Photon and Nominatim attribution is read live in
  `search.spec.ts` and is the model to follow.

- `[V3.1-O15]` **The checkpoint commit `7d02e0e` shipped a red gate step, and that is recorded
  rather than quietly fixed.** I ran `tsc --noEmit` before committing and did not run eslint, so
  `apps/web/scripts/_degen-variants.ts` went in with one `prefer-const` error and two unused
  variables, which fails `eslint . --max-warnings 0` and therefore `make gate` step 3. The
  agent that found it asked before touching it, correctly: a silent fix would have hidden that
  a checkpoint claiming a clean typecheck was never linted. The rule for the rest of the run is
  that a commit runs both, and this run has held other people's work to exactly that standard.
- `[V3.1-P5-5]` **The settings search is fixed per row, not per group.** The instruction was to
  include the `output` group; the implementation instead replaced the group-level exclusion with
  a rule that indexes a row when it writes a `PrintParams` leaf. That is the better fix: the next
  control landing in that bucket becomes searchable without anyone noticing a second time, and
  the help copy stays true by construction rather than by maintenance. The two tests encoding the
  old premise were re-pointed to the new rule rather than relaxed.

- `[V3.1-P7-16]` **`[V3.1-P7-13]`'s "all eight targets met" is amended: it holds only when no
  surface-bearing override exists.** The T7 audit found that a single road override
  (`layer: "road"` with a colour) puts `heights.floor_height_m` back to 2674 ms across 57
  stages, because `surface-overrides` is still keyed on the whole scene and its surface output
  carries handles, so every ground stage's key moves with it. That is the same over-invalidation
  the close-out fixed for the ordinary path, surviving on the path a user reaches the moment
  they colour one road. The claim is therefore conditional until `surface-overrides` is part-keyed
  like the six ground stages, and this is a fix rather than a caveat: a latency target that only
  holds for users who have not touched a feature we shipped in this same run is not met.
  `[V3.1-P7-14]` already withholds two readings pending a quiet host, so the honest present
  statement is seven targets met unconditionally, the eighth met only without a surface override,
  and two rows unmeasured.
- `[V3.1-P7-17]` **The part-digest guard is added to `repair-buildings#footprint` for symmetry.**
  The six ground stages get a scene view whose other layers throw on read, which is what stops a
  part digest going stale in silence, but the `repair-buildings` part digest has no equivalent
  guard: stages receive the whole repair record. All six read `.footprint` only today, so nothing
  is stale, which is exactly when the guard is cheap to add. A safety mechanism that covers one
  of two part digests protects whichever one the next author does not touch.

- `[V3.1-P7-18]` **`mergeRecessRidges` now runs frame-on, closing the `[V3-P7-fix]` follow-up.**
  It was gated to the frame-off path, so a frame-on model kept the ridges between grown recess
  layers and the thin walls they leave. It now runs in both, with grown layers re-cut from the
  later layers that overlap them. This is what took the hillside scene from several places under
  the minimum wall to one, and the test asserts the singular wording rather than accepting either.
- `[V3.1-P7-19]` **Five of the six preset cities now pass the reference validator, and Tokyo
  ships as a stated limitation.** Chicago, New York, Paris, London and San Francisco all read
  ALL CHECKS PASS from the browser engine. Four causes were fixed: Clipper2 rounding of shared
  points left slits in the buildings union (a 1/1024 mm snap after deburr plus a transactional
  sliver sweep took Paris from 180 faces under 1e-7 to none); no slice-profile repair existed in
  the browser engine (a port of the reference's took New York from seven thin regions to none);
  `mergeRecessRidges` was frame-off gated; and `fittedSolid`'s 0.2 mm seam rim stood into deeper
  recesses (London 0.206 to 0.817 mm). Tokyo remains at `min_wall` 0.254 and is OPEN with an
  owner. Two candidate repairs were built and backed out because each caused a worse defect, a
  round-join opening producing 45 false wings and a refused export, and slit fusing producing
  hairline slivers in the tiling test. A defect we have measured, diagnosed and declined to
  paper over is an acceptable ship; an unexplained one is not. README and CHANGELOG say five of
  six.
- `[V3.1-P7-20]` **The STEP writer's grid is closed.** It writes 12 decimals, welds on written
  text and counts `gridLostTriangles`; Chicago at plate 256 now loses no faces to the grid, and
  `residueParts` intersects the simplified and raw openings.

- `[V3.1-P7-21]` **The rail probe caught a real geometry defect, and it was fixed by rule rather
  than by re-pinning the golden.** `regions.rail.proud_mm = -0.5` measured 265.65 mm3 against an
  expected 254.09, and the first reading was that a contract default had moved. It had not:
  `fittedSolid`'s seam trim was removing the RAISED rail's 0.2 mm rim wherever it crossed the
  road and water pockets, worth the whole 11.55 mm3, confirmed by switching the trim off and
  watching the probe pass. The trim now applies to recessed layers only, trimmed by deeper
  recessed layers, which is the reference implementation's own inlay rule, so a raised ribbon
  keeps its rim whether or not it crosses a groove. This is the case the 132-probe matrix exists
  for: a probe that is re-pinned when its number moves would have shipped a rail missing its rim
  on every model, silently. Rail probes are 5 of 5, Paris and London still read ALL CHECKS PASS.
- `[V3.1-P7-22]` **The hillside assertion is pinned to the singular wording.** The frame-on ridge
  merge took that scene from several places under the minimum wall to one, so the test asserts
  `The narrowest wall measures` exactly rather than accepting either wording. It passes today and
  fails the day the count goes back up, which an alternation covering both could never do. The
  `synthetic.test.ts` upper bound of 1.002 is unchanged beside its new `1 - 1e-4` lower bound, so
  the volume is still pinned in both directions.

- `[V3.1-P7-23]` **`[V3.1-P7-16]` is closed: the eighth target now holds with a surface override.**
  `heights.floor_height_m` to the preview is 216-227 ms with one coloured road and 211-215 ms
  without, 36 stages in both, against 2674 ms across 57 stages before. All eight Task 7 targets
  are met without qualification, subject only to the quiet-host re-measurement `[V3.1-P7-14]`
  still owes. Closing the `surface-overrides` hole exposed the next layer down, which is the
  useful part of the finding: `region-override_N` read the buildings whole, so it is now keyed on
  a digest of the recoloured bands, and every building-bearing finish on a hash of sorted owner
  ids. The first attempt keyed on the owner map's keys, which are per-run kernel original ids,
  and that broke warm-equals-cold region hashing. A cache key built from an identifier the kernel
  regenerates each run is not a cache key, and only a warm-against-cold comparison catches it.
- `[V3.1-P7-24]` **The part-exposure table is the mechanism, not a convention.** `ctx.input()`
  serves a part-keyed input through `runner.inputPartView` using a declared `PART_EXPOSURE` map,
  with everything outside a part's exposure throwing and naming the stage, and parts with no
  exposure listed served whole so the table says plainly which are guarded. The registry check
  asserts all seven footprint readers run clean through it. This is what `[V3.1-P7-17]` asked
  for: the guard covers both part digests rather than whichever one an author remembered.
  Separately, the projection's five ground arrays are now shallow-frozen at source, which
  converts the latent aliasing the audit flagged into something the suite would fail on.

- `[V3.1-P2-3]` **The lip rebate caps the north arrow, and that is correct behaviour with an
  outdated test premise.** `test_v2_north_arrow_in_a_real_bake_points_where_project_py_puts_north`
  fails on its own premise line, "4 mm is under the lip's cap, so the size used is the size asked
  for": with `frame_style.lip_depth_mm` at its 0.4 mm default the sight-edge rebate takes the
  lip's flat top face from 6.0 mm to 5.0 mm (`FRAME_SIGHT_EDGE_MM`), so a 4 mm arrow is now
  shrunk and warns. The narrowing is deliberate and `[V3.1-P2-2]` chose it: everything laid on
  the lip, the text band, the ornaments and the arrow's corner square, is sized and centred on
  the flat face so nothing the layout places can land on the step, because a mark straddling the
  rebate prints badly. So the engine is right and the test's premise is stale. The fix is NOT to
  drop the premise assertion: the test keeps proving the arrow points north, its premise is made
  true again by asking for a size the narrowed face can hold, and a new assertion pins the cap
  itself so the narrowing is covered rather than merely tolerated. The reference validator's
  verdict on direction, which is what this test exists for, is untouched.

- `[V3.1-P7-25]` **Fixing five preset cities made Tokyo worse, and that is reported as a
  regression rather than folded into the existing limitation.** Tokyo was FAIL `min_wall` 0.254
  on two regions; at HEAD it is FAIL `min_wall` 0.204 on four, deterministic across two builds.
  The two documented sites are unchanged; the two new ones sit at z 2.5750 and 2.6250 with an
  identical 0.2040 width across the whole ground slice, and both are `recess_probe_zs` heights
  that ran and were clean before, which points at one base ridge standing through both bands.
  The prime suspect is this run's own `[V3.1-P7-18]`, `mergeRecessRidges` moving to the frame-on
  path. A defect that gets worse while its neighbours are fixed is not an acceptable silent
  trade: it is assigned, and if it cannot be fixed the stated limitation carries the measured
  0.204 on four regions, never the friendlier 0.254 on two. Chicago's `min_wall` also moved,
  0.938 to 0.874, with the same verdict, and that is recorded so the next reader does not
  mistake it for drift.
- `[V3.1-P2-4]` **The north arrow's corner anchor was measuring against the wrong point.** Fixing
  the stale premise in the arrow test exposed a real defect rather than only a test problem: the
  corner square was anchored at `FRAME_WIDTH_MM / 2` while the rebate had moved the flat face's
  centre, putting the anchor 0.5 mm outward of where the square actually sits. It now anchors on
  `lip_face_width_mm / 2`. The direction proof is intact at 330.55 degrees against
  `project.LocalFrame`'s 331.42, and still fails by 57.03 degrees when the engine is reverted to
  the `-rotation_deg` bug it was written against. The cap has its own test now, which fails when
  `FRAME_SIGHT_EDGE_MM` is set to zero.

- `[V3.1-P11-3]` **The matrix now covers all eleven `object_overrides` leaves, with nothing
  exempted and every probe pinning a measured quantity.** 155 of 155 in `matrix.test.ts`;
  `EXEMPT` is unchanged at ten leaves and `KNOWN_DEFECTS` is still empty. The probes pin
  quantities rather than directions: the tower's own 10430.27 mm3 moving whole for `hidden`,
  `hero` and `slot`, an exact halving of the roof above the plate for `height_scale`, the ribbon
  mirrored about the plate top for `road_mode`, 14 m times `mmPerM` at 5.88 mm for `width_scale`,
  both faces exactly plus 1.00 mm for `raise_mm`, and the `Kd` row equal to the tint hex. Every
  figure the reach audit measured reproduced exactly on the current tree despite the geometry
  work in between, which is a useful independent confirmation that the snap grid and the repair
